UNIVERSITY: University of Siena
COURSE: [AE003] EDUCATION AND TRAINING SCIENCES
SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unisi.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10255

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 3 years. Program: 2019. Status: Open. Credits: 180 CFU. Language: Italian. Type: Degree Courses. Regulatory Decree: D.M. 270/2004. Degree Class: L-19 - Class of degrees in Education and Training Sciences. Inter-University Course: No. Open Access: Yes. Location: Arezzo. Department: Department of Social, Political and Cognitive Sciences. Website: https://scienze-educazione-formazione.unisi.it. Informative Website: https://scienze-educazione-formazione.unisi.it. Admission Requirements: Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]. Upper Secondary School Diploma. Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse. Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni. Roles and Responsibilities: Student mobility: NICOLINA BOSCO. President/President of the teaching committee: LORETTA FABBRI. Program, texts and objectives. Admission requirements: Upper secondary school diploma or other qualification obtained abroad recognized as suitable. Professional status conferred by the degree. Early childhood educator: The graduate in Education Sciences who has followed the "Early Childhood Educator" curriculum will be able to perform the role of educator and facilitator of development in early childhood services; in particular, they may be an educator in nurseries, micro-nurseries, early childhood centers, family nurseries, company nurseries; educator and socio-educational animator in early childhood services; educator in public and private services for families with children. Social educator: The graduate in Education Sciences who has followed the "Social Educator" curriculum will be able to perform the role of educator in associations or companies or rehabilitation communities in the non-profit sector; they may carry out the professions of educator and technician in state and non-state recreational and cultural services, in territorial services for the person, in social assistance, in services for mediation and integration, guidance and educational assistance for people with disabilities. Characteristics of the final exam: To be admitted to the final exam, the student must have passed all the exams and completed the training activities provided for in the Study Plan. The final exam consists of the drafting and discussion of a written paper (possibly supplemented by a visual or multimedia support) on a topic chosen from those relevant to the course of study, possibly referring to the activities carried out during the internship, or to work experiences or stays abroad, etc., aimed at demonstrating the acquisition of specific knowledge, skills and abilities as well as the ability to reflect critically on them. The exam, deepened by the student under the guidance of a teacher-tutor, is discussed before a specially appointed Commission, which will evaluate the exam out of one hundred and ten, with the possible awarding of honors. In determining the final grade, the student's academic career and participation in activities particularly relevant to their training will also be taken into account. Knowledge required for access: Students in possession of an upper secondary school diploma or other qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable (Art. 6, paragraph 1 of D.M. 270/2004) may access the Degree Course in Education and Training Sciences. Access to the Degree Course requires: a basic preparation that allows students to approach the contents of the disciplines for which study is planned, good analytical and synthesis skills of texts of medium complexity. At the beginning of the academic year, the Entrance Orientation Test will be held as required by D.M. 270/2004. The organization of seminars on university study methodologies and on the writing of scientific texts is guaranteed. If the outcome of the Orientation Test is negative, the Degree Course establishes the specific training obligations that each student must fulfill within the first year of the Course. The Department, in conjunction with the University Language Center, provides for the assessment of knowledge of the English language (knowledge of the language at least equivalent to level A2/2 is required). Degree awarded: Degree in Education and Training Sciences. Teaching delivery method: Conventional. Knowledge and understanding. General area: The graduate in Education and Training Sciences: - has acquired theoretical, epistemological and methodological knowledge relating to the analysis of educational emergencies in their various forms, specificities and differences; - knows the processes of development, learning and social integration relating to the different stages of life, also in relation to individual differences, gender, race, ability and culture; - is able to read and interpret the educational and training needs of individuals, groups and communities present in educational, social and work contexts through the use of specific theoretical and methodological tools; - is able to grasp the cultural, social and organizational specificity of the professional contexts in which they are called upon to operate; - is able to adapt interventions and system actions in response to specific educational needs; - is able to interpret relational dynamics and use group management techniques; - knows the methodologies of communication and the main themes of the debate on educational technologies, on the creation of digital artifacts and on the use of Artificial Intelligence technologies in education; - is able to relate their professional skills to contemporary scientific debate. Pedagogical, sociological, psychological and historical-philosophical knowledge: The graduate in Education and Training Sciences: - knows the main pedagogical theories on the themes of education for children, adult education and training in organizational contexts; - knows the sociological and psychological theories on the processes of individual, group and social learning; - is able to analyze the main socio-material events in the contexts of daily life and the community of belonging; - is able to correctly frame the main social phenomena from the point of view of history and the evolution of philosophical and anthropological paradigms. Connection with the world of work: The graduate in Education and Training Sciences: - has developed the necessary transversal skills to work independently or within organizations; - knows the changes relating to the world of work; - is able to envision the future employment scenarios of educational and training professions; - knows the main types of educational services present in the local, regional and national area; - knows the importance and necessity of specific and constant professional updating. Educational and training design: The graduate in Education and Training Sciences: - knows the theoretical models and methodologies of educational and training design; - knows the individual and contextual problems that can generate an educational or training need; - masters a repertoire of methodologies and tools for the design of activities in educational and training contexts; - is able to differentiate between the main models of educational and training design in formal, informal and non-formal contexts; - understands the need for careful monitoring of the activities carried out; - knows the main tools for evaluating the results and effects of the educational and training activities designed and carried out. Research and educational and training interventions: The graduate in Education and Training Sciences: - knows the emerging problems of research in the socio-educational and training field; - knows the main approaches to empirical research in the educational field; - possesses the basic knowledge to collect, analyze, validate and communicate data; - knows the different logics of educational and training design; - knows the main tools for evaluating educational research and is able to apply them to projects located in specific contexts.

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year.

Program: 2023.
Status: Open.
Credits: 180 CFU.
Language: Italian.
Type: Degree Programs.
Regulation: D.M. 270/2004.
Degree Class: L-11 - Degree in Modern Languages and Cultures.
Inter-University Course: No.
Open Access: Yes.
Location: Arezzo.
Department: Department of Philology and Criticism of Ancient and Modern Literatures.
Website: https://lingue-comunicazione.unisi.it.
Informative Website: https://lingue-comunicazione.unisi.it.
Admission Requirements: Required qualifications: [TSS].
Upper Secondary School Diploma.
Fees and Contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse.
Dates and Deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni.
Roles and Responsibilities: Student Mobility: ALESSANDRA CARBONE, President/Chair of the teaching committee.
LETIZIA CIRILLO.
Program, Texts and Objectives: Admission Requirements: Upper secondary school diploma or other qualification obtained abroad recognized as suitable.
Professional Status conferred by the degree: Linguistic and intercultural mediator in social, tourist, cultural fields and in the field of international cooperation.
In the cultural-touristic field, job opportunities fall within the various sectors related to cultural services and mediation between the cultures of different countries. The main job roles are: media operator; translator and expert in linguistic mediation in literary, artistic, and performing arts fields; tourist operator.
Job opportunities in the socio-cultural field are mainly related, in Italy, to the needs of linguistic and cultural mediation connected to migration phenomena and to the design, in multilingual contexts, of cultural and tourist development, both tangible and intangible; abroad, to the activities of non-profit organizations and associations engaged in socio-economic and cultural development initiatives, as well as to embassies, consulates, and representative offices that maintain exchanges with European and non-European countries.
Job opportunities in the field of mediation, dissemination, and promotion of Italian culture and language for a foreign audience are related, in Italy, to both the cultural tourism sector and the needs related to migration phenomena; abroad, graduates of the Course will be able to work in organizations and companies dedicated to the dissemination of Italian culture, as well as in public and private institutions dedicated to teaching Italian as a foreign language.
Linguistic and intercultural mediator in the economic and commercial field: In the business and commercial field, graduates in Languages for Intercultural and Business Communication are sought by companies of various sizes for marketing needs, intercultural mediation, commercial exchanges, as well as by professional firms that increasingly offer legal and administrative services and consulting to companies operating at national and international levels. The main job opportunities in this field are: foreign correspondent, company operator in the international sector, person in charge of foreign relations, customer service representative, foreign trade representative.
Characteristics of the final exam: To be admitted to the final exam, the student must have passed all the exams and completed the training activities provided for in the Study Plan.
The final exam consists of a written paper of at least 30 pages and no more than 50 pages (or a multimedia paper of similar size). Respecting the student's interests and professional aspirations, translation, contrastive analysis, linguistic and/or philological analysis, critical-literary, sociological, or cultural research on social, linguistic, and cultural phenomena related to one or more languages and cultures studied will be chosen; alternatively, the student may choose to develop an autonomous project in the field of one of the professionalizing disciplines included in his/her study plan, as well as the internship experience.
The student is required to have the ability to master the languages, cultures, and disciplinary knowledge that is the subject of analysis, to have current bibliographic, computer, and critical tools, to be fully aware of the scientific criteria necessary for the preparation of the paper, to demonstrate autonomy of judgment and the ability to mediate between different linguistic and cultural fields.
The exam, prepared by the student under the guidance of a teacher-tutor, is discussed before a specially appointed Commission, which will evaluate the exam out of 110 points, with the possible awarding of honors. The discussion of the final paper also includes a verification of the skills acquired in one of the foreign languages studied. The score will take into account the basic average, the quality of the final paper, the linguistic competence achieved, the obtaining of external certifications, the university curriculum (in progress/not in progress), and any participation in international exchange projects.
4 CFU are awarded for the final exam.
Knowledge required for admission: The student in possession of a high school diploma or other qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable (Art. 6, paragraph 1 of D.M. 270/2004) can access the Degree Course.
The following are required for admission to the Degree Course: basic preparation that allows to approach the contents of the disciplines for which study is foreseen, good analytical and synthesis skills of texts of medium complexity.
At the beginning of the academic year, the entrance test will be carried out as required by D.M. 270/2004. The organization of seminars on university study methodologies and on the writing of scientific texts is guaranteed.
If the outcome of the entrance test is negative, the Degree Course establishes the specific training obligations that each student is required to fulfill within the first year of the Course.
For the languages of study, the following entrance competence is required: i) for the English language, no less than the B1 level of the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages; ii) for those who choose French, Spanish, or German as the language of study, no less than the A1 level; iii) access to the study of Russian and Chinese languages does not require any knowledge of the subject.
Knowledge of all previously studied languages will be verified through grammatical knowledge and receptive skills (reading, listening) through written tests (collective in the classroom and/or individual in the language laboratory) which will make it possible to define the previous skills of each student with respect to the levels of the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages. If the teachers in charge deem it necessary, or at the request of the interested party, the tests may be supplemented by an in-depth interview to assess also the skills of understanding and oral expression.
The teachers of the degree course will be involved in orientation and tutoring activities at the time of registration, according to a pre-established and publicized schedule, to identify and evaluate any training deficiencies and guide students towards recovery paths.
Degree awarded: Degree in LANGUAGES FOR INTERCULTURAL AND BUSINESS COMMUNICATION.
Teaching delivery method: Conventional.
Knowledge and understanding: LINGUISTIC AREA: Graduates of the Course will have acquired:
- full mastery of the Italian language, both spoken and written;
- excellent mastery of the English language and another language provided for in the training offer, in the four skills described in the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages (level C1 for English; at least level B2 for French, Spanish, and German; at least level B1 for Russian and Chinese);
- good knowledge in the field of philology and general linguistics;
- basic knowledge in the teaching of Italian and foreign languages;
LITERARY AREA: Graduates of the Languages for Intercultural Communication curriculum will have acquired:
- advanced knowledge of the history of English literature and culture, and of the literature and culture of the second foreign language studied;
- good knowledge of the history of Italian literature;
- good competence in the use of the main tools of textual analysis and literary criticism (genre, rhetoric, metrics, stylistics, narratology).
Graduates of the Languages for Business curriculum will have acquired:
- basic knowledge of English literature and culture and of the literature and culture of the second foreign language studied;
- good knowledge of the history of Italian literature;
- good competence in the use of the main tools of textual analysis and literary criticism (genre, rhetoric, metrics, stylistics, narratology).
SOCIO-ECONOMIC, HISTORICAL-PHILOSOPHICAL, ARTISTIC AREA: Graduates of the course will have acquired:
- basic knowledge of Italian, European, and extra-European history and geography;
- basic knowledge in at least one area chosen from: history and management of cultural heritage; economic and labor sociology; aesthetics.
TRAINING, ECONOMICS, LAW, COMPUTER SCIENCE AREA: Graduates of the Languages for Intercultural Communication curriculum will have acquired:
- basic knowledge in computer science;
- knowledge in one or more areas chosen from: cultural heritage law, law and literature, business economics, territory marketing.
Graduates of the Languages for Business curriculum will have acquired:
- basic knowledge in computer science;
- basic knowledge in economics and business management;
- knowledge in one or more areas chosen from: administrative law, international law, accounting and balance sheet, territory marketing.

Admission Requirements
Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]
Upper Secondary School Diploma
Optional qualifications (choose from the following): [TS + TSS]
Foreign Qualification: [L2 cons + TSS]
First-Level Degree: [L1 cons + TSS]
Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse
Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan" www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni
Roles and Responsibilities: Student Mobility, NICOLINA BOSCO, President/President of the teaching committee, CARLO OREFICE
Program, texts and objectives: Access to further studies.
A) Research Doctorate Courses - CDR
B) Specialization Courses - CS
C) Second-Level University Master's Courses - CMU2
Admission requirements: The essential curricular requirements for admission to the Master's Degree Course are identified through: a) the origin of graduates from Degree Courses that present a specific and proven cultural and didactic affinity, in particular the class (L-19, Educational Sciences); b) the origin of graduates from Degree Courses in the area of humanities, psycho-social and literary sciences who have acquired a sufficient number of credits (approximately 60-80 in total) in areas and scientific-disciplinary sectors consistent with the structure of the Master's Degree Course, in particular those relating to pedagogical, psychological, sociological, philosophical and historical teachings. Any curricular deficiencies must be made up for before the test to verify individual preparation. The curricular requirements must be met before the verification of individual preparation. Any additional training must be completed before the test to verify individual preparation. The methods of verification will be defined in detail in the didactic regulations of the course.
Professional status conferred by the degree: Expert consultant in the design and management of training processes in organizations. The graduate in "Sciences for Training and Pedagogical Consultancy in Organizations" will be able to access two professional areas: a) consultancy for individuals, families and communities on issues of education, inclusion, disability and personal and professional development; b) consultancy for organizations on issues of human resource management, workplace well-being, coordination, corporate training and the design of training activities for young people and adults.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam consists of the preparation and discussion before a Commission of a written dissertation, the content and development of which take place with the assistance and under the direction of a teacher. To be admitted to the exam, the student must have passed all the exams and training activities provided for in the Study Plan. The exam is designed to demonstrate the acquisition of specific knowledge, skills and competencies and the ability to reflect critically on them, and will be evaluated out of 110, with the possible award of honors.
Knowledge required for admission: To be admitted to the Master's Degree Course in Sciences for Training and Pedagogical Consultancy in Organizations, you must have a degree or a three-year university diploma, or another qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable.
The curricular requirements are met by holding a degree in one of the following degree classes (ex DM 270/2004 or ex DM 509/1999 or other equivalent qualification obtained abroad): Educational Sciences (L-19), Philosophy (L-5), Literature (L-10), Modern Languages and Cultures (L-11), Linguistic Mediation (L-12), Sciences of Administration and Organization (L-16), Sciences of Economics and Business Management (L-18), Communication Sciences (L-20), Sciences of Motor and Sports Activities (L-22), Psychological Sciences (L-24), Economic Sciences (L-33), Political Sciences and International Relations (L-36), Social Sciences for Cooperation, Development and Peace (L-37), Social Work (L-39), Sociology (L-40), History (L-42), Degree Courses in Rehabilitation Health Professions, Professional Educator (L/SNT02), Degree in Nursing (L-SNT01).
Graduates in the listed classes must also have obtained at least 40 CFU in the following scientific-disciplinary sectors: SPS/01, 02, 03, 04, 05, 06, 07, 08, 09, 10, 11, 13, 14; M-PED/01, 02, 03, 04; M-PSI/01, 02, 03, 04, 05, 06; SECS-P/01, 07, 08, 10; M-FIL/01, 02, 03, 04, 05, 06; M-STO/01, 02, 03, 04, 05, 06, 07, 08, 09; L-ART/06; L-FIL-LET/08, 09, 10, 11, 12, 13, 14; M-DEA/01; MED/25, 42, 44, 45, 48; IUS/01, 07, 09, 14; M-EDF/01, 02 and have obtained the degree with a score of no less than 95/110. (if the graduate obtained a degree score of less than 95/110, they must have passed, with a weighted average of no less than 26/30, the courses in their study plan for at least 40 CFU in the following scientific-disciplinary sectors: SPS/01, 02, 03, 04, 05, 06, 07, 08, 09, 10, 11, 13, 14; M-PED/01, 02, 03, 04; M-PSI/01, 02, 03, 04, 05, 06; SECS-P/01, 07, 08, 10; M-FIL/01, 02, 03, 04, 05, 06; M-DEA/01; L-ART/06; L-FIL-LET/08, 09, 10, 11, 12, 13, 14; M-GGR/01; MED/25, 42, 44, 45, 48; IUS/01, 07, 09, 14; M-EDF/01, 02).
Students who do not have the aforementioned requirements may also be admitted to the course of study, provided that they meet the requirements indicated in the didactic regulations of the course of study. For the purposes of admission to the course of study, students who have the required curricular requirements must take a test to verify their personal preparation. The test involves an interview on topics of pedagogy and didactics, general sociology, work psychology and social psychology. The methods of verifying personal preparation are specified in detail in the didactic regulations of the course.
Degree awarded: Master's Degree in Sciences for Training and Pedagogical Consultancy in Organizations.
Teaching delivery method: Conventional
Knowledge and understanding:
Area of training consultancy
- Knowledge of theories on adult learning;
- Knowledge of the theoretical and methodological models of consultancy intervention;
- Knowledge of the evolution of organizational models.
Area of training design
- Know the logic of training design in organizations;
- Know the strategies for cultivating the informal knowledge of communities.

[D002] HISTORICAL AND CULTURAL HERITAGE STUDIES Degree Course HISTORY AND DOCUMENTATION

ARCHAEOLOGY COMMON PATH SPECTACLE HISTORY OF ART HISTORY AND DOCUMENTATION Year
1st YEAR
- A.Y. 2024/2025
OTHER ACTIVITIES
1st YEAR CLASS HOURS REQUIRED
[100217] ARCHIVISTICS A

UNIVERSITY: University of Siena
COURSE: [D002] HISTORICAL AND CULTURAL HERITAGE STUDIES
SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unisi.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10697

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 3 years. Admission: 2021. Status: Open. Credits: 180 CFU. Language: Italian. Type of Degree: Bachelor's Degree. Legal Basis: D.M. 270/2004. Degree Class: L-1 - Class of Cultural Heritage Degrees. Inter-University Course: No. Open Access: Yes. Location: Siena. Department: Department of Historical and Cultural Heritage Studies. Website: https://storia-patrimonio-culturale.unisi.it. Informative Website: http://www.dssbc.unisi.it. Admission Requirements: Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]. Upper Secondary School Diploma. Fees and Contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse. Dates and Deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni. Roles: President/Chairman of the teaching committee: FRANCO CAMBI. Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree: CULTURAL HERITAGE OPERATOR. In light of a labor market that is constantly and rapidly evolving, requiring new skills for the numerous professional profiles capable of collaborating on operational interventions for the protection, safeguarding, and conservation of cultural heritage, as well as those related to the enhancement and enjoyment of the same assets (as per art. 1, Law 110/2014), the knowledge and skills acquired by graduates will enable them to access various employment opportunities related to activities of study, cataloging, documentation, assistance, teaching, and archaeological excavations within public and private bodies operating in the field of management, protection, enhancement, and enjoyment of cultural heritage, including:
- state, regional, and local museums;
- central and local bodies of the MiBACT;
- local authorities;
- archives and libraries;
- photographic, film, and media libraries;
- theaters; companies and professional organizations;
- publishing houses and record companies.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam consists of a discussion of a paper prepared by the candidate on a topic agreed upon with one of the lecturers of the degree course. Candidates must demonstrate that they have acquired the ability to address a basic scientific research, using the most appropriate methodologies and by resorting to updated bibliographic tools, or that they know how to complete technical-operational procedures applied to the knowledge, protection, and enhancement of cultural heritage, always accompanying the paper with a written text that justifies its methodology, validity, and relevance.
In both cases, candidates must demonstrate that they have acquired the appropriate skills to develop and support arguments and to solve specific problems in their field of study.
The final exam can be prepared in the following ways: a) a written essay; b) a paper on a computer that constitutes a system or product (website, CD, etc.); c) a multimedia paper. In the case of types a) and b), the paper must be accompanied by a few pages of text that illustrate its theoretical and/or scientific motivations, structure, execution methods, results, and contribution to the reference discipline.
As a rule, the final exam paper is written in Italian. The Teaching Committee may authorize the final exam and the related discussion to be conducted in one of the other languages of the European Community.
The final exam is worth 9 CFU. The merit score is expressed in 110 (out of 110), with possible honors, and is awarded according to precise parameters: the commission adds the grade awarded to the paper and the discussion to the weighted average of the grades obtained by the student in the exams, assessing its completeness, correctness, validity, and any originality.
Knowledge required for admission: Admission to the Degree Course is subject to the possession of a secondary school diploma or another qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable.
Students who enroll in the degree course are required to have adequate basic preparation consisting of linguistic skills and cultural knowledge normally acquired in high schools and secondary education institutions.
In particular, good skills in the various forms of the Italian language and knowledge of the English language at least at level A2/2 of the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages are required.
The assessment of skills in the various forms of the Italian language is carried out through a written test (Tolc-SU Test on line- Humanities, administered by CISIA - Consortium of Universities for Integrated Systems for Access).
In case of a negative result in this test, access to the degree course is allowed, but additional training obligations (OFA) will be assigned, intended to fill the training gaps highlighted by the test and to be completed in the first year of the course.
In particular, students with OFA will be required to attend an intensive Italian language course with a final assessment.
Regarding knowledge of the English language, students who enroll must take a test to assess their individual language skills, which will allow them to be directed to the most suitable preparation course to achieve the level required by the study plan (B1).
Degree awarded: Bachelor's Degree in Historical and Cultural Heritage Studies. Knowledge and understanding: General Area: The training course for students in the three-year Bachelor's Degree in Historical and Cultural Heritage Studies aims to train graduates with considerable historical and cultural knowledge in a broad sense and with the ability to understand and deepen specific issues in the fields of archaeology, history, and the sciences of books and documents, figurative arts and architecture, film, music, and theater. The textbooks, for each of the learning phases, will be chosen according to criteria of high scientific and cultural level, updating of the state of research, transparency of content, and methodological effectiveness. The acquisition of solid historical foundations, essential for the correct application of knowledge in the cultural field, will be accompanied by the development of appropriate methods of typological, phenomenological, stylistic, paleographic, and diplomatic analysis of the object of study (documentation, codices and books, landscapes, buildings, archaeological finds, works of art, musical compositions, theatrical pieces, films) and by an analytical reading of critical texts, taking into account the context in which they were developed, the author's point of view, and in general the history of the reception and interpretation of the data collected. Substantial contributions to achieving these objectives are made, in addition to lectures, by seminars, exercises, and laboratory activities, as well as, for the Archaeology curriculum, field missions in territorial areas and in archaeological sites of great importance. The interactive nature of these activities will allow teachers to progressively verify the increase of knowledge and the acquisition of analytical methods by students, without delegating the verification to the exams alone. The multiplicity of disciplines that characterizes the course of study will allow the graduate to identify points of contact between the different aspects of cultural heritage and to address the specific problems of their field of interest with openness and awareness of the possibilities of interaction with other fields of knowledge.
Archaeological Area: At the end of the studies, the graduate will have acquired a general knowledge of the different archaeological phases (from prehistory to post-medieval archaeology) and the transformations that affect landscapes, buildings, works of art, and the material culture of the past. The basic knowledge also includes the principles, methodologies, and approaches of contemporary archaeology, useful for understanding the different historical and cultural contexts. A third vector of knowledge involves the themes of organization, conservation, safeguarding, and enhancement of cultural heritage.
The acquisition of this knowledge and skills will be verified during the training course through seminars, written and oral exams, and reports.
Cinema, Music, and Theater Area: At the end of the studies, the graduate will have acquired a general knowledge of the history of cinema, music, and theater. They will know the fundamental aspects of the theoretical foundations of these disciplines and will possess the knowledge necessary to produce critical elaborations of texts and sources.
They will have the ability to conduct historical research, archival investigation, classification of repertoires, compilation of inventories, description of phenomena, and interpretation of data, also aimed at the conservation and enhancement of the material culture of cinematographic, musical, and theatrical practices as cultural heritage.
The basic knowledge will also allow, finally, reconstructions and identification of classes and types of styles, languages, and genres, according to multiple areas of competence, open to the consideration of formal, organizational, productive, communicative, and public consumption aspects.
The acquisition of this knowledge and skills will be verified during the training course through seminars, written and oral exams, and reports.
Historical-Artistic Area: At the end of the three-year period, graduates will have acquired a solid basic knowledge of the developments in the history of art from the ancient world to the contemporary era. The discipline is understood in its broadest methodological criteria: of studying artistic and architectural artifacts in their dual nature as material and cultural products, of the dynamics of creation and reception, of the physical and conservation events, and of the critical and collecting history.
The course aims to stimulate critical analysis skills, develop skills in stylistic and iconographic reading of works of art, and understand the artifacts in relation to their contexts of belonging or origin, often altered or irreparably lost. The objective is to train figures capable of understanding, orienting, and operating in the field of historical-artistic heritage.
Historical-Documentary Area: At the end of the studies, the graduate will have acquired a general knowledge of the different historical periods and the corresponding transformations of an economic, social, political, and cultural type, with full awareness of the historiographical traditions and the problems of periodization, as well as a good ability to synthesize the texts of historical literature.
They will have acquired solid basic knowledge of the principles, methods, and technical aspects of archival science for understanding documentary materials from the medieval, modern, and contemporary periods, which will allow them to place them in the historical and cultural context of reference and to subject them to the necessary critical analysis, as well as to know and address the problems related to their organization, conservation, safeguarding, and enhancement as cultural heritage.
They will have paleographic and codicological knowledge that will allow them to critically investigate the evolution of writing in all its manifestations and, by reconstructing the history of graphic forms, derive elements and data for the history of culture.
The acquisition of this knowledge and skills will be verified during the training course through seminars, written and oral exams, and reports.

Roles: President/Chairman of the teaching committee: MARCO VENTURA
Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
Expert in persuasive and advertising communication.
The expert in persuasive and advertising communication finds professional opportunities in public and private institutions, as well as in specifically political institutions, where they can play the role of critical analyst of communication campaigns and the positioning of various subjects, manage the image of political figures, contribute to the development of programmatic documents and related dissemination strategies. In the private sector (communication agencies), they can play the role of communication manager, curator of the image of public figures (entertainment), carry out consulting activities for institutional, social and political communication campaigns, as well as for advertising campaigns.
Expert in socio-political communication and socio-political analyst.
The employment opportunities for the expert in socio-political communication and socio-political analyst can be found in various sectors. This expert can define communication strategies in the public administration sector, with particular reference to analysis and planning offices, public relations offices and offices for the collection and analysis of needs; furthermore, they can define communication strategies in the third sector and international organizations, with specific attention to the European area.
Expert in interaction and experience design.
The employment opportunities for experts in Interaction and Experience Design, as IU/UX Designers, range in all fields related to the innovation of ICT products in both the public and private sectors.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam consists of a discussion, in front of the degree commission, of a thesis developed in an original way under the guidance of a lecturer of the degree course, who acts as supervisor. The preparation of the thesis and the discussion must verify that the master's graduate has acquired a critical and in-depth knowledge of the strategies of communication, in accordance, respectively, with the disciplinary principles typical of each curriculum within which the thesis work is carried out. Candidates must produce, as a degree thesis, a written work with characteristics of originality that provides for a research effort adequate to the amount of training credits required. The work will focus on one of the scientific-disciplinary sectors of the planned training activities, within which the candidate has followed at least one course.
The student is allowed to write the thesis in English; in this case, a motivated request to the President of the Study Course is required, duly countersigned also by the thesis supervisor, in which the student declares his commitment to submit, together with the dissertation in a foreign language, a summary in Italian of the same, of at least 10 pages. The supervisor and co-supervisor of the work, as a rule, are part of the Degree Commission.
15 CFU are assigned to the final exam, evaluated on the basis of the following criteria: completeness, methodological rigor and originality of the work; expository and argumentative skills in the presentation of the work.
The final exam will be expressed in hundredths with possible honors.
Knowledge required for access: To access the Master's Degree Course in Strategies and Techniques of Communication, a degree, or a four-year degree of the old system, or a three-year university diploma or other qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable, or a First-level Academic Diploma AFAM is required.
Pursuant to Article 6, paragraph 3, of Law No. 268/2002, holders of diplomas issued by the Academies and Conservatories, including the certificates issued at the end of the introductory dance courses, are admitted to the Master's Degree course after recognition of the training credits acquired and provided that they hold a high school diploma.
Access to the Master's Degree Course requires certain curricular requirements and adequate personal preparation. Knowledge of the English language at level B1 is also required.
The curricular requirements are met by holding a degree in one of the following classes:
L-1, L-3, L-4, L-5, L-6, L-8, L-10, L-11, L-12, L-15, L-19, L-20, L-24, L-31, L-36, L-37, L-39, L-40, L-42, L-43
together with the possession of at least 60 CFU in at least two or more of the following SSDs:
M-FIL /01, M-FIL /02, M-FIL /03, M-FIL /04, M-FIL /05, M-FIL /06, M-FIL /07, M-FIL /08, L-LIN /01, L-LIN /02, L-LIN/03, L-LIN/04, L-LIN/05, L-LIN/06, L-LIN/07, L-LIN/08, L-LIN/09 L-LIN/10, L-LIN/11 L-LIN/12, L-LIN/13, L-LIN/14, L-LIN/15, L-LIN/16, L-LIN/17, L-LIN/18, L-LIN/19, L-LIN/20, L-LIN/21, M-PSI/01, M-PSI/02, M-PSI/03, M-PSI/04, M-PSI/05, M-PSI/06, M-PSI/07, M-STO/01, M-STO/02, M-STO/03, M-STO/04, M-STO/05, M-STO/06, M-STO/07, M-STO/08, M-STO/09, SECS-P/01, SECS-P/02, SECS-P/03, SECS-P/04, SECS-P/05, SECS-P/06, SECS-P/07, SECS-P/08, SECS-P/09, SECS-P/10, SECS-P/11, SECS-P/12, SECS-P/13, SECS-S/01, SECS-S/05, SPS/01, SPS/02, SPS/03, SPS/04, SPS/05, SPS/06, SPS/07, SPS/08, SPS/09, SPS/10, SPS/11, SPS/12, SPS/13, SPS/14, ICAR/13, ICAR-15, ICAR/16, ICAR/17, ICAR-18, INF/01, ING-INF/03, ING-INF/05, L-FIL- LET/12, M-DEA/01, M-GGR/01, M-GGR/02, L-ART-02, L-ART/04, LART/05, L-ART/06, L-ART/07, IUS/01, IUS/02, IUS/09.
Sectors of different areas relating to general skills of a scientific nature useful for allowing those who want to orient their basic scientific training towards the communication of science and scientific dissemination to enroll: BIO/07, BIO/11, BIO/16, BIO/18, FIS/01, FIS/03, FIS/05, FIS/07, FIS/08.
For students with degrees in other classes, admission is conditional on having at least 75 CFU in the SSDs indicated above, as well as passing the test to verify personal preparation.
The verification of curricular requirements is carried out on the basis of the documentation provided, from which the exams taken, the related SSDs, the CFU obtained and the degree mark can be seen.
The verification of individual initial preparation is mandatory and only students who meet the curricular requirements can access it.
Initial preparation is assessed through a verification test for all students who intend to enroll in Strategies and Techniques of Communication, and the verification of preparation can be satisfied by having a degree mark of not less than 100/110.
The methods of verification are set out in the Didactic Regulations of the Study Course and are adequately publicized each year on the course website.
Qualification awarded: Master's Degree in Strategies and Techniques of Communication.
Knowledge and understanding: General area: Graduates of the Master's Degree will develop knowledge and understanding skills in the field of communication sciences, up to the point of being able to develop original ideas, especially in the areas of persuasive languages (of politics, advertising, institutional and social communication), communication produced by companies (advertising and marketing strategies), and the design of the user experience of technologies (Experience Design), without neglecting a possible application in a research context. Graduates of the Master's Degree will also acquire the ability to solve problems related to the use and design of new media in an eminently interdisciplinary key (technologies for communication, marketing, rhetoric applied to the analysis of advertising, political discourse, institutions, content design, interaction design).
Persuasive and advertising communication: Graduates who follow the area in Persuasive and Advertising Communication will develop knowledge and understanding skills in relation to both the methods and the tools of persuasive communication, articulated in its two fundamental components: (i) public communication (which in turn can be divided into institutional, social and political communication); (ii) communication produced by companies (advertising and marketing strategies). The basis of these skills will be a broad and comprehensive reflection on: (i) communication models (from the pioneering communication research, to the models developed in the semiotic field, to those of interaction and inference proposed by pragmatics); (ii) theories and techniques of semiotic analysis of the text (advertising and media) (iii) techniques of rhetorical argumentation (including the theory of argumentative fallacies); (iv) sociological and psychological theories of communication; (v) theories and techniques of digital communication.
Public and social communication and journalism: Graduates who follow the area in Social, political and journalistic communication will acquire knowledge that will enable them to analyze and understand the institutional context in which political, economic and social communication takes place, the tools through which to detect the demands coming from the social, economic and political environment and the most suitable ways to communicate and convey relevant messages to different audiences. They will also acquire interdisciplinary skills aimed at the ideation, analysis, use and evaluation of new media, in relation to the potential implications aimed at the investigation of both the social and cognitive components, and those of political, economic and social communication. They will develop skills in analyzing and communicating social transformations and social risk. These skills will be based on knowledge of the most recent psychological theories, individual and collective decision-making and the functioning of the media system.
Technologies and methods for Experience Design: Graduates who follow the area in Technologies and methods for Experience Design will develop knowledge and understanding skills regarding the methods, tools and theoretical foundations of User Experience design. They will appropriate the specific ideation and design process of User-Centered Design, knowing how to appreciate the differences and similarities with other design processes (e.g. Industrial, Software, Architectural, etc.). They will master the methods and software/hardware resources necessary to support the different phases of the user-centered design process, knowing how to adapt methods and resources to the different operational situations in which the design process may take place. They will build knowledge of these methods and tools on the basis of the most accredited and heuristically positive theories of human action and cultural evolution.

UNIVERSITY: University of Siena
COURSE: [D004] LITERARY AND PHILOSOPHICAL STUDIES
SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unisi.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10691

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 3 years. Admission: 2019. Status: Open. Credits: 180 CFU. Language: Italian. Type: Degree Programs. Legal basis: D.M. 270/2004. Degree class: L-10 - Class of Degrees in Literature. Inter-university course: No. Open access: Yes. Location: Siena. Department: Philology and Criticism of Ancient and Modern Literatures. Website: https://studi-letterari-filosofici.unisi.it. Informative website: http://www.dfclam.unisi.it. Admission requirements: Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]. Upper secondary school diploma. Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse. Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni. Roles: President/President of the teaching committee: ALESSANDRO LINGUITI. Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree. Expert in the promotion and management of cultural and communication activities. Graduates of this degree class will be able to carry out professional activities in local authorities and specific institutions, such as libraries, archives and academies, as well as in publishing houses, journalistic offices, foundations, companies and other professional bodies operating in the field of event and cultural activity organization. However, it should be noted that for many professional roles, the basic skills and abilities acquired during the three-year degree course will need to be supplemented by the specific training offered in master's degree courses, first and second-level master's degrees, doctoral courses (starting with those offered at the University of Siena), and subsequent training levels provided for by law for access to teaching. Graduates who have a sufficient number of credits in appropriate subject areas will, as provided by current legislation, be able to participate in the entrance exams for teacher training courses.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam can be carried out in two different ways:
a) It may consist of a written paper of an expository nature, the topic of which falls within the scope of one of the SSDs (scientific-disciplinary sectors) present in the Study Plan of the Degree Course in Literary and Philosophical Studies. This paper must demonstrate the possession of specific knowledge and the ability to set up a basic research project, using appropriate methodologies and up-to-date bibliographic tools;
or b) It may consist of an oral exam, during which the graduate must demonstrate a thorough knowledge of the fundamental texts (primary and secondary bibliography, according to methods that will be specified in the Course Study Regulations) of one of the SSDs present in the Study Plan of the Degree Course in Literary and Philosophical Studies; furthermore, he/she must demonstrate the ability to set up a basic research project, using appropriate methodologies and up-to-date bibliographic tools, referring to at least two written reports prepared as part of the exams taken during the course.
The choice between the two methods of carrying out the final exam will be agreed upon by the graduate and the supervisor, taking into account the specific characteristics of the SSD in which the student is graduating.
The final exam is worth 6 CFU. The final grade is expressed in hundredths, with possible honors. The average score of the exams will be added to the grade awarded for the final exam.
Knowledge required for admission: To be admitted to the Degree Course, applicants must hold a secondary school diploma or another qualification obtained abroad, recognized as equivalent.
Students who enroll in the course are required to have basic skills consisting of linguistic skills and cultural knowledge normally acquired in high schools and secondary education institutions.
In particular, a good command of the Italian language, an elementary knowledge of Latin, and knowledge of the English language at least at level A2/2 of the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages are required.
Entrance tests in Italian and English will be used to verify the personal preparation relating to these skills. No entrance test is required for Latin, but a preliminary assessment of linguistic knowledge will be carried out, as a prerequisite for the first exam in Latin Language and Literature (scientific-disciplinary sector L-FIL-LET/04).
Students who do not pass these tests will still be admitted to the degree course, but they will be required to fulfill additional training obligations in the first year of the course. Students with additional training obligations regarding the expression of the Italian language must attend an intensive Italian workshop; students with additional training obligations regarding knowledge of the English language must attend a remedial course, which will be organized by the University Language Center.
Knowledge and understanding.
Generic area: Graduates of the degree course will have integrated and developed the preparation acquired in secondary school through the acquisition of skills and abilities in the philological-linguistic and classical literary, philological-linguistic and modern literary, and philosophical fields, within a solid basic framework. Verification takes place within the entire training course through written and oral exams (with possible seminars and/or laboratory activities, internships), and through the final exam.
Students must have demonstrated, in order to obtain the degree, that they possess theoretical and methodological knowledge in the various fields, skills in reading and analyzing texts, knowledge of critical methods, and the ability to use the fundamental bibliographic tools for research and to consult and use the latest developments in research.
Area of Philologies, Languages and Classical Literatures: Good command of the Greek and Latin languages, knowledge of the historical development and the main authors and texts of Greek and Latin literatures, the main methods of philological study of classical texts, the main cultural and anthropological issues relating to the ancient world (for students who do not choose a training path oriented towards the classical world, this knowledge is understood to be more general and limited to the Latin field). The main teaching tool is the lecture, possibly accompanied by seminar exercises. The assessment of knowledge typically takes place through oral and/or written exams.
Area of Philology, Linguistics and Italian Literature: Good knowledge of the historical development and the main authors and texts of Italian literature (including theater), mastery of the main methods of philological study of modern texts, awareness of the main cultural issues related to the study of literary production in the Italian language; good knowledge of the diachronic development of the Italian language (for students who choose a training path not oriented towards Italian studies, this knowledge is understood to be more general). The main teaching tool is the lecture, possibly accompanied by seminar exercises. The assessment of knowledge typically takes place through oral and/or written exams.
Area of Philologies, Languages and Modern and Comparative Literatures: General knowledge of the historical development and the main authors and texts of one or more modern foreign literatures; knowledge of one or more modern languages, in addition to English; awareness of the philological, theoretical and historical-critical issues in the study of modern literatures (for students who choose a training path not oriented towards modern and comparative languages and literatures, this knowledge is understood to be more general). The main teaching tool is the lecture, possibly accompanied by seminar exercises. The assessment of knowledge typically takes place through oral and/or written exams.
Historical area: Good knowledge of Western history, from classical antiquity to the present day; awareness of the main historiographical problems relating to sources and their interpretation; mastery of the connections between historical development and cultural events. The main teaching tool is the lecture, possibly accompanied by seminar exercises. The assessment of knowledge typically takes place through oral and/or written exams.
Philosophical area: Good knowledge of the development of philosophical thought from classical antiquity to the present day; awareness of the main theoretical, logical, moral, aesthetic issues and the main methods of analysis of philosophical texts (for students who choose a training path not oriented towards philosophy, this knowledge is understood to be more general). The main teaching tool is the lecture, possibly accompanied by seminar exercises. The assessment of knowledge typically takes place through oral and/or written exams.
Demo-ethno-anthropological area: Knowledge of the main methods and contents of demo-ethno-anthropological research, capable of placing literary and philosophical texts in a broader context of reflection on human cultures (for students who choose a training path oriented towards the demo-ethno-anthropological sciences).

[D006] LANGUAGE AND MIND: LINGUISTICS AND COGNITIVE STUDIES - Master's Degree Course in English

Linguistics and Cognition COMMON PATH Philosophy and Cognition

-----

UNIVERSITY: University of Siena
COURSE: [D006] LANGUAGE AND MIND: LINGUISTICS AND COGNITIVE STUDIES
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: INFO ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unisi.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10693

INFO ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year.
Duration: 2 years
Program: 2015
Status: Open
Credits: 120 CFU
Language: English
Type: Master's Degrees
Regulation: D.M. 270/2004
Degree Class: LM-39 - Master's Degree in Linguistics
Inter-university Course: No
Open Access: Yes
Location: Siena
Department: Department of Social, Political and Cognitive Sciences
Website: https://language-mind.unisi.it
Informative Website: http://www.dispoc.unisi.it
Admission Requirements
Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]
Upper Secondary School Diploma
Optional qualifications (choose from the following): [L2+ CER_LIN+TSS]
First-level Degree, Language Certification [L1+ CER_LIN +TSS]
Degree, Language Certification [L2+ OBG+TSS]
First-level Degree, Open Badge [L1+ OBG +TSS]
Degree, Open Badge [TS +CER_LIN +TSS]
Foreign Qualification, Language Certification [TS +OBG +TSS]
Foreign Qualification, Open Badge
Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse
Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan" www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni
Roles: President/Chairman of the teaching committee: VALENTINA BIANCHI, GIULIANO BOCCI
Program, texts and objectives: Access to further studies.
Specialization courses, Research Doctorate courses, Second-level University Master courses.
Professional status conferred by the degree: The Master's graduate will be a professional figure capable of applying knowledge derived from fundamental research on language as a cognitive ability in various application areas where language is central. They will also be able to carry out theoretical and/or experimental research in one of the following areas: linguistics, logic, philosophy of mind.
The preferred employment opportunities are as follows:
- researchers or graduate technicians in linguistic disciplines (2.6.2.4 - Researchers and graduate technicians in ancient, philological-literary and historical-artistic sciences) or philosophical and cognitive sciences (2.6.2.5 - Researchers and graduate technicians in historical, philosophical, pedagogical and psychological sciences);
- linguistic consultants for companies in the field of information technology (2.5.4.4.1 - Linguists and philologists)
- translators for specific sectors related to advanced research (2.5.4.3 - Interpreters and translators at a high level)
- teachers of foreign languages and Italian as a first and second language (2.6.5.5.5 - language teachers)
In addition, the two curricula, although not providing qualification for teaching, provide skills that can be used in the following areas:
a) in the field of educational services, the Master's graduate will be able to collaborate with support teachers who are dedicated to the education of young people, adults and children with learning difficulties (2.6.5.1 - Specialists in education and training for people with disabilities), or with instructors in the linguistic field (teaching in schools will comply with current regulations);
b) in the field of health services, at specialized centers for the rehabilitation of language disorders;
c) in the field of social services, in public administrations or cooperatives that deal with the problems of multilingualism in the school environment (3.4.5.2 - Social reintegration and integration technicians)
Characteristics of the final exam: The Master's thesis must be a personal, theoretical and/or experimental research work. The work is carried out by the student under the guidance of a supervisor chosen by him/her, a teacher of one of the disciplines of the Master's degree course. The thesis must have a certain consistency and be no less than 100 pages long. A second teacher will act as co-supervisor. The Master's thesis will be discussed by the candidate before a commission. The preparation of the Master's thesis will have a verification moment during the process with a presentation by the candidate of the state of progress of his/her research, within the research seminar dedicated to this in the course schedule. If the presentation at the research seminar is approved by the teachers of the degree course, the candidate will obtain the credits provided for. The Master's thesis will be assigned 18 credits. The score will be assigned based on the average obtained by the student in the exams, calculated on 110. The score resulting from the average may be increased according to the criteria set out in the department's regulations, for an original and high-quality scientific work, to which a distinction may also be awarded.
Knowledge required for admission: The knowledge required for admission and the curricular requirements are defined in the Educational Regulations of the Master's Degree Course, which also indicate the methods for verifying the student's personal preparation.
Degree awarded: Master's Degree in LANGUAGE AND MIND: LINGUISTICS AND COGNITIVE STUDIES
Knowledge and understanding:
Generic Area: The Master's degree in Language and Mind: Linguistics and Cognitive Studies, in addition to opening up to professions in which language and cognition are central, prepares students to undertake more advanced doctoral studies and provides them with specialized knowledge of the research issues in the field. The program is centered on advanced theoretical and experimental research on language and cognition. Students will become able to address a specialized scientific bibliography and to be in contact with qualified publications and scientific journals, both on paper and online. Students will show the level attained in their overall formation in the elaboration and subsequent discussion of their Master thesis, to which the course reserves a significant number of credits. Students will present the state of their work for their thesis at a medium-advanced level of elaboration within the frame of a research seminar, integrated within the schedule of the Master program. Two research seminars are arranged, one for each curriculum; the course program reserves a dedicated numbers of credits to the seminar, whose attendance is mandatory. The research seminar of the linguistic curriculum is articulated into sessions throughout the academic year, consisting in reports about the field work and/or gathering material and/or methodological discussion for the thesis project, where one or more sessions can be integrated by talks given by one of the instructors or by an invited speaker. The research seminar of the philosophical curriculum may be articulated into a Summer School and/or a Workshop, whose attendants are required to write a paper about one of issues discussed, and/or in sessions in which a student presents her/his thesis work at a medium-advanced level of elaboration and discusses it. Professors, classmates in the same curriculum and first year students who have chosen the same curriculum will participate in the seminar. This favours the exchange as well as a direct feedback of the attained knowledge, through a practice that has turned out to be very successful over the years.

Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
Historian or Philosopher. The Master's Degree in History and Philosophy offers employment opportunities in:
- Institutions and public and private bodies for study, research, communication and cultural dissemination;
- Government and local institutions in the fields of cultural services and the recovery of activities, traditions and local identities;
- In the publishing industry related to the dissemination of historical and philosophical information and culture;
- As for the traditional path in teaching, master's degree graduates who have a sufficient number of credits in appropriate subject areas may, as provided by current legislation, participate in the entrance tests for teacher training courses for secondary education.
Master's degree graduates may also participate in the selection processes for access to doctoral research courses and other types of advanced training, such as second-level Master's degrees.
Philosopher. The Master's Degree offers employment opportunities in:
- Public and private research institutions and bodies.
- Public and private institutions and bodies that use philosophical counseling services and/or consulting in the field of communication and applied ethics.
- As for employment in teaching and access to doctoral research courses and other types of advanced training, such as second-level Master's degrees, see the general profile above.
Historian. The Master's Degree offers employment opportunities in:
- Public and private research institutions and bodies.
- Public and private institutions and bodies that deal with the enhancement of historical events at local and national level, prioritizing the public dimension of historical knowledge.
- As for employment in teaching and access to doctoral research courses and other types of advanced training, such as second-level Master's degrees, see the general profile above.
Characteristics of the final exam: To be admitted to the final exam, students must have obtained all the credits required in the study plan. The final exam, to which particular importance is given as a conclusive attestation of the master's study path, consists of the discussion of a written dissertation (thesis) on an original and substantial topic, agreed upon with a teacher-supervisor who follows the student during the research and writing of the text. The text of the dissertation may optionally be supplemented by multimedia materials. In the dissertation and discussion, the student must demonstrate methodological mastery, full ability to use the chosen sources, and in-depth knowledge of the bibliography and debate relating to the topic. The discussion of the thesis before the Commission (with the active participation of at least one co-supervisor) must verify that the master's graduate has acquired advanced knowledge in his/her field of study; that he/she is able to develop original ideas and research perspectives through a critically aware application of the research tools used; that he/she is able to identify innovative perspectives in his/her field of investigation; and that he/she demonstrates the ability to express himself/herself clearly and to argue effectively in writing and orally.
The Teaching Committee may authorize, at the candidate's request, that the final exam and the related discussion be conducted not in Italian but in one of the other languages of the European Community.
A fundamental element of evaluation will be the quality of the work carried out, the correctness of the methods used, the maturity of the analysis and the relevance and originality of the results obtained. The grading of the final exam will be in accordance with the criteria for calculating the merit score defined in the Regulations for the final exam of the master's degree courses approved by the Department. This grade will be assigned taking into account the weighted average of the grades obtained in the exams, the quality and novelty of the thesis work, and will be expressed in hundredths with possible honors.
Knowledge required for admission: To be admitted to the Master's Degree course in History and Philosophy, students must hold a Bachelor's or three-year University Diploma, or another qualification obtained abroad and deemed suitable.
It is also necessary that the student possesses specific curricular requirements and adequate personal preparation, including knowledge of the English language sufficient to read texts in the language used in the various courses.
The curricular requirements are met by holding a degree in one of the following classes (ex DM 270/2004 or ex DM 509/1999 or other equivalent qualification obtained abroad):
Cultural Heritage (L-1), Philosophy (L-5), Literature (L-10), Linguistic Mediation (L-12), Sciences of Education and Training (L-19), Communication Sciences (L-20), History (L-42).
Graduates in the listed classes must also have obtained at least 40 CFU in the following scientific-disciplinary sectors:
L-ANT/02, L-ANT/03; M-GGR/01; M-DEA/01; M-FIL/01, 02, 03, 04, 05, 06, 07, 08; M-PED/01, 03; M-PSI/01; M-STO/01, 02, 03, 04, 05, 06, 07, 08, 09; SECS-P/12; SPS/02, 07, 08.
For those who have a degree other than those of the classes that meet the curricular requirements, it is necessary to have obtained at least 75 CFU in the following SSDs in addition to having obtained the degree with a grade of at least 100/110:
M-FIL/01, 02, 03, 04, 05, 06, 07, 08; M-STO/01, 02, 03, 04, 05, 06, 07, 08, 09; IUS/18, 19, 20; M-DEA/01; M-GGR/01, 02; SECS-P/01, 02, 04, 08, 12; SECS-S/01, 03, 04, 05; SPS/01, 02, 03, 04, 05, 06, 07, 08, 09, 10, 11, 13, 14; MPED/01, 02, 03; M-PSI/01, 05; L-ANT/02, 03; L-ART/04.
Personal preparation is then verified through a mandatory interview, to which students who have the curricular requirements but with a grade lower than that indicated may also have access. The methods for verifying personal preparation are defined in the didactic regulations of the Master's Degree course.
Degree awarded: Master's Degree in History and Philosophy. Knowledge and understanding.
General Area: Master's degree graduates must have acquired a level of understanding post-secondary, developed through the study and critical analysis of classical and specialized texts, also in foreign languages; they must also internalize the specific dialectical relationship between the historical dimension and the philosophical perspective, while remaining open to further disciplinary contributions. The choice of textbooks must guarantee a high scientific level, the updating of the state of research, the transparency of content and the methodological effectiveness. Students in the course must be able to critically use these texts on the basis of the knowledge and skills acquired with the three-year degree and those further developed thanks to the teachings of the master's degree course.
These teachings will alternate lectures and seminars, also providing for the presentation and discussion of written papers, individual study and group discussion; methods aimed not only at transmitting knowledge, but also at fostering familiarity with the practices of communication and critical comparison, in both historical and philosophical fields.
The levels of knowledge and understanding will be assessed by the teachers through the active involvement of students during the lessons and seminars, in order to constantly verify the progress made in individual study. This progress, together with the ability to understand and personally re-elaborate the disciplinary contents, will finally be assessed, for each course, on the occasion of the final exam, which may take place in oral or written form.
The knowledge and skills thus acquired will ultimately allow the graduates to face with sufficient confidence even new or little-studied topics, with a clear methodological approach and a rapid ability to orient themselves in even very vast bibliographies. The acquisition of this knowledge and skills will find a comprehensive verification with the realization of the final dissertation, the culmination of an in-depth and articulated path.
Historical Area: The Master's degree graduate in History must possess in-depth knowledge in the disciplinary areas characterizing the degree course, a solid ability to analyze sources and synthesize texts of historical literature. He/she must therefore be able to critically understand documents and original sources, thanks to the secure knowledge of the methods and languages characteristic of historical interpretation and reconstruction. This knowledge and skills must emerge both in the field of ancient and medieval history and in that of modern and contemporary history, also in relation to the use and enhancement of archival and documentary sources.
Philosophical Area: The Master's degree graduate in Philosophy must possess in-depth knowledge in the disciplinary areas characterizing the degree course, a solid ability to analyze philosophical works and synthesize critical studies in philosophical and historical-philosophical fields. He/she must therefore be able to critically understand texts and original works by individual authors, through the mastery of the specific lexicon, languages and forms of argumentation typical of philosophical research. The privileged areas of study will be those of historical-philosophical, theoretical and moral studies, with specific references to the intercultural, anthropological and religious dimension.

Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]
Upper Secondary School Diploma
Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse
