Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
Advertising Technician
(1) Advertising agencies
(2) Companies, organizations, consulting firms involved in tourism promotion
(3) Communication offices of companies and organizations
(4) External relations offices, public relations offices of public administrations
(5) Media centers, companies operating in mass communications, with their own advertising collection (television, radio, web providers, web publications, commercial blogs)
Public Relations Officer
(1) Companies and consulting firms involved in public relations and event organization
(2) Communication and/or public relations offices of companies or organizations
(3) External relations offices, public relations offices of public administrations
Marketing Technician
(1) Companies or organizations involved in sales and distribution
(2) Company sectors involved in sales and distribution, customer care, and after-sales assistance
(3) Communication and marketing offices of companies or organizations
Characteristics of the final exam: The end of the educational path involves a final exam consisting of the production of an independent work by the student. The objective of the final exam is to: ascertain and evaluate the skills and abilities in the identification and retrieval of sources, methodological skills, mastery of the available scientific literature on the subject, critical skills and the ability to respect the rules of scientific communication.
One or more teachers follow the student in the preparation of the exam (research question, appropriate methodology, formal appropriateness, adequate content, bibliographic selection, formal and ethical correctness). The methods of the exam are agreed upon with the teacher. It can be carried out in Italian or English upon request to the Teaching Committee.
The final exam can be a paper/thesis or another method proposed by the teacher according to the most appropriate methodology for the chosen discipline.
For the range of methods of the final exam, the evaluation criteria, the merit scores and the procedure for assigning a tutor for the final exam, please refer to the Course Regulations.
Knowledge required for access: Those who possess a high school diploma or another qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable can access the degree course.
In order to be admitted to attend the degree course, students must demonstrate that they possess the skills in the following areas of knowledge:
- general culture,
- Italian language,
- logic,
- computer science.
The assessment is carried out through a test, the failure of which does not preclude enrollment in the Degree Course but requires the recovery of educational deficiencies through a personalized path.
Students must have knowledge of the English language equal to at least level A2. The assessment of competence takes place through a diagnostic test of the level of communicative competence in English (computer-based test) or the recognition of certificates already in their possession (IELTS or CAMBRIDGE ESOL).
Degree awarded:
COMMUNICATION SCIENCES
Knowledge and understanding:
Area of communication learning: The basic knowledge is that specific to the founding disciplines of Communication Sciences (L-LIN/01, M-STO/04, SPS/04, SPS/08, L-FIL-LET/12, M-FIL/05); in particular, the socio-linguistic (L-FIL-LET/12) and semiotic (M-FIL/05) and anthropological (M-DEA/01) disciplines, with particular reference to disciplines of a philosophical-theoretical (M-FIL/01), psychological (M-PSI/01) and legal nature in the communicative field (IUS/01). The area of communication learning completes the basic critical knowledge with a series of advanced knowledge in the field of communication. The main forms and types of communication are examined during the educational path, each of which corresponds to one or more professional sectors. Around this framework of characterizing disciplines, students can draw on a selection of related and similar disciplines in different ways and from different perspectives in the disciplinary strands that are: Linguistic-cognitive disciplines, Historical-social disciplines, Languages of social communication and Techniques of communication. The educational path is relevant and can be integrated into the choice of master's degrees and the choice of courses corresponding to three different professionalizing strands: mass communications (SPS/03, SPS/07, SPS/08, SPS/09, M-STO/04, MPSI/01, L-LIN/01); communication technologies (ING-INF/05, L-ART/06, M-GGR/01, M-PSI/01, ICAR/13); business communication (SECS-P/02, SECS-P/08, SPS/08, SECS-S/01). At the end of the course, graduates will have acquired a basic knowledge of the levels of description of language (phonological, morphological-syntactic, lexical, discourse, pragmatic); knowledge of the theoretical models and conceptual frameworks for an analysis of contemporary society; knowledge of the basic principles of communication; knowledge of methodologies for analyzing texts with regard to content, message, expressive capacity and communicative effectiveness of the text; knowledge of the social, economic, historical role of the media and communication technologies; knowledge of the technical resources for the development of messages and content in production processes.

Tuition and fees: For information on tuition and fees, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse

Roles: President/Chairman of the teaching committee: EVA LEHNER
Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
Official in Public and Private Administrations. Master's degree graduates acquire useful skills in a cross-cutting manner: they can carry out professional activities in companies and organizations, particularly in the area of administration, management control and corporate finance, as well as in all areas of Public Administrations, from the administrative departments of state bodies to state administrations, to local public bodies with particular reference to the tax agency, as well as in associations, institutions and private foundations with public purposes.
The employment opportunities and professional activities expected of the degree course can therefore be found in national and international institutions, in private companies operating also in the international market, in public and private organizations with high-level responsibilities. Graduates may also carry out professional activities in the governing structures of national, European and international bodies, developing and implementing public intervention policies, also aimed at territorial management and in the field of tourism.
In the latter sector, the Master's degree graduate also has the possibility of becoming a "tourism consultant", an emerging professional figure that falls within the broader sphere of business consultants.
Professional opportunities are also available in the field of publishing companies, newspapers, public and private press offices, news agencies and in any other sector of information and communication.
The professional opportunities are completed with activities in research institutes, public and private schools, associative, recreational, cultural and sports organizations.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam consists of the preparation of a written thesis and its discussion, within the scope of one of the subjects included in the individual study plan of the candidate, on a topic agreed with the teacher responsible for that subject (Supervisor). A Co-supervisor is required.
The thesis consists of a monograph, the result of research on documentary and doctrinal sources relating to the subject, accompanied by clear bibliographic references and/or the results of experimental investigations.
This work is aimed at verifying the ability to analyze and synthesize in the collection and selection of knowledge with a view to elaborating a personal vision on that topic, carried out with scientifically correct methods.
With the favorable opinion of the Supervisor, the student may write the thesis in a foreign language.
The final exam is assigned a number of credits equal to 18.
The final grade is expressed in hundredths, with possible honors.
Knowledge required for admission: To be admitted to the Master's Degree course, applicants must hold a degree or a three-year university diploma, or another qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable.
To access the course, it is necessary to have adequate personal preparation and certain curricular requirements.
A good knowledge of contemporary history, public law subjects, political economy, political science, and sociology is required.

Degree awarded: Master's Degree in Public Administration
Knowledge and understanding:
General Area: At the end of the studies, the Master's degree graduates have acquired the following knowledge and understanding skills:
a) dynamics of Public Administrations and public and private bodies
b) evolution of regulations, not only at national level
c) organization of political systems and society, both at national and international level.
d) current approaches to the study of financial regulation and economics
e) historical dimension of contemporaneity as a whole and in particular in communications, journalism and the evolution of territorial structures and tourism
f) linguistic dimension
Economic Area: At the end of the studies, the Master's degree graduates have acquired the following knowledge and understanding skills:
a) main themes concerning the functioning of the current international economic system, with particular reference to the various aspects of the European integration process
b) role and functioning of money and finance in the real economy, with particular attention to the problems of the euro
c) organizational structure of companies and the government as articulated in the various territorial levels.
d) analysis of territorial data, combining demographic variables with fundamental economic variables for local development
e) role of the private and public sectors in the efficient provision of non-market goods
Statistical-Quantitative Area: At the end of the studies, the Master's degree graduates have acquired knowledge and understanding skills of the tools useful for an effective structuring of statistical analysis
Legal Area: At the end of the studies, the Master's degree graduates have acquired the following knowledge and understanding skills:
a) main aspects of administrative law, also with reference to territorial governance and the tourism sector,
b) substantive law of the European Union in its various articulations,
c) international law on economic matters,
d) regional public law,
e) institutions of trade union and labor law in relation to national and supranational regulations,
f) normative and jurisprudential profiles of information law,
g) legal profiles of political and institutional communication processes,
h) tax law,
i) foundations of civil and commercial law,
k) foundations of public services law.
Political Area: At the end of the studies, the Master's degree graduates have acquired the following knowledge and understanding skills:
a) European political language,
b) methods of analysis and research, including empirical research, applied in the political and social field
c) political dynamics
d) effects on public opinion of decision-making dynamics
Historical Area: At the end of the studies, the Master's degree graduates have acquired the following knowledge and understanding skills:
a) historical events functional to the construction of the Italian nation-state,
b) historical evolution of the main institutional bodies (political, administrative) present in the territory
c) relationship between mass media and public opinion in the perspective of the history of Italian journalism, also with reference to the transmission of information in the territory
d) evolution of communication and transport systems
e) changes and continuity in the territory and tourism
Autonomy of judgment: Master's degree graduates develop the ability to understand, interpret, elaborate and critically evaluate a series of information and data relating to their areas of study and specialization (legal, statistical-economic, historical-political-social, managerial), to carry out research and analysis activities on specific topics, to develop strategies and judgments in an autonomous way, which demonstrate the ability to act at high levels of responsibility and professionalism.
Autonomy of judgment is achieved through active participation in all training activities of the course of study and verified through oral and written exams.
Learning ability: Master's degree graduates acquire the knowledge and skills that enable them to successfully and autonomously undertake any further training course, as well as to continue their studies in the 3rd cycle (doctoral courses, second-level master's degrees, etc.).
Adequate learning skills can be achieved through participation in all activities of the course of study and can be verified through oral and written exams, as well as through the preparation and subsequent discussion of seminar papers and through the final thesis of the course of study.
Ability to apply knowledge and understanding:
General Area: At the end of the studies, the Master's degree graduates are able to:
a) develop strategies for governance and innovation in the organization, assuming an operational role in the management of the interventions and services provided by (and to) institutions, companies, third sector organizations
b) use the most appropriate theoretical approaches and quantitative methods to address the management problems related to financial regulation and economic analysis.
c) use the most appropriate theoretical and empirical methods in the organization of political systems
d) use the most appropriate legal, economic, statistical-quantitative, political, social, historical approaches to address the dimension and changes of contemporaneity, in particular in communications, journalism and the evolution of territorial structures and tourism
e) be able to analyze critically the theoretical contributions and studies developed by the professional world in order to evaluate their operational implications.
f) apply the acquired skills to the analysis and identification of problems in the areas of general debate related to legal, economic, statistical-quantitative, political, social, historical and historiographical disciplines.
g) abstraction to identify the essence of a problem and apply general principles to specific cases.
h) use the English language in a professional context
Economic Area: At the end of the studies, the Master's degree graduates are able to:
a) apply the main theses concerning the functioning of the current international economic system, with particular reference to the various aspects of the European integration process.
b) identify the expected dynamics of the fiscal and monetary policy of the European Union in the context of the evolution of the world economy
c) identify the evolution of the system of companies and local territorial bodies.
d) manage from the point of view of the public operator the problems related to non-market goods
Statistical-Quantitative Area: At the end of the studies, the Master's degree graduates are able to apply in a correct and efficient way the data and qualitative and quantitative information.
Legal Area: At the end of the studies, the Master's degree graduates are able to:
a) develop processes of interpretation, synthesis and critical and comparative analysis of the legal matter,
b) orient themselves in the field of various sectors and legal systems, of national and international relevance;
c) focus on the relationships between regulations and institutions
d) evaluate the legislation in its practical and jurisprudential reflections,
e) convey their general and specific knowledge to develop organizational, managerial and decision-making paths,
f) create relationships and interactions between institutional bodies, companies and public bodies
g) create relationships and interactions in the field of communication,
h) create relationships and interactions in the management of the territory and tourism
Political Area: At the end of the studies, the Master's degree graduates are able to:
a) have professional mastery of political vocabulary
b) develop critical analyses and evaluations, also in a comparative perspective, of political and social systems, decision-making processes and politics,
c) carry out autonomous research activities using qualitative and quantitative methodologies.
Historical Area: At the end of the studies, the Master's degree graduates are able to:
a) apply conceptual tools useful for interpreting regulations, institutions and phenomena in a critical way and in the perspective of their historical evolution
b) develop strategies and develop intentions in the various legal and economic fields, with particular regard to the sector of mass media, transport, changes in the territory and tourism.
c) carry out studies, research, critical analyses, multidisciplinary projects in both general and specific areas of acquired knowledge
Communication skills: Master's degree graduates acquire specific communication and interaction skills with specialists in their field and with non-specialist interlocutors, with technical language and high expression skills. To this end, it is necessary that at the end of the studies they are able to express themselves professionally on their skills in Italian and at least in another language of the European Union.
These communication skills are obtained through active participation in lectures and exercises, and above all through participation in seminar activities organized in small groups. The activities related to internships and traineeships will serve to complete the picture of the students' communication and elaboration tools. The verification of the acquired communication skills takes place through specific exams and tests.
Employment and professional opportunities for graduates: Official in Public and Private Administrations. Master's degree graduates acquire useful skills in a cross-cutting manner: they can carry out professional activities in companies and organizations, particularly in the area of administration, management control and corporate finance, as well as in all areas of Public Administrations, from the administrative departments of state bodies to state administrations, to local public bodies with particular reference to the tax agency, as well as in associations, institutions and private foundations with public purposes.
The employment opportunities and professional activities expected of the degree course can therefore be found in national and international institutions, in private companies operating also in the international market, in public and private organizations with high-level responsibilities. Graduates may also carry out professional activities in the governing structures of national, European and international bodies, developing and implementing public intervention policies, also aimed at territorial management and in the field of tourism.
In the latter sector, the Master's degree graduate also has the possibility of becoming a "tourism consultant", an emerging professional figure that falls within the broader sphere of business consultants.
Professional opportunities are also available in the field of publishing companies, newspapers, public and private press offices, news agencies and in any other sector of information and communication.
The professional opportunities are completed with activities in research institutes, public and private schools, associative, recreational, cultural and sports organizations.
Official language(s) of instruction and assessment: ITALIAN
Competencies associated with the function: Official in Public and Private Administrations. The graduate of the Master's Degree course acquires and applies the fundamental principles of economic reasoning, the main statistical concepts used in management disciplines, the main legal institutions of public and administrative law, with skills both for the analysis of social contexts and for the evaluation of organizational, financial and management processes. He/she therefore applies skills in the field of tax, financial and economic management of administrations and large companies.
In addition, the skills and knowledge acquired in the political-social, institutional, organizational-managerial, historical, statistical, linguistic fields, as well as in communication and information, are added. He/she therefore applies knowledge and skills in the field of: management and control in the Public Administration and in private companies, organization of work and management of human resources, acquisition of goods and services, evaluation and implementation of policies of public and private bodies, statistical analysis and of social contexts, management and organization of communication flows, press offices and public relations, management of tourism and territorial marketing.
Function in a work context: Official in Public and Private Administrations. Master's degree graduates may carry out functions in various work contexts, in accordance with the different curricula in which the course is structured:
functions in the field of public administration: general activities of public administration; activities of public administration aimed at regulating social activities; activities of public administration aimed at regulating economic activities, with particular reference to the tax sector; activities of centralized support services for the institutional activities of public administrations; Foreign Affairs; National Defense; Compulsory Social Insurance;
functions in the field of tourism and territorial marketing; in education; in the activities of associative organizations; in recreational, cultural and sports activities; activities of news agencies; in extraterritorial organizations and bodies.
functions of financial intermediation and other types of monetary intermediation; credit activities; insurance activities, auxiliary activities of insurance and pension funds and supervision of insurance and pension funds;
functions of research and experimental development in the field of social and humanistic sciences; services to businesses; management and administration of personnel on behalf of third parties; market research and opinion polls; administrative-managerial consulting; business administration; advertising and research, selection and supply of personnel
Description of specific training objectives: The training objectives of the Master's Degree consist in providing in-depth skills in legal, political-social, institutional, economic, managerial, historical, communication and information disciplines. The specificity compared to other Master's degree courses of the same class lies in the multidisciplinary nature that allows to intertwine diversified and complementary knowledge and skills for the effective management of the Public Administration and private companies, for the evaluation of the policies of public and private bodies, for the management of communication flows in the public sector, in the private sector and in large cultural, sports and associative organizations. The course aims to prepare for high-responsibility functions in companies, institutions and organizations and associations at national and international level.
The training course is structured on a common basis of CFU distributed in the characterizing scientific-disciplinary sectors (statistical-quantitative, economic-organizational, legal, decision-making and organizational processes), to which are added the CFU acquired in the related or complementary scientific-disciplinary sectors, for training activities to be chosen, for activities of internships and traineeships and for other knowledge useful for insertion in the world of work, and finally, for the final exam. Master's degree graduates must also consolidate their linguistic training through the acquisition of the B2 level of English language proficiency (Common European Framework). It is nevertheless considered appropriate to deepen the knowledge and mastery of the English language in order to train more competitive professional figures at both national and international level.
These articulations allow the training of a modern management of state bureaucracy and local authorities at national, European and international level, as well as the creation of professional figures capable of dealing with regulatory, organizational, technological innovations, and of designing initiatives for the improvement of services.
The various subjects in which the course is structured are mainly related to five disciplinary areas:
1) Statistical-quantitative, with reference to the knowledge and consequent mastery of the main statistical concepts used in management disciplines.
2) Economic, with reference to the acquisition of those tools of economic theory relevant to the problems of the international and European economy; the tools are also provided to analyze the financial behavior of economic subjects, the functioning of markets and intermediaries, the effects of money and finance on the real economy; the themes of public action are also deepened, also in the territorial field and those relating to the organizational characteristics of companies.
3) Legal, with reference to the deepening of the foundations and institutions of administrative law, also with regard to territorial governance and tourism, civil and commercial law, as well as the substantive law of the EU and international environmental law; the acquisition of knowledge of regional public law, national tax legislation and as it results from Community sources, the dynamics of European social law and criminal law in general; and finally, the learning of the normative and jurisprudential aspects of information law, declined as an autonomous disciplinary field and with reference to the communication processes in the political sector.
4) Political, with reference to the acquisition of the analytical tools necessary for understanding political and social systems, the dynamics of public policy, the methodology of research in political science and the tools, also historical, useful for understanding the political language.
5) Historical, with reference to the knowledge of the historical events that contributed to the construction of the Italian nation-state, to the learning of the history of the main institutional bodies present in the territory, of the history of information and Italian journalism between the 19th and 20th centuries, in a national and international perspective, and always for the same period, also of the history of communication and transport networks.
Satisfaction of graduates and impact on employment Alma Laurea 2016
Alma Laurea 2015
Alma Laurea 2014

Optional qualifications (choose from the following): [L2+ CER_LIN+TSS]

Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse.
Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan" www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni.
Roles: President/Chairman of the teaching committee: DANIELE PASQUINUCCI.
Program, texts and objectives: Access to further studies. Specialization courses, Research Doctorate courses, Second-level University Master's courses. Professional status conferred by the degree: Expert in international and public policies. Administrations and study offices of international governmental organizations, non-governmental organizations, think tanks and associations, companies operating in international cooperation. Specialists in control within the Public Administration. Administrations and study offices of international governmental organizations (Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Ministry of Cultural Heritage, Customs Agency, cultural foundations) and supranational organizations (in particular the European Union). Supranational organizations (Fao, Unesco, etc.). Specialists in human resources. International governmental organizations. Local governments. Cultural associations. Specialists in public relations, image and related professions. International governmental organizations. Local governments. Cultural associations. Analyst of public policies and expert in international relations. Universities. International organizations and research institutions. Associations and cultural companies operating in the field of cooperation. Specialists in conflict resolution in public and international administrations. Territorial administrations in areas of social and intercultural complexity, regional and central administrations. International representations. Characteristics of the final exam: To obtain the Master's degree in Public and Cultural Diplomacy, students must prepare an original thesis, guided by a supervisor. The thesis, written in English, must be related to one of the areas of research of interest to the course, or to a specific training experience carried out by the student, under the supervision of an academic tutor, at one of the partner institutions or organizations of the course. In any case, the thesis must contain original research and/or dissemination contributions. The final grade, expressed in hundredths with possible honors, is determined with reference to the weighted average of the grades obtained in the exams taken.
Knowledge required for admission: To access the Master's Degree Course in Public and Cultural Diplomacy, a degree, or a four-year degree of the old system, or a three-year university diploma or other qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable is required. The student who intends to enroll must have a basic knowledge of the humanities and social sciences. Knowledge of the English language is also required, at least at the B2 level of the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages of the Council of Europe. A minimum degree grade of 100/110 is required. Graduates of the following degree classes are admitted to the Master's Degree Course in Public and Cultural Diplomacy:
• L-1 (Cultural Heritage);
• L-3 (Disciplines of visual arts, music, performing arts and fashion);
• L-5 (Philosophy);
• L-6 (Geography);
• L-10 (Literature);
• L-11 (Modern Languages and Cultures);
• L-12 (Linguistic Mediation);
• L-16 (Sciences of Administration and Organization);
• L-18 (Sciences of Economics and Business Management);
• L-19 (Sciences of Education and Training);
• L-20 (Sciences of Communication);
• L-33 (Economics);
• L-36 (Political and International Relations);
• L-37 (Social Sciences for Cooperation, Development and Peace);
• L-39 (Social Work);
• L-40 (Sociology);
• L-42 (History).
Graduates of the corresponding degree classes of DM 509/1999, as well as graduates in other degree classes, are also admitted. However, it is necessary to have obtained a degree grade of 100/110 and to have obtained at least 75 CFU in at least two of the following groups of SSD:
a. IUS/07 (Labor Law); IUS/08 (Constitutional Law); IUS/09 (Institutions of Public Law); IUS/10 (Administrative Law); IUS/12 (Tax Law); IUS/13 (International Law); IUS/14 (Law of the European Union); IUS/19 (History of Medieval and Modern Law); IUS/21 (Comparative Public Law);
b. M-STO/02 (Modern History); M-STO/03 (History of Eastern Europe); M-STO/04 (Contemporary History); M-GGR/01 (Geography); M-DEA/01 Demoethnoanthropological Disciplines;
c. L-ANT/01 Prehistory and Protohistory; L-ANT/07 Classical Archaeology; L-ANT/08 Christian and Medieval Archaeology; L-ANT/09 Ancient Topography; L-ART/03 History of Contemporary Art; c. SECS-P/01 (Political Economy); SECS-P/02 (Economic Policy); SECS-P/03 (Public Finance); SECS-P/04 (History of Economic Thought); SECS-P/06 (Applied Economics); SECS-P/07 (Business Economics); SECS-P/08 (Economics and Management of Enterprises); SECS-P/12 (Economic History); SECS-S/04 (Demography);
d. SPS/01 (Political Philosophy); SPS/02 (History of Political Thought); SPS/03 (History of Political Institutions); SPS/04 (Political Science); SPS/05 (History and Institutions of the Americas); SPS/06 (History of International Relations); SPS/07 (General Sociology); SPS/08 (Sociology of Cultural and Communication Processes); SPS/09 (Sociology of Economic and Labor Processes); SPS/10 (Sociology of the Environment and Territory); SPS/11 (Sociology of Political Phenomena); SPS/12 (Sociology of Law, Deviance and Social Change); SPS/13 (History and Institutions of Africa); SPS/14 (History and Institutions of Asia).
The verification of curricular requirements is based on the documentation provided, from which the exams taken, the related SSDs, the CFUs obtained and the degree grade can be seen.
The verification of individual initial preparation is mandatory and only students who meet the curricular requirements can access it.
The methods of verification are set out in the Teaching Regulations and are adequately publicized each year on the course website.
Degree awarded: Master's Degree in Public and Cultural Diplomacy. Knowledge and understanding.
General aspects: The Master's degree graduate possesses advanced knowledge relating to a multicultural context within which the fluent use of the English language and knowledge of at least one second language, and the cultures associated with it, present in the areas of the globe at the center of the program, are essential. Area of socio-political studies: Master's degree graduates have advanced skills in the field of comparative analysis of political systems with particular reference to extra-European areas; knowledge of theories and interpretations of the dynamics of international relations and models of intervention in a range of public policies, with particular reference to the area of development cooperation.
Historical-legal analysis of the contemporary international dimension: Master's degree graduates possess specialized knowledge relating to the political-institutional development and the functioning of international organizations in different areas of the globe, with particular reference to human rights, constitutionalism in areas of transition, the history of globalization and international relations.
Area of history of cultures and cultural heritage: Master's degree graduates have in-depth knowledge of long-term transformation dynamics and cultural sedimentation in the areas of the globe involved. They also have specific skills in the design of public policies in a supranational perspective on cultural heritage.
Studies on socio-economic territorial models: Master's degree graduates have specialized skills in the field of geographical and economic analysis and on models of socio-cultural sustainability of systems in developing countries and in general in extra-European areas.

[D078] BIOLOGY Master's Degree Course in Italian and English
Course currently under approval. INFO COURSE STRUCTURE General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 2 years. Program: 2024. Status: Open. Credits: 120 CFU. Language: Italian and English. Type: Master's Degrees. Regulations: D.M. 270/2004. Degree Class: LM-6 - Class of Master's Degrees in Biology. Inter-university Course: No. Open Access: Yes. Location: Siena. Department: Department of Life Sciences. Website: https://biologia.unisi.it. Admission Requirements. Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]. Upper Secondary School Diploma. Optional qualifications (choose from the following): [TS + TSS]. Foreign qualification: [L2 cons+TSS]. First-level Degree: [L1 cons+TSS]. Fees and contributions. For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse. Dates and deadlines. For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni. Roles and Responsibilities for Student Mobility: MARIA FROSINI, President/Chair of the teaching committee. ANTONIO CARAPELLI. Program, texts and objectives. Professional status conferred by the degree.
Responsible for research and analysis, biotechnological and food laboratories, public and private. Responsible for research and pharmaceutical, biotechnological and food laboratories, and public and private bodies. Responsibility for public and private bodies (Municipalities, Parks, Protected Areas, Regions and museums).
Freelance biologist, self-employed.
Graduates with sufficient credits in appropriate subject areas will be able to participate in the entrance exams for training courses for secondary school teachers, as required by current legislation.
Characteristics of the final exam. The activities related to the preparation of the final exam for the Master's Degree in Biology are carried out by the student under the supervision of one or more tutor professors in the form of an internship in a university laboratory or an external public or private body affiliated with the University.
The aim of the internship is for the student to acquire in-depth knowledge of experimental methodology, analytical tools and data analysis and processing techniques.
The final exam involves the presentation of an experimental thesis summarizing the student's experience during the internship period, elaborated in an original way under the guidance of the supervisor. The ultimate goal is to present, discuss and critically share the knowledge acquired. The examination board, based on the results achieved during the course of study and the quality of the thesis and its presentation, assigns a score expressed in one hundred and ten.
Knowledge required for admission. Students who wish to enroll in the Master's Degree Course in Biology must hold a degree or a three-year university diploma, or another qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable. In addition to the curricular requirements, they must also have adequate preparation in the fundamental subjects for the profession of Biologist, such as Mathematics, Physics, Chemistry and Computer Science, as well as in the basic biological disciplines that provide essential knowledge about living organisms at the morphological, functional and structural level.
They must also have knowledge of the biochemical, molecular and cellular mechanisms that regulate heredity, reproduction and development. In particular, they must demonstrate prior acquisition of sufficient knowledge in the following disciplines that characterize the Master's Degree Course: Botany, Zoology, Ecology, Biochemistry, Molecular Biology, Cytology, Histology, Developmental Biology, Anatomy, Pathology, Microbiology, Hygiene, Physiology and Pharmacology.
1.
To be admitted to the Master's Degree in Biology, students must jointly meet the following curricular requirements:
i) have obtained a degree in one of the following classes under D.M. 270/04: L-13 Biological Sciences, L-2 Biotechnology, L-32 Science and Technology for the Environment and Nature, LM-13 Pharmacy and Pharmaceutical Industrial; or have obtained a degree under the regulations prior to D.M. 509/99 (five-year degrees in Biological Sciences);
ii) have obtained at least 40 CFU in the SSD BIO/01, BIO/05, BIO/06, BIO/07, BIO/09, BIO/10, BIO/11, BIO/12, BIO/13, BIO/14, BIO/16, BIO/18, BIO/19, MED/04, MED/07, MED/42.
2.
Graduates with a degree other than those listed above must have obtained at least 75 CFU in the SSD BIO/01, BIO/05, BIO/06, BIO/07, BIO/09, BIO/10, BIO/11, BIO/12, BIO/13, BIO/14, BIO/15, BIO/18, BIO/19, MED/04, MED/07, MED/42.
3.
For graduates from foreign universities, the adequacy of the curricular requirements is assessed on a case-by-case basis based on the consistency between the programs carried out in the different disciplinary areas, the basic training considered necessary for the advanced training offered by the course of study, as well as the linguistic skills.
Admission to the Master's Degree Course in Biology requires knowledge of the English language in written and spoken form at level B1.
The verification of personal preparation, which can only be accessed by those who meet the curricular requirements, takes place according to the methods provided for in the didactic regulations of the course of study.
Specific training objectives.
The Master's Degree Course in Biology aims to train graduates with biomedical/pharmacological, physiological, biochemical and naturalistic skills, differently applied according to relevance in different application areas represented by two area curricula:
1. cellular-molecular;
2. nutritional.
In addition, the training course is completed by Contextual knowledge and final exam.
- CELLULAR-MOLECULAR AREA: Includes courses relevant to the acquisition of knowledge and skills in the cellular and biomolecular field, both in the basic and applied aspects, in areas such as the chemistry of biomolecules in -omics sciences (CHIM/06, BIO/10), cellular and molecular biology and biodiversity in human, animal and plant contexts (BIO/01-03, BIO/05-07, BIO/11, BIO/18) and biomedical microbiology (BIO/19, MED/07, MED/42). It also includes courses in areas such as physiology (BIO/09) and pharmacology and toxicology (BIO/14), both in the basic and applied aspects.
- NUTRITIONAL AREA: Includes courses relevant to the acquisition of knowledge and skills in the nutritional field, both in the basic and applied aspects, in areas such as the chemistry and biochemistry of food (CHIM/06, CHIM/10, BIO/10), molecular biology applied in the field of nutrigenetics and nutrigenomics (BIO/11, BIO/18), pharmacology and nutraceutical principles (BIO/14), biodiversity, environmental sustainability, microbiology, hygiene and quality of food (BIO/01, BIO/03, BIO/05, BIO/19, MED/07, MED/42, AGR/15), applied physiology (BIO/09, MED/49) and pathophysiology (MED/04).
- CONTEXTUAL KNOWLEDGE AND FINAL EXAM: Contextual knowledge completes the training of the Biologist and is achieved through:
a. laboratory and practical activities;
b. collection and processing of experimental data;
c. learning the English language to level B2, for the purpose of communication and understanding of scientific publications, documents and European regulations.
The conclusion of the training course described above involves a final exam based on the work carried out by the student during the internship period, relating to the various aspects of biology, reported in a thesis organized according to the standards accepted by the international scientific community.
Degree awarded.
Master's Degree III - Autonomy of judgment. The graduate acquires conscious autonomy of judgment thanks to the extensive knowledge acquired in the characterizing and/or professionalizing disciplines, together with the internship (experimental and training) and/or internship activities in university laboratories, companies or public or private bodies. Specifically, the graduate develops autonomy of judgment regarding: i) responsibility for projects, structures and personnel; ii) identification of new perspectives and development strategies; iii) evaluation, interpretation and re-elaboration of literature data; iv) critical and responsible approach to ethical and bioethical issues.
The verification of the achievement of adequate autonomy of judgment takes place through tests, oral and/or written, for each course. In addition, the acquisition of autonomy of judgment by the student is verified through journal club activities and during the final exam.
Knowledge and understanding.
Generic area. Master's degree graduates who have successfully completed the course of study must have acquired the ability to understand and explain biological phenomena at the chemical, biochemical, molecular, cellular, physiological-functional, anatomical, pathological, pharmacological and population levels.
This knowledge and ability to understand are acquired and verified mainly through the specific courses and courses of the individual curricula according to their respective exams.
Ability to apply knowledge and understanding.
Generic area. Master's degree graduates who have successfully completed the course of study must: a. possess skills in chemical and biochemical, biomolecular and bioinformatics methodologies, statistics and data analysis with multidisciplinary application;
b. possess skills in the methods of study and understanding in the various biological contexts;
c. have developed complete mastery of the scientific method of investigation.
These skills are acquired using, as study material, texts and articles with both theoretical and experimental content, as well as proposing exercises on concrete and current cases and problems.
Particular emphasis is also placed on the verification of these skills during the evaluation of the final exam.
IV - Communication skills. The graduate acquires adequate skills and tools for communication, with reference to his/her ability to: i) present fluently in English using disciplinary vocabulary; ii) develop and present research and development projects; iii) organize group work; and iv) illustrate the results of the research - 'team working'. These skills are acquired through the presentation of literature data and the work, individually or in groups, produced during the exercises and laboratory activities, as well as during the internship. During the latter, the graduate must regularly present both literature data and the results obtained from his/her laboratory and/or data processing activities, within the framework of journal clubs organized by the respective supervisors. Graduates also participate, where possible, in conferences, where they are encouraged to present abstracts and oral or poster presentations, and where they can interact with scientists and professionals from their areas of interest.
The verification of the achievement of the objective of this descriptor will take place within ongoing tests, journal clubs, exams at the end of the training activities and the final exam.
Language(s) of instruction and assessment.
ITALIAN, ENGLISH V - Learning ability. The graduate will acquire adequate learning skills, with particular reference to the consultation of specialized databases, innovative technologies and the reading and critical analysis of scientific publications. This will take place through the teaching activities and other training activities provided, both within the individual courses and during the laboratory and practical activities.
The verification of the achievement of adequate learning ability will be carried out within ongoing tests, journal clubs, exams at the end of the training activities and the final exam.
Skills related to the function.
Responsible for research and analysis, biotechnological and food laboratories, public and private. Selection and use of methods aimed at the evaluation, certification, management and preservation of biological and environmental quality and biodiversity, in particular in the context of microbiological food and toxicological analysis, molecular diagnostics, quality control of products of biological origin and quality control and certification.
Ability to synthesize and disseminate scientific knowledge.
Freelance biologist. Environmental impact assessments, recovery plans, management plans, environmental restoration, biomonitoring for the assessment of environmental quality and for the study of the dynamics of natural populations.
Assessment of the conservation status of habitats and species included in international Directives and national laws. Assessment of biological damage to cultural assets. Control and assessment of biological quality and forensic expertise.
Setting up and managing food education programs aimed at general subjects or specific groups of individuals, in physiological and pathological conditions, in different ages and practicing or not sports. Evaluation and control of origin, nutritional quality and toxicology of food in chemical/biochemical and legislative and technical regulation, national and community level relating to food policies, hygiene and food control.
Passing the State Exam and registration in the register of biologists are necessary for the exercise of the profession of Biologist.
Function in a work context.
Responsible for research and analysis, biotechnological and food laboratories, public and private. Organization, direction and coordination of biomolecular and biodiversity analyses. Chemical, biochemical and molecular analyses in the functional, qualitative and toxicological evaluation; development and enhancement of functional foods and supplements to support biomedical and sports activities and for maintaining an optimal state of health.
Organization and management in the fields of the food industry and other activities related to the distribution, processing and administration of food.
Evaluation, planning, implementation and control of a research project.
Scientific dissemination and outreach.
Freelance biologist. Autonomous activity.

Location: Siena
Department: Biomedical Technologies
Website: https://dentistry.unisi.it
Admission Requirements: Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]
Upper Secondary School Diploma
Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse
Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan" www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni
Roles: President/Chairman of the teaching committee: SIMONE GRANDINI
Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
Dentist. Graduates with a Master's Degree in Dentistry and Dental Prosthodontics LM-46 have professional opportunities in private practice as a Dentist, after registration with the Professional Register. They can also carry out managerial activities of I and II level within the National Health System, as provided for by current legislation.
Specialists in the reconstruction of hard dental tissues: Private practice and managerial activities of I and II level within the National Health System, after registration with the Professional Register, as provided for by current legislation.
Specialists in the prevention, diagnosis and therapy of diseases of the supporting tissues of the tooth: Private practice and managerial activities of I and II level within the National Health System, after registration with the Professional Register, as provided for by current legislation.
Specialists in oral and implant surgery: Private practice and managerial activities of I and II level within the National Health System, after registration with the Professional Register, as provided for by current legislation.
Specialists in Orthodontics: Private practice and managerial activities of I and II level within the National Health System, after registration with the Professional Register, as provided for by current legislation.
Characteristics of the final exam: To be admitted to the final degree examination, the student must have attended all courses and passed the relevant exams.
Pursuant to Articles 1 and 3 of Law No. 163 of November 8, 2021, the final examination for the award of the single-cycle Master's degree in Dentistry and Dental Prosthodontics - class LM-46 qualifies for the practice of dentistry. To this end, the aforementioned final examination includes the performance of an Evaluative Practical Test (PPT) of the professional skills acquired through an Internal Evaluative Practical Training (TPT) within the study courses, aimed at ascertaining the candidate's level of practical preparation for qualification for the practice of the profession, which precedes the discussion of the degree thesis. The PPT is organized through the student's discussion of 3 multidisciplinary clinical cases treated as the primary operator during the TPT, which involve multidisciplinary treatment plans with procedures related to the majority of the disciplines covered by the TPT, according to the methods established by the protocol drawn up by the Permanent Conference of the Presidents of the Degree Courses in Dentistry and Dental Prosthetics, in agreement with the National Board of Dentists.
In order to pass the PPT, students receive a passing grade, after which they proceed to the discussion of the degree thesis.
To obtain the Master's Degree, a thesis must be prepared in an original way by the student under the guidance of a supervisor. The thesis, written in English, must present the requirements of an experimental research work on clinical or basic topics, or it can be configured as a compilation.
The grade of the final degree examination will be expressed in hundredths, with possible honors.
Knowledge required for access: To access the Single-Cycle Master's Degree Course in Dentistry and Dental Prosthodontics LM-46, it is necessary to have a high school diploma or another qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable.
It is necessary to have adequate initial preparation, acquired in previous studies, regarding mathematics, physics, chemistry and biology.
Access to the course is obtained by passing an entrance test in English, prepared by the Ministry.
The methods for accessing the Single-Cycle Master's Degree Course in Dentistry and Dental Prosthodontics LM-46 and the content on which the entrance test is based are established by Law No. 264 of August 2, 1999 "Provisions on access to university courses" (Official Gazette No. 183 of 6-8-1999), as well as by specific ministerial decrees issued annually, concerning both the methods and content of the entrance tests for courses with programmed access at national level, and the definition of the places available for admissions to the Single-Cycle Master's Degree Course in Dentistry and Dental Prosthodontics LM-46.
Students who are admitted to the course with a score below a predetermined minimum threshold will be assigned additional training obligations, to be completed within the first year of the course.
The adequacy of the student's preparation will be verified according to the methods indicated in the Didactic Regulations of the Study Course.
Certification of knowledge of the English language at the B2 level of the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages of the Council of Europe is also required.
Knowledge and understanding.
General area: The Master's graduate possesses advanced knowledge relating to the anatomy and physiology of the organs of the stomatognathic system.
The Master's graduate knows the etiological factors and understands the pathogenic mechanisms of the main pathological conditions of the oro-facial district. He also knows the rational basis of the various measures for the prevention of dental and oral diseases currently recognized as effective.
The Master's graduate knows the active principles and mechanisms of action of the drugs indicated for the therapy of dental and oral diseases, as well as for the techniques of anesthesia that are practiced in the oral cavity.
The Master's graduate has competence regarding the mechanisms of healing of tissues and surgical wounds, as well as regarding the processes of osseointegration of endosseous implants and the biomechanics of orthodontic tooth movements.
The Master's graduate knows the etiopathogenesis and prognostic evolution of neoplasms of the hard and soft tissues of the oro-facial district.
The Master's graduate is educated to establish with the patient a relationship based on his correct information, an indispensable prerequisite for obtaining consent to therapy, as well as on respect for his psychological dimension.
Basic disciplines: The study of basic disciplines is aimed at making the student understand the foundations of the structural organization and vital functions of the human body, the pathogenic mechanisms, the biological defense mechanisms and the pathological mechanisms of the immune system.
The student acquires basic knowledge of chemistry and biochemistry, biology, anatomy, histology, physiology, general pathology.
Scientific methodology and behavioral sciences: The Master's graduate in Dentistry and Dental Prosthodontics LM-46 must possess adequate knowledge of Biostatistics and Epidemiology, as tools of the scientific method and of the practice of Evidence-Based Dentistry. He must also acquire the general principles of bioethics and general psychology, as indispensable references in the relationship with the patient.
General medical disciplines: The Master's graduate in Dentistry and Dental Prosthodontics LM-46 must know the etiopathogenesis, diagnosis, therapy and prognosis of the most frequent medical and surgical pathologies, with particular reference to those that manifest in the oral cavity. The dentist must also have assimilated the fundamental notions of medical-biological relevance and the deontological rules of the medical profession.
Professionalizing Dental Disciplines: The Master's graduate in Dentistry and Dental Prosthodontics LM-46 must achieve a solid theoretical and practical preparation in the various dental disciplines, with particular regard to the most common diagnostic pathways and therapeutic procedures of restorative dentistry, pediatric dentistry, orthodontics and gnathology, periodontology, and oral surgery.
He must also be able to carry out accurate differential diagnosis in the presence of lesions and neoplasms of the soft tissues of the oral cavity or of the maxillary bones.
The Dentist must also possess adequate knowledge of general and dental radiology, as well as of the clinically relevant properties of dental materials.
The Dentist must also assimilate the fundamental concepts of economics for efficient management of professional activity.

UNIVERSITY: University of Siena
COURSE: [D129] GEOLOGICAL SCIENCES
SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unisi.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10894

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS: General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 3 years. Admission year: 2021. Status: Open. Credits: 180 CFU. Language: Italian. Type of degree: Standard Degree. Legislative Decree: D.M. 270/2004. Degree class: L-34 - Class of Geological Sciences degrees. Inter-university course: No. Open access: Yes. Location: Siena. Department: Department of Physical Sciences, Earth and Environment. Website: https://scienze-geologiche.unisi.it. Informative website: https://www.unisi.it/didattica/uffici-studenti-e-didattica/sportello-ufficio-servizi-agli-studenti-mattioli. Admission requirements: Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]. Upper secondary school diploma. Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse. Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni. Roles and responsibilities: Student mobility, LEONARDO DISPERATI, President/President of the teaching committee, ENRICO TAVARNELLI. Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree: Geologist. The main employment opportunities, relating to the graduate in Geological Sciences, refer to public and private bodies, companies and professional firms operating in geology applied to engineering, geological surveys, hydrogeology, environmental geology, mining and energy geology, territorial planning and soil protection. The graduate's training is a reference point for continuing studies in the Master's degree program in the degree class LM-74 (Geological and technological sciences).
To practice the profession (Presidential Decree 328/01), this is possible after passing the State Exam and registration in section B (Junior Geologist) of the Register of Geologists.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam aims to verify the overall scientific maturity of the candidate and their communication skills through the preparation of a concise individual experimental report, original or compiled, on one or more aspects of the subjects studied; the exam consists of a presentation and discussion in a public session, before a commission.
The preparation of the final exam will be carried out by the student under the supervision of a supervisor, carrying out autonomous activities at the University structures or as an extracurricular internship at companies, design or consulting firms, companies, public bodies (Regions, Provinces, Municipal Technical Offices, ASL, museums, parks, etc...); this activity may involve the acquisition of data on the ground, including geological surveys or environmental monitoring and related processing, or it may be carried out through the analysis of data obtained in technical/scientific laboratories, or it may also consist of a critical re-elaboration of data already existing in the literature.
The criteria for evaluation will be the completeness and thoroughness of the report and the expository skills. The grade of the final exam will be expressed in hundredths, with possible honors; the merit score will take into account, above all, the quality of the entire study path carried out by the student.
Knowledge required for access: To access the degree course in Geological Sciences L-34, it is necessary to have a secondary school diploma or another qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable. It is also necessary to have basic knowledge of mathematics, chemistry and physics.
Knowledge of the English language at level A2/2 is also required.
The assessment of the initial preparation of students takes place through a test. The results of the test are used to establish any additional training requirements, to be met within the first year of the course.
The knowledge required for access to the Degree Course in Geological Sciences and the methods of their verification will be specified in detail in the Didactic Regulations of the study course, where the additional training requirements will also be indicated in the event that the verification is not positive.
Knowledge and understanding.
Generic area: The graduate in Geological Sciences will acquire:
• knowledge in geological disciplines, both basic and applied, but framed in a more general context relating to the environment and environmental sustainability;
• knowledge of the planet Earth and understanding of the geological processes that led to the formation of rocks and sediments;
• knowledge relating to the geometric and compositional characteristics of rock bodies;
• knowledge regarding computer tools, mathematical methods and statistical techniques for the analysis and processing of geological data;
• knowledge regarding global climate change and its consequences for the environment and humans;
• knowledge regarding the environment and environmental sustainability as well as anthropology.
The objective will be achieved through the progressive addition of specific knowledge provided individually by the teaching courses (through lectures, exercises, laboratories and field trips) and critically discussed and reconnected with each other during transversal activities such as seminars, multidisciplinary laboratories and field trips.
Learning area: Scientific and methodological foundations: The graduate in Geological Sciences will acquire knowledge and skills relating to:
mathematical methods and physical and chemical processes relating to the Earth Sciences.
Learning area: Computer science: The graduate in Geological Sciences will acquire knowledge and skills relating to:
geodetic/cartographic reference systems;
techniques of georeferencing through Geographic Information System (GIS);
collection of geological data using Global Positioning System (GPS);
techniques for analysis and processing of geological data.
Learning area: Basics of Earth Sciences: The graduate in Geological Sciences will acquire knowledge and skills relating to:
internal structure of the Earth, plate tectonics, brittle and ductile structures of the Earth's crust;
erosional, transport and accumulation processes, sediments characteristic of sedimentary environments, sedimentary structures and principles of stratigraphy;
compacted packaging structures, crystal chemistry and crystal physics, classification of minerals, techniques for investigating minerals and rocks;
characteristics of the main magmatic, metamorphic and sedimentary rocks;
fundamentals of taxonomy, evolutionary biology, biostratigraphy, paleoecology, paleobiogeography for the Earth Sciences.
Learning area: Geological and paleontological: The graduate in Geological Sciences will acquire knowledge and skills relating to:
techniques of geological surveying, reading and interpretation of geological maps, reading and creation of geological sections;
field activities in biostratigraphic, sedimentological and structural fields for the creation of maps and geological sections;
anatomy and evolution of mountain ranges, geological history of the Alps and Apennines;
geological surfaces from subsurface and point data, geometric methods for the construction of folds.
Learning area: Geomorfological and geological applications: The graduate in Geological Sciences will acquire knowledge and skills relating to:
processes, factors and conditions of land modeling, products of physical and chemical alteration, types of morphologies, time factor and climatic morphology;
stress, deformation and fracture of rocks and soils, physical and mechanical characteristics of soils, in-situ tests for soils;
water cycle and water balance, circulation of water in the subsurface, hydrodynamics of the aquifer in natural and forced conditions, water reserves and resources and their temporal variability;
basic optics and stereoscopic vision, aerial, terrestrial and satellite imaging systems, geological-structural and lithological photointerpretation, multi-temporal analysis;
geo-pedological and geo-archaeological investigations.
Learning area: Mineralogical, petrographic and geochemical: The graduate in Geological Sciences will acquire knowledge and skills relating to:
processes and petrogenetic environments, classification and nomenclature of magmatic, metamorphic and sedimentary rocks, description and classification of rocks by chemical, diffractometric, spectroscopic analysis and observations under polarized light optical microscope and scanning electron microscope, main tecctono-magmatic associations, metamorphic reactions, P-T regimes and P-T-t paths;
physical and chemical processes of weathering of minerals and rocks, geochemical characteristics of soil, atmospheric deposits and surface and groundwater, sampling, preparation and chemical analysis of geomaterial samples;
processes of formation, evolution, ascent and emplacement of magmas and physical-chemical and mineralogical characteristics of volcanic products;
structures of the solid state and relationships between structure and physical-chemical properties of natural and synthetic solids;
environmental impact of the exploitation of sulfide mineralizations in southern Tuscany;
petrographic applications for the analysis of historical buildings and archaeological finds.
Learning area: Geophysics: The graduate in Geological Sciences will acquire knowledge and skills relating to:
concepts and methodologies of geophysical investigations, seismology, estimates of seismic hazard, gravimetry.

UNIVERSITY: University of Siena

TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: INFO ON STUDY PLANS
