INFO ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 2 years. Program: 2023. Status: Open. Credits: 120 CFU. Language: English. Type: Master's Degrees. Regulations: D.M. 270/2004. Degree Class: LM-31 - Master's Degree in Engineering Management. Inter-university Course: No. Open Access: Yes. Location: Siena. Department: Department of Information Engineering and Mathematics. Website: https://engineering-management.unisi.it. Informative Website: https://www.diism.unisi.it/it/didattica. Admission Requirements. Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]. Upper Secondary School Diploma. Optional qualifications (choose from the following): [L2+ CER_LIN+TSS]. Bachelor's Degree, Language Certification [L1+ CER_LIN +TSS]. Bachelor's Degree, Language Certification [L2+ OBG+TSS]. Bachelor's Degree, Open Badge [L1+ OBG +TSS]. Bachelor's Degree, Open Badge [TS +CER_LIN +TSS]. Foreign Qualification, Language Certification [TS +OBG +TSS]. Foreign Qualification, Open Badge. Fees and contributions. For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse. Dates and deadlines. For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni. Roles and responsibilities for student mobility: GIOVANNI GIAMBENE, President/Chairman of the teaching committee. CHIARA MOCENNI, Program, texts and objectives. Admission requirements. Two admission methods are possible for the Master's Degree: direct admission (based on the curriculum) or through an admission test. It is not possible to enroll with outstanding educational requirements. Direct admission is granted to students who meet both of the following conditions: 1. curricular requirements; 2. minimum quality requirements, consisting of a degree grade of no less than 95/110, or a weighted average of no less than 26/30 in courses corresponding to at least 40 CFU in specific Scientific-Disciplinary Sectors (SSD). Curricular requirements. Applicants must hold a degree in one of the following classes: - L-7 Civil and Environmental Engineering (8) - L-8 Information Engineering (9) - L-9 Industrial Engineering (10) - L-18 Economics and Business Management (17) - L-30 Physics (25) - L-31 Computer Science (26) - L-33 Economics (28) - L-35 Mathematics (32) - L-41 Statistics (37 Statistics) or equivalent class according to the former D.M.509/99 (the number of the equivalent class is shown in parentheses, together with the name if it is different). Students who hold a specialist degree under D.M.509/99 or a degree under the previous regulations to D.M.509/99, in engineering, mathematics and physics, also meet the curricular requirements, subject to assessment by the Teaching Committee of an appropriate differentiation of the educational objectives of these study paths with respect to the Master's Degree in Computer and Automation Engineering. For students with a degree awarded by a foreign university, the assessment of the curricular requirements is delegated to the Teaching Committee, which verifies in particular: - that the qualification is awarded upon completion of a course of study lasting at least three years; - that the educational objectives are sufficiently consistent with those of one of the degree classes listed above; - that the grade obtained during the course of study is adequate. The knowledge required for admission to the Master's Degree course is that of the basic and characterizing SSDs of the degree class in Information Engineering (L-8), relating to the disciplinary areas of mathematics, statistics, physics and information, automation, computer science, electronics and telecommunications engineering. Knowledge of the English language at least at level B1 of the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages is also required. Minimum quality requirements. The 40 CFU with the best grades relating to training activities in the basic and characterizing SSDs for the L-8 class listed below are taken into consideration. Basic Activities (SSD): ING-INF/05 Information processing systems, MAT/02 Algebra, MAT/03 Geometry, MAT/05 Mathematical analysis, MAT/09 Operations research, FIS/01 Experimental physics, FIS/03 Matter physics. Characterizing Activities (SSD): ING-INF/01 Electronics, ING-INF/03 Telecommunications, ING-INF/04 Automation, ING-INF/05 Information processing systems, ING-IND/16 Technologies and processing systems, ING-IND/17 Mechanical industrial plants, ING-IND/35 Economic and management engineering. For students who do not meet the minimum quality requirements, the following cases are possible: - Students who meet the curricular requirements but not the minimum quality requirements must take the admission test. - Students who do not meet the curricular requirements may take the admission test only if they have obtained a degree grade of no less than 100/110 and their study curriculum includes at least 36 CFU relating to training activities in the basic SSDs and 45 CFU in the characterizing SSDs, listed above. - Students who do not fall into one of the above cases cannot be admitted to the Master's Degree courses. For students with a specialist degree under D.M.509/99, a degree under the previous regulations to D.M.509/99, or a qualification awarded by a foreign university, the verification of the minimum quality requirements will be assessed on a case-by-case basis by the Teaching Committee. The admission test is held in oral form. Students who are about to graduate and request pre-registration for the Master's Degree are also admitted to the test. Two admission tests are normally held each year, one in October and one in December. Professional status conferred by the qualification. Managers of complex projects and systems. The Master's Degree graduate in Engineering Management finds their natural place within companies, public bodies and research centers, at national or international level, in managerial roles aimed at defining overall strategic choices. The course of study also allows students to pursue independent professional activities as consultants. The Master's Degree graduate in Engineering Management can take the exams necessary to access the Order of Engineers, in the Information Engineering and Industrial Engineering (senior) sections. Characteristics of the final examination. The final examination involves the preparation and discussion of a thesis (Master's Thesis), developed by the student in an original way under the guidance of a supervisor, possibly assisted by one or more co-supervisors. The Master's Thesis can be descriptive or theoretical-experimental. In any case, the final examination aims to highlight the skills acquired by the student for the study, understanding, critical evaluation and design of advanced and original solutions in the scientific/technological field. The skills of synthesis, scientific rigor and organization in the drafting of the paper and in the oral presentation of the work, which is planned at the end of the study path, will also be assessed. In the case of a theoretical-experimental thesis, the Teaching Committee appoints a co-supervisor, who is responsible for evaluating the candidate's original contribution and the organization of the paper. The thesis for the Master's Degree can be written in English, Italian or in an official language of the EU. It is possible to carry out part of the activities relating to the final examination within the framework of internships or traineeships at companies, organizations or research laboratories. Knowledge required for admission. Students with at least one of the following qualifications can access the Master's Degree course in Engineering Management: a) Degree in one of the following classes under D.M.270/04 (or in the equivalent classes under D.M.509/99, the number of which is shown in parentheses): L-7 Civil and Environmental Engineering (8) L-8 Information Engineering (9) L-9 Industrial Engineering (10) b) Degree in one of the following classes under D.M.270/04 (or in the equivalent classes under D.M.509/99, the number of which is shown in parentheses, together with the name if it is different): L-18 Economics and Business Management (17) L-30 Physics (25) L-31 Computer Science (26) L-33 Economics (28) L-35 Mathematics (32) L-41 Statistics (37 Statistics) together with having obtained a minimum number of CFU, as defined in the Didactic Regulations of the Course of Studies, in each of the following groups of Scientific-Disciplinary Sectors: Group A: MAT/xx, FIS/01, FIS/03, SECS-S/01, SECS-S/02, SECS-S/03, SECS-S/06 Group B: ING-INF/xx, INF/01, ING-IND/09, ING-IND/13, ING-IND/16, ING-IND/17, ING-IND/31, ING-IND/32, ING-IND/35, SECS-P/07, SECS-P/08 For students with a qualification awarded by a foreign university or relating to a previous regulation to D.M.509/99, the assessment of the curricular requirements is delegated to the Teaching Committee, which verifies in particular: that the qualification is awarded upon completion of a course of study lasting at least three years; that the educational objectives are sufficiently consistent with those of one of the degree classes listed above. Admission to the Course of Studies, for students who meet the curricular requirements, is subject to verification of personal preparation, according to the methods defined in the Didactic Regulations of the Course of Study. The knowledge required for admission to the Master's Degree course is that of the basic and characterizing SSDs of the degree class in Information Engineering (L-8), relating to the disciplinary areas of mathematics, statistics, physics and information engineering. Knowledge of the English language at least at level B2 of the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages is also required. Students, whose native language is not Italian, who do not reach at least level A1 of knowledge of the Italian language, must include in their study plans training activities aimed at achieving this level. Qualification awarded. Master of Science in Engineering Management. Teaching method. Conventional. Knowledge and understanding. Area of quantitative methodologies for analysis and decision-making. The Master's Degree graduate in Engineering Management knows the formal methods for supporting decisions in real-world situations. Starting from the basic modeling culture acquired in undergraduate studies, he learns to understand which approach is most appropriate in various decision-making contexts, depending on the strategic level of the decision, as well as the amount of information, time and computing resources available. The acquisition of knowledge and the ability to understand are verified through written and/or oral exams. Technological area. The Master's Degree graduate in Engineering Management deepens his knowledge of the aspects of information technologies most related to management issues. To this end, ample space is given to methodologies for the representation and evaluation of complex systems, to IT tools for business management and to the development of innovative technologies in various fields. The acquisition of knowledge and the ability to understand are verified through written and/or oral exams. Economic and management area. The Master's Degree graduate in Engineering Management acquires fundamental knowledge in the field of business management and industrial economics, through the study of specific business functions and processes such as marketing, innovation management and project management. The acquisition of knowledge and the ability to understand are verified through written and/or oral exams. Transversal skills. Knowledge of business realities in the field of engineering management, with particular reference to their specific characteristics and problems. Knowledge of the English language at an advanced level. The verification is represented by the internship, by further activities useful for entering the world of work and by the final examination. The latter makes it possible to evaluate the knowledge and skills acquired by the student, possibly in a business context.

Roles: President/Chairman of the teaching committee: PAOLA PIOMBONI
Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
Operator, manager and coordinator in laboratories for Medically Assisted Procreation (MAP).
The Master's graduate will be able to practice independently in public and private MAP laboratories after registration with the professional register, having passed the state exam. The Master's degree allows access to non-medical area specialization schools, the title of which allows to carry out activities as a level I manager in structures of the National Health System.
Operator, manager and coordinator in semen analysis centers. The Master's graduate will be able to work in public structures within the framework of independent practice in semen analysis laboratories or attached to MAP centers after registration with the professional register, having passed the state exam. The Master's degree allows access to non-medical area specialization schools, the title of which allows to carry out activities as a level I manager in structures of the National Health System.
Operator, manager and coordinator of biological banks for the preservation of reproductive cells and tissues. The Master's graduate will be able to work in public and private centers for the preservation of reproductive cells and tissues, independently or within MAP centers, within the framework of independent practice after registration with the professional register, having passed the state exam. The Master's degree allows access to non-medical area specialization schools, the title of which allows to carry out activities as a level I manager in structures of the National Health System.
Biotechnology researcher in private research centers operating in the field of human reproductive pathophysiology at national and international level. The Master's graduate in Biotechnologies of Human Reproduction LM-9 can hold the role of biotechnology researcher in private, national and international research centers operating in the field of human reproductive pathophysiology. He/she will also be able to participate in clinical trials as an expert in reproductive pathophysiology and in national and international research projects.
Manager and operator of biotechnological and pharmacological laboratories. The Master's graduate in Biotechnologies of Human Reproduction LM-9 can hold the role of manager and operator of biotechnological and pharmacological laboratories in the public sector, such as Universities and Research Institutions, in the private sector in companies active in the pharmaceutical and biomaterials fields, both at national and international level.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam for obtaining the Master's Degree in Biotechnologies of Human Reproduction LM-9 consists of an experimental thesis developed in an original way and written in English under the guidance of a supervisor. This work represents the result of the studies and research carried out in the last year of the course. The results necessary for the final exam can also be obtained through research periods carried out in Italy or abroad, in the form of internships at public and private institutions affiliated with the University. The final exam is discussed before a judging committee which expresses a merit score based on the originality of the subject of the work, on the expository skills, both written and oral, and on the research methodologies. The grade of the final exam is expressed in hundredths with possible honors.
Knowledge required for admission: To access the Master's Degree course in Biotechnologies of Human Reproduction LM-9, it is necessary to have a Bachelor's or Master's degree, a three-year university diploma, a four-year degree of the old system of a biological, biotechnological, medical-health type obtained at any university in the country or another equivalent qualification, deemed suitable, obtained abroad. It is necessary to have adequate basic preparation in cytology, biochemistry, biology, molecular biology, genetics, microbiology, general physiology.
The curricular requirements are met by having a Bachelor's or Master's degree in one of the following classes of D.M. 270/2004 (or corresponding in the former DM 509/99):
L-2 Biotechnology
L-13 Biological Sciences
LM-6 Biology
LM-9 Medical, Veterinary and Pharmaceutical Biotechnologies
LM-41 Medicine and Surgery
LM-13 Pharmacy and Pharmaceutical Industrial
Graduates from different degree classes may also be admitted, provided they have acquired at least 75 CFU in total in the specific disciplinary sectors indicated below and have obtained the degree with a minimum grade, as indicated in the didactic regulations of the study course:
- BIO/10; BIO/11, BIO/13; BIO/14 BIO/18; BIO/19; BIO/06; BIO/16; BIO/17, MED/03; MED/07, MED/46
For students who meet the curricular requirements, their personal preparation will be verified, according to the methods indicated in the didactic regulations of the study course.
In addition, possession of the certification of knowledge of the English language at level B2 of the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages of the Council of Europe is required.
Knowledge and understanding.
General area: The Master's graduate in Biotechnologies of Human Reproduction LM9 has advanced knowledge relating to the anatomy, histology, physiology and pathology of the male and female reproductive systems, the structure and functions of the biological systems involved in the reproductive process, such as the biology of male and female gametes, assisted fertilization techniques, the principles of embryology and the genetics of infertility. The Master's graduate has skills on the epidemiological aspects of infertility and on the management of the infertile couple from a gynecological, andrological and psychological point of view. The Master's graduate knows the active ingredients and mechanisms of action of the drugs used in assisted reproductive practices and in the management of couple pathologies. The Master's graduate has skills on the ethical and legal aspects related to the field of assisted reproduction. Furthermore, the ability to keep this knowledge up to date and connected to the most innovative and advanced aspects in the contexts of exercising one's profession must be developed.
Scientific methodology and behavioral sciences: The Master's graduate in Biotechnologies of Human Reproduction LM 9 has in-depth knowledge of epidemiology as a tool of the scientific method, as well as the current regulations in the field of assisted reproduction. The Master's graduate also acquires the general principles of general psychology, which are fundamental for interacting with the infertile couple.
General medical-biological disciplines: The Master's graduate in Biotechnologies of Human Reproduction LM 9 must have knowledge of the physio-pathology and histology of the male and female reproductive systems. He/she must acquire in-depth knowledge of human infertility genetics, as well as have skills related to the active ingredients and mechanisms of action of the drugs used in assisted reproductive practices and in the management of couple pathologies. Finally, he/she must acquire knowledge of the study of microorganisms involved in the most common infections of the male and female genital tract.
Disciplines characterizing the field of assisted reproduction: The Master's graduate in Biotechnologies of Human Reproduction LM 9 must have in-depth knowledge of the biology of oocytes, spermatozoa and embryos, must have knowledge of the management of gametes and embryos in the laboratory and their cryopreservation. Working closely with the gynecologist and andrologist, the Master's graduate must have adequate knowledge of gynecology and andrology that allows him/her to follow a complete diagnostic and therapeutic path of the infertile couple.

Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan" www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni
Roles: President/President of the teaching committee: FRANCESCA MARI
Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
Genetic counseling assistants: The Master's degree graduate in Genetic Counselling has professional opportunities in both private and public organizations that offer genetic counseling services.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam for obtaining the Master's Degree in Genetic Counselling, which is worth 12 credits, consists of writing a thesis developed in an original way by the student under the supervision of a supervisor. The thesis is the result of the studies and research conducted during the last year of the course.
The final exam is discussed orally before an appropriate examining board, which evaluates the merit score based on the following criteria: originality of the topic of the paper, expository skills both in writing and orally, research methodologies. The grade for the final degree exam is expressed in hundredths, with possible honors.
Knowledge required for admission: To be admitted to the Master's Degree course in Genetic Counselling, it is necessary to have a degree or a three-year university diploma or another qualification obtained abroad that is recognized as suitable.
The curricular requirements are met by having a degree in one of the following classes of D.M. 270/2004 (or equivalent in the former DM 509/99):
L-2 Biotechnology
L-13 Biological Sciences
L-24 Psychological Sciences and Techniques
L-27 Chemical and Technological Sciences
L-39 Social Work
L/SNT1 Health, Nursing and Obstetric Health Professions
L/SNT2 Health Rehabilitation Professions
L/SNT3 Health Technical Professions
L/SNT4 Health Prevention Professions
LM-13 Pharmacy and Pharmaceutical Industrial
Graduates from different degree classes may also be admitted, provided that they have obtained at least 75 credits in the following specific disciplinary sectors:
-BIO/06, BIO/09, BIO/10, BIO/11, BIO/12, BIO/13, BIO/14, BIO/15, BIO/16, BIO/18, MED/03, MED/04, MED/07, MED/08, MED/09, MED/10, MED/11, MED/12, MED/13, MED/14, MED/15, MED/16, MED/25, MED/26, MED/28, MED/30, MED/31, MED/32, MED/33, MED/34, MED/35, MED/38, MED/40, MED/46, M-PSI/01.
In addition, possession of a certification of knowledge of the English language at level B2 of the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages of the Council of Europe is required.
The student's initial preparation is verified through an interview on topics of genetics and the main biological processes of living organisms. Only those who meet the curricular requirements can attend the interview.
The methods of verification will be indicated in detail in the didactic regulations of the master's degree course.
Knowledge and understanding:
Human Genetics: The Master's degree graduate in Genetic Counselling must have knowledge of human genetics, medical genetics, and laboratory genetics. Therefore, it is necessary for them to acquire basic knowledge: i) of human genetics, ii) of molecular biology, iii) of the molecular mechanisms responsible for pathology, iv) of the available techniques for their identification, including the latest generation technologies; v) of typical and atypical inheritance patterns, vi) of how to calculate the probabilities of disease occurrence in individuals and offspring. Knowledge and understanding are acquired through lectures and curricular internship activities and are verified through ongoing tests and final exams in each specific sector.
Clinical Genetics: The Master's degree graduate in Genetic Counselling must have i) in-depth knowledge of the major syndromic and non-syndromic pathologies in various fields of medicine, including fetal pathology, ii) of the technologies available for carrying out genetic tests, both traditional and latest-generation, iii) of the therapeutic approaches already available and those under development for the most common and less-well-known pathologies. Knowledge and understanding are acquired through lectures and curricular internship activities and are verified through ongoing tests and final exams in each specific sector and during practical activities.
Genetic Counseling: The Master's degree graduate in Genetic Counselling must know: i) the basis of human psychology related and unrelated to pathological conditions, ii) the main psychological aspects that may arise from the diagnosis or risk of recurrence of a genetic disease, iii) the necessary approaches to interact with the patients and their family, iv) the legal aspects that may be related to genetic counselling. These skills are acquired through theoretical and practical study of all aspects of clinical and medical genetics, psychology, communication, and legislation. Lectures in counselling techniques and curricular practical internships represent the occasion to verify the acquired knowledge and the ability to explain and apply such knowledge.

Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse

Roles:
Study plan approval manager: GIAN MARCO BACCARI.
Chairman/Chairman of the teaching committee: GIAN MARCO BACCARI.
Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
LABOR CONSULTANT: To practice the profession of labor consultant, the graduate must complete a mandatory internship period at the office of an accredited labor consultant for a duration of eighteen months, six of which may also be completed during the third year of the degree course, in accordance with the agreement signed between the University of Siena and the Provincial Council of Labor Consultants of Siena.
Once the certification of completed training has been obtained, the interested party may participate in the annual accreditation exam, organized on a regional basis following the relevant announcement issued by decree of the Ministry of Labor.
Once the accreditation exam has been passed, the new labor consultant may register with the Council of the Order in the province where he/she intends to practice the profession.
The labor consultant can operate in a freelance capacity, with his/her own office, or in companies, public or private, trade unions, associations representing typical professional interests in the world of work and business. Other employment opportunities relate to the professional profiles of Judicial Services Technician, Occupational Safety Technician and Employment Services Technician.
Legal operator in companies and public bodies: The training course is designed for immediate entry into the labor market in central and local state administrations, with the exception of senior management, in territorial administrations, in public companies, in the organizational structures of administrations operating in the sectors of the environment, cultural heritage and immigration; in public and private companies, especially those that operate most frequently in contact with public administrations.
Characteristics of the final exam: The degree is obtained by passing a final exam, which consists of the evaluation, by the degree commission, of the final work drawn up by the student under the supervision and responsibility of the supervisor.
The final work consists of a written paper accompanied by the necessary bibliographic references on a topic agreed upon between the student and the supervisor, according to the methods defined in the didactic regulations of the study course.
To pass the final exam, the student must demonstrate that he/she has acquired adequate knowledge and understanding of the assigned topic, autonomous learning and research skills, the ability to correctly use the literature and sources consulted, and the ability to present and support scientific arguments in a logical and coherent manner.
The final work may also be written in English.
3 CFU are awarded for the final degree exam.
Knowledge required for access: To be admitted to the Degree Course, applicants must have:
- a secondary school diploma or another qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable;
- proficiency in English at level A2/2, which may be obtained in training courses organized by the University Language Center. The verification of the qualifications submitted attesting to level A2/2 is carried out by the University Language Center according to the usual procedures.
For those who enroll in the Degree Course in Legal Services, tests are prepared to verify the "students' initial knowledge," for which basic knowledge of general culture and current political events, civic education, with particular reference to constitutional principles, and modern and contemporary history is required.
Participation in these tests is mandatory, but does not affect admission or enrollment in the Degree Course. The methods of carrying out the verification test will be better defined in the didactic regulations of the Degree Course.
Students who do not pass the verification will be assigned additional training obligations to be completed in the first year of study through the appropriate remedial courses organized by the Department of Law; the preparation acquired will be verified at the end of the remedial courses. In case of persistent shortcomings, additional reading will be recommended and the student's entire training course will be periodically monitored by the tutor teachers.
Degree awarded:
First Level Degree in Legal Services.
Knowledge and understanding:
Public law area: The graduate:
- possesses adequate knowledge of constitutional law, with particular reference to the rules on the organization of the State and the production of law.
- is aware of the most current issues of administrative law and the doctrinal and jurisprudential interpretations;
- knows the substantive and procedural rules of most taxes;
- possesses good knowledge of the fundamentals of European Union law and international law;
- has knowledge of the general principles of criminal law and some of the specific offenses of the special part relating to the organization of the company and labor, crimes against the PA, as well as criminal procedure law.
- has knowledge of some of the most modern branches of the legal system, such as the law of new technologies and the law of privacy and data protection.
- possesses adequate knowledge to address the legal issues related to the organization of the world of work, the Public Administration, local authorities, and those specific to some sectors such as cultural heritage, tourism, consumer and trade of food products.
Private law area: The graduate:
- acquires the notions of general theory (legal system, sources, constitution, code, interpretation);
- possesses excellent knowledge of the fundamental institutions of private law, with particular regard to the aspects that concern the professional skills of labor consultants and experts in industrial relations;
- has in-depth knowledge of the fundamental principles of commercial law and civil procedure law, with particular reference to the procedures for judicial and extrajudicial resolution of labor disputes.
- has a deep knowledge of the legislation on privacy and new technologies;
- possesses excellent legal knowledge to address the issues related to the tourism sector, as well as the production, consumption and trade of food products, with particular reference to the issues relating to agricultural production, labeling and enhancement of typical products of the territories.
Historical-philosophical area: The graduate:
- possesses adequate knowledge of the historical evolution of the legal models of the company and labor, also within the framework of public local and national organization, both as regards the origin of the fundamental institutions and as regards the development of the protection systems.
- knows the fundamental notions of the theory of law.
Labor law area: The graduate:
- has in-depth knowledge of the rules on labor and trade union matters, the regulations relating to the protection of worker health, safety in the workplace, the regulations on social security, and the conciliation procedures;
- has adequate knowledge of European legislation on labor;
- has a solid mastery of the discipline of labor in the PA;
- is fully aware of the ways of interaction between the different social parties present in the labor market.

Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse

Roles and Responsibilities: Student Mobility, PASQUALE RUGGIERO, President/Chairman of the teaching committee, PAOLO PIN, President, PAOLO PIN
Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
Economist: Graduates in Economics can work in government and international organizations, public and private companies, banks and other financial institutions, trade associations and trade unions.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam consists of a discussion before a special degree commission of a thesis developed in an original way under the guidance of a supervisor. It must verify that the master's graduate has acquired advanced knowledge on relevant economic issues, both theoretically and professionally, which allows him to develop or apply original ideas through a systematic and critically aware understanding, that he has the ability to apply the knowledge acquired and to solve problems on innovative topics also in an interdisciplinary context, that he has the ability to clearly and correctly expose and argue in written and oral form. The thesis is written in English. The merit score awarded to the final exam takes into account the level of theoretical depth, the ability demonstrated by the candidate in the use of quantitative methodologies appropriate to the objective of the research, the effectiveness of the empirical analyses carried out, the originality of the arguments proposed and the effectiveness of the discussion before the examining commission.
Knowledge required for access: To access the Master's Degree in Economics, it is necessary to have a Bachelor's degree or a three-year university diploma, or another qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable. The didactic regulations of the degree course define the requirements for admission to the Master's Degree in Economics with reference to the different first-level degree classes or, for graduates in classes different from those specified, the CFU obtained in specific disciplinary sectors.
In particular, to meet the curricular requirements, it is necessary to have a three-year degree in economics, statistics, mathematics or political science and international relations. Specifically, with reference to the ex DM 270/2004 classes: L-33 Economics, L-18 Economics and Business Management, L-41 Statistics, L-35 Mathematics, L-36 Political Science and International Relations, L-37 Social Sciences for Cooperation, Development and Peace. The requirements are also met by having obtained degrees and diplomas of previous degree programs deemed equivalent to those indicated or qualifications obtained abroad that are equivalent and recognized as suitable.
Admission is also permitted with the possession of a suitable qualification in a degree class other than those specified above, provided that at least 75 CFU have been obtained in specific scientific-disciplinary sectors of the areas:
- economic (SECS-P/01, SECS-P/02, SECS-P/03, SECS-P/05, SECS-P/06, SECS-P/07, SECS-P/08, SECS-P/09, SECS-P/10, SECS-P/11, SECS-P/12, M-GGR/02, AGR/01, SPS/04)
- mathematical-statistical (SECS-S/01, SECS-S/02, SECS-S/03, SECS-S/04, SECS-S/05, SECS-S/06, MAT/01, MAT/02, MAT/03, MAT/04, MAT/05, MAT/06, MAT/09, ING-INF/05, INF/01, CHIM/01, CHIM/12, BIO/07, ICAR/15)
- legal/sociological/political (IUS/01, IUS/04, IUS/05, IUS/06, IUS/07, IUS/09, IUS/10, IUS/12, IUS/13, IUS/14, SPS/01, SPS/02, SPS/04, SPS/05, SPS/07, SPS/10, M-STO/04, M-FIL/01, M-FIL/06)
of which at least 40 CFU in the first two areas (economic and mathematical-statistical).
The credits in the specific scientific-disciplinary sectors provided as curricular requirements must be obtained before enrollment (enrollment with educational debts is not allowed).
Admission also requires a knowledge of the English language at an adequate level (B2 level).
For graduates with a qualification obtained abroad, the knowledge required for access is defined by the didactic regulations of the study course based on the minimum content deemed necessary for the different disciplinary areas, including those relating to the Italian language and the English language.
For the purposes of admission to the study course, students who have the required curricular requirements must pass a selection test to verify their personal preparation, the methods of which are specified annually in the admission notice for the course.
Knowledge and understanding:
AREA OF FUNDAMENTAL KNOWLEDGE: Master's graduates in Economics must master the knowledge necessary for carrying out autonomous economic research. The courses taught in the first year must provide students with the basic analytical tools of a mathematical-statistical, economic (both microeconomic and macroeconomic) and econometric type, which will enable them to easily navigate various topics of economic theory and application, and to continue in the deepening of specific topics up to their most recent developments. They must also be able to critically analyze theories and identify appropriate methods of analysis for solving policy problems.
AREA OF SPECIALIZED ECONOMIC KNOWLEDGE: Students must acquire advanced knowledge, both theoretical and applied, in a variety of areas of economic discipline, so that they can achieve a complete understanding of the functioning of the economic system, the actions of economic actors, the available options for economic policy, and the interpretation of long-term processes.
The courses available cover topics in macro and microeconomics, are both theoretical and applied, and respond to a variety of theoretical approaches. The aim is to provide a comprehensive but not narrowly focused overview of a single theoretical approach.
AREA OF PUBLIC POLICIES AND INSTITUTIONAL CONTEXT: As also highlighted by consultation with representative organizations, it is important for master's graduates in this type of study course to acquire knowledge and skills useful for the interpretation and understanding of the main public policies, also in their institutional and regulatory dimension.
Some of the courses offered, of an economic and legal nature, provide useful knowledge for this purpose, combining economic analysis with an understanding of the concrete tools of economic policy and the problems of implementing political decisions.
AREA OF MANAGERIAL AND FINANCIAL KNOWLEDGE: Master's graduates in Economics must have acquired knowledge relating to the functioning of financial markets and the strategies of internationalization of companies. These are complementary knowledge to those in the strictly economic field, aimed at completing the preparation, especially for those who wish to enter the world of business or finance.

Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse.
Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni.
Roles and Responsibilities: Student Mobility: PASQUALE RUGGIERO, President/Chairman of the teaching committee: SILVIA TIEZZI, President: SILVIA TIEZZI.
Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
Environmental-economic analyst. The skills and knowledge acquired during the course of study allow the Master's graduate in Economics for the Environment and Sustainability to access many types of professions and employment. In particular, the Master's graduate in EAS can work at:
- international, national and local agencies for research and applied analysis in the field of environmental economics;
- private and public companies, with managerial, operational and consulting functions, with particular reference to the design and implementation of environmental investments and the management of scarce environmental resources (water, waste, energy);
- public administrations and public, private and non-profit companies with management and advisory functions;
- companies operating in the service sector (utilities sector), consulting and auditing, in particular as experts in social accounting and sustainability.
They can also carry out consultancy activities for the enhancement of the historical, cultural and environmental heritage of a territory.
Thanks to the solid economic and quantitative knowledge acquired, they are not excluded from entering the banking and financial sector, with particular reference to the ethical finance sector, and in the field of research (possibly after further specialization in a doctoral course) at universities, research centers and agencies, national and international.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam consists in the preparation of a paper that deals, in an original way, with theoretical topics or carries out empirical analyses relating to the subjects of study. The preparation work is carried out under the supervision of a supervisor and a co-supervisor and serves to verify that the Master's student has acquired in-depth knowledge on relevant economic and environmental issues, both theoretically and professionally, which allows him to develop or apply original ideas through a systematic and critically aware understanding.
The thesis is written in Italian or, at the student's request, in English. The final exam is worth 21 CFU and a maximum of 8 points, in line with the important role that the study program attributes to the development of an autonomous capacity for research and analysis on the part of the student.
Knowledge required for access: To access the Master's Degree Course in Economics for the Environment and Sustainability (EAS), it is necessary to have a degree or a three-year university diploma or another equivalent qualification obtained abroad. The curricular requirements are met by having a degree in one of the following classes of D.M. 270/2004 (or corresponding in the former DM 509/99):
Economics L-33 (28).
Business and Management Sciences L-18 (17).
Statistics L-41 (37).
Mathematical Sciences L-35 (32).
Political and International Relations L-36 (15).
Social Sciences for Cooperation, Development and Peace L-37 (35).
Alternatively, it is necessary to have obtained a degree in the previous four-year program in economics, statistics, mathematics or political science and international relations.
In addition, possession of the certification of knowledge of the English language at level B1 of the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages of the Council of Europe is required.
For graduates who do not have these requirements, possession of the following curricular requirements is required:
a) Having obtained a three-year degree under DM 270/2004 or DM 509/1999, or a four-year degree in the previous system.
b) Having obtained at least 75 CFU in the following SSDs:
group 1 - economic area SECS-P/01 SECS-P/02 SECS-P/03 SECS-P/05 SECS-P/06 SECS-P/07 SECS-P/08 SECS-P/09 SECS-P/10 SECS-P/11 SECS-P/12 M-GGR/02 AGR/01 SPS/04
group 2 - quantitative area SECS-S/01 SECS-S/02 SECS-S/03 SECS-S/04 SECS-S/05 SECS-S/06 MAT/01 MAT/02 MAT/03 MAT/04 MAT/05 MAT/06 MAT/09 ING-INF/05 INF/01 CHIM/01 CHIM/12 BIO/07, ICAR/15
group 3 - legal/sociological/political area IUS/01 IUS/04 IUS/05 IUS/06 IUS/07 IUS/09 IUS/10 IUS/12 IUS/13 IUS/14 SPS/01 SPS/02 SPS/04 SPS/05 SPS/07 SPS/10 M-STO/04 M-FIL/01 M-FIL/06
of which at least 40 CFU in groups 1 (economic area) and 2 (quantitative area).
c) Possess knowledge of the English language at level B1, based on the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages.
For graduates who have obtained a degree abroad, the adequacy of the curricular requirements will be assessed on a case-by-case basis by the Teaching Committee, taking into account the consistency of the programs carried out in the various disciplinary areas with the basic training considered necessary for the advanced training offered by the course of study, as well as the linguistic knowledge.
For the purposes of admission to the course of study, students who have the required curricular requirements must pass a test to verify their personal preparation. The test is oral. It deals with topics of political economy (microeconomics, macroeconomics) at an introductory level and topics of mathematics, statistics and econometrics. The verification is carried out by an examining board appointed by the Department Council, on the proposal of the Teaching Committee. The dates of the interviews, the details of the topics on which the admission test will be based and the indication of recommended texts for preparation are published annually on the course website.
The Teaching Committee, or a person delegated by it, may consider that graduates who, in consideration of the grades obtained and the content acquired in the previous three-year degree, have adequate personal preparation to attend the degree course profitably, have passed the personal preparation verification.
Knowledge and understanding:
Generic Area: The courses provided in the EAS degree course will provide students with the tools to understand and explain the evolution and mechanisms of the economic system in relation to the environment and the use of environmental resources.
The courses are structured to provide knowledge suitable for identifying and evaluating the various economic and environmental policy and regulation tools used or to be used to address environmental challenges at the local or supralocal level. At the same time, the EAS graduate will have in-depth knowledge of the main techniques for evaluating and enhancing intangible assets. To this end, the basic knowledge necessary to frame the complex economic and environmental phenomena is provided mainly in the first year of the course, leaving the training activities of the second year with the task of providing the student with advanced analytical and quantitative knowledge, to be acquired also through the preparation of projects, the completion of assignments and the analysis of case studies.
The combination of courses and activities of different kinds, appropriately combined, will allow the EAS student to acquire interdisciplinary knowledge that will allow him a more complete understanding of the problems and phenomena under study.
Economic Area: Students must acquire advanced knowledge in the various areas of environmental economics, both theoretical and empirical-applied. This knowledge must allow them to achieve a complete understanding of the functioning of the economic system and its interactions with social and environmental phenomena.
Mathematical-statistical area: Students must necessarily acquire adequate statistical knowledge which, in synergy with the other knowledge acquired, allows them to form the figure of the environmental economist.
Business area: Students must also acquire economic and business knowledge to understand the environmental aspects most directly related to business management and its related environmental and social sustainability.
Legal area: In order to complete the picture of the knowledge necessary for a fruitful analysis of economic and environmental phenomena, the graduate in Economics for the Environment and Sustainability may acquire adequate knowledge of environmental or sustainable development law.

UNIVERSITY: University of Siena
COURSE: [D303] SUSTAINABLE INDUSTRIAL PHARMACEUTICAL BIOTECHNOLOGY
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: INFO ON STUDY PLANS

INFO ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 2 years. Program: 2020. Status: Open. Credits: 120 CFU. Language: English. Type: Master's Degrees. Regulations: D.M. 270/2004. Degree class: LM-8 - Class of Master's Degrees in Industrial Biotechnology. Inter-university Course: No. Open Access: Yes. Location: Siena. Department: Biotechnology, Chemistry and Pharmacy. Website: https://sustainable-biotechnology.unisi.it/it. Informative website: https://sustainable-biotechnology.unisi.it/it. Admission requirements. Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]. Upper Secondary School Diploma. Optional qualifications (choose from the following): [L2+ CER_LIN+TSS]. Bachelor's Degree, Language certification [L1+ CER_LIN +TSS]. Bachelor's Degree, Language certification [L2+ OBG+TSS]. Bachelor's Degree, Open Badge [L1+ OBG +TSS]. Bachelor's Degree, Open Badge [TS +CER_LIN +TSS]. Foreign qualification, Language certification [TS +OBG +TSS]. Foreign qualification, Open Badge. Fees and contributions. For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse. Dates and deadlines. For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni. Roles and responsibilities for student mobility: ELENA PETRICCI, MATTIA MORI. President/President of the teaching committee: ADALGISA SINICROPI. Program, texts and objectives. Professional status conferred by the degree: Sustainable Industrial Pharmaceutical Biotechnologist. The original combination of biotechnologies linked to a key industrial sector for the country in an international context with innovative and now essential aspects of sustainability should ensure that graduates of the Sustainable Industrial Pharmaceutical Biotechnology program have increasing employment opportunities. Below is a list of the main career paths:
- Researchers in the public and private sectors, to carry out research, research and development, research and management roles in bio-industrial production and the various related processing processes; for the needs of human health and in general for sustainable development; for the management and design of production facilities in the biotechnology, pharmaceutical, diagnostic, chemical, environmental, agri-food, renewable energy industries.
- Managers of research and development laboratories and production and quality control departments in biotechnology companies and other companies interested in sustainable biotechnological innovation, such as pharmaceutical, diagnostic, cosmetic, agri-food, chemical (fine chemistry, bioenergy, innovative materials), renewable energy companies and in the field of the bioeconomy, i.e. companies interested in planning industrial activities aimed at sustainable development.
- Managers responsible for biotechnological plants.
- Entrepreneurs of sustainable biotechnology companies, to transfer innovative ideas from green, eco-sustainable, bioeconomic and circular economy research to commercial application.
- Professionals in the field of assessing the economic, commercial and financial impact of a biological or biotechnological technology, and in the protection of industrial and intellectual property.
- Professionals for public and private institutions active in the field of environmental impact analysis and environmental certification services.
- Manager of public and private laboratories for biotechnological, environmental, agri-food, cosmetic, chemical, clinical, control and research analysis services.
- Manager in the commercial field, for the development and management of the presentation of sustainable biotechnological products or processes to a sector-specific or non-specialized audience.
- Manager in the bodies responsible for the development of patent regulations concerning the exploitation of products and/or processes of the bioindustry; commercial and documentation organizations.
- Employees in the field of public communication of science, through the integration of their knowledge with notions of communication science, for the dissemination of biotechnologies in specialized environments and to the general public, through traditional and innovative means of communication or at scientific dissemination agencies and specialized press.
- Employees in the field of quality certification companies and organizations.
- Trainers, through the integration of their scientific knowledge with notions of communication, sociology, and science of education for the transfer of knowledge and for technical and scientific training and updating.
- Freelancers, for example, registration with the Register of Biologists, the Order of Chemists, or the National Association of Italian Biotechnologists, after passing the state exam.
- Figures and profiles that may increasingly play a role in emerging production processes, where the spread of intelligent technologies creates a demand for profiles dedicated to monitoring, storing and analyzing the information generated.
- Figures with technical and professional characteristics linked to the key of the near future in the digital factory: the analysis of big data, artificial intelligence and biotechnology.
The Master's graduate will also be well prepared to continue their education in Doctoral Research, Specialization Schools and Second-Level Master's programs at universities and research centers, both national and international.
Collaboration with the Department of Science of Education, Humanities and Intercultural Communication of our University will also allow the Master's graduate in Sustainable Industrial Pharmaceutical Biotechnology to adequately and effectively carry out teaching duties in the following teaching classes: Code A060 - Natural Sciences, Chemistry and Geography, Microbiology and Code A057 - Food Science, thus contributing to bridging a gap in training and learning for Italian students in the field of Science, compared to the European average, as recently described in the OECD-Pisa 2018 report.
Characteristics of the final exam. To be admitted to the final exam, all credits must be obtained in the training activities provided for in the study plan.
The training activities relating to the preparation of the final exam for the award of the degree and the related verification in the preparation of a final thesis, carried out under the guidance of one or more supervisors and consisting of two individual papers, the first based on in-depth studies of topics covered in the seminar activities, through meta-analysis and data mining in teamwork with final individual elaboration, the second resulting from an original individual experimental activity (linked to a project or carried out within a traineeship) in Italian or foreign university laboratories, or in research and development laboratories of external companies and institutions accredited, according to the methods established in the Regulations.
The dissertation must demonstrate mastery of the subjects, critical ability, the ability to operate independently and a good level of communication skills. In addition to the experimental content and its scientific value, the clarity of presentation, the ability to synthesize and the degree of experience gained in the use of multimedia communication tools are evaluated.
Knowledge required for access. To access the Master's Degree course in "Sustainable Industrial Pharmaceutical Biotechnology" (LM-8), it is necessary to have a Bachelor's degree or a three-year university diploma or another qualification obtained abroad recognized as suitable.
To be admitted to the Master's degree course, it is necessary to have certain curricular requirements and adequate personal preparation.
Curricular requirements:
The curricular requirements are met by having a degree in one of the following classes of DM 270/2004 (or equivalent in the former DM 509/99):
- Biotechnology - L-2
- Biological Sciences - L-13
- Agricultural and Forestry Sciences and Technologies - L-25
- Food Science and Technology - L-26
- Chemical Sciences and Technologies - L-27
- Pharmaceutical Sciences and Technologies - L-29
- Physical Sciences and Technologies - L-30
Graduates from different degree classes may also be admitted, provided that they have obtained at least 75 CFU in total in the specific disciplinary sectors indicated below:
BIO/10 Biochemistry BIO/11 Molecular Biology BIO/18 Genetics BIO/19 General Microbiology CHIM/01-CHIM/12
MAT/01-MAT/09
FIS/01-FIS/08
It is also required to have a certification of knowledge of the English language at the B2 level of the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages of the Council of Europe.
For foreign students, verification of adequate knowledge of the Italian language is required. Students who do not have these language skills must attend specific courses to reach the required level, as part of further training activities (Art. 10, paragraph 5, letter d).
Verification of the adequacy of personal preparation:
The verification consists of an interview or a test. The methods of verification will be indicated in detail in the didactic regulations of the Master's degree course.
Knowledge and understanding.
Biotechnological and biomolecular area. In order to effectively combine knowledge and skills, the new Master's Degree in Sustainable Industrial Pharmaceutical Biotechnology has been co-designed with scientific, humanistic, economic, legal departments and, above all, with companies. The training program leaves ample space for co-teaching and seminar activities from the industrial and extra-academic world on topics that cannot be covered by conventional study courses, thus drawing on the experiential training from the world of work. Students will be trained with innovative and immersive teaching methods, with laboratories spread throughout the territory, prioritizing the development of passion, interest, curiosity, emotional intelligence, and the ability to work as a team, rather than an academic path that is traditionally very individual in Italy.
The Master's graduate in Sustainable Industrial Pharmaceutical Biotechnology:
- knows the sustainable methods for the design, production, purification and development of bioactive molecules of pharmaceutical or diagnostic interest
- knows the biocatalytic methods necessary for the requalification of industrial processes
- understands and applies the spectroscopic and computational techniques used for the determination of the structure of biological macromolecules
- understands a process scheme for the production of a biotechnological product, and selects the most appropriate process based on the type of product to be isolated and the required purity, knowing how to perform mass and energy balances for multi-component systems in single equipment and in complex systems
- knows the specific technological platforms ('omics', post-genomic platforms, high-throughput screening systems, molecular graphics programs, big data analysis)
- is able to analyze and discuss with mastery problems of organic chemistry, bio-organic chemistry, biochemistry, molecular biology, life cycle thinking and understand the methodologies used for these studies
- knowledge of technology transfer and intellectual property
- can propose experimental strategies, design and manage processes, through the selection and application of appropriate analytical methods
- can apply the 10 top skills indicated by the third World Economic Forum.
Bioinformatics and industrial area. The Master's degree course in Sustainable Industrial Pharmaceutical Biotechnology aims to train new professional figures who possess, in addition to an in-depth knowledge of biomolecular and bioinformatics sciences, also specific knowledge of sustainability applied to the industrial biotechnological-pharmaceutical field, in its scientific, economic, environmental and international regulatory aspects. To achieve this objective, it will make use of teaching courses that have integrated and consequently coordinated scientific-disciplinary content, grouped into five thematic training areas (Pharmaceutical Products, Biobased Products, Sustainability, Bioinformatics & Big Data, International management & Regulatory Affairs) in order to arrive at a completely original and professional knowledge building that represents the most innovative aspect of the biological sciences linked to the drug and industrial biotechnological applications, allowing the student to understand and manage the main technologies applicable to the problems of development and management of renewable resources.
The Master's graduate:
- has solid chemical, biochemical and bioinformatics knowledge of the structure-function relationships of biological macromolecules and the cellular processes connected to them
- has solid knowledge of the instrumental and methodological approaches for the design and sustainable and green preparation/production of synthetic and biotechnological drugs
- has solid knowledge of the instrumental and methodological approaches for the study and characterization of bioactive molecules and biological macromolecules
- knows the fundamental procedures in the various fields of industrial biotechnology, with particular attention to multidisciplinary approaches
- acquires in-depth knowledge of the methodologies applied to the design, production and characterization of bioactive products and biomaterials in a biorefinery, bioeconomy and circular economy perspective
- knows the latest developments in the production of biofuels, bioplastics and biomaterials from waste, understanding the evolution of a bioprocess from the laboratory scale to the pilot scale and then to the industrial scale
- acquires in-depth knowledge of the analysis of environmental impact assessments of industrial biotechnological processes and products in an integrated sustainability perspective
- has solid methodological knowledge to carry out big data analysis in the field of bio-chemical computational analysis, also with a view to their applications in industrial biotechnology and Industry 4.0
- knows in depth the scientific elements for the international development of companies, strategies and policies of internationalization and the related legislative, regulatory and intellectual property aspects of biotechnology with respect to national and international guidelines.
The knowledge listed above is acquired through activities that include participation in lectures which will be integrated for each individual course by experimental laboratory activities and interactive teaching activities. The knowledge acquired will be verified at the time of the final exam relating to the courses by means of written and/or oral tests designed to assess the use of logical rigor and also the ability to understand sustainable biotechnological processes and, where possible, the ability to work in a group.
The language used will be English, in order to allow students to acquire fluency in the reference language for the sector, improve their presentation and communication skills in scientific vocabulary and facilitate direct interaction with international experts from the industrial world and/or who will provide seminar-type activities.
A significant part of the knowledge will be acquired through the acquisition and experimentation of Soft Skills (the 10 top skills indicated by the third World Economic Forum) and seminar-type activities, also administered in the form of webinars, by prominent figures from the industrial, international academic or extra-academic sector, internship activities, interviews and visits to companies and institutions in the biotech-pharma and sustainability sector, which will allow the student to deepen additional and multidisciplinary professional knowledge, which can provide a meaningful vision of the real-time evolution of the production world, promoting the development of skills to solve problems in contexts broader than the conventional ones.
These additional and multidisciplinary professional knowledge will include topics such as: Socioeconomics, Bioeconomy, Circular economy, Ethics, Industry 4.0, Green Chemistry, New Drugs, Precision medicine, -omics, Eco-innovation, Globalization, EU Law, Bioprocess technology, Sustainable biochemicals, System & synthetic biology, Cell as a factory, From discovery to product, Biotechnology & health, Sustainable health, Rare diseases, Advanced therapeutics, Digital transformation, Innovative services, EU project design, as well as the rules relating to patent legislation, the procedures for authorizing biotechnological products and the economic dynamics of the biotechnology sector, the characteristics of companies in the sector, investments and sources of funding, with particular regard to international sources of funding.
These seminar and internship activities will be an integral and fundamental part of the training plan and will constitute highly qualifying moments of the training process. The Master's student will be required to deepen in a group (teamworking) one or more topics addressed in the seminar teaching activities through meta-analysis and data mining in order to produce an original and individual elaboration that will be included in the final thesis.

Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
Specialist Social Worker
Master's graduates in Social Sustainability and Welfare Management can take the state exam to qualify for the profession and register in the A register of specialist social workers.
The specialist social worker can work, also with management and leadership functions, in public bodies (ASL, Prefectures, Ministries - Justice, Labor, Health, etc. - Local authorities, etc.). Furthermore, the Master's graduate in Social Sustainability and Welfare Management can work in management positions in Third Sector organizations (social cooperatives, volunteer associations, NGOs, foundations, mutual aid societies, etc.) and private companies (residential facilities for the elderly, minors, asylum seekers, etc.).
This professional can also carry out independent professional activities in the areas of consulting, research and social design.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam consists of the discussion of a thesis chosen by the student in a course among those present in the individual study plan and on a topic agreed with the teacher responsible for that course (thesis supervisor). A co-supervisor is required. The thesis consists of a written paper and is discussed before the Degree Commission. The student may use paper, computer, audiovisual, multimedia or other types of support. The thesis consists of a number of pages appropriate for a monograph, with the following characteristics: a) be the result of thorough research; b) demonstrate the candidate's ability in the organization and articulation of the work; c) be provided with adequate bibliographic references and precise in the references and citation of sources; d) verify the issues related to the object of study with a scientifically correct method, also showing the candidate's critical skills. At the student's discretion, and subject to the favorable opinion of the thesis supervisor, the student may present the thesis in a foreign language.
The final grade is expressed in hundredths with possible honors.
Knowledge required for admission: To access the Master's Degree in Social Sustainability and Welfare Management, it is necessary to have a degree or a university diploma of three years' duration, or other qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable. To access the course of study, it is necessary to have specific curricular requirements and adequate personal preparation.
The curricular requirements are met by having a degree or a Master's degree in one of the following classes of D.M. 270/2004 (or equivalent in the former DM 509/99):
L-12 (Linguistic Mediation)
L-14 (Sciences of Legal Services)
L-16 (Sciences of Administration and Organization)
L-18 (Sciences of Economics and Business Management)
L-19 (Sciences of Education and Training)
L-20 (Sciences of Communication)
L-21 (Sciences of Territorial Planning, Urban Planning, Landscape and Environmental Planning)
L-24 (Psychological and Technical Sciences)
L-36 (Political and International Relations Sciences)
L-37 (Social Sciences for Cooperation, Development and Peace)
L-39 (Social Work)
L-40 (Sociology)
L-41 (Statistics)
LMG/01 (Law)
L/SNT1 (Nursing and Obstetric Health Professions)
L/STN/2 (Health and Rehabilitation Professions)
Admission is also allowed with the possession of a suitable qualification in a degree class other than those specified above, provided that they have at least 75 CFU obtained in at least two of the specific scientific-disciplinary sectors listed below:
- sociological/historical/political (SPS/03, SPS/04, SPS/05, SPS/07, SPS/08, SPS/09, SPS/10, M-STO/04, M-FIL/01, M-FIL/06);
- legal (IUS/01, IUS/02; IUS/04, IUS/05, IUS/06, IUS/07, IUS/08, IUS/09, IUS/10, IUS/12, IUS/13, IUS/14; IUS/19);
- economic (SECS-P/01, SECS-P/02, SECS-P/03, SECS-P/05, SECS-P/06, SECS-P/07, SECS-P/08, SECS-P/09, SECS-P/10, SECS-P/11, SECS-P/12, SECS-S/01, SECS-S/03, SECS-S/04, M-GGR/01, M-GGR/02);
- anthropological/psychological/pedagogical (M-DEA/01; M-PSI/05; M-PSI/06; M-PED/01; M-PED/02)
The adequacy of the student's preparation is verified through an interview, the methods of which will be described in the didactic regulations of the course of study and adequately publicized in good time to guarantee enrollment.
Only students who have the curricular requirements can access the verification of personal preparation.
Knowledge and understanding:
Area: Theoretical paradigms and analysis of social change. At the end of the course of study, the Master's graduate must possess multidisciplinary knowledge aimed at the critical analysis of specific social problems and the reflection on the dynamics in which social work is inserted. The acquisition of this knowledge is pursued through a mix of disciplinary in-depth studies with lectures, seminars, exercises and simulations. This area aims to organize teaching and training activities aimed at providing students with the knowledge and skills to understand the main dynamics of social change in a sociological, historical, anthropological, pedagogical and economic perspective. These are knowledge and skills that are linked to and develop those typically associated with the first cycle and that allow to elaborate and apply original ideas also starting from the relationship between social sustainability and design, programming, organization, management and evaluation of social interventions in the welfare sector.
These training objectives will be pursued through: lectures, case studies, personal and group study, verification activities provided for the completion of theoretical and practical experiences.
Area: Support tools. This area includes advanced knowledge aimed at equipping students with the tools to transfer theories and reference paradigms into professional practices. Based on the teaching activities carried out in this specific area, students are able to demonstrate knowledge and skills in: a. the legal and institutional systems that underlie social change and regulate the possibilities of intervention; b. public communication systems, with particular attention to the meeting between citizens and services.
These training objectives will be pursued through: lectures, case studies, personal and group study, verification activities provided for the completion of theoretical and practical experiences.
Professionalizing area and management of social services. This area includes advanced knowledge aimed at developing and supporting the most typical elements of the specialist social worker profession. Based on the teaching activities carried out in this specific area, students are able to demonstrate knowledge and skills in the dynamics related to the design, programming and organization of innovative welfare interventions; students will develop specific managerial skills in the area of coordination of human resources and management of complex structures.
These training objectives will be pursued through: lectures, case studies, personal and group study, verification activities provided for the completion of theoretical and practical experiences.
