Program, texts and objectives Admission requirements Candidates who hold a degree awarded by an Italian university in one of the following classes:
L-7 Civil and Environmental Engineering
L-8 Information Engineering
L-9 Industrial Engineering
L-30 Physical Sciences and Technologies
L-31 Computer Science and Technologies
L-35 Mathematical Sciences
L-41 Statistics with a grade above a threshold specified in the didactic regulations of the study course, are directly admitted to the Master's Degree course in Computer and Automation Engineering.
The curricula of candidates who do not meet the requirements described above will be examined by a special Committee, in order to verify the level of preparation in the basic sectors of information engineering (mathematics, physics, fundamentals of computer science).
Candidates who do not hold a degree awarded by an Italian university must have an equivalent three-year degree (Bachelor's level) in engineering, mathematics or physics. Students with other qualifications will only be admitted if they have a solid basic preparation in mathematics and physics. In any case, admission will be decided by the Committee based on the candidate's curriculum.
Professional status conferred by the degree.
Intelligent Systems Engineer The Master's graduate in Artificial Intelligence and Automation Engineering naturally finds their place within companies, public bodies and research centers, at national or international level, which deal with the design, development and management of complex intelligent systems. The Study Course also allows to undertake autonomous professional activities as a consultant.
Automation Engineer The Master's graduate in Artificial Intelligence and Automation Engineering naturally finds their place within companies, public bodies and research centers, at national or international level, which deal with the design, development and management of industrial automation and robotic systems, or complex systems that integrate the various components typical of information engineering. The Study Course also allows to undertake autonomous professional activities as a consultant.
Characteristics of the final exam The final exam involves the preparation and discussion of a thesis (Master's thesis), developed by the student in an original way under the guidance of a supervisor, possibly assisted by one or more co-supervisors.
The Master's thesis can be of a compilation or theoretical-experimental nature. In any case, the final exam aims to highlight the skills acquired by the student for the study, understanding, critical evaluation and design of advanced and original solutions in the scientific/technological field. The skills of synthesis, scientific rigor and organization in the drafting of the paper and in the oral presentation of the work, which is planned at the end of the study path, will also be evaluated.
In the case of a theoretical-experimental thesis, the Teaching Committee appoints a co-supervisor, who is responsible for evaluating the candidate's original contribution and the organization of the paper.
The thesis for the Master's Degree can be written in English, Italian or in an official language of the EU.
It is possible to carry out part of the activities related to the final exam within the framework of internships or traineeships at companies, organizations or research laboratories.
Knowledge required for access Access to the study course is reserved for holders of a degree or university diploma of three years' duration, or other qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable. Access requires that candidates meet curricular and personal preparation requirements. It is not possible to enroll with outstanding entrance requirements.
Curricular requirements It is required to have a degree in one of the following classes:
- L-7 Civil and Environmental Engineering (8)
- L-8 Information Engineering (9)
- L-9 Industrial Engineering (10)
- L-30 Physical Sciences and Technologies (25)
- L-31 Computer Science and Technologies (26)
- L-35 Mathematical Sciences (32)
or equivalent class according to the former D.M.509/99 (the number of the equivalent class is shown in parentheses).
Those who hold a specialist degree under D.M.509/99 or a degree under the previous system to D.M.509/99, in engineering, mathematics and physics, also meet the curricular requirements, subject to assessment by the Teaching Committee of an appropriate differentiation of the training objectives of these study paths with respect to the Master's Degree course.
For students with a qualification obtained from a foreign university, the assessment of the possession of curricular requirements is delegated to the Teaching Committee, which verifies in particular:
- that the qualification is awarded at the end of a course of study of at least three years' duration;
- that the training objectives are sufficiently consistent with those of one of the degree classes listed above.
Knowledge of the English language is required at least at level B2 of the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages.
Students whose native language is not Italian, who do not reach at least level A1 of knowledge of the Italian language, must include in their study plans training activities aimed at achieving this level.
Personal preparation requirements Admission to the Study Course, for students who meet the curricular requirements, is subject to verification of personal preparation, according to the procedures defined in the Didactic Regulations of the Study Course. The verification concerns knowledge of the disciplines of mathematics, statistics, physics and information engineering. For students with a specialist degree under D.M.509/99 or a degree under the previous system to D.M.509/99, the verification of personal preparation requirements will be assessed on a case-by-case basis by the Teaching Committee.
For students with a qualification obtained abroad, the Teaching Committee carries out a selection which involves the assessment of personal preparation based on the study curriculum and an admission test in written and/or oral form.
Qualification awarded: Master of Science in Artificial Intelligence and Automation Engineering
Method of teaching: Conventional
Knowledge and understanding: Intelligent Systems In-depth knowledge of the specific skills for the analysis and design of advanced and technologically complex computer systems.
Knowledge of the architectures of high-performance processing systems and their programming techniques.
Knowledge of the algorithms underlying the design of intelligent systems capable of learning.
Knowledge of the design issues of integrated hardware/software systems in distributed environments.
Knowledge of the design tools for high-performance database systems.
Knowledge in application areas of computer systems such as language processing and bioinformatics.
Knowledge of advanced image processing techniques and the basics of computer vision.
Knowledge of the theory and algorithms of optimization on graphs.
The acquisition of knowledge and understanding is verified through written and/or oral profit exams.
Robotics and Automation In-depth knowledge of the specific skills for the analysis and design of robotic and industrial automation systems.
Knowledge of modeling and control techniques for complex systems, such as nonlinear, multivariable and uncertain systems.
Knowledge of the models and theory of discrete event systems.
Knowledge of the technologies for the design of human-machine interfaces in complex robotic systems.
Knowledge of the algorithms and models for data analysis and identification of dynamic systems.
Knowledge of the technology and design methodologies of sensors in control systems.
Knowledge of the theory and algorithms of optimization on graphs.
The acquisition of knowledge and understanding is verified through written and/or oral profit exams.
Contextual knowledge and final exam Knowledge of business realities in the field of information engineering and industrial automation, with particular reference to their specific characteristics and problems.
Knowledge of the English language at an advanced level.
Verification is represented by the internship and the final exam, which allows to evaluate the knowledge and skills acquired by the student, possibly in a business context.

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year.
Duration: 3 years.

Status: Open.

Language: Italian.
Type: Degree Programs.

Programmed Access: Yes.
Location: Siena.
Department: Department of Medical, Surgical and Neurological Sciences.
Website: https://tecniche-audioprotesiche.unisi.it/it
Admission Requirements:
Required qualifications: [TSS]
Upper Secondary School Diploma.
Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse
Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan" www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni
Roles: President/Chairman of the teaching committee: LORENZO SALERNI
Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
Audio-Prosthetist Technician. The Audio-Prosthetist Technician works under supervision or as a freelance consultant in public or private organizations that deal with audiological rehabilitation in both childhood and adulthood.
Characteristics of the final exam: Pursuant to Article 6, paragraph 3, of Legislative Decree No. 502/1992 and subsequent amendments, and Article 6 of the Interministerial Decree of February 19, 2009 (Determination of the degree classes of health professions), the final exam of the Degree Course has the value of a state exam enabling the exercise of the profession.
The final exam consists of a practical test, in which the student must demonstrate that they have acquired the theoretical and practical knowledge and technical-operational skills, and the preparation of a thesis, assigned under the guidance of a supervisor and discussed with the commission and the co-supervisor.
The final exam is worth 5 CFU.
Knowledge required for admission: To be admitted to the Degree Course in Audio-Prosthetic Techniques, applicants must have a secondary school diploma or another qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable.
Access to the degree course is programmed annually on a national basis, as provided for in Article 1 of Law No. 264 of August 2, 1999 (Provisions on access to university courses), also based on the availability of teaching staff, teaching facilities and facilities that can be used for the practical activities.
Admission requires an exam through a national test with multiple-choice questions.
The number of students admitted to the first year of the course is defined pursuant to Article 3 of the same Law 264/1999 with a ministerial decree.
Among the knowledge required for admission, knowledge of the English language is required, at least at level A2/2 (CEFR).
The student's initial preparation will, however, be assessed through the analysis of the errors found in the logic, chemistry, biology, mathematics and physics quizzes (multiple-choice questions) administered in the entrance exam.
If the test is not positive or results below the minimum established, additional training obligations will be assigned to be completed in the first year of the course. In this sense, the teachers of the relevant modules will carry out a brief course to recover the main knowledge gaps that must still be filled in the first year.
Knowledge and understanding.
PROPAEDEUTIC AND CLINICAL AREA
1. Understand the basic notions of Biochemistry, Biology, Genetics, Physiology, Histology and Anatomy that are the basis of the physiological and pathological processes that develop in childhood, adulthood and old age.
2. Understand the basic notions of Physics applied (with an in-depth study of Acoustic Physics), Informatics (applied to the Biomedical field) and Medical Statistics.
3. Understand notions of electronics and technology of materials.
4. Understand the Psychological and Pedagogical aspects of patients with hearing loss and/or language problems in both pediatric and adult age.
5. Study of the English language with particular regard to the scientific aspects of the area of Medicine.
6. Know basic aspects of business economics and labor law.
7. Know the basic aspects of health management.
The assessment of this knowledge and the ability to understand, acquired through lectures and professional clinical training, as well as the seminar and laboratory activities planned over the three years of the course, will be verified and consolidated through the following verification methods: oral exams, written exams, simulated practical tests, practical tests on patients, written reports, case reports. Teaching tools: classroom activities, practical lessons in outpatient clinics and/or in equipped classrooms, clinical training, reading and interpretation of international literature.
APPLICATION AND PROFESSIONALIZATION AREA In the area of activities characterizing the student in audio-prosthetic techniques, the following must be acquired:
- the knowledge relating to the most relevant diseases of the otological, rhinological and laryngeal district
- ability to integrate the audiological data detected with indicators of pathology
- the knowledge relating to the activities of public health (surveillance, prevention and health education)
- knowledge of the legislative and economic principles.
The main problems concerning occupational medicine must also be known and basic principles of forensic medicine must be acquired - the knowledge that constitutes the communication process and all the elements that compose it
- the daily themes of the work of the affiliated audiometrists, which guide him in the acquisition of the audiological techniques of investigation of the auditory and vestibular apparatus
- knowledge in the field of general pediatrics and neonatology. The peculiar aspects of the various pathologies of the developmental age and the problems related to the existence of audiological pathologies will be considered; the neuro-psychiatric and rehabilitative aspects of children will be explored.
- knowledge in the field of interdisciplinary and medical-surgical sciences for the understanding of the physio-pathological elements applicable to the various clinical situations.
- knowledge in the field of prevention and health services sciences
- knowledge in the field of statistical sciences, quantitative methods and scientific updating, for the development of knowledge of statistical tools useful for planning the research process, some analytical methods to be chosen and used in the exercise of the profession, using the best scientific evidence available in the literature, in the field of audiological sciences, as well as the ability to evaluate epidemiological data
- knowledge in the field of hygiene and preventive sciences for the understanding of health determinants, risk factors, prevention strategies both individual and collective and interventions aimed at promoting the safety of healthcare workers and users.
Autonomy of judgment.
The graduate in Audio-Prosthetic Techniques, at the end of the study path, will be able to know and apply, independently, the audioprosthetic rehabilitation intervention using clinical reasoning as a guiding tool in the choice criterion, within the possible options that make up the various phases of the process. This autonomy will be achieved through the acquisition and consolidation, as well as the experimentation, of the skills and abilities to effectively respond to individual and/or collective health needs, as a single operator or in a team, opting for the best therapeutic strategies, identifying the appropriate approaches and tools, carrying out data collection (including analysis of the social context), patient evaluation, appropriate audiological evaluations, defining prognosis and therapeutic objectives, in the short, medium and long term, contributing to the formulation of a rehabilitation project, elaborating the therapeutic program, choosing and applying rehabilitation methodologies and techniques to the clinical case (including intensity, time, the type of technical professional approach) according to the possible and available scientific evidence, according to international guidelines and Evidence Based; in the ethical field, it will manifest in the respect of the reference regulations, professional, legal, health and deontological. The autonomy of judgment of the graduate in Audio-Prosthetic Techniques will be fully realized through the habitual and judicious use of communication, knowledge, technical skills, clinical reasoning (diagnostic and evaluative process), emotions, values, reflection in daily practice for the benefit of the individual or the community, especially through clinical professional training and small group laboratories.
The autonomy of judgment is achieved through the following teaching tools: classroom activities, practical lessons in laboratory and/or in equipped classrooms, clinical training, reading and interpretation of international scientific literature and verified through written reports of in-depth study on technical/professional topics, discussion of clinical cases, definition of the Rehabilitation Project/Program and on real clinical cases. Annual exam of clinical training.
Ability to learn.
The graduate in Audio-Prosthetic Techniques will be able to conduct a self-assessment of his own training level in order to maintain his knowledge at the highest level required for professional practice and design self-training paths to implement his skills, with particular reference to Evidence Based Practice in the audiological and audioprosthetic rehabilitation field.
In the three years, he will acquire learning skills and abilities that will allow, at the end of the course, the continuation of studies, which include a master's degree in the LM/SNT/02 class, scientific and high-level permanent and recurring training courses (1st and 2nd level masters, refresher courses), updating and in-depth courses (Continuing Medical Education), with a high degree of autonomy.
Teaching tools through which learning skills are acquired:
- hospital and company training
- professional seminars
- lectures
- self-study hours
- theoretical and practical exercises
- professionalizing laboratory. The verification of learning takes place through in-progress tests aimed at detecting the effectiveness of the contents of the various teaching modules. The profit exams can take place in several stages, also with practical tests, but will end with an oral test aimed at ascertaining the candidate's degree of preparation on all the subjects included in the Integrated Course.
Ability to apply knowledge and understanding.
PROPAEDEUTIC AND CLINICAL AREA At the end of the training course, the graduates will be able to apply the work process, within the rehabilitation setting, both in a simulated form and on the patient, also adopting the problem-solving methodology (clinical reasoning) and putting into practice the procedures that allow the realization of the care and rehabilitation interventions in hearing disorders in the various ages and in the various physiological and pathological alterations of the auditory system. The application of knowledge will be developed taking into account fundamental aspects such as interaction with the real-life context of people, interpersonal and inter-professional relationship, organizational management of the aspects directly connected to the type of work performed. This ability will be achieved fully and completely through the attendance (mandatory) of professional training and laboratory activities (60 CFU of training distributed over 3 years in a gradual manner; 3 CFU of laboratory training as a training ground for technical and relational learning), where each student has at his disposal clinical tutors specifically appointed. The acquisition of professional skills is systematically verified through a booklet that certifies his presence and a "Core Values" evaluation sheet that measures attitudes, behaviors and general skills, and is compiled by the clinical tutor at the end of each training experience; the set of knowledge and skills is evaluated with a structured test, placed at the end of each academic year (in the 1st year it gives rise to an "idoneity", while at the end of the 2nd and 3rd it gives rise to an exam evaluated in 30 exams). The set of these activities is planned, monitored and verified by the Coordinator of the theoretical/practical and training activities who promotes meetings with the tutors, with the students themselves, proposes training activities, facilitates the student in the development of self-training, access to bibliographic sources, etc.
Teaching tools: classroom activities, practical lessons in audiological and otological outpatient clinics and in the acoustics laboratory, reading and interpretation rooms of international literature. The assessment of this knowledge and the ability to understand will be carried out through the following verification methods:
theoretical/practical exams, written reports on the technical aspects, oral and written presentation of rehabilitation projects, simulated practical tests and/or on patients.
APPLICATION AND PROFESSIONALIZATION AREA In the area of activities characterizing the student in audio-prosthetic techniques, the following must be acquired:
The knowledge acquired with training activities and adequate training activities will allow an excellent adaptation to the work tasks entrusted to him through the use of the acquired specialist knowledge;
The possibility of applying the learned techniques to carry out critical analyses of the information provided and propose appropriate solutions to them;
To use the learned diagnostic techniques to carry out critical analyses of the results and propose appropriate audioprosthetic solutions, evaluating the results over time and possible solutions to failures.
Communication skills.
The graduate in audio-prosthetic techniques will be able to:
- establish professional relationships and collaborate with other healthcare professionals, aware of the specificities of the different professional roles
- demonstrate effective communication skills with users and in relationships with other professionals
- communicate effectively in verbal, non-verbal and written form the evaluations and technical-assistance decisions in interdisciplinary healthcare teams
- collaborate with the care team to implement and develop protocols and guidelines
- communicate effectively with patients, families and other professionals to provide optimal healthcare.
Communication skills are acquired through classroom activities and training and verified through written reports on the communicative aspects of the rehabilitation setting; briefings with tutors and the coordinator; oral and written presentation of projects; training.
Expected professional and employment opportunities for graduates.
Audio-Prosthetist Technician. The Audio-Prosthetist Technician works under supervision or as a freelance consultant in public or private organizations that deal with audiological rehabilitation in both childhood and adulthood.
Language(s) of instruction and assessment.
ITALIAN.
Competencies associated with the function.
Audio-Prosthetist Technician. The activity of the Audio-Prosthetist Technician is aimed at the application of prosthetic aids by taking the impression of the external auditory canal, the construction and application of ear molds or other acoustic coupling systems and the administration of prosthetic evaluation tests.
The Audio-Prosthetist Technician has didactic and scientific skills in Audiological Prosthetics and can be included in multidisciplinary teams for clinical and research activities.
Function in the work context.
Audio-Prosthetist Technician. The Audio-Prosthetist Technician is responsible for the selection, supply, fitting and control of prosthetic aids for the prevention and correction of hearing loss; provides counseling for the use of hearing aids and operates on the doctor's prescription through professional acts that imply full responsibility and the consequent autonomy.
He also collaborates with other professional figures in the programs of prevention and rehabilitation of deafness through the supply of prosthetic aids and training in their use.
Description of specific training objectives.
The Degree Course in Audio-Prosthetic Techniques aims to train graduates with a high level of scientific and technological competence in the management, training and research processes in the field of application, programming and evaluation of Audioprosthetics.
The training course has specific training objectives, defined for its own Professional Profile according to the DM of the Ministry of Health of September 14, 1994, n. 668.
In particular, the graduate in Audio-Prosthetic Techniques must achieve the following training objectives:
- Possession of adequate basic training in the fields of physics, statistics, informatics and chemistry oriented to the application aspects;
- Learning the basics of anatomy, physiology and the pathophysiology of the auditory system;
- Acquisition of adequate theoretical knowledge in the technical, medical (general and specialist) and social disciplines suitable for the exercise of the profession;
- Knowledge of the instrumental methodologies to carry out an otoscopic evaluation;
- Acquisition of the methods of threshold and suprathreshold audiometry, impedance audiometry and behavioral audiometry;
- Learning the basics of the recording of bioelectrical phenomena;
- Knowledge of the methods of rehabilitation of deafness;
- Acquisition of the theoretical, technical, technological and methodological principles in the realization and functioning of hearing aids;
- Knowledge of the principles and methodologies suitable for the selection, coupling, fitting, adaptation, control, technical assistance for prosthetic aids, in the theoretical and application aspects;
- Development of adequate practical experience, preparatory to the transfer of theoretical knowledge to professional applications;
- Ability to work in a multidisciplinary team, to interact with colleagues and other healthcare and non-healthcare professionals, to operate with defined degrees of autonomy and to adequately integrate into the work environment;
- Ability to communicate and relate with the person being assisted, with caregivers, with the social context and other healthcare and non-healthcare professionals, respecting their cultural and behavioral differences;
- Development of cultural and methodological skills and an aptitude for lifelong learning, capable of maintaining a level of technical-professional, decision-making, operational and managerial autonomy;
- Acquisition of skills in training, teaching and research in the field of Audiological Prosthetics;
- Acquisition of adequate knowledge of business and health management.
The degree course has a duration of three years with the acquisition of a total of 180 CFU (of which 60 to be obtained through professionalizing training activities of training), which allow the achievement of a full and autonomous professional competence.
Training course: The first year of the course is aimed at providing the biomedical knowledge as well as the fundamentals of the professional discipline as a requirement to face the first training experience aimed at orienting the student to the reference professional areas and the acquisition of basic skills, through lessons activated on the following scientific-disciplinary sectors:
FIS/07, INF/01, ING-INF/07, ING-IND/22, ING-INF/06, BIO/09, BIO/10, BIO/13, BIO/16, BIO/17, MED/03, MED/04, MED/09, MED/18, MED/31, MED/32, MED/43, MED/50, M-PSI/01, M-PSI/04, IUS/07, SPS/07, SECS-P/07, SECS-P/10, ENGLISH LANGUAGE. The second year of the course is aimed at deepening audiological and audioprosthetic knowledge in adults and children. Several training experiences are planned both in the outpatient clinics of the NHS and in private audioprosthetic centers, in whose contexts the student can put into practice and deepen the knowledge and techniques learned in the lessons activated on the following scientific-disciplinary sectors:
MED/10, MED/25, MED/26, MED/31, MED/32, MED/36, MED/39, MED/50, ING-INF/05, SECS-S/02, ENGLISH LANGUAGE. The third year of the course is aimed at expanding specialist and interdisciplinary knowledge as well as acquiring methodologies related to the exercise of the profession and the ability to work in teams and in complex organizational contexts. Furthermore, the professionalizing training experiences that the student will carry out with supervision and his own gradual assumption of autonomy and responsibility will be of considerable importance. The training activities of the third year are activated on the following scientific-disciplinary sectors:
MED/8, MED/17, MED/31, MED/32, MED/50, ING-INF/04, ING-INF/06.
The set of knowledge that will be acquired by the graduate, equal to 180 CFU, will constitute the cultural, scientific and relational baggage necessary to acquire full professional competence, and to understand, face and manage the pathological events that require rehabilitation and/or therapeutic intervention in all age groups. These knowledge will be acquired through participation in lectures, practical lessons in equipped classrooms with rehabilitation aids, followed by teachers and tutors belonging to the specific professional profile, as well as personal study (library, databases) of the subjects offered by the Degree Course.

UNIVERSITY: University of Siena
COURSE: [D339] ECONOMICS AND MANAGEMENT - English
SECTION: INFO ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unisi.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/11098

INFO ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 3 years. Program: 2021. Status: Open. Credits: 180 CFU. Language: English. Type: Degree Courses. Regulatory Framework: D.M. 270/2004. Degree Class: L-18 - Class of degrees in Economics and Business Management. Interclass: L-33 - Class of degrees in Economics. Inter-University Course: No. Open Access: Yes. Location: Siena. Department: Department of Economics and Statistics. Website: https://economics-management.unisi.it/en. Informative Website: https://orientarsi.unisi.it/studio/triennali/corso/economics-and-management. Admission Requirements: Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]. Upper Secondary School Diploma: [CER_LIN]. Language Certification. Fees and Contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse. Dates and Deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni. Roles and Responsibilities: Student Mobility: PASQUALE RUGGIERO, President/Chairman of the teaching committee. MICHELANGELO VASTA, President. MICHELANGELO VASTA. Program, Texts and Objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree. Accounting Expert and Business Analyst. Graduates of the course, with reference to the business context, will be able to perform the managerial and operational functions already mentioned in the context of: private companies of any size and operating in any economic sector; business consulting firms; non-profit organizations; public companies; business, category and trade union associations. Graduates will also be able to carry out professional consulting activities of an administrative and management control nature in all of the above contexts. Graduates will also be able to practice as independent professionals (after passing the necessary state exams to register with professional bodies, registers, etc.).
Banker and Financial Advisor. Graduates of the course, with reference to the banking and financial sector, will be able to perform the operational functions already mentioned in banks, insurance companies and financial intermediation companies, or front and back office tasks. They will be able to work as financial promoters and/or independent financial advisors after passing the necessary exams to register with professional registers.
Economic Analyst. Graduates of the course, with reference to the economic and social context, will be able to use their skills in performing the functions already mentioned in the context of: private companies operating in any economic sector; research and study offices of national and international organizations and bodies; economic consulting firms; non-profit and third sector organizations; public companies; business, category and trade union associations. Graduates will also be able to carry out operational activities in professional consulting contexts, with particular regard to aspects related to economic activities.
Characteristics of the Final Exam: The degree is obtained by passing a final exam, which consists of the evaluation by the degree commission of the final paper prepared by the student under the supervision of a lecturer.
The final paper consists of a short paper, accompanied by the necessary bibliographic references, on a topic of a theoretical or empirical nature.
To pass the final exam, the student must demonstrate that they have acquired, in addition to adequate knowledge and understanding of the chosen topic, autonomous learning and research skills; the ability to correctly use the literature and data sources consulted; the ability to present and support scientific arguments in a logical and coherent manner.
The final exam is worth 3 CFU.
Knowledge Required for Admission: To be admitted to the Degree Course, applicants must hold a secondary school diploma or another qualification obtained abroad, recognized as equivalent. The knowledge required for admission to the Degree Course is that acquired at the upper secondary school level, with particular reference to the mathematical field, as well as a good general basic culture. The specific content of the mathematical knowledge required for admission will be indicated in the course's didactic regulations. Knowledge of the English language at least at level B2 (CEFR) is also required.
The assessment of the student's initial preparation will be carried out according to the criteria and methods defined in detail in the didactic regulations of the course. It will be based on a test, developed and carried out on a national basis, designed to assess the aptitude and minimum preparation required to undertake economic studies, through tests of verbal comprehension, basic knowledge in the mathematical field and simple elementary logic problems. The assessment of knowledge of the English language will be carried out through tests carried out in collaboration with the Language Center of the University of Siena. The didactic regulations of the course establish the additional training obligations foreseen in the event of a negative assessment of the entrance test and the English language test; these obligations must be recovered during the first year of the course, also in the form of specific remedial courses in mathematics and English organized by the relevant Departments.
Knowledge and Understanding: General Area. With reference to the business context, the graduate in Economics and Management possesses the basic knowledge to critically address problems of planning and management control, and to prepare and analyze documents for measuring and reporting business performance. Furthermore, they know and understand the role and functioning of capital markets in the allocation of financial resources.
With reference to the banking and financial sector, the graduate possesses adequate knowledge of the various financial intermediaries, with particular regard to the risks assumed in the intermediation processes. They know and understand the functioning of markets and financial instruments, the processes of construction and analysis of a portfolio. They know and understand the role of technological innovation and the importance of its applications to the financial field (Fintech).
With reference to the economic field, the graduate possesses a good knowledge of the main economic models, both microeconomic and macroeconomic, and the determinants, also from a historical point of view, of the main economic variables (GDP, inflation rate, exchange rate). They know and understand the economic and social context of reference for the behavior of consumers and companies, the market conditions that influence their behavior both from an individual and social point of view, and the social impact of economic policy measures aimed at counteracting market failures.
With reference to the legal field, the graduate knows and understands legal terminology, even in an international context; they master the main institutions of private law and public law and the principles relating to the legal regulation of individual and collective enterprises, also with reference to EU law.
With reference to the quantitative field, the graduate in Economics and Management knows the mathematical tools useful for making economic decisions; they master the basic statistical tools, understanding their use both in descriptive and inferential contexts. They know the mathematical tools for micro and macroeconomic choices, for the analysis of interest rates, annuities and amortizations. They know the main tools for data analysis.
The graduate in Economics and Management has excellent knowledge of the English language and has a high ability to understand written and spoken English. This knowledge and ability to understand must be acquired and verified mainly through the curricular courses and the respective profit exams in the various areas.
Business Area. The graduate in Economics and Management possesses a good mastery of the theory and techniques of business management, the theory and techniques of preparing a company balance sheet, both in the case of a private company and a public company. They have a good knowledge of national accounting principles (OIC) and international principles (IFRS); they know analytical accounting and are able to understand its results in terms of costs, revenues and operating margins relating to specific business segments.
The graduate will acquire basic knowledge of Financial Intermediaries, Financial Markets, Corporate Finance and the tools to analyze and understand investment choices.
The graduate knows and understands the role and functioning of capital markets in the allocation of financial resources. In this context, they know and understand the structure and functioning of financial markets, financial intermediaries and the essential characteristics of financial instruments and products. They know and understand the main differences between credit intermediaries, insurance intermediaries, and securities intermediaries, with particular regard to the types of risks assumed. They know and understand the functions and characteristics of debt, equity and derivative instruments in a portfolio context.
The graduate in Economics and Management knows and understands the specificities of banks and insurance companies and the new role they have assumed in the financial system and in the allocation and management of savings as a result of the implementation of technological innovations that are changing traditional business models. They know the banking operations and have a good knowledge of the managerial balances of the bank, with particular reference to the management of risks and the asset and economic balances, as well as the determinants of bank performance. They know the evolutionary processes present in the banking and financial world related to Fintech.
Economic Area. The graduate in Economics and Management possesses a good knowledge of the historical evolution of the economy and is able to understand how economic variables have influenced the decisions of economic operators over time.
The graduate possesses a good knowledge of the main economic models, both microeconomic and macroeconomic, and the determinants of the main economic variables (GDP, inflation rate, exchange rate) and is able to understand and interpret the economic and social context of reference, to carry out an analysis of the market conditions in which the company operates and to develop a consequent business strategy, also with regard to international markets.
Depending on the curriculum and the courses chosen, the graduate possesses:
a) a rigorous basic knowledge of the main quantitative methods and for data analysis, which allows them to have the tools to set up, process and evaluate a basic empirical project, also through the learning of the functionalities and use of econometric packages for the social sciences;
b) knowledge of the issues related to international markets, globalization and policies to control the effects on inequality, based on the most recent theory and modeling;
c) knowledge of the issues concerning the social and economic aspects highlighted by behavioral theories and the application of analytical tools specific to experimental economics;
d) knowledge of the theoretical and empirical foundations of markets as imperfect markets (monopoly, oligopoly, monopolistic competition) which includes the analysis of the level of competition/collusion with reference to variables such as market power, price discrimination, vertical relations, product differentiation, advertising, research and development.
Legal Area. The graduate in Economics and Management knows and understands legal terminology; they know the main institutions of private law (rights of persons, rights on things, obligations and their sources) and public law (the Italian constitution and the political organization of the Italian State with reference to the European order).
The graduate knows the principles relating to the legal regulation of individual and collective enterprises, with particular attention to the practical and applied aspects of the institutions studied and, depending on the curriculum chosen, a complete knowledge of the harmonized European legislation relating to the law of enterprises and companies.
Also depending on the optional courses chosen in the third year, the graduate knows and understands the basics of special domestic and Community legislation relating to banking, financial and insurance companies and, in particular, has a full knowledge at the institutional level of the mechanisms underlying the formation of the banking system, the specific characteristics of banks (and the products offered) and the supervisory methods (national and Community) applicable to them. Finally, the graduate, through the analysis of the main institutions that characterize the administration from the point of view of its organization, its activity and the relations with the other subjects of the legal system, the tools necessary to understand the framework of principles and rules to which public and private subjects must adhere (under certain profiles).
Mathematical-Statistical Area. The graduate in Economics and Management has a good knowledge and understanding of the basic tools of mathematical analysis and linear algebra necessary for statistical, economic, financial and social analysis. Furthermore, they know the tools of probability calculus and the main methods of descriptive and inferential statistics; in particular, the graduate has knowledge aimed at the detection, analysis, synthesis and interpretation of economic, business and financial data.
The graduate knows and understands the mathematical techniques for the analysis of interest rates, annuities and amortizations. They have a good mastery of the fundamental elements for the quantitative study of the profiles related to financial instruments.
Finally, depending on the curriculum and the courses chosen, they have a rigorous basic training regarding econometric theory, with particular reference to the multiple regression model, and a good knowledge of statistical methods for the analysis of multivariate data.

Program, texts and objectives Professional status conferred by the degree.
Responsible in international, national and regional agencies for the environment and biodiversity.
International, national and regional agencies for the environment and biodiversity.
Researcher at universities and other public and private research institutions.
State and private universities, public and private research institutions.
Graduates who have obtained a sufficient number of credits in appropriate subject areas may, as provided by current legislation, participate in the entrance exams for training courses for secondary school teachers.
Responsible for research lines and laboratories in public and private bodies.
Public and private bodies (Ministries, Regions, Provinces, Municipalities).
Responsible in local authorities, cooperatives and consortia for the management of museums, parks, protected areas and nature reserves.

Public and private biological and environmental analysis laboratories.
Freelance biologist.
Freelance profession
Characteristics of the final exam The activities related to the preparation of the final exam for obtaining the Master's Degree in Biodiversity, Conservation and Environmental Quality are carried out by the student under the supervision of one or more tutor professors in the form of an internship at a university laboratory or an external public or private body affiliated with the University.
The aim of the internship is for the student to acquire in-depth knowledge of experimental methodology, analytical tools and data analysis and processing techniques.
The final exam consists of the presentation of a thesis developed in an original way by the student under the guidance of the supervisor. The final grade is expressed in hundredths with possible honors.
Knowledge required for access To access the Master's Degree Course in Biodiversity, Conservation and Environmental Quality, it is necessary to have a degree or a three-year university diploma, or another qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable.
To be admitted to the Master's Degree Course, it is necessary to have certain curricular requirements and adequate personal preparation.
The curricular admission requirements are met by having a degree in one of the following classes of D.M. 270/2004 (or equivalent in the former DM 509/99):
L-13 (Biological Sciences)
L-32 (Science and Technology for the Environment and Nature)
L-2 (Biotechnology).
Admission is also permitted with the possession of a suitable qualification in a degree class other than those specified above, provided that the following have been obtained:
a) at least 66 CFU in basic biological disciplines (BIO/01, BIO/02, BIO/04, BIO/05, BIO/06, BIO/07, BIO/09, BIO/10, BIO/11, BIO/18, BIO/19), 12 CFU in basic chemical disciplines (CHIM/01, CHIM/02, CHIM/03, CHIM/06), and 15 CFU in basic mathematical, physical and computer disciplines (MAT/01-09, FIS/01-08 and INF/01), in accordance with the guidelines of the College of Biologists of Italian Universities (CBUI);
b) a degree grade of not less than 100/110
For graduates from foreign universities, the adequacy of the curricular requirements is assessed on a case-by-case basis based on the consistency between the programs carried out in the various disciplinary areas, the basic training considered necessary for the advanced training offered by the course of study, as well as language skills.
It is also required to have a certification of knowledge of the English language at the B2 level of the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages of the Council of Europe.
For foreign students, verification of adequate knowledge of the Italian language is required. Students who do not have these language skills must attend specific courses to reach the required level, as part of the additional training activities.
The verification of personal preparation is mandatory and will be carried out through a test or interview, according to the methods provided for in the didactic regulations of the course of study. Those who have obtained a degree with the curricular requirements and have obtained a degree grade of not less than 95/110 will be exempt from this test, as they are recognized as having adequate preparation.
Knowledge and understanding.
Generic Area Graduates who have successfully completed the course of study will have acquired knowledge relating to the study of biodiversity, from the molecular to the ecosystem level, and will have understood the mechanisms that regulate processes, interactions and the effects of various environmental parameters on living organisms. Graduates will have the knowledge and methodological basis for the study of biological and cultural diversity to carry out monitoring and ecosystem management activities. This knowledge and ability to understand will be acquired and verified through participation in lectures, field and/or laboratory exercises and other curricular activities, through the carrying out of internships in qualified research laboratories and with the final exam which involves the discussion of an original paper. Learning activities can also be carried out abroad through agreements with various universities within the Erasmus program and other international mobility programs.
Biodiversity and Environment Area Graduates who have successfully completed the course of study will have acquired knowledge of:
- biodiversity of microorganisms,
- biodiversity of fungi and their economic and ecological importance,
- biodiversity of plants,
- biodiversity of terrestrial animals,
- role, importance and conservation of biodiversity in ecological systems.
Graduates may also acquire knowledge of:
- plant-animal interactions,
- legal basis for nature protection,
- plant biotechnology,
- techniques for monitoring biodiversity.
The main teaching tool is the lecture, possibly supplemented by experimental demonstrations and laboratory and/or field activities. The assessment of knowledge takes place through oral and/or written examination.
Biomolecular Area Graduates who have successfully completed the course of study will have acquired knowledge of:
- biological diversity at the morphological, genomic and molecular level,
- genomics and the role of genetic variability in evolution with particular reference to eukaryotic organisms,
- biodiversity as a source of pharmacologically active substances,
- knowledge of the effects of biodiversity derivatives on human health,
- specific skills in the field of traceability and authenticity of food.
The main teaching tool is the lecture, possibly supplemented by experimental demonstrations and laboratory activities. The assessment of knowledge takes place through oral and/or written examination.
Contextual knowledge and final exam Knowledge and understanding of scientific instrumentation, organization and management of research laboratories in the ecological-environmental field, also through biomolecular-cellular methodologies.
Knowledge and understanding of practical tools for the acquisition and processing of data and information, with computer tools.
Graduates must be able to use fluently, in written and oral form, the English language with particular reference to disciplinary lexicons.
The assessment of knowledge takes place through oral and/or written examination.
The preparation and discussion of the degree thesis represent a further tool for verifying the knowledge acquired during the course of study.

UNIVERSITY: University of Siena
COURSE: [D341] AGRIBUSINESS Inter-University
SECTION: INFO ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unisi.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/11095

INFO ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 3 years. Program: 2023. Status: Open. Credits: 180 CFU. Language: Italian. Type: Degree Courses. Regulatory Framework: D.M. 270/2004. Degree Class: L-P02 - Technical professions in agriculture, food and forestry. Inter-University Course. Affiliated Universities: University of PISA. Admission: Programmed Access. Location: Siena. Department: Department of Life Sciences. Website: https://agribusiness.unisi.it/it. Admission Requirements: Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]. Upper Secondary School Diploma. Tuition and Fees: For information on tuition and fees, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse. Dates and Deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni. Head of Program/Head of Teaching Committee: MASSIMO NEPI. Program, Texts and Objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree. Qualified agricultural technician. The Agribusiness degree (L-P02) is a professionally oriented degree with immediate employment opportunities, therefore enrollment in a master's degree program does not constitute a natural path for graduates. The degree program aims to train qualified agricultural technicians. This figure will find employment opportunities in the diversified agricultural production sector, where they can be employed in the following areas:
- freelance work;
- consulting in the agricultural sector for public and private bodies;
- public administration with non-managerial positions;
- agricultural companies;
- cooperatives and consortia;
- trade associations;
- large-scale retail;
- analysis laboratories for agriculture and the environment;
- national and international institutions;
- service companies for the primary sector.
The professionally oriented degree in Agribusiness can qualify graduates for the professions of licensed agricultural surveyor, licensed agricultural technician, and licensed industrial technician (food technology section) after a practical evaluation test to assess the knowledge, skills, abilities, and operational autonomy necessary for the exercise of these professions, to be carried out prior to the final exam.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam for obtaining the professional degree in Agribusiness qualifies graduates to practice as licensed agricultural technicians and/or licensed agricultural surveyors and/or licensed industrial technicians registered in the "food technology section".
The final exam includes the completion of a practical evaluation test (PPV) which precedes the final exam. The PPV aims to verify the knowledge, skills, abilities, and operational autonomy necessary for the exercise of the profession(s) and focuses on topics consistent with professional activities and the specificities of the course of study.
The final exam represents an important opportunity for individual training and consists of the discussion of a written paper that explores practical or applied/project-based topics addressed during the practical evaluation internship activities. This paper is prepared independently by the student under the guidance of one or more supervising professors, possibly assisted by external professional or company figures.
The purpose of the final exam is to ascertain that the graduate has acquired knowledge, comprehension skills, and independent judgment in the field of Agribusiness.
The final exam can also be taken in English.
The grade for the final exam is expressed in hundredths, with the possible award of honors.
Knowledge required for admission: To be admitted to the degree program, applicants must hold a secondary school diploma or another equivalent qualification obtained abroad and must possess basic knowledge in the fields of mathematics, chemistry, and biology. The knowledge required for admission and the methods for its mandatory verification are defined in the Didactic Regulations of the course of study, which also indicate any additional training requirements in the event that the verification of the required knowledge is not positive. These additional training requirements must be met within the first year of the course.
The course has a limited number of places, and the number of students admitted will be based on the availability of internships, the capacity of the laboratories, and the needs of the labor market.
Knowledge of the English language at a level of competence of at least A2/2, as defined by the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages, is required for admission to the degree program.
Knowledge and understanding.
General area: The expected learning outcomes in the area of basic disciplines are the adequate knowledge and ability to understand the disciplines in the fields of statistics, chemistry, biology, and ecology, with a focus on their applications in the agricultural sector.
These outcomes can be achieved through attendance of lectures during the course of study and participation in in-depth seminars, specific laboratory activities, and practical evaluation internship activities. The results of learning are verified through ongoing assessments, tests, and written and/or oral exams.
The final evaluation of the student is based on both the criterion of evaluating the knowledge of the content of the program and the criterion of evaluating the ability to critically re-elaborate the content for its possible application in the various problems that may arise during professional experience.
Techno-agricultural area: The expected learning outcomes in the area of crop and animal production and agricultural mechanics are the adequate knowledge and ability to understand the sectors of agricultural product production, quality, and safety, as well as agricultural machinery and equipment.
These outcomes can be achieved through attendance of lectures during the course of study and participation in in-depth seminars, specific laboratory activities, and practical evaluation internship activities. The results of learning are verified through ongoing assessments, tests, and written and/or oral exams.
The final evaluation of the student is based on both the criterion of evaluating the knowledge of the content of the program and the criterion of evaluating the ability to critically re-elaborate the content for its possible application in the various problems that may arise during professional experience.
Economic and legal area: The expected learning outcomes in the area of economic and legal disciplines are the adequate knowledge and ability to understand the sectors of business management, agricultural and food economics and marketing, and legislation in the agricultural sector.
These outcomes can be achieved through attendance of lectures during the course of study and participation in in-depth seminars, laboratory activities, and practical evaluation internship activities. The results of learning are verified through ongoing assessments, tests, and written and/or oral exams.
The final evaluation of the student is based on both the criterion of evaluating the knowledge of the content of the program and the criterion of evaluating the ability to critically re-elaborate the content for its possible application in the various problems that may arise during professional experience.

Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree: Chemist (senior)
The main areas of employment are:
- Freelance profession, after registration with a professional register,
- Private analysis laboratories: facilities specializing in environmental analysis, where chemical analyses are carried out,
- Public research laboratories and centers: university laboratories and centers that carry out, promote, disseminate research and transfer it to private companies,
- Private companies: industries in various industrial sectors (chemical, pharmaceutical, cosmetic, dyes, new materials, food products, etc.) in the areas of research and development, production, quality control,
- Institutes, Research Centers, public or private bodies, public administration at central or local level, which deal with territorial planning, issuing production authorizations, ...
The Master's Degree Course in Chemistry allows students to take the state exam for admission to the practice of the profession of Chemist, after registration with the Order of Chemists (Section A).
Master's degree graduates in Chemistry can also access the Doctoral Program, in Italy or at other international institutions.
Characteristics of the final exam: The Master's Thesis in Chemistry must contain the experimental and/or theoretical work developed by the student during a period of internship at a research laboratory of the department, or at companies or other public and private research institutions (in Italy or abroad).
The student must demonstrate that they have acquired autonomy and skills in research work, in the design and implementation of experimental measurements, in their interpretation, as well as in the ability to apply mathematical, statistical and computer algorithms for the rationalization of data and the development of interpretation models.
The final degree examination will consist of a dissertation by the student, of the experimental thesis developed and presented, and will be in English.
The grade will be expressed out of 110, with possible honors.
Knowledge required for access: To access the Master's Degree Course in Chemistry (LM-54), it is necessary to have a degree or a three-year university diploma or another equivalent qualification obtained abroad.
To be admitted to the master's degree course, it is necessary to have certain curricular requirements (as indicated below) and adequate personal preparation in the subjects fundamental to the training of a chemist, such as mathematics, physics, chemistry and computer science.
The required curricular requirements are met by having a degree in one of the following classes:
Science and technology - L-27 (D.M. 270/2004) or 21 (D.M. 509/1999)
Graduates from different degree classes may also be admitted, provided that they have acquired at least 90 CFU in the Scientific-Disciplinary Sectors (SSD) from CHIM/01 to CHIM/12 in their educational path.
Furthermore, knowledge of the English language is required at a level of competence of at least B2/2, as defined by the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages.
Verification of personal preparation is mandatory and only students who meet the curricular requirements can access this verification.
For international students, admission will be decided by a representative appointed by the Unified Teaching Committee through the analysis of the curriculum, possibly supplemented by an interview, also in telematic mode and/or an online entrance test.
For foreign students, verification of adequate knowledge of the Italian language is required. Students who do not have these language skills may attend specific courses to reach the required level, as part of the additional training activities (Art. 10, paragraph 5, letter d).
Knowledge and understanding:
Molecular and biomolecular area: Master's degree graduates in Chemistry will acquire in-depth knowledge of the investigation techniques relating to organic, inorganic and organometallic compounds useful in the pharmaceutical, nutraceutical, environmental, bio-materials and industrial biotechnology fields.
Particular attention will be paid to knowledge in the field of bio-organic and bio-inorganic chemistry in order to understand the mechanism of action of biologically active molecules and biomaterials through the use of synthesis, analytical, spectroscopic and microscopy techniques. Furthermore, theoretical-computational studies will be given great emphasis for the investigation of complex reaction mechanisms of biological interest.
This knowledge will be acquired through the fundamental teaching tools, such as lectures and laboratory exercises (to be carried out through individual and group activities and under the guidance of a teacher and direct tutoring) and the preparation of a final experimental thesis, under the guidance of a teacher/supervisor.
The achievement of these objectives will be verified through written and/or oral exams at the end of the training activity, and the discussion of the final thesis.
Area of environmental monitoring and sustainable development: Master's degree graduates in Chemistry will acquire in-depth knowledge of the issues related to the analytical characterization of quality, especially in the environmental, nutraceutical and food sectors. Particular attention will be paid to the latest methodologies (NMR, mass spectrometry, EPR, etc.) which are the basis for the modification of the standards required at European level for the certification of product quality.
Furthermore, the master's student will deepen the study of environmental issues related to the correct thermodynamic use of energy, the minimization of pollution and the analysis of production cycles to be developed in a sustainable way in the environmental context, in accordance with the need to move towards a society that knows how to combine the protection of the environment, the social and economic development of local communities.
Much attention will be paid to knowledge in the field of environmental remediation and the control of the quality of surface water bodies.
This knowledge will be acquired through the fundamental teaching tools, such as lectures and laboratory exercises (to be carried out through individual and group activities and under the guidance of a teacher and direct tutoring) and the preparation of a final experimental thesis, under the guidance of a teacher/supervisor.
The achievement of these objectives will be verified through written and/or oral exams at the end of the training activity, and the discussion of the final thesis.

Roles: President/Head of the teaching committee: SILVIA CALAMAI
Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
Public Relations Manager:
- multilingual and multicultural communication officer in national and international organizations;
- consultant or expert supporting the management of internationally-oriented companies;
- translator of specialist and scientific texts for national, European and non-European companies and organizations.
Graduates who have earned a sufficient number of credits in appropriate subject areas will be able to participate in programs aimed at accessing teaching positions in secondary schools, in accordance with current legislation. The LM-38 Master's degree is a prerequisite for the A 24 (Foreign Languages and Cultures in Secondary Education) and A 25 (English and Second Community Language in Lower Secondary School) degree classes, according to Presidential Decree 14.2.2016 no. 19. The knowledge provided by this Master's degree course can also serve as a foundation for careers in research.
Consultant in charge of linguistic and cultural cooperation and mediation:
- expert in organizations involved in development cooperation;
- expert in companies, institutions, educational structures, organizations and public and private bodies operating in intercultural and multilingual contexts;
- linguistic consultant in linguistic and cultural mediation at diplomatic representations, European Union institutions (European Parliament, European Commission) and other national and international bodies, such as the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, embassies and consulates;
- expert in organizations involved in the reception, integration and training of foreign subjects in Italian language and culture;
- linguistic consultant in cultural mediation in prisons;
- expert in associations, foundations and cultural cooperatives;
- linguistic consultant in courts and prisons.
Graduates who have earned a sufficient number of credits in appropriate subject areas will be able to participate in programs aimed at accessing teaching positions in secondary schools, in accordance with current legislation. The LM-38 Master's degree is a prerequisite for the A 24 (Foreign Languages and Cultures in Secondary Education) and A 25 (English and Second Community Language in Lower Secondary School) degree classes, according to Presidential Decree 14.2.2016 no. 19. The knowledge provided by this Master's degree course can also serve as a foundation for careers in research.
Consultant in the field of business internationalization: Specialist in the internationalization of small and medium-sized enterprises, in foreign branches of Italian companies and/or in foreign branches in Italy, in chambers of commerce, in press offices that require knowledge of foreign languages and the ability to interact with different languages and cultures.
Graduates who have earned a sufficient number of credits in appropriate subject areas will be able to participate in programs aimed at accessing teaching positions in secondary schools, in accordance with current legislation. The LM-38 Master's degree is a prerequisite for the A 24 (Foreign Languages and Cultures in Secondary Education) and A 25 (English and Second Community Language in Lower Secondary School) degree classes, according to Presidential Decree 14.2.2016 no. 19. The knowledge provided by this Master's degree course can also serve as a foundation for careers in research.
Characteristics of the final exam: To be admitted to the final exam, the student must have passed all the exams and completed the training activities provided for in the Study Plan.
The final exam consists of a written paper of at least 50 pages (or an equivalent multimedia paper), prepared under the guidance of a supervisor in one of the two foreign languages studied, or in Italian, based on linguistic and/or cultural issues, also in a comparative perspective, relating to one of the subjects studied, with the advice of the supervisor. It will be possible to collect and analyze empirical data, and also to carry out an original and critical review and analysis of the state of the art of the reference literature with respect to a specific area. Alternatively, the graduate may choose to develop an autonomous project in the field of one of the more practical disciplines included in his/her study plan, as well as in the internship experience.
The student is required to have the ability to master the two foreign languages at an advanced level, as well as advanced disciplinary knowledge, to have current and up-to-date bibliographic, computer and critical tools, to demonstrate autonomy of judgment, originality of thought and the ability to mediate between different linguistic and cultural contexts.
The exam is discussed before a specially appointed Commission, which will evaluate the exam out of 110, with the possible award of honors.
Knowledge required for admission: To access the Master's Degree in "Languages for Business and Development", it is necessary to have a Bachelor's degree or a three-year university diploma, or another qualification obtained abroad and recognized as equivalent, together with the required curricular requirements.
The curricular admission requirements are met by holding a degree in one of the following classes under the former DM 270/04 (or corresponding under DM 509/99): L-11 and L-12 (former DM 270/04), classes 3 and 11 (former DM 509/99), four-year degrees in Foreign Languages and Literatures.
Graduates in other classes (of the new system under DM 270/04, of the previous system under DM 509/99, of the old four-year degrees and of foreign universities) are also admitted, provided that they have obtained the degree with a minimum score, as indicated in the didactic regulations of the study course, and that they have acquired at least 18 CFU in the first language and 18 CFU in the second language, among the following offered in the study course:
L-LIN/04 LANGUAGE AND TRANSLATION - FRENCH LANGUAGE L-LIN/12 LANGUAGE AND TRANSLATION - ENGLISH LANGUAGE L-LIN/21 SLAVISTICS (RUSSIAN LANGUAGE AND TRANSLATION)
L-OR/21 LANGUAGES AND LITERATURES OF CHINA AND SOUTHEAST ASIA For each of the two languages of study chosen, a minimum B1 level (Common European Framework of Reference for Languages) is required for Russian and Chinese; at least B2 level for English and French.
Students who do not have a certification of their language level must take the relevant level test at the University Language Center, Arezzo campus.
It is also necessary to have acquired:
- at least 21 CFU in literary, cultural, historical studies, with particular reference to the SSD L-LIN/03, L-LIN/10, L-LIN/11, L-LIN/21, L-FIL-LET/10, L-FIL-LET/11, L-FIL-LET/14, L-OR/09, L-OR/21, M-STO/01, M-STO/02, M-STO/03, M-STO/04, M-STO/05, M-GGR/01, M-GGR/02;
- at least 18 CFU in linguistics, philology and language teaching, with particular reference to the SSD L-LIN/01, L-LIN/02, L-LIN/12, L-FIL-LET/09, L-FIL-LET/12, L-FIL-LET/15 (of which at least 6 in L-LIN/01 and 6 in L-FIL-LET/12).
Candidates who do not have these requirements may acquire the missing CFUs by enrolling in individual courses at the University of Siena or at other legally recognized universities and passing the relevant exams. The missing CFUs for meeting the curricular requirements must, however, be obtained before the verification of adequate preparation. The Teaching Committee reserves the right to verify each individual case.
For graduates from foreign universities, the adequacy of the curricular requirements is assessed on a case-by-case basis on the basis of the consistency between the programs carried out in the different disciplinary areas, the basic training considered necessary for the advanced training offered by the study course, and the linguistic knowledge. Foreign students are required to have adequate knowledge of Italian and English (at least at level B1).
The verification of personal preparation is mandatory and will be carried out through a test or interview. Graduates in classes L-11 and L-12 who have obtained the degree with a final score above a minimum threshold will be recognized as having adequate preparation.
The detailed information will be indicated in the didactic regulations of the study course.
Knowledge and understanding.
LINGUISTIC AND COMMUNICATION AREA The Graduate in LIMS:
- will have acquired advanced skills in written and oral interaction in at least two foreign languages in addition to Italian (at least level C1 for English and French; at least level C1 for Russian and Chinese);
- will have in-depth knowledge of the theoretical assumptions and mechanisms of interlinguistic and intercultural interaction;
- will possess excellent mastery of theoretical and practical tools for the decoding, production and translation of specialist texts of various kinds in the field of intercultural, business or international administrative communication.
This knowledge is acquired through: i) advanced-level lectures with the aid of up-to-date and innovative bibliographic and teaching materials; ii) seminar courses; iii) exercises on the translation of documents of particular complexity from the point of view of specialist and sector-specific language and intercultural mediation; iv) participation in conferences, seminars, study days of high scientific value held by national and international experts; v) involvement in regional, national and international research projects coordinated by teachers of the degree course; vi) preparation of original and methodologically rigorous oral and written reports. In the case of foreign language lessons, space is also given to exercises in the classroom with CEL staff.
The general structure of the study course also involves a considerable amount of time dedicated to guided and autonomous individual study, which allows the development of the ability to interpret the different socio-linguistic and cultural codes in communicative relationships in contexts of life and work, with particular attention to multilingual and multicultural contexts.
ECONOMIC AND LEGAL AREA The Graduate:
- will have solid basic skills in the field of business economics and international law;
- will know the contexts and mechanisms of international trade and the internationalization process of companies;
- will be able to understand the theoretical foundations of argumentation;
- will know the fundamental legal institutions for professional and commercial activities;
- will understand the structures and dynamics of the global economy and a constantly evolving market.
This knowledge is acquired through lectures, seminar courses, exercises, seminars, discussions in the classroom, case studies, presentation and discussion of quantitative and qualitative analyses, in-depth studies of specialist languages, meetings with experts on specific topics.
The general structure of the study course also involves a considerable amount of time dedicated to guided and independent individual study, which allows the development of the ability to interpret the different socio-linguistic and cultural codes in economic and legal relations between different countries, with particular attention to multilingual and multicultural contexts.
APPLIED AREA Based on the options indicated in the elective courses and the chosen work-oriented workshops, the graduate:
- will have solid basic skills in relation to the themes of social, economic and environmental sustainability;
- will have solid basic skills in European project design;
- will have transversal skills (soft skills);
- will have skills in statistics and data analysis;
- will have solid skills to orient themselves among the different theoretical and methodological approaches to specialist translation.
This knowledge is acquired through workshops, lectures, seminar courses, exercises, seminars, discussions in the classroom, case studies, specific training in quantitative and qualitative analysis, in-depth studies of specialist and sector-specific languages, meetings with experts on topics consistent with the degree course.
