-----

UNIVERSITY: University of Siena
COURSE: [D450] ELECTRONICS AND COMMUNICATIONS ENGINEERING
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: INFO ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unisi.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/11227

INFO ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 2 years. Program: 2023. Status: Open. Credits: 120 CFU. Language: English. Type: Master's Degrees. Regulations: D.M. 270/2004. Degree Class: LM-27 - Class of Master's Degrees in Telecommunications Engineering. Inter-University Course: No. Open Access: Yes. Location: Siena. Department: Department of Information Engineering and Mathematics. Website: https://electronics-communications.unisi.it. Informative Website: https://www.diism.unisi.it/it/didattica. Admission Requirements. Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]. Upper Secondary School Diploma. Optional qualifications (choose from the following): [L2+ CER_LIN+TSS], Bachelor's Degree, Language certification [L1+ CER_LIN +TSS], Degree, Language certification [L2+ OBG+TSS], Bachelor's Degree, Open Badge [L1+ OBG +TSS], Degree, Open Badge [TS +CER_LIN +TSS], Foreign qualification, Language certification [TS +OBG +TSS], Foreign qualification, Open Badge. Fees and contributions. For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse. Dates and deadlines. For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan" www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni. Roles and Responsibilities: Student Mobility, GIOVANNI GIAMBENE, President/Chairman of the teaching committee, TOMMASO ADDABBO, Program, texts and objectives. Professional status conferred by the degree. Research, design, development, production and management in the field of information processing and transmission systems and in the design of electronic devices and systems. The Master's degree in Electronics and Communications Engineering naturally places its graduates within industries, companies, organizations and research centers where research and development, design, production and management of information processing and transmission systems, fixed and mobile telecommunications networks, and complex electronic systems are carried out. They also possess the engineering skills necessary to start independent professional activities and to found high-tech companies. The study program offered in Siena is characterized by content aimed at training a Master's graduate with skills applicable in the field of telecommunications, electronics, biomedical, security, automotive, energy, preservation and enjoyment of cultural heritage, entertainment, home automation, and industrial automation. Master's graduates can access the Order of Engineers, Information Engineering sector, section A.
Characteristics of the final exam. The final exam involves the preparation and discussion of a thesis (Master's Thesis), developed by the student in an original way under the guidance of a supervisor, possibly assisted by one or more co-supervisors. The Master's Thesis can be of a compilation or theoretical-experimental nature. In any case, the final exam aims to highlight the skills acquired by the student for the study, understanding, critical evaluation and design of advanced and original solutions in the scientific/technological field. The skills of synthesis, scientific rigor and organization in the drafting of the paper and in the oral presentation of the work, which is planned at the end of the study program, will also be evaluated. In the case of a theoretical-experimental thesis, the Teaching Committee appoints a co-supervisor, who is responsible for evaluating the candidate's original contribution and the organization of the paper. The thesis for the Master's Degree can be written in English, Italian or in an official language of the EU. It is possible to carry out part of the activities related to the final exam within the framework of an internship or traineeship at companies, organizations or research laboratories.
Knowledge required for access. Access to the study program is reserved for those who hold a Bachelor's degree or a three-year university diploma, or another qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable. Access requires that candidates meet curricular and personal preparation requirements. It is not possible to enroll with outstanding entrance requirements.
Curricular requirements. It is required to have a degree in one of the following classes or equivalent class according to the former D.M.509/99 (the number of the equivalent class is indicated in parentheses, together with the name if it is different):
- L-7 Civil and Environmental Engineering (8)
- L-8 Information Engineering (9)
- L-9 Industrial Engineering (10)
- L-30 Physical Sciences and Technologies (25)
- L-31 Computer Science and Technologies (26)
- L-35 Mathematical Sciences (32)
- L-41 Statistics (37 Statistical Sciences)
Those who hold a specialist degree according to D.M.509/99 or a degree according to the previous regulations to D.M.509/99, in engineering, mathematical and physical disciplines, also meet the curricular requirements, subject to evaluation by the Teaching Committee of an appropriate differentiation of the training objectives of these study programs with respect to the Master's Degree program.
For students with a degree from a foreign university, the assessment of the possession of curricular requirements is delegated to the Teaching Committee, which verifies in particular:
- that the qualification is issued at the end of a course of study of at least three years;
- that the training objectives are sufficiently consistent with those of one of the degree classes listed above.
Knowledge of the English language is required at least at the B2 level of the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages. Students whose native language is not Italian who do not reach at least the A1 level of knowledge of the Italian language must include in their study programs activities aimed at achieving this level.
Personal preparation requirements. Admission to the Study Program, for students who meet the curricular requirements, is subject to verification of personal preparation, according to the procedures defined in the Didactic Regulations of the Study Program. The verification concerns knowledge of the disciplines of mathematics, statistics, physics and information engineering. For students who hold a specialist degree according to D.M.509/99 or a degree according to the previous regulations to D.M.509/99, the verification of personal preparation requirements will be evaluated on a case-by-case basis by the Teaching Committee.
For students with a qualification obtained abroad, the Teaching Committee carries out a selection that provides for the evaluation of personal preparation based on the curriculum and an admission test in written and/or oral form.
Knowledge and understanding.
In-depth scientific and methodological knowledge. In-depth study of the mathematical/scientific tools necessary for the complete understanding of the subjects covered, for the modeling and solution of problems in complex systems and for the analysis and design of advanced and technologically complex systems. In-depth study of the analysis tools provided by information theory and digital communications theory. The acquisition of knowledge and understanding is verified through written and/or oral exams.
Information processing and transmission systems. Knowledge of digital coding and transmission techniques. Knowledge of the mechanisms of operation and analysis methodologies of fixed and mobile telecommunications systems and networks. In-depth knowledge of architectures, network protocols and network management. Knowledge of the technological solutions used in the latest generation of mobile networks, including the Internet of Things. Knowledge of advanced techniques for image and signal processing. Knowledge of the solutions adopted to guarantee the integrity and security of data transmission and the protection of multimedia information. The acquisition of knowledge and understanding is verified through written and/or oral exams.
Electromagnetic fields and propagation. Knowledge of the mechanisms of operation and analysis methodologies of microwave systems and components in waveguide and planar technology. Understanding of the design techniques of microwave systems and components and the main application scenarios. Knowledge of the mechanisms of operation and analysis methodologies of wire, planar and reflector antennas. Understanding of the design techniques of antennas and the main application scenarios (telecommunications, space, radar). Knowledge of the mechanisms of operation and analysis methodologies of RFID tags and readers. Understanding of the design and integration techniques of RFID systems and the main application scenarios. Knowledge of the main algorithms for calculating electromagnetic fields. Understanding of electromagnetic simulation methodologies using commercial software for the analysis and design of microwave systems, antennas, RFID systems. The acquisition of knowledge and understanding is verified through written and/or oral exams.
Electronics and electronic measurements. Knowledge of advanced techniques for the design of analog and digital electronic systems. Knowledge of complex "mixed-signal" electronic systems based on programmable logic. Knowledge of secure design techniques. Knowledge of methodologies for the analysis and evaluation of the reliability of components and systems. Knowledge of embedded systems for measurement and control for industrial applications. Knowledge of technological solutions for the measurement of signals: structure of sensors, microsensors and microsystems and interfacing techniques. The acquisition of knowledge and understanding is verified through written and/or oral exams.
Contextual knowledge and final exam. Knowledge of companies in the electronics and telecommunications engineering sector, also with reference to applications in the energy sector. Knowledge of the English language at an advanced level. The acquisition of knowledge and understanding is verified through written and/or oral exams. A further verification is represented by the internship and the final exam, which allows to evaluate the knowledge and skills acquired by the student, possibly in a company context.

Program, texts and objectives Professional status conferred by the degree.
Statistician
- The acquired knowledge allows the graduate to operate with a high level of responsibility in:
- National and international research institutions (Istat, Eurostat, CNR, etc.)
- Public bodies (INPS, local authorities, etc.).
- International organizations (FAO, WHO, etc.)
- Public Administration.
- Healthcare companies, hospital companies, local health agencies.
- National and international pharmaceutical companies.
- Companies in the credit, insurance, and market research sectors.
- Freelance work as a business consultant, insurance consultant, or banking consultant.
- The course trains Master's graduates who can successfully access master's and doctoral research programs.
Characteristics of the final exam The final exam consists of a discussion, before a special degree commission, of a thesis developed in an original way under the guidance of a supervisor. It must verify that the Master's graduate has acquired advanced knowledge on topics of methodological and applied statistics in the field of data science and that they have the ability to clearly present and argue in written and oral form. The thesis can be written and presented in Italian or English.
For each Master's thesis, a co-supervisor is appointed, at the proposal of the supervisor, and a counter-supervisor is appointed at the proposal of the competent teaching body.
The merit score awarded to the final exam will take into account, in particular, the level of theoretical depth, the ability demonstrated by the candidate in the use of quantitative methodologies appropriate to the objective of the research, the effectiveness of the empirical analyses carried out, the originality of the arguments proposed, and the effectiveness of the discussion. The merit score awarded to the final exam contributes to the formation of the final grade of the Master's Degree together with the average of the course exams, according to the criteria defined by a specific Regulation. The grade of the Master's Degree is expressed in one hundred tenths. If the sum of the merit score awarded to the final exam and the average grade of the profit exams is greater than 110/110, at the proposal of the supervisor, the Master's Degree Commission may unanimously award honors.
Knowledge required for admission Students who wish to enroll in the Master's Degree course in Statistical Methods and Data Analytics must have a degree or a three-year university diploma, or another qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable. They must have adequate preparation in mathematics and statistics, as well as the curricular requirements indicated below.
For graduates from the L-8, L-9, L-18, L-33, L-35, and L-41 classes, the curricular requirements for admission are automatically considered to be met, provided that they have acquired:
- at least 16 CFU in the SSD SECS-S/01- S/02-S/03-S/04-S/05-MAT/06-SECS-P/05;
- at least 3 CFU in specific IT skills.
Graduates from degree classes other than those indicated can also enroll, provided that they have acquired:
- SECS-S/01-S/02-S/03-S/04-S/05-S/06 (16 CFU)
- MAT/01-02-03-04-05-06-07-08-09 (8 CFU)
- SECS-P/01-02-03-04-05-06 (16 CFU)
- INF/01 and ING-INF/05 (8 CFU)
- SPS/03-04-07 (24 CFU)
- L-LIN/12 (3 CFU).
The following are required for admission to the Master's Degree course in Statistical Methods and Data Analytics:
- specific knowledge in mathematics and statistics.
- basic IT skills;
- knowledge of the English language at least at level B1.
The verification of personal preparation is carried out in oral form. The subjects, the composition of the examining commission, and the criteria for evaluating the test will be defined by the didactic regulations of the study course. Personal preparation is considered to be verified for graduates in the L-8, L-9, L-18, L-33, L-35, and L-41 classes, who have obtained the degree with a grade higher than a minimum threshold defined by the didactic regulations of the study course.
Knowledge and understanding.
Generic Area The Master's graduate in MSDA, in addition to possessing a good knowledge of statistical methodology and data analytics, is aware of the numerous interactions of statistics with various disciplines and has also acquired the necessary tools for the design and management of sample surveys, and the consequent analysis of data with the most advanced methods, in the economic-financial, social, and ecological-environmental fields. Finally, the Master's graduate is able to understand the literature in the field, both advanced texts and scientific articles published in specialized journals, also in English, and to rework their contents in relation to specific theoretical and applied objectives.
This knowledge and ability to understand will be acquired and verified mainly through the curricular teachings and their respective profit exams in the various areas.
Mathematical-Statistical Basic mathematical foundations for probability and statistics.
Fundamentals of statistics for inference from design and from model.
Data science and analytics Economics Elements of microeconomics and econometrics.
Notions of economic dynamics.

UNIVERSITY: University of Siena
COURSE: [D456] PHARMACY
TYPE: Master's Degree
CURRICULUM: Single-Cycle Program, 5 years
SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unisi.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/11224

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year.
Duration: 5 years. Admission: 2023. Status: Open. Credits: 300 CFU. Language: Italian. Type: Single-cycle Master's Degrees. Regulations: D.M. 270/2004. Degree Class: LM-13 - Pharmacy and Pharmaceutical Sciences. Inter-University Course: No. Admission: Open Access. Location: Siena. Department: Biotechnology, Chemistry and Pharmacy. Website: https://farmacia.unisi.it. Admission Requirements: Required qualifications: [TSS]. Upper Secondary School Diploma. Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse. Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni. Roles: President/Chairman of the teaching committee: SANDRA GEMMA. Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree. Pharmacist. Obtaining a Master's Degree in Pharmacy allows graduates to practice as a pharmacist as: Owner, director, or employee of public or private pharmacies; Owner, director, or employee of drugstores; Manager in the National Health Service, with particular reference to hospital pharmacy and territorial pharmaceutical services, after obtaining the specialist title in hospital pharmacy according to current regulations; Scientific information officer for the pharmaceutical, cosmetic, and dietary-food sectors; Employee and manager in the pharmaceutical, cosmetic, and dietary-food industries for the dissemination of scientific information on drugs and health products; Expert for the pharmaceutical, cosmetic, and dietary-food industries; Employee and Manager in: companies producing medicinal substances, pharmaceutical companies for production, packaging, and analysis on behalf of third parties, cosmetic and dietary-food companies; research and development departments in pharmaceutical, cosmetic, and dietary-food industries; company sections dealing with regulatory affairs, marketing, and quality assurance; research laboratories of national and international public institutions.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam consists of preparing and publicly discussing a thesis, which can be either a compilation or an experimental thesis, developed originally by the student, under the supervision of a teacher from a relevant academic field within the course of study, and related to a topic relevant to the curricular disciplines. The compilation thesis consists of an in-depth bibliographic study on a topic agreed upon between the teacher and the student, and the writing of an original written work. The experimental thesis consists of conducting original research or collecting and processing original information from clinical-scientific databases. The experimental thesis can be carried out in a university laboratory, a company, or a research institution, in Italy or abroad, with which the University has established a convention or international agreement.
The final exam includes the completion of a practical test to evaluate the professional skills acquired during the practical internship, which precedes the discussion of the degree thesis and aims to assess the candidate's level of technical preparation for the authorization to practice the profession. The final dissertation is carried out in the presence of a degree commission composed of expert teachers in the subject and members appointed by the professional order; the student must demonstrate mastery of the topic of the thesis, critical ability, and good communication skills. The final grade is expressed in hundredths, with a possible distinction.
Knowledge required for admission: To be admitted to the course of study, applicants must hold a secondary school diploma or another qualification obtained abroad and recognized as equivalent. Knowledge of the English language is also required at a level of competence of at least A2/2, as defined by the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages.
Students enrolled in the Master's Degree program in Pharmacy must possess adequate initial preparation in chemistry, biology, and mathematics, which is verified through the TOLC-F test. This test is mandatory but not binding for the admission procedure.
Minimum scores are established for the evaluation of the test. For those students who do not reach these scores, the teachers of the subject will verify the recovery of the educational requirements that students are required to complete within the first year of study.
Knowledge and understanding: Basic physical-mathematical and chemical area: The graduate acquires knowledge of the basic scientific aspects of mathematics, computer science, and physics; general and inorganic chemistry and stoichiometry; analytical chemistry and organic chemistry; behavioral and safety rules in an experimental laboratory. Biological area: The graduate acquires knowledge of animal and plant biology, biochemistry, applied biochemistry, and clinical biochemistry, biochemistry of nutrition, molecular biology, microbiology, and epidemiology. Basic biomedical area: The graduate acquires knowledge of human anatomy, general physiology, and the pathology of human diseases, and becomes familiar with medical terminology. Pharmacological and toxicological area: The graduate knows medicinal plants and their pharmacologically active principles and acquires knowledge of phytotherapy; knows pharmacology, pharmacotherapy, and toxicology in order to have a complete understanding of the action of drugs, diagnostic products, and other health products; also acquires knowledge of clinical pharmacology and pharmacovigilance. Chemical-pharmaceutical area: The graduate knows the chemistry of the main classes of drugs; knows the different synthetic processes and the methodologies used for quality control of the main classes of drugs; knows the qualitative and quantitative analysis methods of medicinal products, even in complex matrices, and the physicochemical characteristics of the raw materials used in the formulations of therapeutic preparations, including pharmaceutical polymers; knows the chemistry and analysis methods of herbal products, nutraceuticals, food supplements, biotechnological drugs, and cosmetic products. Technological-legislative and professionalizing area: The graduate acquires knowledge of pharmaceutical technology and knows the procedures for the formulation of galenic and magistral products and cosmetic products; acquires knowledge of innovative pharmaceutical forms and medical devices; knows the legislative and deontological rules that regulate the profession of pharmacist; acquires basic knowledge of the use of computer science in the company and useful knowledge for the economic management of the pharmacy and relations with the public; acquires linguistic competence in the field of specific content related to pharmaceutical disciplines, scientific literature, and the profession of pharmacist.

UNIVERSITY: University of Siena
COURSE: [D457] CHEMISTRY AND PHARMACEUTICAL TECHNOLOGY
TYPE: Master's Degree
CURRICULUM: Five-Year Single-Cycle Program
SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unisi.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/11223

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 5 years. Program: 2023. Status: Open. Credits: 300 CFU. Language: Italian. Type of Degree: Single-Cycle Master's Degrees. Regulations: D.M. 270/2004. Degree Class: LM-13 - Pharmacy and Pharmaceutical Industry. Inter-University Course: No. Admission: Open Access. Location: Siena. Department: Biotechnology, Chemistry and Pharmacy. Website: https://chimica-tecnologia-farmaceutiche.unisi.it. Admission Requirements: Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]. Upper Secondary School Diploma. Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse. Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni. Roles: President/Chairman of the teaching committee: ANNALISA SANTUCCI. Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree. Scientific expert or information provider in the pharmaceutical, cosmetic and food sectors, Pharmacist, Chemist. Completion of the Master's Degree in CTF offers the following employment opportunities: pharmaceutical industry; chemical, toxicological, clinical chemistry and biochemical-clinical analysis laboratories; public and private quality control laboratories; structures of the National Health System; School (graduates who have a sufficient number of credits in appropriate subject areas may, as provided by current legislation, participate in the entrance exams for training courses for secondary school teachers), University and other public and private bodies; distribution and marketing of pharmaceuticals.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam consists of the preparation and public discussion of an experimental thesis, developed in an original way by the student, under the supervision of a teacher from a relevant SSD (Scientific Disciplinary Sector) within the course of study, and relating to a topic relevant to the curricular disciplines. The experimental thesis can be carried out in a university laboratory, a company or research institution, in Italy or abroad, with which the University has established a convention or international agreement.
The final exam includes the completion of a practical test to evaluate the professional skills acquired during the practical internship, which precedes the discussion of the degree thesis and aims to assess the candidate's level of technical preparation for the authorization to practice the profession of Pharmacist. The final dissertation takes place in the presence of a degree commission composed of expert teachers in the subject and members appointed by the professional order; the student must demonstrate mastery of the subject of the thesis, critical ability and good communication skills.
The methods and criteria for the final evaluation take into account the student's entire academic career within the Course of Study, the timing and methods of acquiring the CFU (university credits), previous training activities and the final exam. The final grade is expressed in hundredths, with possible honors.
Knowledge required for admission: To be admitted to the course of study, it is necessary to have a secondary school diploma or another qualification obtained abroad and recognized as equivalent. Knowledge of the English language is also required at a level of competence of at least A2/2, as defined by the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages.
Students enrolled in the Master's Degree program in CTF must have adequate initial preparation in chemistry, biology and mathematics, which is verified through the TOLC-F test. This test is mandatory but not binding for the admission procedure.
Minimum scores are established for the evaluation of the test. For those students who do not reach these scores, the teachers of the subject will verify the recovery of the training obligations that students are required to complete within the first year of study. Knowledge and understanding.
Generic Area: The entire Course of Study progresses from the basic and preparatory disciplines towards the characterizing and related disciplines. Master's degree graduates acquire knowledge and the ability to understand and elaborate concepts in the chemical, biological, pharmaceutical and pharmacological fields. In particular, they acquire knowledge of the scientific approach to solving problems related to the structural design of biologically active molecules, synthesis, development, evaluation of biological and/or pharmacological activity, preparation of the pharmaceutical form, quality control in production, patent and drug registration procedures, as well as knowledge of the legislative contexts in which pharmaceutical industries and pharmacies operate. By consulting scientific literature, the master's degree graduate must be able to quickly assimilate the scientific and technological innovations that are occurring rapidly in these areas and be able to develop and apply original ideas that are up to the state of the art, even in a research context. The verification of learning involves traditional exams in which the candidate demonstrates to a commission of experts in the subject their ability to assimilate and integrate the knowledge acquired and to communicate it clearly and unambiguously. In addition to exams, depending on the assessment and at the discretion of the teachers of the courses, written reports and intermediate discussions on specific topics may be required. These types of knowledge assessment are designed to verify whether the student is able to integrate and formulate judgments on the knowledge acquired and on their ability to continue their studies and acquire specific knowledge independently.
Area of Basic Disciplines: The basic courses prepare the student to address the subsequent characterizing topics by acquiring a rigorously scientific approach. The student must know the principles underlying chemistry and biology at the molecular level and apply the basic mathematical operators of thermodynamics. They must understand the correlation between the molecular level and the complexity of organization typical of cells, tissues, organs, systems and organisms.
The student must acquire in-depth knowledge of general and inorganic chemistry and analytical chemistry, especially with regard to the analysis of complex systems, including biological systems. Particular importance is given to organic chemistry as a central discipline for the subsequent study of biochemistry, pharmacology and pharmaceutical chemistry. The student must know the physical and chemical processes that regulate a living organism and its relationship with the external environment, including the physiological processes that take place within it. They must know the eukaryotic cell, the organization and development of animal tissues and human anatomy of organs and systems. They must have knowledge of plant and animal biology, the basic principles of the organization of the immune system and the pathogenic mechanisms of viruses, bacteria and parasites.
Area of characterizing disciplines - Chemical-Pharmaceutical-Technological Area: The courses in the Chemical-Pharmaceutical-Technological area include disciplines that contribute to defining the following areas of learning:
1) The synthesis and molecular aspects of the activity of active pharmaceutical ingredients
2) The techniques of formulation and "delivery" of a drug and the legislation. Thematic area 1: Starting from the basic knowledge of organic chemistry, the student must know the problems of the molecular structures associated with the various classes of drugs, deepening the aspects related to the structure/activity relationships and the processes of synthesis and production of active ingredients. The student must understand the main models of study of the pharmaceutical activity of an organic molecule, its pharmacokinetics and pharmacodynamics, always at the molecular level. The student must understand the main modes of operation of an active ingredient, correlating the molecular structures with the biological properties of the molecules, including their toxicology.
Through the practical exercises, which consist of 4 single-station laboratories for a total of 160 hours of activity, the student will have the opportunity to put into practice the theoretical knowledge acquired in the various courses, being able to carry out analyses and preparations of pharmaceutical active ingredients. The student must also learn the use of the main laboratory instruments, demonstrating that they are able to process the experimental data obtained. The student will also be trained in bibliographic research and report writing.
Thematic area 2: This thematic area aims to provide the student with knowledge and skills to evaluate the different ways of administering a drug and the related formulation techniques. The student must know the methodologies for preparing and controlling the various pharmaceutical forms in a context of evaluating the critical issues related to the incorrect application, in the various stages of the supply chain, of good practices. They must know the operation of the production techniques applied to the different types of pharmaceuticals, in order to be able to evaluate the suitability and quality in compliance with hygiene and safety criteria. Furthermore, the student must know the legislation relating to the production, registration and distribution of a drug. Also in this area, a single-station laboratory activity (40 hours) is planned, where the student will personally carry out a pharmaceutical/galenic preparation and must be able to draw up a "master format". Finally, the student will acquire skills in the food field with the study of the molecular processes associated with the preparation and metabolism of food supplements.
Area of characterizing disciplines - Area of Biological and Pharmacological Disciplines: The student must know the problems of the biochemical mechanisms that are the basis of the correct functioning of the cell and the resulting problems related to a pathology, deepening the aspects related to the problems of identification of a biological process, its analysis and the interpretation of the results, giving priority, where possible, to the holistic aspect of the analysis. The student must acquire and understand the main models of study of cellular and molecular biology, being able to describe with appropriate scientific language the notions relating to the functioning of biological processes and their interpretation.
The student must know the principles of operation of a drug, the drug/receptor interactions and the type of routes of administration and intake of drugs, pharmacokinetics (absorption, distribution, biotransformation, excretion, pharmacokinetic models), pharmacodynamics (drug-receptor interaction and mechanisms of action), the factors that modify the response to drugs, the interactions between drugs and the side effects, the formation of residues and the suspension times. They must know the concepts of pharmacovigilance and pharmacosurveillance and the relevant current legislation. They must also understand the fundamental notions of statistics and its main applications in the field of medicine, especially with regard to clinical trials. Through the practical exercises, the student will have the opportunity to put into practice the theoretical knowledge acquired, being able to carry out biochemical analyses associated with the pharmaceutical activity of some active ingredients. The student must also learn the use of the main laboratory instruments in the biological field and acquire the basic notions to be able to work with animal tissues and to follow the metabolism of a drug in an organism.

[D521] APPLIED MATHEMATICS Master's Degree Course in English
Course currently under approval. INFO CURRICULUM INFORMATION General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 2 years. Program: 2024. Status: Open. Credits: 120 CFU. Language: English. Type: Master's Degrees. Regulations: D.M. 270/2004. Degree class: LM-40 - Class of Master's Degrees in Mathematics. Inter-university course: No. Open access: Yes. Location: Siena. Department: Department of Information Engineering and Mathematics. Website: https://applied-mathematics.unisi.it/it. Informative website: https://www.diism.unisi.it/it/didattica. Admission requirements. Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]. Upper Secondary School Diploma. Optional qualifications (choose from the following): [L2+ CER_LIN+TSS]. Bachelor's Degree, Language certification [L1+ CER_LIN +TSS]. Bachelor's Degree, Language certification [L2+ OBG+TSS]. Bachelor's Degree, Open Badge [L1+ OBG +TSS]. Bachelor's Degree, Open Badge [TS +CER_LIN +TSS]. Foreign qualification, Language certification [TS +OBG +TSS]. Foreign qualification, Open Badge. Fees and contributions. For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse. Dates and deadlines. For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni. Roles and responsibilities. Student mobility: GIOVANNI GIAMBENE, President/Chairman of the teaching committee. SIMONE RINALDI, Program, texts and objectives. Professional status conferred by the degree. Expert in mathematics and its applications. Organizations and companies that require data processing and analysis and the study of their structure. Organizations and companies involved in the modeling and optimization of complex systems. Research and experimental development in the field of mathematical, engineering, bioinformatics, economic and social sciences. Teaching of Mathematics, after completing the relevant ministerial introductory courses.
Characteristics of the final exam. The final exam involves the preparation and discussion of a thesis (Master's Thesis), developed by the student in an original way under the guidance of a supervisor, possibly assisted by one or more co-supervisors. The Master's Thesis can be of a descriptive or theoretical-experimental nature. In any case, the final exam aims to highlight the skills acquired by the student for the study, understanding, critical evaluation and design of advanced and original solutions in the scientific/technological field. The skills of synthesis, methodological rigor and organization in the drafting of the paper and in the oral presentation of the work, which is planned at the end of the study program, will also be evaluated. The Teaching Committee appoints a co-supervisor, who is responsible for evaluating the candidate's original contribution and the organization of the paper. The thesis for the Master's Degree can be written in English, Italian or in an official language of the EU. It is possible to carry out part of the activities related to the final exam within the framework of internships or traineeships at companies, organizations or research laboratories.
Knowledge required for access. Access to the study program is reserved for those who hold a Bachelor's or University degree of three years' duration, or other qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable. Access requires that candidates meet curricular and personal preparation requirements. It is not possible to enroll with outstanding entrance requirements.
Curricular requirements. It is required to have AT LEAST ONE of the following requirements:
Degree in class L35 (Mathematical Sciences) or in class L-32 ex dm 509/99.
Degree in one of the following classes ex D.M.270/04 (or in the equivalent classes ex D.M.509/99, whose number is indicated next to it in parentheses, together with the name if it is different):
L-7 Civil and Environmental Engineering (8)
L-8 Information Engineering (9)
L-9 Industrial Engineering (10)
L-18 Economics and Business Management Sciences (17)
L-27 Chemical Sciences and Technologies (21)
L-30 Physical Sciences and Technologies (25)
L-31 Computer Science (26)
L-33 Economic Sciences (28)
L-41 Statistics (37 Statistical Sciences)
together with having obtained a minimum number of CFU in the MAT/xx sectors, as defined in the Didactic Regulations of the Study Course.
For students whose qualification was awarded by a foreign university, the assessment of the possession of curricular requirements is delegated to the Teaching Committee, which verifies in particular: that the qualification is awarded at the end of a study course of at least three years' duration; that the studies followed are adequate with respect to the requirements of the degree classes indicated above.
Knowledge of the English language is required at least at level B2 of the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages.
Students, whose native language is not Italian, who do not reach at least level A1 of knowledge of the Italian language, must include in their study plans training activities aimed at achieving this level.
Personal preparation requirements. Admission to the Study Course, for students who have the curricular requirements, is subject to verification of personal preparation, according to the methods defined in the Didactic Regulations of the Study Course.
For students with a qualification obtained abroad, the Teaching Committee carries out a selection which provides for the evaluation of personal preparation based on the study curriculum and an admission test in written and/or oral form.
Knowledge and understanding.
Mathematical methodologies. Master's degree graduates in Applied Mathematics acquire further knowledge that complements their basic mathematical skills, covering all the main areas of mathematics (mathematical logic, algebra, geometry, mathematical analysis, mathematical physics, statistics, numerical analysis, optimization) fundamental to addressing complex and challenging problems in the field of mathematics and beyond. Subsequently, the student can choose between courses focusing on:
- in-depth study of specific areas of pure mathematics, aimed at introducing new recent developments and research problems in mathematics, in particular in Logic, Algebra, Geometry, Mathematical Analysis;
- topics in which the support of advanced mathematical methods is fundamental in recent scientific research and in the development of technologies.
In particular, students acquire knowledge of the developments of modern mathematical theories and the problems they address, as well as the ability to continuously update themselves through the reading of advanced texts and scientific journals.
Mathematical models. The Study Course provides for some courses that complement the basic mathematical skills, covering some areas of Mathematics necessary to deepen the application topics (Mathematical Analysis, Geometry, Statistics, Numerical Analysis, Optimization). Subsequently, the student can choose between courses with more application-oriented content, which include the study of applications of data analysis, artificial intelligence and machine learning, bioinformatics and information systems to understand the techniques of modeling, analysis and simulation of processes derived from other scientific disciplines, such as engineering, physics, biology or medicine.
The student is thus trained to interact with teachers and researchers who work in scientific and technological areas where advanced mathematical tools are required. The acquired skills include the ability to retrieve methodological information from advanced mathematical literature.
Verification of the achievement of objectives is assessed in the final exams of the courses and through the final exam.

SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 3 years. Program: 2017. Status: Open. Credits: 180 CFU. Language: Italian, English. Type: Degree Courses. Regulatory Framework: D.M. 270/2004. Degree Class: L-18 - Class of degrees in Economics and Business Management. Inter-University Course: No. Open Access: Yes. Location: Siena. Other locations: Arezzo. Department: Department of Business and Legal Studies. Website: https://economia-commercio.unisi.it. Informative Website: https://orientarsi.unisi.it/studio/triennali/corso/economia-e-commercio. Admission Requirements: Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]. Upper Secondary School Diploma. Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse. Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni. Roles and Responsibilities: Student Mobility: PASQUALE RUGGIERO, President/Chairman of the teaching committee. MARIA CLEOFE GIORGINO, President. MARIA CLEOFE GIORGINO. Program, texts and objectives: Admission requirements: Upper secondary school diploma. Professional status conferred by the degree: Expert in business administration and control. Graduates in Economics and Commerce can perform the aforementioned functions in: - private companies of any size operating in any economic sector, from agriculture to commerce to the service sector; - business consulting firms; - non-profit organizations; - public companies; - business, category and trade union associations. They can carry out administrative and managerial consulting activities (management control) and practice as a freelancer after passing the admission exams to the register. Commercial, administrative and financial manager of the company. Graduates in Economics and Commerce can perform the function of junior manager in: - private companies of any size; - business consulting firms; - non-profit organizations; - public companies. Graduates can also join the management bodies of the public administration. Expert in the process of internationalization of the company. Graduates in Economics and Commerce can perform these functions in: - private companies of any size; - business consulting firms; - public companies; - in business, category and trade union associations. Characteristics of the final exam: The degree in Economics and Commerce is obtained by passing a final exam, which consists of the evaluation by the degree commission of the final paper prepared by the student under the supervision of a lecturer. The final paper consists of a short paper, accompanied by the necessary bibliographic references, on a theoretical or empirical topic chosen by the student according to the procedures defined in a regulation on the final exam prepared by the competent teaching structure. To pass the final exam, the student must demonstrate that they have acquired, in addition to adequate knowledge and understanding of the chosen topic, autonomous learning and research skills; the ability to correctly use the consulted literature and the necessary data sources; the ability to present and support scientific arguments in a logical and coherent manner. The final paper can also be written in English; it must be written in English if students graduate in a study program taught in English. The final exam is worth 3 CFU. The score of the final exam is expressed in hundredths. The commission assigns a score to the final exam, which takes into account the average expressed in hundredths of the scores obtained in the exams, increased by any additional points provided for in the regulation for the final exam for specific curricular merit profiles, and the quality of the written paper. If the overall score is higher than 110/110, the commission may unanimously award honors. Knowledge required for access: The knowledge required for access to the degree course is that acquired at the upper secondary school level (certified for Italian students by obtaining the Upper Secondary School Diploma), with particular attention to knowledge in the mathematical field and a good basic general culture. The specific content of the mathematical knowledge required for access will be indicated in the didactic regulation of the study course. Knowledge of the English language is also required, at least at level A2/2 (CEFR). For the curriculum in English, knowledge of the English language is required, at least at level B2 (CEFR). The assessment of the student's initial preparation will be carried out according to the criteria and procedures defined in detail in the didactic regulation of the study course. It will be based on a test, developed and conducted on a national basis, designed to assess the aptitude and minimum preparation required to face economic studies, through tests of verbal comprehension, basic knowledge in the mathematical field and simple elementary logic problems. The assessment of knowledge of the English language will be carried out through tests carried out in collaboration with the Language Center of the University of Siena. The didactic regulation of the study course establishes the additional training obligations provided for in the event of a negative assessment of the entrance test and the test of knowledge of the English language, also in the form of specific remedial courses in mathematics and English organized by the Departments. In the case of foreign students, the didactic regulation of the study course determines the methods by which the actual presence of the basic knowledge necessary to attend the degree course with profit can be verified. Degree awarded: Bachelor's degree (first level) in Economics and Commerce. Teaching delivery method: Conventional. Knowledge and understanding. General area: The graduate in Economics and Commerce knows the basic models and theories in the economic field, with attention to the historical evolution of economic processes. He/she knows the basic theories and techniques that allow him/her to analyze the organization and performance of the company; he/she has knowledge and understanding of the methods of operation and management of the company, regardless of its size. The graduate knows and understands legal language and the fundamentals of public and private law. He/she has a good knowledge of basic mathematical and statistical tools. The graduate knows the most widely used computer tools and has a good knowledge of the English language. To this end, the knowledge offered to students is based on criteria of scientific rigor, updating and relevance to the world of work. Compliance with these criteria ensures the transfer to students, in the appropriate teaching forms and in the appropriate support materials, of updated, appropriate and reliable knowledge. This knowledge and understanding are acquired and verified mainly through the courses and their respective exams. Economic area: The graduate in Economics and Commerce has a good knowledge of the historical evolution of the economy and is able to understand how economic variables have influenced the decisions of economic operators over time. The graduate in Economics and Commerce has a good knowledge of the main economic models, both microeconomic and macroeconomic, and of the determinants of the main economic variables (GDP, inflation rate, exchange rate) and is able to understand and interpret the economic context, to carry out an analysis of the market conditions in which the company operates and to develop a consequent business strategy. Depending on the chosen curriculum, the graduate in Economics and Commerce has a) an in-depth knowledge of the interpretative models of the role of the State in the economy and is able to understand the mechanisms through which the tax and public expenditure system influence the decisions of consumers and companies; b) knowledge of the behavioral model of the company in the various market forms in which it operates, with particular attention to price and production strategies in situations of strategic interdependence and technological innovation strategies. Business area: The graduate in Economics and Commerce has a good command of the theory and technique of business management, the theory and technique of preparing a company balance sheet, both in the case of a private company and a public company. He/she has a good knowledge of national accounting principles (OIC) and (depending on the courses chosen) a good knowledge of international accounting principles (IFRS); he/she knows analytical accounting and is able to understand its results in terms of costs, revenues and operating margins relating to specific business segments. The graduate in Economics and Commerce has a good knowledge of the theories and tools of reporting and business planning, knows the budget system and is able to understand the interdependencies between the target values contained in it; he/she will have a good knowledge of the management reporting system and the main indicators useful for measuring business performance. The graduate in Economics and Commerce knows the main theories for the analysis of consumer choices, for the study of product markets and sales strategies, the elements of marketing and business sales strategies (especially in one of the two curricula in which the degree course is organized). Legal area: The graduate in Economics and Commerce knows and understands legal terminology; he/she knows the main institutions of private law (rights of individuals, rights over things, obligations and their sources) and public law (the Italian constitution and the political organization of the Italian State with reference to the European system). The graduate in Economics and Commerce knows the principles relating to the legal regulation of individual and collective companies, with particular attention to the practical and applied aspects of the studied institutions. Depending on the curriculum and the courses chosen, the graduate knows and understands the basics of the legislation regulating the labor market and the relations between employees and companies, the tax legislation with particular attention to what concerns the tax obligations of the company, the international legislation on trade and transnational investments, the administrative law and the legal relations between private individuals and the bodies of the State. Quantitative area: The graduate in Economics and Commerce must know the tools of mathematical analysis with particular attention to the mathematical tools useful for making economic decisions (basic tools of maximization and optimization). The graduate in Economics and Commerce must know the main statistical tools, understanding their use both in a descriptive and inferential context; he/she must know the main institutions of descriptive statistics and probability, understanding their relevance and effectiveness within the business management process. The graduate in Economics and Commerce knows and understands the mathematical tools for the analysis of interest rates, annuities and amortizations. He/she knows the mathematical tools for analyzing and pricing bonds and for analyzing the structure of the bond and financial market.

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 3 years. Admission: 2020. Status: Open. Credits: 180 CFU. Language: Italian, English. Type: Degree Courses. Regulatory Framework: D.M. 270/2004. Degree Class: L-33 - Class of Economics Degrees. Inter-University Course: No. Open Access: Yes. Location: Siena. Department: Department of Economics and Statistics. Website: https://scienze-economiche-bancarie.unisi.it. Informative Website: https://orientarsi.unisi.it/studio/triennali/corso/scienze-economiche-e-bancarie. Admission Requirements: Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]. Upper Secondary School Diploma. Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse. Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni. Roles and Responsibilities: Student Mobility: PASQUALE RUGGIERO, President/Chairman of the teaching committee. MARIA CLEOFE GIORGINO, President. MARIA CLEOFE GIORGINO. Program, texts and objectives: Admission requirements: Upper Secondary School Diploma. Professional status conferred by the degree: Operational support in banking and insurance intermediaries. Front and back-office duties in banks and insurance companies. Staff involved in the production of contracts or sales. Administrative staff. Financial and insurance consultants. Operational support within research offices of provincial, regional and national bodies, or in the financial intermediation sector. Economic and market analysis within the research, analysis, planning and control departments of financial institutions, companies and public and private bodies. Statistical technician in financial institutions, companies and public bodies. Accounting expert. Freelance work. Entry into management consulting, tax and labor companies. Accounting expert. The traditional employment opportunities for graduates who have chosen a curriculum with a significant focus on statistical activities and data analysis can be found in: - research institutions that conduct socio-economic research, - research institutions that conduct statistical-environmental research, - banks and insurance companies, - pharmaceutical companies, - the entire industrial sector with the management of production lines, market research and statistical quality control, - organizations that deal with territorial analysis in the environmental, geological, physical, etc. fields. Characteristics of the final exam: The degree is obtained by passing a final exam, which consists of the evaluation by the degree commission of the final paper prepared by the student under the supervision of a lecturer. The final paper consists of a short paper, accompanied by the necessary bibliographic references, on a theoretical or empirical topic chosen by the student according to the procedures defined in a regulation on the final exam prepared by the relevant teaching structure. To pass the final exam, the student must demonstrate that they have acquired, in addition to adequate knowledge and understanding of the chosen topic, autonomous learning and research skills; the ability to correctly use the literature consulted and the data sources necessary; the ability to present and support scientific arguments in a logical and coherent manner. The final paper can also be written in English; it must be written in English if the students graduate in a study program conducted in English. The final exam is worth 3 CFU. The score for the final exam is expressed out of one hundred. The commission assigns a score to the final exam, which takes into account the average score out of one hundred of the grades obtained in the exams, increased by any additional points provided for in the regulation for the final exam for specific curricular merits, and the quality of the written paper. If the overall score is higher than 110/110, the commission may unanimously award honors.
Knowledge required for admission: To be admitted to the Degree Course, applicants must hold a secondary school diploma or another qualification obtained abroad, recognized as equivalent. The knowledge required for admission to the Degree Course is that acquired at the upper secondary school level, with particular reference to the mathematical field, as well as a good general knowledge. The specific content of the mathematical knowledge required for admission will be indicated in the didactic regulations of the study course. Knowledge of the English language at least at level A2/2 (CEFR) is also required. The didactic regulations of the study course will specify the methods for ascertaining this knowledge, and may provide for different and more stringent requirements for courses taught in English.
The assessment of the student's initial preparation will be carried out according to the criteria and methods defined in detail in the didactic regulations of the study course. It will be based on a test, developed and conducted on a national basis, designed to assess the aptitude and minimum preparation required to undertake economic studies, through tests of verbal comprehension, basic knowledge in the mathematical field and simple elementary logic problems. The assessment of knowledge of the English language will be carried out through tests carried out in collaboration with the Language Center of the University of Siena. The didactic regulations of the study course establish the additional training obligations provided for in the event of a negative assessment of the entrance test and the English language test; these obligations must be completed in the first year of the course, also in the form of specific remedial courses in mathematics and English organized by the relevant departments.
Degree awarded: Bachelor's Degree (First Level) in Economics and Banking. Teaching method: Conventional. Knowledge and understanding: General area. With reference to the business context, the graduate in Economics and Banking knows and understands the role and functioning of capital markets in the allocation of financial resources. In this context, he/she knows and understands the structure and functioning of financial markets, financial intermediaries and the essential characteristics of financial instruments and products. He/she knows and understands the main differences between insurance intermediaries, securities intermediaries and credit intermediaries, with particular regard to the types of risks assumed. He/she knows and understands the functions and characteristics of bonds, shares and derivatives, also from a portfolio perspective.
As regards the economic context, the graduate in Economics and Banking has a good knowledge of the main economic models, both microeconomic and macroeconomic, and of the determinants of the main economic variables (GDP, inflation rate, exchange rate) and is able to understand and interpret the economic context of reference, to conduct an analysis of the market conditions in which the company operates and to develop a consequent business strategy.
As regards the legal context, the graduate in Economics and Banking knows and understands the legal terminology; he/she masters the main institutions of private law and public law and the principles relating to the legal regulation of individual and collective enterprises.
With specific reference to the quantitative context, the graduate in Economics and Banking knows the mathematical tools useful for making economic decisions; he/she masters the basic statistical tools, understanding their use both in descriptive and inferential contexts. He/she knows the mathematical tools for the analysis of interest rates, annuities and amortizations.
The graduate in Economics and Banking has a good knowledge of the English language and has the ability to understand written and spoken English.
He/she knows the main computer tools useful for economic and business analysis.
These knowledge and understanding skills must be acquired and verified mainly through the curricular courses and the respective exams in the various areas.
Business area: The graduate in Economics and Banking has a good mastery of the theory and techniques of business management, the theory and techniques of preparing a company balance sheet, both in the case of a private company and a public company. He/she has a good knowledge of national accounting principles (OIC) and (depending on the courses chosen) a good knowledge of international accounting principles (IFRS); he/she knows analytical accounting and is able to understand its results in terms of costs, revenues and operating margins relating to specific business segments.
Depending on the curriculum, the graduate acquires the basic knowledge of corporate finance and the tools to analyze and understand corporate financial decisions.
The graduate knows and understands the role and functioning of capital markets in the allocation of financial resources. In this context, he/she knows and understands the structure and functioning of financial markets, financial intermediaries and the essential characteristics of financial instruments and products. He/she knows and understands the main differences between insurance intermediaries, securities intermediaries and credit intermediaries, with particular regard to the types of risks assumed. He/she knows and understands the functions and characteristics of bonds, shares and derivatives, also from a portfolio perspective.
Among the financial intermediaries, the graduate in Economics and Banking knows and is able to understand the specific characteristics of the bank, its role in the payment system, in the allocation of savings, in the management of savings. He/she knows the different types of banking operations. He/she has a good knowledge of the bank's management balances, with particular reference to risk management. He/she has a good knowledge of the bank's balance sheet and the determinants of bank performance.
Economic area: The graduate in Economics and Banking has a good knowledge of the historical evolution of the economy and is able to understand how economic variables have influenced the decisions of economic operators over time.
The graduate in Economics and Banking has a good knowledge of the main economic models, both microeconomic and macroeconomic, and of the determinants of the main economic variables (GDP, inflation rate, exchange rate) and is able to understand and interpret the economic context of reference, to conduct an analysis of the market conditions in which the company operates and to develop a consequent business strategy.
Depending on the curriculum and the courses chosen, the graduate in Economics and Banking has:
a) a rigorous basic knowledge, relating to econometric theory and the main quantitative methods, which allows him/her to have the tools to set up, process and evaluate a basic empirical project, also through the learning of the functionalities and use of the STATA econometric package;
b) knowledge of the functioning and policies of the labor market, both in Italy and in Europe, based on the analysis of the most relevant issues according to the latest theory and modeling;
c) knowledge of the issues relating to externalities and environmental public goods, which includes the analysis of the spectrum of environmental policy instruments proposed by economic theory (command and control instruments and market instruments such as taxes, subsidies and tradable permits);
d) knowledge of the theoretical and empirical foundations of markets as imperfect markets (monopoly, oligopoly, monopolistic competition), which includes the analysis of the level of competition/collusion with reference to variables such as market power, price discrimination, vertical relations, product differentiation, advertising, research and development, which also considers antitrust policies and forms and models of regulation.
Legal area: The graduate in Economics and Banking knows and understands the legal terminology; he/she knows the main institutions of private law (rights of persons, rights on things, obligations and their sources) and public law (the Italian constitution and the political organization of the Italian State with reference to the European organization).
The graduate in Economics and Banking knows the principles relating to the legal regulation of individual and collective enterprises, with particular attention to the practical and applied aspects of the institutions studied and, depending on the curriculum chosen, a complete knowledge of the harmonized European legislation relating to the law of enterprises and companies.
Depending on the curriculum and the courses chosen, the graduate knows and understands the basics of special domestic and Community legislation relating to banking, financial and insurance companies and, in particular, has a full knowledge at the institutional level of the mechanisms underlying the formation of the banking system, the specific characteristics of banks (and the products offered) and the supervisory methods (national and Community) applicable to them. The graduate understands the legal system that currently governs individual and collective labor relations, paying attention also to the historical evolution of the discipline and the dynamics of the labor market. Finally, the graduate, through the analysis of the main institutions that characterize the administration from the point of view of its organization, its activity and the relations with the other subjects of the legal system, the tools necessary to understand the framework of principles and rules to which public and private subjects must adhere (under certain profiles).
Mathematical-Statistical area: The graduate in Economics and Banking has a good knowledge and understanding of the basic tools of mathematical analysis and linear algebra necessary for statistical, economic, financial and social analysis. He/she also knows the tools of probability calculus and the main methods of descriptive and inferential statistics; in particular, the graduate has knowledge aimed at the detection, analysis, synthesis and interpretation of economic, business and financial data.
The graduate in Economics and Banking knows and understands the mathematical techniques for the analysis of interest rates, annuities and amortizations. He/she has a good mastery of the fundamental elements for the quantitative study of the issues related to financial instruments.
Finally, depending on the curriculum and the courses chosen, the graduate in Economics and Banking has a rigorous basic training relating to econometric theory, with particular reference to the multiple regression model, and a good knowledge of statistical methods for the analysis of multivariate data.

Admission requirements: A degree in one of the degree classes provided for by the course regulations or an equivalent foreign qualification. Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam consists of a discussion, before the degree commission, of a thesis developed in an original way under the guidance of a supervisor. It must verify that the master's graduate has acquired advanced knowledge on topics of financial intermediation, relevant both theoretically and professionally, which allows him/her to develop or apply original ideas through a systematic and critically aware understanding; that he/she has the ability to apply the knowledge acquired and to solve problems on innovative topics, also in an interdisciplinary context; that he/she has the ability to clearly expose and argue in written and oral form. The thesis can be written and presented in Italian or English. The credits awarded for the final exam are 18. For each master's thesis, a co-supervisor is appointed, at the proposal of the supervisor, and a counter-supervisor, at the proposal of the competent teaching body. The merit score awarded for the final exam will take into account, in particular, the level of theoretical depth, the ability demonstrated by the candidate in the use of quantitative methodologies appropriate to the objective of the research, the effectiveness of the empirical analyses carried out, the originality of the arguments proposed and the effectiveness of the discussion. The merit score awarded for the final exam contributes to the formation of the final degree score together with the average of the exams of the study course, according to the criteria defined by a specific Faculty Regulation. The degree score is expressed in one hundred tenths. If the sum of the merit score awarded for the final exam and the average score of the exams is greater than 110/110, at the proposal of the supervisor, the degree commission may unanimously award the honors. Knowledge required for access: The knowledge required for access to the Master's Degree in Economics and Management of Financial Intermediaries relates to the economic, business, mathematical-statistical and legal fields. The knowledge in the business field must also include that relating to the basic profiles of the economics of financial and credit intermediation. The knowledge in the mathematical field must also include that relating to the basic profiles of financial mathematics. Basic computer skills are also required, at least at the level of the ECDL core (with particular reference to the use of spreadsheets), and knowledge of the English language corresponding to at least the B1 level of the Council of Europe's Common European Framework of Reference for Languages. The didactic regulations of the study course define the curricular requirements with reference to the different first-level degree classes as well as the number of training credits to be acquired in specific disciplinary sectors. For graduates in possession of a qualification obtained abroad, the knowledge required for access will be defined by the didactic regulations of the study course based on the minimum contents deemed necessary for the different disciplinary areas, including the linguistic ones. For the purposes of admission to the study course, students in possession of the required curricular requirements must pass a selective test to verify their personal preparation, the methods of which are defined by the didactic regulations of the study course. The admission test for verifying personal preparation is selective in nature and is carried out in writing, by solving multiple-choice questions. The subjects, the composition of the examining commission and the criteria for evaluating the test will be defined by the didactic regulations of the study course. Graduates in possession of the required curricular requirements who have obtained the qualification with a score higher than a minimum threshold defined by the didactic regulations of the study course will be exempt from the admission test. The didactic regulations of the study course may also define specific methods for evaluating the initial preparation of graduates of foreign universities, also with reference to the necessary linguistic knowledge. Qualification awarded: Master's Degree (second-level degree) in Economics and Management of Financial Intermediaries. Teaching delivery method: Conventional. Knowledge and understanding. Generic Area: Master's graduates who have successfully completed the course of study must have acquired the following knowledge and understanding skills: a) ability to understand and explain the policies and operational strategies of the main categories of financial intermediaries. b) knowledge of the theoretical evolution that underlies the different models of portfolio management and their implications in terms of risk and the development capacity of the intermediaries. c) ability to understand and explain the interrelationships between the different components of financial systems, both at the internal and international levels. d) knowledge of current approaches to financial regulation and the implications they have both at the macroeconomic level and for the operations of intermediaries. e) knowledge of the regulatory and institutional framework of financial systems, with particular reference to the European Union. This knowledge and understanding skills will be acquired and verified mainly through the curricular teachings and the respective exams in the different areas. Business Area: Operational policies and strategies of the main categories of financial intermediaries. Theories, tools and markets for risk management. Organizational models of intermediaries. Economic Area: Structural and institutional characteristics of financial systems. Evolution of the theoretical debate on the relationship between financial systems, growth and instability. Legal Area: Institutional architecture of the EU. Regulation of banks and other financial intermediaries. Corporate governance of regulated companies. Responsibility of directors and other company bodies. Quantitative Area: Tools and models for managing financial and insurance risks. Econometric methods relevant to financial applications. Autonomy of judgment. Master's graduates who have successfully completed the course of study must: a) Be able to use the data relevant to the analysis of the main risk profiles of financial intermediaries, to evaluate their management choices and their implications on performance. b) Be able to use micro- and macroeconomic data and qualitative information necessary to carry out scenario and stress analyses. c) Be able to evaluate alternative strategies and formulate policy proposals, taking into account the constraints that may arise in their implementation. To consolidate these skills, the collaboration of highly qualified experts from the banking and financial world is planned. Particular emphasis will also be placed on verifying these skills in the evaluation of the final exam. Learning ability. The methodological training, the specialized knowledge and the required critical skills allow to face subsequent study programs at the level of a second-level university master's degree and a doctoral degree. The same skills and abilities allow to learn independently, and therefore to continue to grow professionally and develop new and/or more advanced skills. These skills are acquired and verified mainly through the specialized content teachings of both the business and economic areas, as well as in the training activity for the preparation of the final exam. Ability to apply knowledge and understanding. Generic Area: Master's graduates who have successfully completed the course of study must: a) Be able to use the most appropriate theoretical approaches and quantitative methods to address the management problems related to financial intermediation, even in a context of change. b) Be able to critically analyze the theoretical contributions and studies developed by the professional world to evaluate their operational implications. c) Ability to apply the acquired skills to the analysis and identification of problems in the general debate areas related to economic and financial disciplines. d) Ability to abstract to identify the essence of a problem and apply general principles to specific cases. These skills will be acquired and verified using, as study material, articles with both theoretical and empirical content, reports by central banks, strategic plans of financial institutions, as well as proposing in class and during the exam exercises on concrete and current cases and problems. Particular emphasis will also be placed on verifying these skills in the evaluation of the final exam. Business Area: Evaluation of the impact of prudential rules and accounting standards and their changes. Comparative analysis of risk-adjusted performance for the different business areas. Compliance risk and effectiveness of governance and internal control processes. Skills in the implementation of methods and models for managing and hedging financial and operational risks. Analysis and measurement of credit risk with the different models proposed by theory and professional practice. Economic Area: Evaluation of macro-prudential policies. Scenario analysis. Econometric tools for the analysis of financial data. Legal Area: Analysis of the process of regulatory convergence in the different jurisdictions. Specificity of financial contracts and implications in terms of risk. Analysis of the mechanisms for managing and resolving financial crises. Quantitative Area: Application of quantitative tools to the analysis of micro- and macroeconomic data. Scenario analysis. Analysis of volatility and correlation for the measurement of risks and the calculation of hedges.

Status: Open.
