Language: English.
Type: Master's Degrees.

Inter-University Course: No.
Open Access: Yes.
Location: Siena.
Department: Department of Economics and Statistics.
Website: https://finance.unisi.it.
Informative Website: https://orientarsi.unisi.it/studio/magistrali/corso/finance.
Admission Requirements:
Mandatory qualifications: [TSS].
Upper Secondary School Diploma.
Optional qualifications (choose from the following): [L2+ CER_LIN+TSS], Bachelor's Degree, Language certification [L1+ CER_LIN +TSS], Bachelor's Degree, Language certification [L2+ OBG+TSS], Bachelor's Degree, Open Badge [L1+ OBG +TSS], Bachelor's Degree, Open Badge [TS +CER_LIN +TSS], Foreign qualification, Language certification [TS +OBG +TSS], Foreign qualification, Open Badge.
Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse.
Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan" www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni.
Roles and Responsibilities: Student mobility, PASQUALE RUGGIERO, President/Chairman of the teaching committee, CLAUDIO PACATI, President, CLAUDIO PACATI.
Program, texts and objectives: Admission requirements: Bachelor's degree in one of the degree classes provided for in the course regulations or an equivalent foreign qualification.
Professional status conferred by the degree: Financial operations assistant, Portfolio manager, Trader in financial markets, Financial risk analysis and management assistant, Risk manager, Financial consultant, Market analyst, Manager in financial intermediaries, Manager in the financial departments of non-financial companies.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam consists of a discussion, before a special degree commission, of a thesis developed in an original way under the guidance of a supervisor. It must verify that the master's graduate has acquired an advanced knowledge of the various profiles of finance, relevant both theoretically and professionally, which allows him/her to develop or apply original ideas through a systematic and critically aware understanding; that he/she has the ability to apply the knowledge acquired and to solve problems on innovative topics, also in an interdisciplinary context; that he/she has the ability to express himself/herself clearly and to argue in writing and orally. The thesis must be written and presented in English.
The merit score awarded for the final exam will take into account, in particular, the level of theoretical depth, the ability demonstrated by the candidate in the use of quantitative methodologies appropriate to the objective of the research, the effectiveness of the empirical analyses carried out, the originality of the arguments proposed and the effectiveness of the discussion. The merit score awarded for the final exam contributes to the formation of the final degree score together with the average of the exams of the study course, according to the criteria defined by a specific Regulation. The degree score is expressed in hundredths. If the sum of the merit score awarded for the final exam and the average score of the exams is greater than 110/110, at the proposal of the supervisor, the degree commission may unanimously award the honors.
Knowledge required for access: To access the Master's Degree course in Finance, the following are required:
• Specific knowledge in economics, business and mathematical-statistical fields. Knowledge in the economic field must also include knowledge of the basics of microeconomics. Knowledge in the business field must also include knowledge of the basic profiles of financial market economics. Knowledge in the mathematical field must also include knowledge of the basic profiles of financial mathematics.
• Basic computer skills.
• Knowledge of the English language at least at level B2.
These skills are also a prerequisite for the admission of foreign students and are part of the student exchange agreements with foreign universities already established and of any future agreements (see Article 6 and 7 of the course regulations in the appendix).
The admission test to verify personal preparation is selective in nature. The subjects, the composition of the examining commission and the criteria for evaluating the test will be defined by the didactic regulations of the study course (see Article 8 and 9 of the course regulations in the appendix).
Graduates in possession of the required curricular requirements who have obtained the degree with a score higher than the minimum threshold defined by the didactic regulations of the study course (Article 10 of the course regulations in the appendix) will be exempt from the admission test.
No specific additional training obligations are foreseen.
Degree awarded: Master's Degree (Second-level Degree) in Finance.
Teaching delivery method: Conventional.
Knowledge and understanding.
General aspects: Master's graduates will have the knowledge necessary to address in a conscious and in-depth way problems of a financial nature, both theoretical and practical. They will be able to choose the theoretical framework, the methodologies and the analytical tools best suited to the purpose, using both qualitative and quantitative approaches.
Master's graduates must demonstrate the ability to deepen and provide original contributions in the financial field through the study of the essential pillars of the course: economics, mathematics/statistics and finance.
Specific knowledge of the quantitative area: Master's graduates will possess knowledge and understanding skills in the field of the main mathematical, statistical and econometric methods and models for the analysis of financial variables, for the valuation of financial contracts, for the measurement and management of financial risks. They will be able to develop and apply original ideas, even in a context of careful study and analysis based on systematic and critically aware understanding.
Specific knowledge of the economic area: Master's graduates must know and understand the main economic themes and phenomena that are the basis of the functioning of financial markets, such as information asymmetries, game theory, contract theory, monetary economics, international economics, emerging markets and sustainable development, behavioral phenomena, moral hazard and adverse selection, the theory of efficient markets. They must know, understand and be able to use appropriate econometric techniques, typically used in the analysis of financial phenomena.
Specific knowledge of the business area: Master's graduates will possess knowledge and understanding skills in the field of the theory and practice of the functioning of financial markets and the main financial instruments, the management of risk also through financial derivative instruments, corporate finance. As a form of in-depth study, they may know and understand adequately further topics, such as the theory and practice of portfolio management, financial engineering, interest rate derivatives, structured finance of insurance, alternative investments, international financial accounting, corporate treasury management.
Specific knowledge of the legal area: Master's graduates will possess knowledge and understanding skills of European and international regulations in the field of solvency of financial intermediaries, stability of the economic system and protection of consumers and investors. As a form of in-depth study, they may know and understand the main techniques of international tax planning and the main tax structures adopted in the countries of the European Union and non-European countries.
Instrumental knowledge: Master's graduates will have knowledge and understanding of the fundamentals of computer programming and information systems in financial companies. They will also have adequate knowledge of the technical English used in international business and financial practice.

UNIVERSITY: University of Siena
COURSE: [GC002] LAW
TYPE: Master's Degree
CURRICULUM: Single-Cycle Program, 5 years
SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unisi.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10031

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 5 years. Program: 2012. Status: Open. Credits: 300 CFU. Language: Italian. Type: Single-cycle Master's Degrees. Regulations: D.M. 270/2004. Degree Class: LMG/01 - Class of Master's Degrees in Law. Inter-University Course: No. Open Access: Yes. Location: Siena. Department: Law. Website: https://giurisprudenza.unisi.it. Informative Website: https://www.unisi.it/didattica/uffici-studenti-e-didattica/sportello-ufficio-servizi-agli-studenti-mattioli. Admission Requirements: Required qualifications: [TSS]. Upper Secondary School Diploma. Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse. Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni. Roles: Study plan approval manager: ALESSANDRO PALMIERI. Teaching committee president: ALESSANDRO PALMIERI. Program, texts and objectives: Admission requirements: Upper secondary school diploma. Professional status conferred by the degree: Legal professions (notary, lawyer); magistracy; State lawyer; positions, with reference to functions characterized by high responsibility, in various fields of social, socio-economic and political activity or in institutions, public administrations, independent authorities, private companies, trade unions, in the field of IT law, in the field of comparative, international and community law (European jurist), as well as in international organizations. Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam consists of a written dissertation on a topic assigned relating to one of the educational activities carried out by the student. The thesis, of a monographic nature, must be discussed before a commission of at least 5 teachers. The evaluation is expressed in 110 tenths, with possible honors. The increase in CFU for degree theses (which is in line with what is provided for by other Law Faculties) derives from the decision to require a greater in-depth study for the final paper. The thesis must therefore result from a broad and reasoned examination of the sources and show a motivated critical analysis of the assigned topic, according to the model of a monographic treatment. This increased work responds to the felt need to further refine the techniques of orderly and systematic written presentation of the legal profiles that characterize every social fact in young jurists. Knowledge required for access: Students in possession of a secondary school diploma or another similar qualification obtained abroad, for which its suitability is recognized, are admitted. Tests are carried out to verify the initial knowledge of the student, which focus on topics of general culture, with particular regard to the principles of the Italian legal system, current political events, modern and contemporary history, aptitude for legal reasoning, vocabulary and the elementary operations of discourse. Participation in these tests is mandatory. Specific remedial courses are organized for students who do not achieve a positive result. Degree awarded: Master's Degree in Law. Teaching delivery method: Conventional. Knowledge and understanding: General description: The five-year training course and the considerable effort required for the preparation of the final thesis will allow the Master's graduate in Law to acquire in-depth knowledge and high levels of professional qualification, in accordance with the challenges constantly posed by the complexity of contemporary legal reality. This knowledge will be mostly acquired through lectures, during which, however, the teacher will take care to encourage discussion and comparison between and among students. The lectures - an essential component of the so-called traditional teaching - will frequently be supplemented by exercises aimed at maximizing the direct involvement and critical learning of each participant. It is planned that the preparation will be verified through optional intermediate tests and a mandatory final test, mostly in oral form. PRIVATE LAW: Within the area of private law learning, the Master's graduate: - possesses in-depth knowledge of the institutions of private law; - knows the civil law institutions in the light of doctrinal and jurisprudential contributions; - has in-depth knowledge of the relevant legal issues relating to agricultural, commercial, industrial and labor law; - knows the European and international aspects relating to the private law area. PUBLIC LAW: The Master's graduate - possesses in-depth knowledge of the system of sources of national law, with particular reference to constitutional norms, the characteristics of the state legal system, the structure and its powers, as well as a high level of knowledge and understanding of the administrative organization in the articulations of the central and local government and the institutions of administrative justice; - also knows the tax discipline in its substantive and procedural dimension. ECONOMIC LAW: The Master's graduate - acquires in-depth knowledge and high levels of understanding regarding institutional elements of political economy (microeconomics and macroeconomics) and introduces the main themes of the economic analysis of law. HISTORICAL-PHILOSOPHICAL-METHODOLOGICAL LAW: The Master's graduate - possesses the historical-philosophical-methodological knowledge necessary for the historical-cultural analysis of contemporary law, with particular reference to the theory of interpretation and the theory of argumentation; - knows the origin and evolution of the legal, anthropological experience and the search for normative constants; - acquires the knowledge necessary to understand in a diachronic perspective the unitary character of the legal experience with respect to the systematic framework and disciplinary segmentation. INTERNATIONAL LAW: The Master's graduate - acquires in-depth knowledge of the institutional profiles of European Union law, also with reference to the relationship between European law and national law; - knows the norms of public and private international law; - knows the instruments for resolving disputes, both of a diplomatic nature and of a judicial and arbitral nature, provided for by international law.

Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]
Upper Secondary School Diploma.
Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse
Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan" www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni
Roles and Responsibilities: Student Mobility, GIOVANNI GIAMBENE, President/Chairman of the teaching committee, MAURO DI MARCO.
Program, texts and objectives: Access to further studies.
Specialist degree, master's degree, first-level master's degree.
Admission requirements: To enroll in a degree course, you must have a high school diploma or an equivalent qualification obtained abroad.
Professional status conferred by the degree: Industrial Engineer.
Main employment opportunities: Companies and organizations operating in various sectors, in particular service companies (such as banks and financial institutions), utilities (energy providers), manufacturing (e.g., the fashion industry), process industries (e.g., pharmaceutical companies), providers of solutions and services related to information technology, and public administration bodies. These areas are of particular interest to the local business area, which is predominantly service-oriented and consists of many medium and small companies, for which rational management of production and information processes is critical to achieving business objectives.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam consists of preparing a written paper on a topic assigned by a lecturer from the Department (referent lecturer) or a description of the activities carried out during the practical training. The aim of the exam is to verify the student's analytical and synthesis skills in relation to the technical description of a work carried out independently. In particular, for students who carry out their practical training in a company or external organization, the final exam consists of preparing a technical report on the activities carried out. In this case, the referent lecturer usually coincides with the university tutor of the practical training.
Knowledge required for admission: To be admitted to the Degree Course, you must have a high school diploma or another qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable. Knowledge of the English language is also required, at least at level A2/2.
Basic knowledge of mathematics and physics is also required, which must normally have been acquired at the end of high school. More specifically, for mathematics, reference can be made to the official document, approved in 2006 by the Joint Commission of the Italian Mathematical Union-Conference of Engineering Deans, which identifies the following as the main prerequisites:
Algebra; operations, powers, approximation; numerical calculation.
Progressions, exponentials, logarithms.
Elements of Trigonometry.
Elements of Euclidean Geometry, geometry of space.
Elements of Analytical Geometry.
Elementary Logic.
For physics, in addition to those already indicated for mathematics, the prerequisites are, indicatively:
Concept of physical quantity, measurements and errors.
Vectors.
Uniform and uniformly accelerated rectilinear motion, uniform circular motion.
Mass and density.
Basic skills in logical reasoning and verbal comprehension are also required.
The knowledge for admission is necessarily verified through an entrance test, which aims to verify the student's personal preparation with respect to knowledge of mathematics, physics and logical reasoning and verbal comprehension skills. If the test is not successful, additional training obligations will be assigned, which must be fulfilled in the first year of the course.
Degree awarded: Degree in Industrial Engineering.
Teaching method: Conventional.
Knowledge and understanding: Scientific and methodological foundations.
At the end of the studies, the graduate has acquired knowledge and skills in understanding the fundamental methodological aspects of the physical-mathematical and computer science disciplines. The acquisition of knowledge and skills is verified through written and/or oral exams.
Economic and management engineering: At the end of the studies, the graduate has acquired knowledge and skills in understanding the approaches, models and tools for analyzing and understanding the organizational, operational and strategic management of companies. The acquisition of knowledge and skills is verified through written and/or oral exams.
Information engineering: At the end of the studies, the graduate has acquired knowledge and skills in understanding the fundamental methodological aspects of some of the main disciplines of information engineering, including electrical engineering, modeling and control of dynamic systems, data management and processing, and telecommunications systems. The acquisition of knowledge and skills is verified through written and/or oral exams.
Contextual knowledge and final exam: At the end of the studies, the graduate has acquired knowledge and skills in understanding specific aspects of business organization and management, with particular reference to the needs of the local area; direct knowledge of organizations and companies through internships or traineeships; knowledge of the English language.
Verification is represented by the internship and the final exam, which allow to evaluate the knowledge and skills acquired by the student, possibly in a business context.
Management of information technologies: At the end of the studies, the graduate has acquired knowledge and skills in understanding the control of systems and industrial automation technologies, electronics and computer networks. The acquisition of knowledge and skills is verified through written and/or oral exams.
Management of business processes: At the end of the studies, the graduate has acquired knowledge and skills in understanding business management, lean management, plant management, management and regulation of environmental services, mechanical systems. The acquisition of knowledge and skills is verified through written and/or oral exams.

Professional status conferred by the degree: Engineer in the field of information engineering

The acquisition of knowledge and skills is verified through written and/or oral exams.

COURSE: [LG004] HISTORY OF ART
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year.

Program: 2022
Status: Open
Credits: 120 CFU
Language: Italian
Type: Master's Degrees
Regulation: D.M. 270/2004
Degree Class: LM-89 - Class of Master's Degrees in History of Art
Inter-University Course: No
Open Access: Yes
Location: Siena
Department: Department of Historical and Cultural Heritage Sciences

Admission Requirements
Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]
Upper Secondary School Diploma
Optional qualifications (choose from the following): [TS + TSS]
Foreign qualification: [L2 cons+TSS]
First-Level Degree: [L1 cons+TSS]
Fees and contributions. For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse
Dates and deadlines. For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan" www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni
Roles and Responsibilities: Student Mobility, LUCA QUATTROCCHI, President/Chairman of the teaching committee, LUCA QUATTROCCHI
Program, texts and objectives. Admission requirements:
- having obtained a degree in one of the following classes: L-1 (Cultural Heritage), L-3 (Disciplines of visual arts, music, performing arts and fashion), L-43 (Technologies for the conservation and restoration of cultural heritage) according to D.M. 270/2005; classes 13 (Cultural Heritage Sciences), 23 (Visual Arts, Music, Performing Arts and Fashion Sciences and Technologies), 41 (Technologies for the conservation and restoration of cultural heritage) according to D.M. 590/1999;
- possessing the curricular requirements;
- knowledge of the English language;
- computer skills
Professional status conferred by the degree.
Art Historian. Master's graduates in Art History can find employment opportunities in all public and private bodies and companies engaged in the research, protection, conservation, management and enhancement of historical and artistic heritage. In particular:
- public bodies, state or local, such as superintendencies, museums and art galleries, archives; or in private bodies such as foundations, museums, collections
- publishing houses specializing in art publishing
- auction houses and companies operating in the field of antiques and the art market
- associations and cooperatives that offer services in the field of research and documentation, training, cultural tourism.
Furthermore, Master's graduates in Art History can:
- as required by current legislation, participate in the entrance exams for training courses for teaching Art History in secondary schools
- participate, if they have a sufficient number of credits in appropriate subject areas, as required by current legislation, in the entrance exams for training courses for teaching other subjects in secondary schools in addition to Art History
- access, after passing an entrance exam, the postgraduate training of the specific Schools of Specialization in Historical and Artistic Heritage for management careers in the Ministry of Cultural Heritage and Activities
- access, after passing an entrance exam, the postgraduate training represented by Doctoral Programs
Characteristics of the final exam. The Master's thesis represents a formative moment of absolute importance in the student's career, during which he/she has the opportunity to make the most of the knowledge and skills acquired in his/her field of study, as well as the critical and methodological skills developed. The topic of the Master's thesis, to be identified and developed under the guidance of the supervisor, should preferably be partially or totally original, should offer opportunities for research of both a bibliographic and archival nature, and should be able to be configured as an innovative contribution to studies in the field and, possibly, present operational implications within the disciplinary area.
In the drafting of the thesis, the candidate must demonstrate confidence in bibliographic orientation (also in foreign languages) and ability in archival research, where this is, as is desirable, necessary; the secure and precise use of the scientific terminology of the sector in which the topic of the thesis falls; the ability to elaborate autonomous critical thinking; the ability to manage any interdisciplinary connections. Naturally, the candidate must be able to argue and present the results of his/her research in a clear and articulate manner, both in written and oral form.
The Master's thesis cannot be written in a foreign language.
The final exam is worth 27 credits; and the final merit score (degree grade) will take into account, based on the student's curriculum, the quality and novelty of the thesis work in its various methodological, historical and critical implications. The final merit score will be expressed in hundredths, with possible honors.
See the Regulations of the final exam for Master's degree courses approved by the Department.
Knowledge required for admission. To be admitted to the Master's degree course in Art History, applicants must hold a Bachelor's or three-year University Diploma, or another qualification obtained abroad and deemed suitable.
It is also necessary for the student to possess specific curricular requirements and adequate personal preparation.
The curricular requirements are met by holding a degree in one of the following classes (or another equivalent qualification obtained abroad): L-1 (Cultural Heritage), L-3 (Disciplines of visual arts, music, performing arts and fashion), L-43 (Technologies for the conservation and restoration of cultural heritage) according to D.M. 270/2005; classes 13 (Cultural Heritage Sciences), 23 (Visual Arts, Music, Performing Arts and Fashion Sciences and Technologies), 41 (Technologies for the conservation and restoration of cultural heritage) according to D.M. 509/1999.
Graduates in the listed classes must also have obtained certain credits in the following SSD groups:
- L-ART/01, L-ART/02, L-ART/03, L-ART/04, ICAR/15, ICAR/16, ICAR/18
- L-FIL-LET/01, L-FIL-LET/02, L-FIL-LET/03, L-FIL-LET/04, L-FIL-LET/05, L-FIL-LET/06, L-FIL-LET/07, L-FIL-LET/08, L-FIL-LET/09, L-FIL-LET/10, L-FIL-LET/11, L-FIL-LET/12, L-FIL-LET/13, L-FIL-LET/14, L-FIL-LET/15
- M-STO/01, M-STO/02, M-STO/03, M-STO/04, M-STO/05, M-STO/06, M-STO/07, M-STO/08, M-STO/09
The specific curricular requirements are defined in the Educational Regulations of the Master's Degree Course.
For graduates with qualifications other than those indicated above, specific curricular requirements are required, as defined in the Educational Regulations of the Master's Degree Course.
Personal preparation is verified through an interview to which only students who meet the curricular requirements can access. The methods for verifying personal preparation are defined in the Educational Regulations of the Master's Degree Course.
Degree awarded.
Master's Degree in Art History. Teaching and assessment methods.
Conventional. Knowledge and understanding.
Generic Area. Master's graduates must have acquired the ability to understand at a post-secondary level, developed through the study and critical comparison of specialized texts, also in foreign languages, and be able to internalize the inseparable dialectical relationship between the history of art (and other humanities) and the history of culture in general. In particular, textbooks will be chosen according to their high scientific level, the updating of the state of research, the transparency of content and the methodological effectiveness, but also taking into account an increasing degree of problematicity, which students must be able to face with precise awareness and understanding based on the knowledge acquired with the Bachelor's degree and during the specialist courses taught in the Master's degree course.
These knowledge and skills will enable them to face with sufficient confidence even unpublished or little-studied topics, with a clear methodological approach and a rapid ability to orient themselves in vast bibliographies. The acquisition of this knowledge and skills will be verified during the training process through seminars, written and oral exams, reports.
Historical-artistic area. High level of knowledge and understanding for the areas of:
History of art, History of architecture, History and methodologies of conservation, Art literature, History of art criticism. Historical area. Good level of knowledge and understanding for the areas of:
History, Archival studies. Theatrical, musical, film areas. Good level of knowledge and understanding for the areas of:
Theater, Music, Cinema. Autonomy of judgment.
At the end of the Master's degree course, the graduate must have the ability to formulate, based on the knowledge acquired, the study of existing bibliography (updated and international) and a solid and correct methodological approach, an autonomous judgment regarding the topics, authors or works that will be the subject of his/her research. To encourage and stimulate personal reflections, the training process seeks to provide the most comprehensive and diversified overview possible of the different critical positions on the topics covered in the courses, highlighting the strengths and weaknesses of each; as well as the seminar aspect of many segments of the training process, and the intervention of external teachers who can usefully contribute to the in-depth comparison of different assessments, should allow the student to develop his/her ability to articulate autonomous judgments anchored to an objectively verifiable context. The inclusion in the training process of artistic and architectural production in the broader contemporary historical and cultural context (with references to literature, music, theater, cinema) also stimulates the student to establish connections, influences and dependencies between the different creative expressions.
The achievement of autonomy of judgment takes place through the teaching activities carried out by the course, seminars, exercises. The acquisition of this ability will be verified during the training process through exercises, seminars, written and oral exams, reports, and must be fully expressed in the Master's thesis.
Ability to learn.
Master's graduates must be able to face with confidence and, above all, with clear awareness, any continuation of their studies. The training process, moreover, is structured according to a gradual and progressive increase in the complexity of the topics addressed, with a parallel increase in the request and verification of the ability to learn and the ability to critically assess, up to the Master's thesis, which is a fundamental opportunity to effectively and autonomously apply the skills and knowledge acquired. The knowledge of foreign languages, the ability to navigate confidently in a vast and articulated bibliography or database, the full mastery of methodological tools, the enrichment derived from the internship period, the solid autonomy of judgment, are all essential for undertaking further studies after the Master's degree, and as such represent a constant reference, like a framework, of the entire training process of the course.
The ability to learn is acquired through the teaching activities carried out by the course, study seminars and exercises, the internship activity, and is continuously verified in the training process through oral exams, written reports, seminars, reports from the host organizations regarding the internship; and finds in the preparation, drafting and discussion of the Master's thesis a fundamental moment of overall verification.
Ability to apply knowledge and understanding.
Generic Area. Master's graduates must have acquired the ability to apply knowledge and understanding in the various relevant occupational sectors, demonstrating that they are able to turn "knowledge" into "know-how", even in multidisciplinary and international contexts. To this end, the training process carried out during the Master's degree course is never separated from concrete examples and exercises in the application of theoretical knowledge, which in the specific sector of art history may relate to the design and organization of an exhibition (with the issues related to exhibition spaces, the loan and movement of works, insurance values, the press office), the structuring of the scientific catalog, the drafting of the work sheets; or to the design, organization and management of a study day; or, again, to the methods of cataloging works and related documents. The interactive nature of these activities will allow to verify in progress the ability to apply the acquired knowledge.
Furthermore, the students' confrontation with representatives of the world of work is a useful and effective element to increase awareness of the inseparable link that exists in the various fields of historical and artistic heritage between history, theory and practice and between individual knowledge and interaction with the environment. Particularly significant in this respect are the laboratory activities and the internship activities, which always take place in institutions (public or private) whose activity is closely related to the field of study of the course (museums, superintendencies, cultural centers and associations, private galleries, cultural foundations, etc.).
Historical-artistic area. High ability to apply knowledge and understanding in relation to:
Reconstruction of the historical events of artistic and architectural artifacts, Reconstruction of the critical events of artistic and architectural artifacts, Completeness of bibliographic research, Sound and original critical analysis. Historical area. High ability to apply knowledge and understanding in relation to:
Insertion of artistic events in the context of historical events, Conducting archival research. Theatrical, musical, film areas. Good ability to apply knowledge and understanding in relation to:
Insertion of artistic events in the context of those related to theater, music, cinema. Communication skills.
The Master's graduate must be able to communicate effectively the knowledge acquired during his/her studies, as well as to make the acquired skills easily shareable, both in written and oral form. He/she must also be able to dose and differentiate his/her level of communication: depending on whether the interlocutors are specialists in the field of art history, or a more general and non-specialist audience, in a similar way to what he/she will presumably have to face in the workplace (from speaking at a scientific conference to a guided tour for schools, from a scientific essay to a catalog card to a popular text). To stimulate and verify communication skills, many of the courses taught include seminar-style exercises, as well as an oral exam, in which the student is required to use the scientific terminology of the sector in a conscious manner.
To accustom the student to interact with an audience and to support his/her positions, students organize study seminars every year, with the supervision and coordination of the teachers, which last for a day, consisting of brief presentations by the students on agreed-upon topics on specific moments in the history of art, and the subsequent discussion. This is a very useful exercise not only to refine communication skills, but also to learn to use the time available effectively (the same for everyone) to illustrate, in a clear and concise manner, the chosen topic. In addition, both in the preparatory work and on the day of the presentations, these seminars are of great importance in stimulating students to interact and work in groups, with a view to achieving an optimal collective result.
The verification of communication skills takes place during the training process: oral exams, written reports, seminars, discussion of the final exam.
Expected employment and professional opportunities for graduates.
Art Historian. Master's graduates in Art History can find employment opportunities in all public and private bodies and companies engaged in the research, protection, conservation, management and enhancement of historical and artistic heritage. In particular:
- public bodies, state or local, such as superintendencies, museums and art galleries, archives; or in private bodies such as foundations, museums, collections
- publishing houses specializing in art publishing
- auction houses and companies operating in the field of antiques and the art market
- associations and cooperatives that offer services in the field of research and documentation, training, cultural tourism. Furthermore, Master's graduates in Art History can:
- as required by current legislation, participate in the entrance exams for training courses for teaching Art History in secondary schools
- participate, if they have a sufficient number of credits in appropriate subject areas, as required by current legislation, in the entrance exams for training courses for teaching other subjects in secondary schools in addition to Art History
- access, after passing an entrance exam, the postgraduate training of the specific Schools of Specialization in Historical and Artistic Heritage for management careers in the Ministry of Cultural Heritage and Activities
- access, after passing an entrance exam, the postgraduate training represented by Doctoral Programs.
Language/s of instruction and assessment.
ITALIAN. Skills related to the function.
Art Historian. The Master's graduate in Art History must possess:
- advanced historical and methodological skills applied to the research and critical analysis of artistic and architectural heritage from the Middle Ages to the present day
- high skills in carrying out and managing bibliographic, archival and iconographic research
- knowledge of a museological and museographic nature, both in a historical perspective and in their contemporary developments
- theoretical and applied knowledge relating to the history and methodologies of conservation of artistic artifacts
- knowledge of the disciplinary terminology in the historical-artistic, architectural, museological, protection and conservation fields
- high ability to express oneself in writing and orally, both at a scientific and popular level
- ability to work in a team and to interact with different professionals
- ability to design and direct an exhibition event of a historical-artistic nature
- good knowledge of the English language, and possibly other languages of the European Union.
Description of admission tests.
Test to verify the student's personal preparation
1. The test to verify the student's personal preparation takes place in oral form, through an interview with three teachers of the Master's Degree course (belonging to the SSDs L-ART/01, L-ART/02 and L-ART/03 and appointed annually by the Teaching Committee) aimed at assessing the candidate's knowledge of the artistic and architectural manifestations of the Middle Ages, the modern age and the contemporary age.
2. The test will be considered passed if the candidate demonstrates a good ability to orient himself chronologically and stylistically and a sufficient ability to critically read the works.
3. The test may be held in one or more sessions. If more than one session is scheduled, those who have not been admitted to the first may reapply for the second.
4. Graduates who meet the curricular requirements referred to in the previous art. 7, paragraphs 1, 2 and 5, and students in the final year of study belonging to the courses of study referred to in the previous art. 7, paragraph 1, who have obtained, by the date of the test, at least 120 total credits, including all those referred to in the previous art. 7, paragraph 1, letters b), c), d), may participate in the test.
5. Students who have passed the verification test will be admitted with reservation and may enroll provided that they obtain the degree within the deadlines for the closing of enrollments.
Art. 10 - Direct admission
1. Graduates who meet the curricular requirements referred to in Article 7, paragraph 1, and who have obtained the degree with a grade of not less than 100/110, or who, although having obtained a lower grade, have obtained a weighted average of not less than 27/30 for the credits provided for in their study plan in the following SSDs: L-ART/01, L-ART/02, L-ART/03, L-ART/04, ICAR/15, ICAR/16, ICAR/18, are exempt from the verification test.
2. Graduates in the Master's degree courses referred to in Article 7, paragraph 4, who meet the curricular requirements are also exempt from the admission test.
3. Graduates who, although entitled to participate in the test pursuant to Article 9, paragraph 3, have, by the date of the test, already passed all the courses provided for in their study plan in the SSDs: L-ART/01, L-ART/02, L-ART/03, L-ART/04, ICAR/15, ICAR/16, ICAR/18, with a weighted average of not less than 27/30, will also be exempt from the test. In the absence of this requirement, students who intend to graduate within the deadline for the closing of enrollments may choose to take the test, without prejudice to the fact that, regardless of the outcome of the test, they will be admitted if the grade obtained within the deadlines is not less than 100/110.
Function in the work context.
Art Historian. The Master's degree course in Art History trains highly skilled professionals who can access functions and roles as art historians in public and private institutions that deal with cultural heritage, both in relation to its study, conservation and protection, and in relation to its management and enhancement.
In particular, they can:
- carry out activities as an official or curator/conservator in public bodies, state or local, such as superintendencies, museums and art galleries, archives; or in private bodies such as foundations, museums, collections
- carry out activities of writing texts, iconographic research and cultural promotion in publishing houses specializing in art publishing
- carry out activities of cataloging, cataloging and archiving in the historical-artistic sector in public or private bodies
- carry out activities of design and realization of exhibitions, events and cultural events, museum and exhibition itineraries, and related educational activities
- carry out research and promotion activities in the field of the art market and auction houses
- carry out autonomous activities within associations and cooperatives that offer services in the field of research and documentation, training, cultural tourism
- offer consultancy in the historical-artistic field and carry out appraisals and expert opinions.
Finally, for graduates who wish to acquire further skills and achieve higher levels of responsibility, the training process can continue in the Schools of Specialization, in the Second-Level Master's Degrees, in the Doctoral Program.
Additional information.
http://www.unisi.it/dipartimenti/dipartimento-scienze-storiche-e-dei-beni-culturali
Description of specific training objectives.
The specific training objectives of the Master's degree course in Art History are based on two main lines, which take into account the high professional skills required by the institutions, both public and private, currently operating in the cultural field. Primarily, the course aims to provide a solid training in the historical-artistic field according to a progressive deepening of specialization: in fact, the course is characterized and distinguished, compared to similar courses at the national level, by the possibility offered to the student to choose one of the three areas in which the discipline is articulated, namely Medieval Art, Modern Art and Contemporary Art. Secondly, the course also includes more operational aspects than the historical-critical ones, which are, however, closely related, relating to the field of contemporary museums and the protection and conservation of artistic heritage, according to an approach capable of bringing students into contact with specific working contexts.
In the first year, the training objectives focus on a solid preparation with respect to the artistic and architectural manifestations from the early Middle Ages to the present day and their critical analysis (historical-artistic learning area, 24 credits), which, based on the knowledge acquired in the Bachelor's degree, can be further deepened in terms of the topics covered. Also in the first year, and following the area chosen by the student (Medieval Art, Modern Art or Contemporary Art), these deepening will be accompanied by an indispensable knowledge of the contemporary historical events, without which a full understanding of the motivations, genesis and outcomes of artistic and architectural production would be impossible (historical learning area, 12 credits). Among the characterizing courses, archival studies and paleography are also included, which for students specializing in medieval and modern art represent a fundamental aid (historical learning area, 6 credits); while among the optional courses there are courses on theater, music, cinema, which can effectively contribute to establishing connections and parallels with contemporary artistic production (theatrical, musical, film learning areas, 12 credits).
Also in the first year, a course focused on contemporary museography is included, which represents a first approach to one of the possible employment opportunities for Master's graduates in Art History, namely museums. The knowledge of the historical and contemporary methods of "presenting" cultural heritage in its museographic and museological components aims to develop in the student a critical awareness of the fundamental operation of exhibition choices and to provide him/her with the basic tools to deal with the complex work in the museum field.
The area chosen by the student in the first year finds its full application in the second year of the course, as it is dedicated exclusively to specialized historical-artistic courses differentiated according to the three areas that characterize the course (18 credits). The possibility of deepening knowledge and methodological issues, together with an increasing awareness and a greater capacity for autonomous judgment, characterize all the courses in the second year.
In this perspective, the 6 credits reserved for internship activities or other knowledge useful for entering the world of work should also be considered, as well as an operational comparison with the real professional context in the field of knowledge, management, protection and enhancement of cultural heritage.
Field trips and visits to exhibitions, museums, urban and architectural complexes, restoration sites, environmental art installations in both urban and natural contexts are an integral part of the training process and represent, for all the courses, a fundamental moment to bring students into direct contact with artistic and architectural artifacts and with the issues related to their knowledge, protection and management, and to develop their ability to critically read.
Within the structures of the Department, the "Giovanni Previtali" Photographic Archive (rich in over 50,000 photographs of works of art) and the Laboratory of informatics applied to iconography and iconology are available to students during the entire period of the study course, and can effectively integrate the training process both in the preparation of exams and in the research for written works or for the thesis.
The Master's thesis, which plays a fundamental role in the entire training process, is assigned 27 credits.
Thanks to its structure and content, the course allows access, after passing an entrance exam, to postgraduate training courses, such as the Doctoral Program in Art History or the School of Specialization in Historical and Artistic Heritage (both active at the University).

Location: Siena. Department: Department of Social, Political and Cognitive Sciences. Website: http://www.dispoc.unisi.it. Informative website: http://web.lett.unisi.it/os/facolta/.

Roles: President/Chairman of the teaching committee: ARMANDO CUTOLO. Program, texts and objectives: Access to further studies. Specialization courses, Research Doctorate courses, Second-level University Master's courses. Admission requirements: - having obtained a degree in one of the following classes: L-1, L-3, L-5, L-6, L10, L-11, L-12, L-15, L-19, L-20, L-24, L-36, L-37, L-39, L-40, L-42, L-43 according to D.M. 270/2004 or in one of the following degree classes according to D.M. 509/1999: 3, 5, 6, 11, 13, 14, 15, 18, 23, 29, 30, 34, 35, 36, 38, 39, 41. - possessing the curricular requirements. - knowledge of the English language. Professional status conferred by the degree. Cultural anthropologist. Employment opportunities are certainly within a highly specialized area which, at present, appears to be constantly growing, albeit in the forms of flexibility and portability of skills that make the overall profile of these graduates difficult to measure on a quantitative statistical level. The follow-up of graduates in previous specialist degrees and in the interdisciplinary degree from which the current course originated, allows us to appreciate a wide range of employment opportunities in the medium term, from which the following opportunities can be extrapolated.
Master's graduates, according to the individual in-depth path pursued, may apply for positions of high professional content in public bodies and in private institutions operating in the field of cultural heritage, artistic heritage, museum and archival management, protection of demo-ethno-anthropological assets, enhancement of traditional knowledge and expressive skills. Master's graduates who have delved into the anthropology of image and spectacle may, alternatively, access the employment areas of expressive design, artistic production and various creative and entertainment activities.
Master's graduates may also take on positions of high professional level in public bodies, volunteer and development organizations, governmental and non-governmental, where they can contribute with the cognitive methods and the critical tools of the multidisciplinary training that will constitute their professional baggage.
The Master's degree also opens up the field of research, consisting both of public university research and that supported by foundations and private bodies.
For graduates in the LM-1 Master's degree class, the field of volunteering and public intervention in the social sphere is also open, as far as concerns bodies that are interested in phenomena such as migration flows and policies of reception and control of processes of exclusion (racism, gender discrimination) and dependence (forms of access to work, to political representation). Institutions that oversee the positive processes of enhancing phenomena of hybridization (school integration, neighborhood integration), affirmation of new social subjects and new forms of family and collective life, and enhancement of the communicative and expressive heritage of popular and grassroots culture will also be interested in these skills.
In summary, professional opportunities can be identified in cultural and research institutions (foundations, private institutes and universities), as well as in organizations (governmental and non-governmental) for cooperation, in social and cultural volunteer associations, in scientific journalism, in publishing, in cultural tourism, in territorial promotion bodies, in expressive design both in the social field and in the cultural industry. The flexibility of the proposed training is only partially represented in the codification of professional opportunities as provided for by the Istat codes. The following summary should therefore be considered as a generic and incomplete survey of an area of professional specialization that is distributed across several areas, from information to the cultural market, from cooperation to development and local authorities. The Master's degree program offered allows access to research doctorates specific to the anthropological area and to the areas of interdisciplinary intersection (semiotics, theater studies, cognitive psychology) and therefore to aspire to a future access to the roles of University Professor, within the humanities, as well as, based on previous experience, to positions of Director of a cultural organization or Director of a humanitarian organization.
Characteristics of the final exam. The final exam consists of the preparation of a written paper (thesis) based on a research experience or, alternatively, on an adequate bibliographic and documentary survey. For those who have specialized in other expressive/communicative modalities, the written paper assumes the function of supporting the audiovisual, hypertextual or multimedia product which will be considered as the main paper, to be considered primarily for the purposes of the final evaluation.
The preparation of the Master's thesis may in any case involve the adoption of documentary procedures of various kinds (audiovisual, hypertextual, digital, etc.) and must be the result of an autonomous and individual research; to this end, if the research initiative develops into a larger project, the personal contribution must be clearly recognizable. The process of defining the object, the methodological options, the updating of the bibliography and the analytical deepening, will constitute an integral part of the elaboration of the final product, which will be proposed for evaluation by the Master's degree examination board. The final exam must therefore demonstrate that the student has acquired autonomous skills in the design and implementation of original research; that he/she can rely on analytical and theoretical-methodological synthesis skills; completeness of the investigation; originality in the conclusions. The Master's thesis must also demonstrate skills and expertise in accessing and using international bibliography, also with extensive use of materials in foreign languages.
These elements - solidity of the theoretical framework, exhaustiveness of the comparison with the existing and relevant scientific literature, methodological rigor in both the ethnographic and the editorial phase, originality of approach and innovation of the contribution - together with the oral presentation and discussion of one's paper with the members of the examination board, contribute to the formulation of the final grade.
The final exam is carried out according to the procedure of presenting the research, with the contribution of the tutor and thesis supervisor, by the candidate, followed by the discussion introduced by the report of the co-supervisor and open to the interventions of the other members of the examination board. The projection/exhibition of final audiovisual, multimedia, hypertextual or other expressive/communicative products is allowed, provided that they can be unequivocally attributed to the work of the candidate.
The evaluation of the thesis paper (and of the exam) is added to the admission grade, which is the weighted average of the profit grades, calculated to the thousandth and related to the traditional base of one hundred and ten. Additional grades are available to the Master's degree examination board, in the amount already defined by the Faculty of Letters and Philosophy, and now under consideration by the Department of Social, Political and Cognitive Sciences. Of the five grades currently available, one is awarded automatically if there is an experience of study abroad within the University's internationalization programs.
A total of 27 credits are awarded for the Master's thesis.
Knowledge required for admission. The knowledge required for admission to the Master's degree consists of a solid general basis in the humanities (anthropological, sociological, historical, philosophical, literary), certified by the possession of a degree (referable to one of the following degree classes: L-1, L-3, L-5, L-6, L10, L-11, L-12, L-15, L-19, L-20, L-24, L-36, L-37, L-39, L-40, L-42, L-43 according to D.M. 270/2004); a core of skills amounting to at least 42 CFU, in the set of disciplinary groupings that characterize the training project (courses in artistic, philosophical, psycho-pedagogical, oriental, historical, geographical, demographic, economic and sociological disciplines, as well as, of course, courses in the anthropological field); knowledge of the English language is required, as a basic resource to cope with the extensive use of bibliographies and international studies, and in anticipation of achieving the B2 level as a requirement for graduation; basic computer skills are required, documented. Other more technical measures are defined in detail in the didactic regulations of the course, together with the criteria for evaluating curricular credits derived from master's degrees or other master's degrees, and the methods for verifying skills in cases where the average of profit grades is below the threshold of 27/30.
The test to verify personal preparation takes place in oral form, through an interview with a commission composed of three teachers of the Master's Degree course, appointed annually by the Teaching Committee, aimed at exploring the candidate's knowledge in relation to the chosen field of study and the congruence of his/her training project with the training path offered. The test will be considered passed if the candidate demonstrates a good ability to orient himself/herself in the theoretical-methodological themes and in the main turning points of the history of studies, as well as a sufficient ability to identify and frame, theoretically and methodologically, an area of research within which he/she has started or intends to develop a personal specialization.
Degree awarded. Master's Degree in Anthropology and Languages of the Image. Mode of delivery: Conventional. Knowledge and understanding.
Generic area. Having acquired skills of a higher level than the university training of the first cycle, and having put them to the test by completing a personal research project, aimed at the Master's thesis, the Master's graduates will be able to act in a theoretical and methodological way in the anthropological/ethnographic, semiotic and theory of languages, theory and history of spectacle and visual and audiovisual representation.
In particular, Master's graduates must be able to design and carry out original research, based on solid methodological skills in ethnography/semiotics/sociology and history, to carry out studies in the areas of:
. cultural representation;
. anthropology of the contemporary world;
. languages of spectacle and visual representation.
The graduates of the Master's course must master the fundamental tools relating to the intersection of the disciplinary areas that converge on the anthropological theoretical/methodological axis (semiotics, theater studies, cognitive psychology), as well as having developed research and in-depth experience to produce original research, in full critical and scientific autonomy, and to orient themselves in the professional field among the technical and specialized needs of the labor market in relation to the knowledge of cultural systems, their representative practices, cultural mediation and the management of cultural heritage.
This objective can be achieved through personal study and through a teaching that will alternate traditional lectures with active seminar and laboratory practice, particularly aimed at intertwining different knowledge and methodologies, in order to concretely show the student in full training phase what intertwining of skills and points of view constitutes the characterizing nucleus of this project.
The assessments of learning planned during the course will take place in written and oral form, but also through the preparation of personal research and applications preparatory to the demanding work of the final thesis, in which the multiple methodologies acquired are synthesized.
The Master's degree, in connection with doctoral schools, with public institutions in the city and the territory, with subjects of cultural enterprise, aims to establish itself as a hub for promoting meetings of study and communication of the most advanced scientific and didactic methodologies, and particular emphasis will be given to the international experience of students, through specific collaboration protocols with other universities and other bodies and foundations engaged in scientific training, such as the École de Hautes Études en Sciences Sociales (EHESS), the London School of Economics, the University of Toronto, International School of Theatre Anthropology (LISTA) of Holstebro DK, The University of Eurasian Theatre, Istituto Italiano di Scienze Umane (SUM), Centro Interdisciplinare per lo Studio delle Arti (CISA) of Bucharest, University of Arts and Design of Taik (Finland), Centro di Semiotica delle Arti Visive of Puebla, University Paris VIII - S.Denis, Complutense University of Madrid.

Program, texts and objectives Access to further studies.
Research Doctorate, Doctoral School, Second-Level Master's Degree
Admission requirements:
- having obtained a degree in one of the following classes: ex DM 270/04: L-1 (Cultural Heritage), L-3 (Disciplines of visual arts, music, performing arts and fashion), L-10 (Letters), L-43 (Technologies for the conservation and restoration of cultural heritage); or in one of the following degree classes ex D.M. 509/99: 13 (Sciences of cultural heritage); 23 (Sciences and technologies of visual arts, music, performing arts and fashion), 5 (Letters), 41 (Technologies for the conservation and restoration of cultural heritage)
- possessing the curricular requirements;
- knowledge of the English language;
- computer skills
Professional status conferred by the degree.
Archaeologist. The Master's Degree in Archaeology offers employment opportunities in:
- Institutions and public bodies responsible for excavation, conservation and enhancement of archaeological heritage, such as Superintendencies, Museums and Archaeological Parks;
- Public and private institutions that deal with the study, research and dissemination of archaeological heritage, such as local authorities and foundations;
- Companies, cooperatives or specialized companies in consulting and services for Archaeological Assets.
It should be noted that for some positions of greater responsibility (for example, at the Superintendencies, management positions in the MIBACT, management of Archaeological Museums, research institutions, etc.), the acquisition of further postgraduate qualifications (Research Doctorates, Specialist Schools, Master's Degrees) is required.
Characteristics of the final exam The final exam consists of a public discussion, before a commission composed of a number of teachers indicated in the University and Department Regulations, of a thesis, which is the product of original research and the result of an organic personal re-elaboration of knowledge acquired during the training course.
The thesis and its discussion must demonstrate the candidate's ability to use autonomously the specific sources of his/her field of study, the relevant research tools and the set of knowledge resources necessary to develop an original product that constitutes a significant advancement for knowledge in the investigated sector. They must also demonstrate the candidate's ability to present clearly and effectively and to adequately argue the results of his/her research work.
The thesis work will be carried out under the supervision of one of the teachers of the Course and will be discussed with the participation of a co-supervisor.
The evaluation of the final exam will be expressed in hundredths with possible honors. In addition to considering the average of the grades obtained during the course of study, the merit score is awarded mainly on the basis of the methodological rigor and originality of the research work carried out.
Knowledge required for access Admission to the Master's Degree Course in Archaeology is open to candidates who hold a Degree or a University Diploma of three years' duration or another qualification obtained abroad and deemed suitable.
It is also necessary that the student possesses specific curricular requirements and adequate personal preparation, including knowledge of the English language at level B1.
The curricular requirements are met by holding a degree in one of the following degree classes (ex DM 270/2004 or ex DM 509/1999 or other equivalent qualification obtained abroad):
Cultural Heritage (L-1);
Letters (L-10);
Technologies for the conservation and restoration of cultural heritage (L-43);
Letters (5);
Sciences of cultural heritage (13);
Technologies for the conservation and restoration of cultural heritage (41).
Graduates in the listed classes must also have obtained at least 54 CFU in the following scientific-disciplinary sectors: BIO/08, ICAR/19, INF/01, L-ANT/01, L-ANT/02, L-ANT/03, L-ANT/04, L-ANT/05, L-ANT/06, L-ANT/07, L-ANT/08, L-ANT/09, L-ANT/10, L-ART/04, L-FIL-LET/01, L-OR/05, M-DEA/01, M-GGR/01, M-STO/01; basic computer skills equivalent to ECDL core are also required.
The curricular requirements are also met for graduates in degree classes other than those previously listed, provided that they have obtained at least 60 CFU in the following scientific-disciplinary sectors: BIO/08, ICAR/19, INF/01, L-ANT/01, L-ANT/02, L-ANT/03, L-ANT/04, L-ANT/05, L-ANT/06, L-ANT/07, L-ANT/08, L-ANT/09, L-ANT/10, L-ART/04, L-FIL-LET/01, L-OR/05, M-DEA/01, M-GGR/01, M-STO/01; basic computer skills equivalent to ECDL core are also required.
Personal preparation is then verified through an interview, to which only students who meet the curricular requirements can access. The methods of verifying personal preparation are defined in the Didactic Regulations of the Master's Degree Course.
Degree awarded: Master's Degree in Archaeology
Method of teaching: Conventional
Knowledge and understanding.
General Area The training course for students in the Master's Degree Course aims at building and developing a solid professional preparation, through the provision of lessons dedicated to deepening the procedures and purposes of research, protection, conservation and enhancement of archaeological heritage. The training space allowed by the two-year period will allow the student to identify a Study Plan whose contents are proposed within aggregations of disciplines created with logical sense, identifiable in chronological order in the three macro-themes of Prehistoric Archaeology, Classical Archaeology and Medieval Archaeology. The student will also be guided in the construction of his/her Study Plan to achieve a greater level of in-depth study in the area of his/her interest, subject to approval by the Teaching Committee of the Master's Degree Course in Archaeology.
Based on these design lines, the Course offers a course structured in two years:
Year 1 The student will be able to acquire a solid cultural base on the archaeologies of the Mediterranean and European area in relation to the themes of Prehistoric and Protohistoric Archaeology, Classical Archaeology and Medieval Archaeology. Some in-depth studies will concern: historical, philological and demo-ethno-anthropological disciplines; the museumization and management of archaeological heritage; the most advanced methodologies applied to landscape archaeology, excavation techniques, the reading and interpretation of archaeological contexts and the most modern archaeometric technologies.
Year 2 Specific in-depth studies are planned, providing the student with further historical, archaeological, computer and legislative skills in the archaeological sector. In the second year, the student has the opportunity to deepen the experience in the field, through participation in excavations, experimental research projects, internships and training internships, together with the acquisition of linguistic skills and the preparation of a final exam, preparatory to the award of the Master's Degree.
Methodology and understanding of archaeological sources Traditional tools of investigation and study and new methodologies and technologies
Archaeological and monumental heritage Elaborate data and build interpretative models
Autonomy of judgment.
Graduates in the Master's Degree course will acquire a secure capacity for autonomous judgment which will be expressed, in particular in:
* use of the knowledge, skills, abilities and capacities acquired during the course of study to express autonomous, articulated, motivated and original judgments on specific problems within the disciplines and the area of study of interest;
* ability to manage in a conscious and critical way the diversity of possible interdisciplinary and multidisciplinary approaches to build new research themes and new ways of conducting the research itself;
* ability to confidently navigate within the theoretical and methodological reflection relating to the area of study of interest;
* ability to critically treat different systems of sources in order to preliminarily evaluate their overall reliability and usability for research purposes, also with reference to the qualitative and quantitative aspects of the statistical sample they represent;
* ability to evaluate and to express the social value of scientific research on the past of man understood in the broadest and most articulated sense of the term.
Teaching methods and tools for achieving objectives:
Specific lessons or specific segments within the lessons of the curriculum dedicated to the theoretical-methodological aspects of the different disciplines and to the issues of interoperability between related disciplinary areas.
Verification methods:
Evaluation of the maturity of judgment regarding these aspects within the curricular exams, also through the discussion of autonomous elaborations.
Learning ability.
Graduates in the Master's Degree course will have fully developed all those learning skills (linguistic, lexical, dialectical, argumentative, etc.) that will enable them to continue their training in the most advanced instances. In particular:
* ability to use autonomously bibliographic tools in traditional form and/or with the support of new technologies;
* ability to participate in a conscious, critical and active way in moments of advanced training within formal courses or on the occasion of events of various kinds (seminars, conferences, etc.);
* ability to outline their own autonomous path in the development of the training process, selecting the training opportunities most suitable for achieving the cognitive, research and work objectives that have been set.
Teaching methods and tools for achieving objectives:
Specific segments within the disciplinary lessons dedicated to the illustration of the main tools and procedures for individual research, also with the aid of computer resources;
Seminar activities at different levels
Verification methods:
Verification of the skills achieved in this area through the evaluation of written and audiovisual elaborations and the degree of active participation in the didactic and seminar activities provided.
Ability to apply knowledge and understanding.
General Area Graduates in the Master's Degree course will be able to apply the complex of knowledge and understanding skills acquired in:
* Construction of their own individual cultural identity and a solid professional approach to work in different areas of activity (research, protection, enhancement, training, dissemination, etc.).
* Identification of areas and research problems potentially fruitful in the sector of archaeological disciplines relating to antiquity in its broadest sense, to be investigated through the development of autonomous research projects.
* Creation and management of small working groups aimed at conducting autonomous research in the disciplinary areas of interest.
* Creation and management of innovative investigation tools related to the application of new technologies to archaeological disciplines.
* Design and implementation of diversified forms of public communication (scientific and popular publication, new media, events, etc.) in relation to the area of interest.
Teaching methods and tools for achieving objectives:
Segments of the curricular courses specifically aimed at developing methodological reflection.
Seminar and applied research activities in the field and in the laboratory.
Verification methods:
Curricular exams.
Evaluation of participation in seminar and research activities in the field and in the laboratory.
Methodology and understanding of archaeological sources Application of the most appropriate procedures for the study and interpretation of archaeological data, using the most advanced methodologies and techniques, accessible to students during research in the field and in laboratory exercises.
Archaeological and monumental heritage The final objective is to achieve the skills necessary to understand the historical, material and artistic aspects of the Archaeological and monumental heritage and to propose its adequate enhancement.
