Roles: President/Chairman of the teaching committee: ELENA ANNA SPANDRI. Program, texts and objectives. Admission requirements. To access the Master's Degree course in Modern Literature, applicants must hold a degree or a three-year university diploma or another qualification obtained abroad and deemed suitable. In particular, for admission to the Master's Degree course in Modern Literature, certain curricular requirements and adequate personal preparation are required, as specified and verified below. Curricular requirements: possession of a degree in one of the following classes according to D.M. 270/2005: L-10 (Literature), L-11 (Modern Languages and Cultures), L-12 (Sciences of Linguistic Mediation), L-20 (Sciences of Communication); or possession of a degree in one of the following degree classes according to D.M. 509/99: 5 (Literature), 11 (Modern Languages and Cultures), 3 (Sciences of Linguistic Mediation), 14 (Sciences of Communication). The candidate must also have obtained at least 42 CFU in total in the following SSDs: L-FIL-LET/04 Latin Language and Literature, L-FIL-LET/09 Romance Philology and Linguistics, L-FIL-LET/10 Italian Literature, L-FIL-LET/11 Contemporary Italian Literature, L-FIL-LET/12 Italian Linguistics, L-FIL-LET/13 Philology of Italian Literature, L-FIL-LET/14 Criticism of Literature and Comparative Literatures, L-FIL-LET/15 Germanic Philology, L-LIN/01 Glottology and Linguistics, L-LIN/02 Teaching of Modern Languages, L-LIN/03 French Literature, L-LIN/04 French Language, L-LIN/05 Spanish Literature, L-LIN/06 Hispanic-American Literatures, L-LIN/07 Spanish Language, L-LIN/08 Portuguese and Brazilian Literature, L-LIN/09 Portuguese Language, L-LIN/10 English Literature, L-LIN/11 Anglo-American Literature, L-LIN/12 English Language, L-LIN/13 German Literature, L-LIN/14 German Language, L-LIN/21 Slavic Studies (Russian Language and Russian Literature), M-GGR Geography, M-DEA Demo-ethno-anthropological disciplines, M-STO/M-FIL, within the 42 CFU: - at least one exam (6 CFU) in the SSD L-FIL-LET/10 Italian Literature; at least one exam (6 CFU) in the SSD L-FIL-LET/11 Contemporary Italian Literature or L-FIL-LET/14 Criticism of Literature and Comparative Literatures; at least one exam (6 CFU) in the SSD L-FIL-LET/12 Italian Linguistics; at least one exam (6 CFU) in L-FIL-LET/09 Romance Philology and Linguistics or L-FIL-LET/13 Philology of Italian Literature. For those coming from a degree class other than those indicated above, the following are required: a) having obtained at least 72 CFU in the following SSDs: L-FIL-LET/04 Latin Language and Literature, L-FIL-LET/09 Romance Philology and Linguistics, L-FIL-LET/10 Italian Literature, L-FIL-LET/11 Contemporary Italian Literature, L-FIL-LET/12 Italian Linguistics, L-FIL-LET/13 Italian Philology, L-FIL-LET/14 Literary Criticism and Comparative Literatures, L-FIL-LET/15 Germanic Philology, L-LIN/01 Glottology and Linguistics, L-LIN/02 Teaching of Modern Languages, L-LIN/03 French Literature, L-LIN/04 French Language, L-LIN/05 Spanish Literature, L-LIN/06 Hispanic-American Literatures, L-LIN/07 Spanish Language, L-LIN/08 Portuguese and Brazilian Literature, L-LIN/09 Portuguese Language, L-LIN/10 English Literature, L-LIN/11 Anglo-American Literature, L-LIN/12 English Language, L-LIN/13 German Literature, L-LIN/14 German Language, L-LIN/21 Slavic Studies (Russian Language and Russian Literature), M-GGR Geography, M-DEA Demo-ethno-anthropological disciplines, M-STO/M-FIL. Within the 72 CFU: - at least 12 CFU in the SSD L-FIL-LET/10 - at least 6 CFU in the SSD L-FIL-LET/11 or L-FIL-LET/14 - at least 6 CFU in the SSD L-FIL-LET/12 - at least 6 in L-FIL-LET/09 or L-FIL-LET 13. Students enrolling in the Master's Degree course in Modern Literature must have knowledge of the English language at least at level B1. The verification of the possession of the curricular requirements is the task of the Teaching Committee, which evaluates the individual admission requests. Verification of personal preparation: The mandatory verification of personal preparation, carried out using the methods indicated in the Didactic Regulations of the study course, is accessible only to students who possess the curricular requirements. Enrollment with educational debts is not allowed. Professional status conferred by the degree. Expert in the field of the cultural and publishing industry. Master's degree graduates in Modern Literature who have obtained a sufficient number of credits in appropriate sector groups may, as provided by current legislation, participate in the admission tests for training courses for secondary school teaching. The Master's degree in Modern Literature also provides an excellent basis for accessing doctoral research courses in the humanities and philological disciplines. The Master's degree graduate in Modern Literature has a global, versatile and flexible training, which manages to integrate experiences of specific in-depth study in the fields of classical and modern literature, history, philosophy and anthropology. Master's degree graduates in Modern Literature may carry out professional activities in local authorities and specific institutions, such as libraries, archives and academies, as well as in publishing houses, editorial offices, foundations, companies and other professional bodies operating in the organization of events and cultural activities. Characteristics of the final exam. The final exam consists of the discussion of an original thesis consistent with the training objectives of the study course, developed under the guidance of a supervisor and discussed before a commission. In the paper, the candidate must demonstrate, in addition to the overall knowledge acquired, in-depth specific skills, the ability to address and solve scientific problems in an autonomous and original way, independent judgment, and full mastery of oral and written expressive means. The text will be composed according to the conventions of academic writing and with appropriate disciplinary terminology. The final degree grade will be awarded based on the originality of the contribution, the completeness of the research, the mastery of the method and scientific tools, the quality of the writing, and the level of presentation. The maximum grade is one hundred and ten out of one hundred and ten (110/110), to which the degree commission may add honors. Knowledge required for access. To access the Master's Degree course in Modern Literature, applicants must hold a degree or a three-year university diploma or another qualification obtained abroad and deemed suitable. In particular, for admission to the Master's Degree course in Modern Literature, certain curricular requirements and adequate personal preparation are required, as specified below. Curricular requirements: possession of a degree in one of the following classes according to D.M. 270/2004: L-10 (Literature), L-11 (Modern Languages and Cultures), L-12 (Sciences of Linguistic Mediation), L-20 (Sciences of Communication); or possession of a degree in one of the following degree classes according to D.M. 509/99: 5 (Literature), 11 (Modern Languages and Cultures), 3 (Sciences of Linguistic Mediation), 14 (Sciences of Communication). For those coming from a degree class other than those indicated above, the following are required: a) having obtained at least 75 CFU in the following SSDs: L-FIL-LET/04 Latin Language and Literature, L-FIL-LET/09 Romance Philology and Linguistics, L-FIL-LET/10 Italian Literature, L-FIL-LET/11 Contemporary Italian Literature, L-FIL-LET/12 Italian Linguistics, L-FIL-LET/13 Italian Philology, L-FIL-LET/14 Literary Criticism and Comparative Literatures, L-FIL-LET/15 Germanic Philology, L-LIN/01 Glottology and Linguistics, L-LIN/02 Teaching of Modern Languages, L-LIN/03 French Literature, L-LIN/04 French Language, L-LIN/05 Spanish Literature, L-LIN/06 Hispanic-American Literatures, L-LIN/07 Spanish Language, L-LIN/08 Portuguese and Brazilian Literature, L-LIN/09 Portuguese Language, L-LIN/10 English Literature, L-LIN/11 Anglo-American Literature, L-LIN/12 English Language, L-LIN/13 German Literature, L-LIN/14 German Language, L-LIN/21 Slavic Studies (Russian Language and Russian Literature), M-GGR Geography, M-DEA Demo-ethno-anthropological disciplines, M-STO/M-FIL. b) within the 75 CFU - at least 12 CFU in the SSD L-FIL-LET/10 - at least 6 CFU in the SSD L-FIL-LET/11 or L-FIL-LET/14 - at least 6 CFU in the SSD L-FIL-LET/12 - at least 6 in L-FIL-LET/09 or L-FIL-LET 13. Students enrolling in the Master's Degree course in Modern Literature must have knowledge of the English language at least at level B1. Verification of curricular requirements and personal preparation. The verification of the possession of the curricular requirements is the task of the Teaching Committee, which evaluates the individual admission requests. The verification of personal preparation is mandatory and is accessible only to those who possess the curricular requirements. The methods of verification are better specified in the Didactic Regulations of the Course. Enrollment with educational debts is not allowed. Degree awarded. Master's Degree in Modern Literature. Teaching delivery method: Conventional. Knowledge and understanding. General area. The Master's Degree course aims to train graduates who have solid historical-cultural and linguistic preparation and who are able to produce texts, also in foreign languages. Graduates will be able to analyze literary, non-literary and critical texts, taking into account the context in which they were produced, the author's point of view, the history of reception, and the conflict between interpretations. Textbooks will be chosen based on their scientific level, their updating with respect to the state of research, the transparency of their content, and their methodological effectiveness. The variety of disciplines present will allow students to address the specific themes of their field of interest with an interdisciplinary approach. The critical literature supporting their training will aim to broaden the range of their knowledge and train them in the plurality of methodological and scientific debate. As regards the basic theoretical and cultural training, the Master's degree graduate will be progressively put in a position to acquire autonomy and awareness of the study method. The training is aimed at mastering literary history, philology, history of language, literary theory, and history of criticism. The acquisition of solid historical foundations, essential for the correct application of knowledge, will be accompanied by an in-depth study of philological, linguistic and theoretical-literary methods. The in-depth study of foreign literatures, with the related philologies, is also useful for a sound comparative analysis. In general, knowledge and understanding skills will be acquired by Master's degree candidates through advanced-level lectures with the aid of updated and innovative bibliographic and didactic materials; exercises on texts and sources of particular philological, linguistic and literary relevance; participation in conferences and seminars of high scientific value held by national and international experts; preparation of original and methodologically rigorous reports; preparation of the final exam paper. Knowledge and skills will be verified through the examination tests, including the final one. Area of Philology, Linguistics and Italian Literature. Mastery of the Italian language, oral and written. Ability to read Italian literary texts from different eras (from the Middle Ages to the present day). Good knowledge of the history of the Italian language. Excellent knowledge of Italian literature, both with regard to the historical-cultural contexts and with regard to the individual authors. The main teaching tool is the lecture, possibly accompanied by a seminar-type exercise. Evaluation takes place through written and/or oral exams, or through the preparation of essays. Area of Philologies, Modern Languages and Comparative Literatures. Ability to understand one or more foreign languages at the highest level possible (with the exception of English, for which level B2 is required). To this end, exercises are provided by the Language Centers (CEL) at different levels of language skills. Ability to express oneself in the foreign languages studied, at different levels depending on the skills that the student has been able to achieve. Good knowledge of the history of the foreign country whose language is the subject of study (at least as regards the historical period studied). Ability to orient oneself within a text, not only literary, in one of the foreign languages studied. Ability to place the text in the context of which it is a product. As regards foreign philologies (Romance and Germanic), good knowledge of the oldest phases of the literatures and languages studied, especially in relation to the cultural dynamics of medieval Europe. The main teaching tool is the lecture. In the case of language lessons, a great deal of space is given to exercises in the classroom with the CEL. The evaluation of knowledge takes place through written and/or oral exams, through exercises and seminar activities. Historical area. Good knowledge of Western history and the main tools of historical and historiographical analysis; ability to understand the connections between historical contexts and artistic and cultural products (especially literary). The main teaching tool is the lecture. Evaluation takes place through written and/or oral exams.

Chairman of the Coordination Committee: SIMONE BETA
Chairman/Chairman of the teaching committee: MARIO LENTANO, CRISTIANO VIGLIETTI
Program, texts and objectives
Admission requirements:
- having obtained a degree in one of the following classes: ex D.M. 270/2004: L-1 (Cultural Heritage), L-3 (Disciplines of visual arts, music, performing arts and fashion), L-10 (Literature), L-19 (Sciences of education and training), L-42 (History); or in one of the following degree classes ex D.M. 509/99: 5 (Literature), 13 (Sciences of cultural heritage), 18 (Sciences of education and training), 23 (Sciences and technologies of visual arts, music, performing arts and fashion), 38 (Historical Sciences).
- possessing the curricular requirements;
- knowledge of the English language;
- computer skills
Professional status conferred by the degree.
Expert in ancient sciences, philologist.
Upon obtaining the Master's degree in Classical Studies, subject to possessing the credits indicated by the program in specific subject areas, as provided by current legislation, students may participate in the entrance exams for teacher training programs for secondary education; similarly, the Master's degree graduate in Classical Studies may continue their educational path by applying for a doctoral degree and thereby gain the possibility of accessing university teaching, according to current regulations. Other employment opportunities can be found in the following sectors:
- public and private bodies and institutions operating in the fields of conservation and enhancement of Italian cultural heritage;
- cultural information and mass communication
- publishing
- organization of cultural events of various kinds.
Characteristics of the final exam The final exam will consist of a public discussion, before a commission composed according to the methods indicated in the University's Teaching Regulations and the Teaching Regulations of the study program, of a thesis, which is the product of original research and the result of an organic personal re-elaboration of knowledge acquired during the educational path.
The thesis and its discussion must demonstrate the candidate's ability to autonomously use the specific sources of their field of study, the relevant research tools and the set of knowledge resources necessary to develop an original product that constitutes a significant advancement for knowledge in the investigated sector. They must also demonstrate the candidate's ability to present and argue the results of their research work clearly and effectively.
The thesis work, for which 27 CFU are awarded, will be carried out under the supervision of one of the teachers of the Course and will be discussed with the participation of a co-supervisor.
The evaluation of the final exam will be expressed in hundredths with possible honors.
Knowledge required for access To access the Master's degree program in Classical Studies, students must possess a degree or a three-year university diploma or another qualification obtained abroad and deemed suitable. Students who possess the curricular requirements and adequate personal preparation may be admitted. Enrollment with outstanding educational debts is not permitted. Any deficiencies in educational credits can be remedied, as indicated by the relevant teaching structures, even during the period between the first-level degree and the closing of enrollments in the Master's degree programs, by passing the exams of individual courses offered at the University.
The curricular requirements are considered to be met if:
a) having graduated in one of the following degree classes:
L-1 (Cultural Heritage)
L-3 (Disciplines of visual arts, music, performing arts and fashion)
L-10 (Literature)
L-19 (Sciences of education and training)
L-42 (History)
or in one of the following degree classes ex D.M. 509/99:
5 (Literature)
13 (Sciences of cultural heritage)
18 (Sciences of education and training)
23 (Sciences and technologies of visual arts, music, performing arts and fashion)
38 (Historical Sciences).
b) having obtained at least 48 CFU in total in the following scientific-disciplinary fields:
L-ANT/02 Greek History and/or L-ANT/03 Roman History (at least 12 CFU); L-ANT/07 Classical Archaeology (at least 6 CFU); L-FIL-LET/02 Greek Language and Literature (at least 12 CFU); L-FIL-LET/04 Latin Language and Literature (at least 12 CFU); L-FIL-LET/05 Classical Philology (at least 6 CFU).
For those coming from a degree class other than those indicated above (letter a), it is necessary to have obtained at least 75 CFU in total in the following scientific-disciplinary fields:
L-ANT/02 Greek History and/or L-ANT/03 Roman History (at least 12 CFU); L-ANT/07 Classical Archaeology (at least 6 CFU); L-FIL-LET/02 Greek Language and Literature (at least 12 CFU); L-FIL-LET/04 Latin Language and Literature (at least 12 CFU); L-FIL-LET/05 Classical Philology (at least 6 CFU).
The verification of the possession of the curricular requirements is the task of the Teaching Committee, which evaluates the individual admission requests. The verification of personal preparation is mandatory and can only be accessed by those who possess the curricular requirements. The methods of verification are specified in the Teaching Regulations of the study program.
Degree awarded:
Master's Degree in Classical Studies
Method of teaching: Conventional
Knowledge and understanding
Generic Area
The Master's degree graduate in Classical Studies will possess:
* Solid and advanced disciplinary knowledge, both content-based and methodological, relating to the ancient and medieval world, understood in its chronological, spatial and thematic complexity.
* Ability to critically use literary and extra-literary sources, as well as other knowledge tools (specialist texts, databases, various types of computer supports) to build their own organic and solid system of knowledge about the ancient world, with particular reference to the specific disciplinary area chosen.
* Knowledge of ancient languages at a level that allows them to be used to build autonomous critical elaborations with reference to the specific field of study chosen.
* Specific theoretical, methodological and operational knowledge relating to the chosen disciplinary area and the peculiar forms in which modern philological and linguistic research, as well as historical and archaeological research, are expressed in it.
* Strong awareness of the importance of ancient studies in modern society and specific interest in the transmission of this awareness.
Literary and philological area
Knowledge of ancient languages.
Solid and advanced disciplinary knowledge - content-based and methodological - relating to classical literatures.
Ability to critically use literary sources, with particular reference to the specific disciplinary area chosen.
Anthropological area
Knowledge of ancient languages.
Solid and advanced knowledge relating to the ancient world, understood in its chronological, spatial and thematic complexity.
Ability to critically use literary and extra-literary sources, with particular reference to the specific disciplinary area chosen.
Strong awareness of the importance of ancient studies in modern society and specific interest in the transmission of this awareness.
Historical area
Knowledge of ancient languages.
Solid and advanced disciplinary knowledge relating to the ancient and medieval world, understood in its chronological and spatial complexity.
Ability to critically use literary and extra-literary sources, with particular reference to the specific disciplinary area chosen.

Upper Secondary School Diploma.
Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse.
Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni.
Roles and Responsibilities: Student Mobility: ANTONELLA NALDINI, President/President of the teaching committee.
ALESSANDRA GAMBERUCCI, President.
ALESSANDRA GAMBERUCCI.
Program, texts and objectives: Admission requirements: Students with a High School Diploma or an equivalent foreign qualification may be admitted.
Knowledge: mathematics (level: scientific high school); computer science (knowledge of the main applications and programs for PC); English language (level A2/2); general culture (more specifically history at the high school level, civic education with specific reference to the Italian Constitution).
Professional status conferred by the degree: Biotechnologist. The course allows students to acquire the necessary skills for the profession of junior biologist (after passing the state exam and registering with the professional association) and to find employment opportunities in the role of:
Laboratory technician, in a public or private structure, capable of applying the most appropriate analytical and diagnostic techniques in the biotechnological production of health products (drugs, vaccines, medical devices).
Laboratory technician, in a public or private structure, capable of correctly applying analytical and diagnostic techniques for quality control, safety and efficacy in the production processes of health biotechnological products (drugs, vaccines, medical devices), in compliance with good manufacturing, quality and safety practices.
Laboratory technician in a diagnostic structure (public or private), capable of working in the diagnostics of the health sector (analysis laboratories of a public or affiliated structure).
Laboratory technician in a public or private structure (company) authorized for the certification and quality control of biotechnologically derived drugs, vaccines and medical devices.
- entrepreneur of biotechnology companies in the health sector (red biotech).
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam will consist of a discussion before a committee of teachers on a written paper, in Italian or English. The experimental thesis will constitute the result of the studies and research carried out during the last year of the course, also through internships or research periods that may take place in Italy or abroad. The thesis work will thus represent the fundamental goal of the path taken over the three years, of which it is a substantial part. Between 10 and 20 CFU will be awarded for the final exam. The student must demonstrate that they have acquired, in addition to adequate knowledge and understanding of the chosen topic, autonomous learning and research skills; the ability to correctly use the consulted literature and the necessary data sources; the ability to present and support scientific arguments in a logical and coherent manner. The grade for the final exam is expressed in hundredths, with possible honors. The criteria for calculating the merit score are defined by the Course Regulations.
Knowledge required for access: The student who enrolls in the Biotechnology Course of Study must have a high school diploma obtained in Italy or a qualification obtained abroad, after at least 12 years of schooling, and recognized as suitable according to current regulations, with adequate knowledge of basic scientific disciplines - biology (level: scientific high school); English language (level A2/2); mathematics, physics, chemistry - and understanding of basic logic and general culture.
To guarantee the quality of teaching, access to the Course of Study may be subject to local programmed admission, in relation to the sustainability of the facilities.
The methods for evaluating the student's initial preparation will be indicated in the Didactic Regulations of the degree course, where the additional training obligations required in the event that the assessment is not positive will also be indicated. With the start of training, the teachers of the basic disciplines proceed with a verification of the minimum requirements of knowledge in the areas of biology, chemistry and physics, which may involve the student having to fulfill training debts (OFA) in the specific area. The verification of the knowledge indicated above will take place according to the methods, criteria and procedures established by the didactic regulations of the Course of Studies. Students who are found to be lacking in one or more areas, with the support of the reference teachers and the teaching committee, must complete the OFA found within the first year of the course.
Degree awarded: DEGREE IN BIOTECHNOLOGY.
Method of delivery of teaching: Conventional.
Knowledge and understanding.
Generic Area: With reference to the system of descriptors of study titles adopted at European level (Dublin descriptors), the Biotechnology Degree Course is designed so that its graduates, at the end of the study path, acquire knowledge and understanding skills in the field of biotechnology. Transversal learning objectives are:
a) ability to understand simple and complex phenomena and models relating to chemical, physical, mathematical and statistical disciplines;
b) knowledge of the biological bases and physiological mechanisms underlying biological phenomena at the molecular and cellular level and their integration at the level of the system and the organism as a whole;
c) knowledge of the main techniques used in the biotechnological field and their theoretical foundations;
d) knowledge of national and international legislation of interest in biotechnology, with particular regard to the rules for patenting and bioethics.
In order to clearly and concisely illustrate how the expected learning outcomes described above relate to the teachings provided in the training course, please refer to the link of the course https://biotecnologie.unisi.it/it where you can find the programs and evaluation methods of the individual teachings.
Area: Mathematics, Physics, Chemistry. Knowledge and understanding: The student acquires knowledge of the methods and basic tools of mathematics and computer science, physics and chemistry for the understanding and interpretation of biological systems.
In detail, graduates in Biotechnology are able to:
1) critically understand a mathematical-statistical reasoning.
2) understand the statistical bases associated with the life sciences.
3) understand the physical phenomena in the various fields of Biotechnology.
4) understand the chemical phenomena in the various fields of Biotechnology. These skills will be developed mainly with traditional teaching tools, through participation in lectures, exercises and laboratory activities and through personal study of texts and scientific publications.
Area: Molecular, Cellular and Organisms. Knowledge and understanding: The student acquires knowledge of biological systems, with respect to the cellular and molecular aspect, which provide cognitive tools relating to the structure and function of eukaryotic cells and multicellular organisms with reference to functional, biochemical, molecular and genetic aspects. Specifically, they acquire:
1) the knowledge necessary to understand the mechanisms that regulate the transmission of hereditary traits, the regulation of gene expression and the biochemical processes underlying cellular communication and cellular and organism physiology.
2) the knowledge relating to the structure-function relationships of macromolecules, cells and tissues.
4) the knowledge necessary to understand the structure of the human genome and the principles of the methods used for its analysis.
6) the knowledge necessary to understand the molecular mechanisms underlying diseases and the definition and classification of pharmacological targets and the understanding of the different aspects of pharmacodynamics underlying the action of drugs.
7) the knowledge necessary to understand the molecular mechanisms underlying bacterial and viral infections, the understanding of the molecular phenomena of host-microorganism interaction and their treatment and the immune response of organisms.
The knowledge and skills are acquired and verified in the following training activities which, in addition to lectures, may include exercises both in the classroom and in the laboratory, which involve the use of the optical microscope, for the observation and recognition of histological and anatomical preparations colored with conventional methods, in order to distinguish the morphological characteristics corresponding to the different tissues and organs.
Area: Biotechnological. Ability to apply knowledge and understanding: The student in Biotechnology acquires knowledge and understanding skills of the main diagnostic and biotechnological techniques and the basic principles of pharmacology.
- experiments and acquires knowledge of the most recent applications of biotechnology to the fields of medicine, public health and diagnostics.
- acquires knowledge of the techniques of synthesis and biosynthesis of therapeutic peptides and recombinant antibodies, integrating the biotechnological and chemical knowledge essential for the development of new therapeutic products.
- acquires knowledge of biological databases automatically with bioinformatics tools, and reads/writes shell scripts and PYTHON programs.
- acquires basic knowledge relating to the patent discipline of the ethical and economic aspects of scientific research and biotechnological applications in the medical field.
The knowledge and skills are acquired and verified in the following training activities which, in addition to lectures, include exercises both in the classroom and in the laboratory.
The verification of the acquired knowledge and the ability to understand the different areas will be verified during the respective profit exams which will take place with exams mainly oral or with final written exams and in progress in the form of multiple-choice or open-ended tests.

Program, texts and objectives: Access to further studies: RESEARCH DOCTORATE COURSES (CDR), SPECIALIST DEGREE COURSES/MASTER'S DEGREE COURSES - CLS/CLM, FIRST LEVEL UNIVERSITY MASTER COURSES.
Admission requirements: Students in possession of a High School Diploma or equivalent foreign qualification may be admitted.
The required knowledge will include: elements of general culture and logical reasoning, mathematics, physics, chemistry, biology.
Knowledge of the English language is also required, at least at level A2/2 (CEFR).
The number of students admitted to the degree course is programmed each year based on regional needs.
The entrance exam is common to all degree courses in the health field and is held on the same day in all Italian universities.
Professional status conferred by the degree: The Environmental and Workplace Prevention Technician is the health worker responsible, within the scope of their competence, for all prevention, verification and control activities in the field of environmental hygiene and safety in living and working environments, food and beverage hygiene, and public and veterinary hygiene. The environmental and workplace prevention technician, operating in services with inspection and monitoring tasks, is, within the limits of their responsibilities, a judicial police officer: they carry out investigative activities aimed at issuing authorizations or technical-health opinions for activities subject to control.
The degree obtained qualifies for the profession and allows to carry out the professional activity, in an employed or freelance capacity, within the national health service (85.1 Health services activities) at all prevention, control and monitoring services provided for by current legislation (85.14.2 Independent paramedical professional activities; 85.2 veterinary services; 90.0 disposal of solid waste, wastewater, etc.).
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam has the value of a state exam qualifying for the exercise of the profession (Legislative Decree 502/1992, art. 6, paragraph 3) and consists of:
a) a practical test during which the student must demonstrate that they have acquired the theoretical and practical knowledge and skills and the technical-operational skills specific to the professional profile;
b) drafting of a thesis and its discussion. See DM of February 19, 2009, art. 7.
5 CFU will be awarded for the final exam.
The final exam cannot be repeated more than once.
The criteria for awarding the merit score will include: evaluation of the skills demonstrated during the presentation and discussion of the paper; assessment of the technical skills demonstrated during the practical test.
The final score is expressed in hundredths.
If the overall score of 110/110 is reached, the president of the final exam commission puts to the vote the awarding of honors, which can only be awarded unanimously by those present.
Knowledge required for access: Students in possession of a High School Diploma or equivalent foreign qualification may be admitted. The required knowledge will include: elements of general culture and logical reasoning, mathematics, physics, chemistry, biology. Knowledge of the English language is also required, at least at level A2/2 (CEFR).
Degree awarded: DEGREE IN TECHNIQUES OF PREVENTION IN THE ENVIRONMENT AND WORKPLACES.
Teaching method: Conventional.
Knowledge and understanding:
- Acquire the basic statistical and epidemiological knowledge necessary to understand and address the issues related to the organization, research and teaching in the field of health sciences of prevention;
- Use the methods and tools of research in the area of organization of health services and in the evaluation of their quality;
- Acquire elements for social research based on the collection of information through the use of questionnaires;
- Evaluate the results of interventions in the field of research and health promotion;
- Deepen the understanding of the organization and content of scientific publications including the main statistical and epidemiological analyses;
- Know the basics of research;
- Use a widely used spreadsheet (Excel, Microsoft) for the concise and graphical representation of data.
Professional area: Acquire a basic knowledge of the technical, operational and managerial aspects typical of the health professions of prevention (class 4) and use this knowledge for the organization and management of health services:
- Know the main models of economic, social and health development and environmental protection in industrialized countries;
- Know the organization of health services at national and international level and understand the dynamics of their development;
- Know basic aspects related to vaccinations in a preventive perspective;
- Know the chemical and ergonomic risk factors and some tools for the evaluation of risk in the field of occupational health and public health;
- Know basic aspects of medical parasitology in a preventive perspective;
- Know the fundamental principles of transmissible infectious diseases, their methods of diffusion and control, the methods of notification and the principles of screening and individual protection;
- Know the principles of infectious risk management in the health field by the Risk Manager;
- Know the tools for improving the quality of health care services in the area of prevention;
- Know the classification of occupational diseases, their active research in different exposure and risk scenarios, with particular reference to agriculture;
- Know the programming and optimization of resources for the management of territorial health services with preventive purposes.
Management area:
- Acquire the basic concepts of economic-business analysis applied to health systems (public, private and accredited private);
- Understand and evaluate the functioning of the organization and the role of the managerial functions of a health company;
- Acquire and be able to use skills aimed at promoting and supporting innovation, creativity and organizational change;
- Understand the psychological aspects of work behaviors, both individual and in the context of the group and the organization;
- Know and evaluate the structural characteristics of communication and its contents, examining the specific communicative aspects within a professional team;
- Manage and solve organizational problems based on the integrated evaluation of the technical, social, individual and institutional components, and in relation to the objectives to be achieved in terms of service to users and the role of the health structure;
- Know the regulatory references relating to the prevention and protection of health and safety in the workplace;
- Understand the relationships between programming and control systems and the concepts of effectiveness, efficiency and cost-effectiveness of the health company;
- Learn the basic notions of trade union law, labor law, professional liability in the health field, the main instruments for the protection of health and the role of the main international organizations.
Biomedical sciences: The graduate is able to:
- possess the knowledge and ability to understand: the composition and fundamental biochemical processes of living matter, the morphological-functional characteristics of the cell, the principles and mechanisms of gene expression and the transmission of hereditary traits.
- possess the basic knowledge necessary to understand the macro and microscopic structure of organs and the organization of the human body (systems) as well as the main models of functioning of the body districts and the pathogenetic mechanisms underlying the most common pathologies.
- possess the basic knowledge to distinguish the different microorganisms and their modes of diffusion and transmission and to understand the main analytical techniques for recognizing the presence of microorganisms in biological and environmental matrices.

The required knowledge will include: elements of general culture and logical reasoning, mathematics, physics, chemistry, biology.

Professional status conferred by the degree.
Employment opportunities (DM 19 February 2009, Art. 4, paragraph 6).
Nursing graduates carry out their professional activities in healthcare facilities, public or private, in the area and in home care, on a dependent or freelance basis. According to the ISTAT report "Universities and Work" 2009, the employment rate is 98.4%, so the short-term employment prospects are excellent throughout the national territory and in particular all graduates at the University of Siena, in the last year, are currently employed.
With regard to employment opportunities, reference can be made to the "classification of economic activities - ATECO 2007" reported below: general hospitals and nursing homes, specialized hospitals and nursing homes, university institutes, clinics and polyclinics, long-term care hospitals and nursing homes, general medical practice clinics, health services provided by surgeons, outpatient clinics and polyclinics of the National Health Service, radiotherapy centers, dialysis centers, homeopathy and acupuncture clinics, aesthetic medicine centers, other specialist medical and polyclinic clinics, dental clinics, radiographic laboratories, clinical analysis laboratories, hygiene and prophylaxis laboratories, other independent paramedical activities, ambulance services, blood banks and other health services. Residential nursing care facilities for the elderly, residential care facilities for people with mental retardation, mental disorders or who abuse drugs, residential care facilities for the elderly and disabled, nursery services, daytime assistance for disabled minors. Holiday villages, seaside and mountain colonies. Civil protection activities. University and post-university education; academies and conservatories, training courses and professional updating courses. Activities of federations and councils of professional orders and colleges, activities of professional associations, activities of trade unions of employed workers. Agents and representatives of health products and medical, surgical and orthopedic devices; devices for aesthetic centers. Activities of sterilization of medical equipment.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam has the value of a state exam enabling the exercise of the profession (Legislative Decree 502/1992, art. 6, paragraph 3) and consists of:
a) a practical test during which the student must demonstrate that he/she has acquired the theoretical and practical knowledge and technical-operational skills specific to the professional profile;
b) drafting of a thesis and its discussion. See DM 19 February 2009, art. 7.
5 CFU will be awarded for the final exam.
The final exam cannot be repeated more than once.
The criteria for awarding the merit score will include: evaluation of the skills demonstrated during the presentation and discussion of the paper; assessment of the technical skills demonstrated during the practical test.
The final score is expressed in hundredths.
If the overall score of 110/110 is reached, the chairman of the examination board puts to the vote the awarding of honors, which can only be awarded unanimously by those present.

The required knowledge will include: elements of general culture and logical reasoning, mathematics, physics, chemistry, biology. Knowledge of the English language is also required, at least at level A2/2 (CEFR).
Degree awarded:
BACHELOR'S DEGREE IN NURSING
Method of teaching: Conventional.
Knowledge and understanding.
Area of basic disciplines: Nursing graduates who have successfully completed the course of study will be able to:
1. describe, interpret and infer implications on the qualitative and quantitative bases of biological, physiopathological and pathological phenomena.
2. describe and interpret the molecular, morphological and functional bases of vital, physiological and pathological processes.
3. describe, interpret and discuss the fundamental principles of hygiene, health promotion and disease prevention.
4. describe, interpret and infer implications and meanings on the basic concepts of the humanities applied to professional contexts.
The learning objectives will be achieved through didactic tools of the ex-cathedra, seminar and interactive type. The verification of learning will be carried out through in-course tests, suitability assessments and final exams.
Area of professional disciplines: Nursing graduates who have successfully completed the course of study must have acquired the following knowledge and understanding skills:
1. Define, describe, interpret, distinguish and infer implications, relationships and meanings on the main conceptual models and theories of nursing care.
2. Describe, interpret and infer implications, relevance, relationships and meanings on the technical-practical methods for diagnosing, planning, organizing, implementing and evaluating general nursing care.
3. Describe and discuss the current knowledge of the disciplinary areas of pharmacology, medicine and surgery, general and specialist, in the maternal-infant area, in mental health and in the emergency-urgency area.
4. Describe, interpret and infer implications, relevance, relationships and meanings on the nursing disciplines applied to the general and specialist medical and surgical areas, to chronicity and disability in both hospital and community settings.
5. Describe, interpret and infer implications, relevance, relationships and meanings on the nursing disciplines applied to the maternal-infant area, to mental health and to emergency-urgency.
6. Define, describe, interpret, distinguish and infer implications, relationships and meanings on the relational principles and supportive methods applied to individual professional contexts.
7. Describe and discuss the operational methods related to the continuity of care in both hospital and territorial settings.
8. Define, describe, interpret, distinguish and infer implications, relationships and meanings on the bioethical, deontological, safety of care, legal and medico-legal principles applied to professional contexts.
The learning objectives will be achieved through didactic tools of the ex-cathedra, seminar and interactive type, as well as through demonstrations and laboratory simulations. The verification of learning will be carried out through in-course tests, suitability assessments and final exams.
Area of integrative disciplines: Nursing graduates who have successfully completed the course of study must have acquired the following knowledge and understanding skills:
1. Describe, interpret and infer implications on the fundamental principles of management, organization and health economics.
2. Describe and discuss the knowledge relating to biomedical and nursing research, as well as the use of information systems for managing health information systems, with particular reference to the methods of acquiring scientific information for their own training and updating.
The learning objectives will be achieved through didactic tools of the ex-cathedra, seminar and interactive type. The verification of learning will be carried out through in-course tests, suitability assessments and final exams.
General Area: At the end of the studies, the graduate will have acquired knowledge and understanding skills relating to preventive, curative, palliative and rehabilitative nursing care, in its technical, relational and educational aspects:
- disease prevention
- care of the sick and disabled of all ages
- health education
Autonomy of judgment: Nursing graduates will develop a critical analysis capacity that will allow them to act independently, taking responsibility for choice and judgment in relation to different situations and priorities. In this sense:
1. they will recognize the main human reactions and responses to illness and suffering, as well as the behavioral differences related to the culture of belonging.
2. they will evaluate the clinical, psychological and socio-cultural manifestations related to the course of the main diseases, treatment, lifestyle, reactions to illness, hospitalization, home care and care interventions.
3. they will monitor critical situations related to the clinical, even psychological, worsening of the person being cared for, promptly activating other professionals.
4. based on the evaluation of the person being cared for and in accordance with the profile of the support staff, they will assign the appropriate care activities to it and evaluate the results.
5. with respect to the management function:
5a. they will identify the critical elements of the care setting with regard to the safety conditions for the person being cared for and the healthcare workers.
5b. they will be able to identify and promote elements of quality, efficiency and effectiveness of care services in the care setting.
6. based on the identification of training needs, they will be able to design and deliver training programs, evaluating the results.
7. with respect to the research function:
7a. they will be able to carry out bibliographic research on the main biomedical databases and critically read scientific articles, as well as apply the results based on the best available evidence in order to improve the quality of care.
7b. they will carry out research projects independently or in collaboration with other professionals.
8. with regard to the consultancy function, they will provide technical/professional opinions.
The learning objectives will be achieved through didactic tools of the interactive type and through clinical training in the structures of the Faculty and the affiliated Healthcare Companies. The learning will be verified through in-course tests, suitability assessments and final exams in the contexts of theoretical and practical teaching, as well as clinical training.
Learning ability: Nursing graduates will:
1. develop critical thinking skills that will enable them to select training opportunities that are consistent with their own training needs.
2. identify strategies for training and self-training for their own professional growth.
3. independently organize their own permanent training, according to correct metacognitive strategies.
The learning objectives will be achieved through didactic tools of the ex-cathedra, seminar, interactive type and through clinical training in the structures of the Faculty and the affiliated Healthcare Companies. The acquired learning skills will be verified through assessments in progress based on structured observation methods during the clinical and tutorial activities.
Ability to apply knowledge and understanding.
Area of basic disciplines: Nursing graduates who have successfully completed the course of study will be able to:
1. identify the health needs of the person and the community.
2. use the cultural bases of physiopathology necessary to analyze the medical acts in their practical applications.
3. identify, analyze, distinguish and recognize the psychological and socio-cultural manifestations related to the course of the main diseases, treatment, lifestyle, reactions to illness, hospitalization and care interventions.
4. identify, distinguish and recognize the main reactions of the person to illness, suffering, hospitalization, respecting the behavioral differences related to the culture of belonging.
5. plan, design, manage and evaluate interventions for health promotion and education aimed at healthy people and groups.
The learning objectives will be achieved through didactic tools of the ex-cathedra, seminar and interactive type as well as laboratory and clinical training. The verification of learning will be carried out through in-course tests, suitability assessments and final exams, in particular the final exam of the internship is aimed at measuring the achievement of the expected skills through specific tests for each area of learning: cognitive, psychomotor and affective.
Area of professional disciplines: Nursing graduates who have successfully completed the course of study will be able to:
1. identify the nursing care needs of the person and the community and formulate the related care objectives.
2. design, draw up and share nursing care plans tailored to the clinical needs of the sick and disabled person, respecting their gender, ethnicity, culture and stages of development.
3. design, manage and evaluate the nursing care intervention.
4. ensure the correct application of diagnostic-therapeutic prescriptions.
5. monitor, prevent and manage critical situations related to the clinical, even psychological, worsening of the person being cared for, promptly activating other professionals.
6. establish and maintain supportive relationships with the person being cared for and their family, applying the fundamentals of relational dynamics.
7. plan, design, manage and evaluate educational interventions aimed at therapeutic education for self-management of the disease, treatment and rehabilitation.
8. critically apply to individual clinical cases the general bioethical, deontological, legal and medico-legal principles, as well as the principles related to clinical safety, relevant to the performance of professional activities.
9. they will independently carry out consultancy functions, providing technical/professional opinions.
The learning objectives will be achieved through didactic tools of the ex-cathedra, seminar and interactive type as well as laboratory and clinical training. The verification of learning will be carried out through in-course tests, suitability assessments and final exams, in particular the final exam of the internship is aimed at measuring the achievement of the expected skills through specific tests for each area of learning: cognitive, psychomotor and affective.
Area of integrative disciplines: Nursing graduates who have successfully completed the course of study will be able to:
1. retrieve, analyze, evaluate critically and implement in clinical practice the best available scientific evidence for improving the quality of nursing care.
2. organize their own permanent training and updating independently.
3. design and implement research protocols in the nursing field, aimed at producing new knowledge.
4. use and manage the information systems of hospital and territorial health services.
5. guide the training of support staff; plan and organize the tutoring of students in training independently.
6. apply the principles of health organization to the provision of nursing care.
The learning objectives will be achieved through didactic tools of the ex-cathedra, seminar and interactive type as well as laboratory and clinical training. The verification of learning will be carried out through in-course tests, suitability assessments and final exams, in particular the final exam of the internship is aimed at measuring the achievement of the expected skills through specific tests for each area of learning: cognitive, psychomotor and affective.
General Area: At the end of the studies, the graduate will be able to:
- participate in the identification of the health needs of the person and the community.
- identify the nursing care needs of the person and the community and formulate the related objectives.
- plan, manage and evaluate the nursing care intervention.
- ensure the correct application of diagnostic-therapeutic prescriptions.
- act both individually and in collaboration with other health and social workers.
- make use of the support staff, where necessary.
- carry out their professional activities in public or private healthcare facilities, in the area and in home care, on a dependent or freelance basis.
- contribute to training.
- contribute to scientific updating and research.
Communication skills: Nursing graduates will be able to:
1. establish and maintain helping relationships with the person being cared for of different ages, abilities, gender and socio-cultural conditions and with their family, applying the fundamentals of relational dynamics aimed at promoting appropriate coping modalities and developing resilience skills.
2. they will develop communication strategies necessary for teamwork with the various professionals working in the socio-health field.
3. they will design and implement health education interventions aimed at healthy people and groups, and therapeutic education interventions aimed at self-management of the disease, treatment and rehabilitation.
The learning objectives will be achieved through didactic tools of the ex-cathedra and interactive type, through simulations and through clinical training in the structures of the Faculty and the affiliated Healthcare Companies. The acquired communication skills will be verified through assessments in progress based on structured observation methods during the clinical activities.
Expected employment and professional opportunities for graduates.
Nurse (DM Health n. 739 of 14/9/94)
Nurses carry out their professional activities in hospital companies, local health companies, private institutes for hospitalization and care, RSA, cooperative services for home care in a freelance regime. Currently, it is estimated that there is an annual turnover of approximately 18,000 professionals at national level.
Language/s of instruction and assessment:
ITALIAN
Skills related to the function:
Nurse (DM Health n. 739 of 14/9/94)
Nursing graduates are the health professionals to whom the tasks provided for in DM of the Ministry of Health n. 739 of 14/9/94, and subsequent amendments and additions, as well as the specific Code of Ethics, are assigned. The graduates will be responsible for general preventive, curative, palliative and rehabilitative nursing care, of a technical, relational and educational nature; their functions will concern the prevention and health education, care, therapeutic education, management, training, research and consultancy. Nursing graduates will promote the culture of health in the community, design and implement, in collaboration with other professional figures, training and educational interventions aimed at the person and the family, and will carry out autonomous professional activities aimed at the prevention, care and safeguarding of individual and collective health, using methodologies of planning for objectives of care for the person in the developmental, adult and geriatric age.
With regard to the specific training objectives of the course, nursing graduates will be able to: participate in the identification of the health needs of the person and the community; identify the nursing care needs of the person and the community and formulate the related objectives; plan, manage and evaluate the nursing care intervention, making use of support staff where necessary; ensure the correct application of diagnostic-therapeutic prescriptions. They will carry out self-training and training activities for students, support staff and newly hired staff; they will develop research activities aimed at producing new knowledge for the continuous improvement of care; they will participate in interdisciplinary working groups for the care of the citizen; they will provide consultancy for the development of care services.
Based on the specific training objectives described, the training course is structured over 3 years, including 6 semesters, and provides for a total of 180 CFU, of which 65 are to be acquired in training activities aimed at developing specific professional skills (internship). In order to achieve the specific objectives of the course, nursing graduates will have adequate theoretical and theoretical/practical preparation in three disciplinary areas aimed at the specificity of the profession:
1. Area of basic disciplines, aimed both at understanding the most relevant elements that are the basis of the physiological and pathological processes to which preventive, therapeutic, rehabilitative and palliative intervention is directed, and at maximum integration with other professions. The training activities include basic biomedical disciplines (BIO/10 Biochemistry, BIO/13 Biology and Fundamentals of Genetics, BIO/16 Anatomy and Elements of Histology, BIO/09 Physiology and Science of Nutrition, MED/04 General Pathology and Pathophysiology, MED/07 General and Clinical Microbiology and MED/08 Pathological Anatomy) and basic humanities disciplines (M-DEA/01 Demoethnoanthropology, M-PSI/01 General Psychology, SPS/07 Sociology of Health). Particular attention is paid to the Sciences of Prevention and Health Services (MED/44 Occupational Medicine and MED/42 Health Promotion and Hygiene).
2. Area of professional disciplines, aimed at acquiring the specific knowledge in the disciplines that characterize professional training. These include strictly characterizing disciplines (MED/45 General Nursing and Theory of Nursing, Clinical and Applied Nursing, Relational and Community Nursing, Pediatric Nursing, MED/47 Obstetric Assistance), strictly characterizing clinical disciplines (BIO/14 Pharmacology, MED/09 Internal Medicine and Medical Therapy, MED/18 General Surgery, MED/17 Infectious Diseases, MED/09 Geriatrics, MED/26 Neurology, MED/33 Orthopedics, MED/38 Neonatology and Pediatrics, MED/40 Gynecology and Obstetrics, MED/25 Psychiatry, M-PSI/08 Clinical Psychology), basic disciplines in the field of first aid (MED/09 Emergency Medicine, MED/18 Emergency Surgery, MED/41 Anesthesiology and Intensive Care, MED/45 Emergency and Critical Care Nursing). Particular attention is paid to ethics and professional deontology (MED/43 Forensic Medicine and MED/45 Professional Deontology). The internship (65 CFU) and the nursing laboratories (3 CFU), which are fundamental parts of the Degree Course, are given particular attention, both as regards the choice of locations and the timing of implementation with respect to the progress of the course of studies.
3. Area of integrative disciplines, including both the disciplines of health management (IUS/07 Labor Law, SECS-P/10 Business Organization, MED/45 Nursing Management) developed for the basic understanding of the management mechanisms of healthcare companies and in the perspective of an in-depth study in specialist degree courses, and the disciplines aimed at preparing statistical-informatics through basic modules of statistics (MED/01 Medical Statistics) and applied informatics (ING-INF/05 Information Processing Systems) which, with the carrying out of laboratory activities of statistics and informatics as well as the outlines of scientific research (MED/45 Methodology of Nursing Research and MED/42 Epidemiology), provide the theoretical/practical means for adequate and autonomous technical-scientific updating.
Students are also provided with a course in English language (Medical English) aimed also at understanding the scientific literature, with the possibility, provided by the University language services, of obtaining certifications such as PET and/or FCE.

Description of admission tests: The number of students admitted to the degree course is programmed each year based on regional needs.
The entrance exam is common to all degree courses in the health field and is held on the same day in all Italian universities.
The exam consists of a multiple-choice test on the following subjects: general culture, logical reasoning, mathematics, physics, chemistry and biology.
The ranking is drawn up on the basis of merit.
Function in the work context: Nurse (DM Health n. 739 of 14/9/94)
The functions performed concern the prevention of diseases, nursing care for the sick and disabled of all ages, health education and the health, the nurse:
a) participates in the identification of the health needs of the person and the community;
b) identifies the nursing care needs of the person and the community and formulates the related objectives;
c) plans, manages and evaluates the nursing care intervention;
d) ensures the correct application of diagnostic-therapeutic prescriptions;
e) for the performance of the functions, makes use of the support staff, where necessary.
Additional information:
http://www.unisi.it/dipartimenti/dipartimento-scienze-mediche-chirurgiche-e-neuroscienze
Description of specific training objectives: Nursing graduates are the health professionals to whom the tasks provided for in DM of the Ministry of Health n. 739 of 14/9/94, and subsequent amendments and additions, as well as the specific Code of Ethics, are assigned. The graduates will be responsible for general preventive, curative, palliative and rehabilitative nursing care, of a technical, relational and educational nature; their functions will concern the prevention and health education, care, therapeutic education, management, training, research and consultancy. Nursing graduates will promote the culture of health in the community, design and implement, in collaboration with other professional figures, training and educational interventions aimed at the person and the family, and will carry out autonomous professional activities aimed at the prevention, care and safeguarding of individual and collective health, using methodologies of planning for objectives of care for the person in the developmental, adult and geriatric age.
With regard to the specific training objectives of the course, nursing graduates will be able to: participate in the identification of the health needs of the person and the community; identify the nursing care needs of the person and the community and formulate the related objectives; plan, manage and evaluate the nursing care intervention, making use of support staff where necessary; ensure the correct application of diagnostic-therapeutic prescriptions. They will carry out self-training and training activities for students, support staff and newly hired staff; they will develop research activities aimed at producing new knowledge for the continuous improvement of care; they will participate in interdisciplinary working groups for the care of the citizen; they will provide consultancy for the development of care services.
Based on the specific training objectives described, the training course is structured over 3 years, including 6 semesters, and provides for a total of 180 CFU, of which 65 are to be acquired in training activities aimed at developing specific professional skills (internship). In order to achieve the specific objectives of the course, nursing graduates will have adequate theoretical and theoretical/practical preparation in three disciplinary areas aimed at the specificity of the profession:
1. Area of basic disciplines, aimed both at understanding the most relevant elements that are the basis of the physiological and pathological processes to which preventive, therapeutic, rehabilitative and palliative intervention is directed, and at maximum integration with other professions. The training activities include basic biomedical disciplines (BIO/10 Biochemistry, BIO/13 Biology and Fundamentals of Genetics, BIO/16 Anatomy and Elements of Histology, BIO/09 Physiology and Science of Nutrition, MED/04 General Pathology and Pathophysiology, MED/07 General and Clinical Microbiology and MED/08 Pathological Anatomy) and basic humanities disciplines (M-DEA/01 Demoethnoanthropology, M-PSI/01 General Psychology, SPS/07 Sociology of Health). Particular attention is paid to the Sciences of Prevention and Health Services (MED/44 Occupational Medicine and MED/42 Health Promotion and Hygiene).
2. Area of professional disciplines, aimed at acquiring the specific knowledge in the disciplines that characterize professional training. These include strictly characterizing disciplines (MED/45 General Nursing and Theory of Nursing, Clinical and Applied Nursing, Relational and Community Nursing, Pediatric Nursing, MED/47 Obstetric Assistance), strictly characterizing clinical disciplines (BIO/14 Pharmacology, MED/09 Internal Medicine and Medical Therapy, MED/18 General Surgery, MED/17 Infectious Diseases, MED/09 Geriatrics, MED/26 Neurology, MED/33 Orthopedics, MED/38 Neonatology and Pediatrics, MED/40 Gynecology and Obstetrics, MED/25 Psychiatry, M-PSI/08 Clinical Psychology), basic disciplines in the field of first aid (MED/09 Emergency Medicine, MED/18 Emergency Surgery, MED/41 Anesthesiology and Intensive Care, MED/45 Emergency and Critical Care Nursing). Particular attention is paid to ethics and professional deontology (MED/43 Forensic Medicine and MED/45 Professional Deontology). The internship (65 CFU) and the nursing laboratories (3 CFU), which are fundamental parts of the Degree Course, are given particular attention, both as regards the choice of locations and the timing of implementation with respect to the progress of the course of studies.
3. Area of integrative disciplines, including both the disciplines of health management (IUS/07 Labor Law, SECS-P/10 Business Organization, MED/45 Nursing Management) developed for the basic understanding of the management mechanisms of healthcare companies and in the perspective of an in-depth study in specialist degree courses, and the disciplines aimed at preparing statistical-informatics through basic modules of statistics (MED/01 Medical Statistics) and applied informatics (ING-INF/05 Information Processing Systems) which, with the carrying out of laboratory activities of statistics and informatics as well as the outlines of scientific research (MED/45 Methodology of Nursing Research and MED/42 Epidemiology), provide the theoretical/practical means for adequate and autonomous technical-scientific updating.
Students are also provided with a course in English language (Medical English) aimed also at understanding the scientific literature, with the possibility, provided by the University language services, of obtaining certifications such as PET and/or FCE.

UNIVERSITY: University of Siena
COURSE: [ME006] DIETETICS (QUALIFYING FOR THE HEALTHCARE PROFESSION OF DIETITIAN)
SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unisi.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10361

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year.
Duration: 3 years.
Program: 2020.
Status: Open.
Credits: 180 CFU.
Language: Italian.
Type: Degree Programs.
Regulation: D.M. 270/2004.
Degree Class: L/SNT3 - Class of degrees in technical health professions.
Inter-university Course: No.
Admission: Open Admission.
Location: Siena.
Department: Department of Medical, Surgical and Neurological Sciences.
Website: https://dietistica.unisi.it.
Informative Website: http://192.167.114.8/index.php.
Admission Requirements: Mandatory qualifications: [TSS].
Upper Secondary School Diploma.
Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse.
Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni.
Roles and Responsibilities: Student Mobility: ALESSANDRA VIVIANI, President/Chair of the teaching committee.
LAURA NIGI, Program, texts and objectives.
Admission requirements: Students in possession of a Secondary School Diploma or an equivalent foreign qualification may be admitted.
The required knowledge will include: elements of general culture and logical reasoning, mathematics, physics, chemistry, biology.
Knowledge of the English language is also required, at least at level A2/2 (CEFR).
The number of students admitted to the Degree Course is determined annually based on regional needs.
The entrance exam is common to all Degree Courses in the health field and is held on the same day in all Italian Universities.
Professional status conferred by the degree: Dietitian.
The dietitian, in addition to the NHS, also works in private facilities that have agreements with the NHS, both for diagnostics and research, accredited and non-accredited facilities, such as clinics and rehabilitation centers, Residential Healthcare Facilities, spas and wellness centers, medical and/or specialist clinics, school and company canteens, etc.
At the NHS level, there is expected to be an increase in the need for this figure in relation to the priority health problems highlighted by the WHO, such as cardiovascular accidents, nutrition-related diseases and lifestyle diseases, overweight and obesity, etc., for which the dietitian is a key figure in the prevention and rehabilitation process.
Current regulations allow for the performance of independent professional activities, in individual or associated professional practices, sports companies of various types, service cooperatives, etc.
The profession of dietitian is included in item 85.14.2 (Independent professional activities) of the ATECO classification of economic activities: health activities not carried out by hospitals or doctors, but by legally recognized personnel.
Characteristics of the final exam: The Degree Course qualifies students to practice the profession of Dietitian.
The final exam is worth 5 CFU and consists of:
a) Practical test during which the student must demonstrate that they have acquired the theoretical and practical knowledge and technical-operational skills specific to the professional profile of a Dietitian, the successful completion of which is considered a pass and allows access to the thesis discussion.
b) Preparation of a thesis and its presentation.
The grade is expressed in hundredths, with a possible distinction.
Knowledge required for admission: Students who have a Secondary School Diploma or another qualification obtained abroad and recognized as equivalent by the University may be admitted to the Degree Course in Dietetics.
The student must have adequate initial preparation, acquired in previous studies, regarding biology, chemistry, mathematics, and computer science. Knowledge of the English language is also required, at least at level A2/2 (CEFR).
The student's initial preparation will be assessed through the analysis of the errors found in the logic, chemistry, biology, physics-mathematics quizzes (multiple-choice questions) administered in the entrance exam, which is common to all Degree Courses in the health field.
If the verification of the knowledge required for admission is not positive, additional training obligations will be assigned to be completed in the first year of the course, as specified in the Didactic Regulations of the Course of Study.
Degree awarded: DEGREE IN DIETETICS.
Method of teaching: Conventional.
Knowledge and understanding: Area of Basic Disciplines.
Graduates in Dietetics who have successfully completed the Course of Study will possess basic knowledge of physics, chemistry, biochemistry, anatomy and histology, physiology, general pathology, medical genetics, microbiology, medical statistics,
in addition, they will acquire fundamental knowledge regarding, among other things, pathological anatomy, pharmacology, internal medicine, general surgery, anesthesiology, psychology, demo-ethno-anthropology, general sociology.
Therefore, they will be able to:
a) describe and interpret biological, physiopathological and pathological phenomena;
b) describe and interpret the molecular, morphological and functional bases of vital processes.
The learning objectives will be achieved through didactic tools of the ex-cathedra, seminar and interactive type.
The assessment of learning will be carried out through in-course tests, suitability assessments and final exams.
Area of Professionalizing Disciplines: During the course of study, graduates in Dietetics will acquire theoretical and practical knowledge in the various areas of competence, both for the public and private sectors, relating to biomedical research and assistance aimed at the numerous health fields involved with proper nutrition.
In particular, the Dietitian will be able to:
a) Assess the nutritional status of subjects in order to implement appropriate dietary prescriptions.
b) Collaborate with the bodies responsible for protecting the hygienic-sanitary aspects of the food service.
c) Elaborate, formulate and implement the diets prescribed by the doctor and checks their acceptability by the patient.
d) Collaborate with other professionals in the multidisciplinary treatment of eating disorders.
e) Study and develop the composition of food rations to meet the nutritional needs of population groups and plan the organization of food services for communities of healthy and sick people.
f) Carry out educational and informative activities aimed at disseminating principles of correct nutrition in order to allow the recovery and maintenance of good health of the individual, of communities and of population groups.
g) Organize and coordinate the specific activities relating to food in general and dietetics in particular.
The learning objectives will be achieved through didactic tools of the type: a) ex-cathedra lessons; b) professionalizing activity carried out in outpatient clinics and/or wards; c) attendance at the facilities where food is prepared (canteens of schools, hospitals, etc.; d) establishments that produce and/or modify food); e) attendance at laboratories and carrying out professionalizing internships; f) seminar and interactive activities.
The assessment of learning will be carried out through in-course tests, suitability assessments and final exams.
Area of Related and Integrative Disciplines: Dietitians must know and understand the English language; the processes of computerized management of information; acquire adequate statistical and computer skills; ability to understand international scientific literature and ability to exchange information;
The learning objectives will be achieved through didactic tools of the ex-cathedra, seminar and interactive type. The assessment of learning will be carried out through in-course tests, suitability assessments and final exams.
