UNIVERSITY: University of Siena
COURSE: [ME007] PHYSIOTHERAPY (QUALIFICATION FOR THE HEALTH PROFESSION OF PHYSIOTHERAPIST)
SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unisi.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10362

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year.
Duration: 3 years.
Program: 2020.
Status: Open.
Credits: 180 CFU.
Language: Italian.
Type: Degree Courses.
Regulation: D.M. 270/2004.
Degree Class: L/SNT2 - Class of degrees in Health rehabilitation professions.
Inter-university Course: No.
Programmed Access: Yes.
Location: Siena.
Department: Department of Medical, Surgical and Neurological Sciences.
Website: https://fisioterapia.unisi.it.
Informative Website: http://192.167.114.8/index.php.
Admission Requirements: Mandatory qualifications: [TSS].
Upper Secondary School Diploma.
Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse.
Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni.
Roles and Responsibilities: Student mobility: ALESSANDRA VIVIANI, President/President of the teaching committee. FEDERICA GINANNESCHI, President. FEDERICA GINANNESCHI.
Program, texts and objectives: Admission requirements: Students in possession of a Diploma of Upper Secondary School or an equivalent foreign qualification may be admitted.
The required knowledge will include: elements of general culture and logical reasoning, mathematics, physics, chemistry, biology.
Knowledge of the English language is also required, at least at level A2/2 (CEFR). The number of students admitted to the degree course is programmed annually based on regional needs.
The entrance test is common to all degree courses in the health field and is carried out on the same day in all Italian Universities.
Professional status conferred by the degree: Physiotherapist. The Physiotherapist works within the National Health System, in private accredited and contracted structures with the NHS, private practices and rehabilitation centers, Residential Health Facilities, spas, medical and/or polyclinic clinics, associations and sports clubs of various types, service cooperatives, non-governmental organizations, public or private prevention services.
The training provided by the Degree Course in Physiotherapy allows for rapid entry into the world of work, as well as access to the Master's Degree Course in Rehabilitation Sciences of Health Professions, or to University Master's Courses of the first level.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam consists of (D.I. 19 February 2009, art. 7):
a) a practical test during which the student must demonstrate that they have acquired the theoretical and practical knowledge and skills, as well as the technical and operational skills specific to the professional profile.
b) drafting of a thesis and its discussion.
The final exam is evaluated by a Commission. It consists of at least seven and no more than eleven members and includes at least two members appointed by the Professional Order, identified according to current regulations. During this test, the student must demonstrate that they have acquired scientific and theoretical-practical preparation, a methodology and skills that allow them professional, decision-making and operational autonomy for the exercise of the profession. The final exam contributes to the total credit load for 5 CFU and the attribution is expressed in 110/110 with possible honors.
Knowledge required for access: The course is programmed nationally pursuant to Article 1 of Law 264/1999; the provisions on national programming are annually issued by the Ministry of University and Research (MIUR) and reported in the competition notices.
Candidates who are in possession of a Diploma of Upper Secondary School or another qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable may be admitted to the Degree Course in Physiotherapy. The number of eligible students (potential training) is proposed based on the availability of teaching staff, teaching structures (classrooms, laboratories) and, in particular, the clinical-assistance structures that can be used for the conduct of practical activities, applying the standards defined by the competent teaching structure.
The number of students admitted to the Course is subsequently determined based on the relevant national and regional programming.
Among the knowledge necessary for admission, knowledge of the English language is required, at least at level A2/2 (CEFR). Basic knowledge of biology, chemistry, mathematics, physics and general culture, which characterize the entrance test for the courses of study in Health Professions, is also required. As a rule, given the high number of applications for admission to the Physiotherapy Degree Course and the resulting high score required, passing the entrance test guarantees an adequate level of basic knowledge. The student's initial preparation will, however, be evaluated through the analysis of the errors found in the logic, chemistry, biology, mathematics and physics quizzes (multiple-choice questions) administered in the entrance test.
In the event that the verification is not positive or results below the minimum established, additional training obligations will be assigned to be fulfilled in the first year of the course. In this sense, the teachers of the relevant modules will proceed with a brief course to recover the main knowledge in debt, which must still be filled in the first year.
Degree awarded: DEGREE IN PHYSIOTHERAPY.
Method of delivery of teaching: Conventional.
Knowledge and understanding: Area of Basic Sciences. Graduates in physiotherapy must have acquired the following knowledge and understanding:
a) knowledge of the biochemical and genetic mechanisms underlying human life, including the biological phenomena that occur in the cell and tissues;
b) ability to understand the structure of the human body and know its physiological mechanisms of functioning, with particular reference to the somatic and autonomic nervous system, the musculoskeletal and cardiovascular-respiratory system;
c) knowledge of the elements of psychology underlying the relationships between people, especially in a healthcare setting, and the pedagogical principles for correct communication and interpersonal relationships;
d) knowledge of the mechanisms of the organism's response to the main pathogenic factors and the etiology and pathogenesis of the main acute and chronic pathologies of interest to physiotherapy, including the general effects of their treatment.
Area of Physiotherapy Sciences. Graduates in physiotherapy must have acquired the following knowledge and understanding:
a) knowledge of the fundamental principles, and their historical evolution, of the methodology of rehabilitation and physiotherapy in all age groups;
b) knowledge of the etiopathogenesis and clinical manifestations of disorders of the musculoskeletal, neurological, cardiovascular-respiratory, visceral system, in all age groups;
c) knowledge of the techniques and methods of physiotherapy and rehabilitation applied to the specific clinical conditions of the musculoskeletal and cardiovascular-respiratory system, the central, peripheral and autonomic nervous system in all age groups;
d) knowledge of the methodology of research, the methods of scientific updating, the statistical and computer sciences applied to the specific field;
e) ability to understand the complexity of communication and relational aspects in the context and for the type of patients who need rehabilitation at all stages of life.
Managerial area. Graduates in physiotherapy must have acquired the following knowledge and understanding:
a) knowledge of the principles of maintaining well-being, health and preventing diseases through an appropriate lifestyle and the correct practice of physical exercise;
b) knowledge of the health problems of the individual, including disabilities, and/or of a community, also within the scope of health emergencies;
c) knowledge of the current professional regulations and the principles of medical law and the code of ethics applied to physiotherapy.

UNIVERSITY: University of Siena
COURSE: [ME009] ORTHOPTICS AND OPHTHALMOLOGICAL ASSISTANCE (ENABLING QUALIFICATION FOR THE HEALTHCARE PROFESSION OF ORTHOPTIST AND OPHTHALMOLOGY ASSISTANT)
SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unisi.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10364

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year.

Inter-University Course: No.

Location: Siena.
Department: Department of Medical, Surgical and Neurological Sciences.
Website: https://ortottica.unisi.it.
Informative Website: http://192.167.114.8/index.php.
Admission Requirements: Mandatory qualifications: [TSS].
Upper Secondary School Diploma.
Fees and Contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse.
Dates and Deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni.
Roles and Responsibilities: Student Mobility: ALESSANDRA VIVIANI, President/President of the teaching committee.
GIAN MARCO TOSI, President.
GIAN MARCO TOSI.
Program, Texts and Objectives: Access to further studies: RESEARCH DOCTORATE COURSES (CDR), SPECIALIST DEGREE COURSES/MASTER'S DEGREE COURSES - CLS/CLM, FIRST-LEVEL UNIVERSITY MASTER COURSES.
Admission Requirements: Students with a Diploma of Upper Secondary School or an equivalent foreign qualification may be admitted.
The required knowledge will include: elements of general culture and logical reasoning, mathematics, physics, chemistry, biology.
Knowledge of the English language is also required, at least at level A2/2 (CEFR).
The number of students admitted to the Degree Course is determined annually based on regional needs.
The entrance exam is common to all Degree Courses in the health field and is held on the same day in all Italian Universities.
Professional status conferred by the qualification.
Orthoptists and ophthalmology assistants can find employment within the National and Regional Health System (see national health contract), and can independently carry out activities provided for in the Professional Profile (14/09/1994 no. 743) in various fields: in orthoptic centers and hospital departments; in accredited university facilities and private practices; in pediatric neuropsychiatry services, rehabilitation services and pediatric departments; in forensic and occupational medicine services; in schools and low vision centers.
They carry out their work in collaboration with doctors and other healthcare professionals, and are personally responsible for the planning and quality of the professional activities carried out.
The generational turnover requires a necessary renewal to maintain and expand the number of orthoptic staff employed by the NHS.
The increase in central vascular accidents; the increase in degenerative retinal phenomena related to aging; road and workplace injuries; the increase in congenital refractive defects that imply good visual function, determine a forecast of an increase in the number of Orthoptists.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam has the value of a state exam enabling the exercise of the profession (Legislative Decree 502/1992, art. 6, paragraph 3) and consists of:
a) a practical test during which the student must demonstrate that they have acquired the theoretical and practical knowledge and technical-operational skills specific to the professional profile;
b) drafting of a thesis and its discussion.
See DM of February 19, 2009, art. 7.
5 CFU will be awarded for the final exam.
The final exam cannot be repeated more than once.
The criteria for awarding the merit score will include: evaluation of the skills demonstrated during the presentation and discussion of the paper; assessment of the technical skills demonstrated during the practical test.
The final score is expressed in hundredths.
If the overall score of 110/110 is reached, the president of the final exam commission puts to the vote the awarding of honors, which can only be awarded unanimously by those present.
Knowledge required for access: Students with a Diploma of Upper Secondary School or an equivalent foreign qualification may be admitted.
The required knowledge will include: elements of general culture and logical reasoning, mathematics, physics, chemistry, biology.
Knowledge of the English language is also required, at least at level A2/2 (CEFR).
Degree awarded: DEGREE IN ORTHOPTICS AND OPHTHALMOLOGICAL ASSISTANCE.
Teaching method: Conventional.
Knowledge and understanding: General Area.
At the end of the training course, graduates in Orthoptics and Ophthalmological Assistance will have acquired a wide range of knowledge, both in basic sciences and in the characterizing disciplines, as well as in the field of communication and professional management.
In particular, they must acquire solid cultural and scientific foundations in the field of:
- Propedeutic, basic, biomedical and psychological sciences, for the understanding of biological phenomena, the mechanisms of functioning of the ocular apparatus, motor and psychomotor development, physiological phenomena, also in correlation with the psychological, social and environmental dimensions of the disease.
- Orthoptic sciences, necessary to identify the preventive and rehabilitative needs of the individual through the evaluation of clinical data in the ophthalmological, orthoptic, psychomotor, cognitive and functional fields, for the extent and significance of the symptoms.
- Understanding the components that underlie the pathological processes on which the rehabilitative and/or therapeutic intervention is focused, in childhood, adulthood or geriatrics.
- Developing the best therapeutic, technical and methodological approaches to solve the patient's health problems.
- Designing and verifying the orthoptic intervention using objective evaluative methods and effective therapeutic methods, and all the appropriate diagnostic tools to resolve the case, proposing any therapeutic treatments such as occlusion, use of conventional or prismatic lenses and/or aids for the visually impaired, also in relation to the indications and prescriptions of the ophthalmologist.
- Interdisciplinary and medical-surgical sciences for the understanding of the physiopathological elements applicable to the different clinical situations, also in relation to diagnostic parameters.
- Sciences of Prevention and health services, to acquire the ability to analyze the health problems of the individual and/or a community and the responses of the health and social-assistance services to the main needs of citizens.
- Statistical sciences, quantitative methods and scientific updating, for the development of knowledge of statistical tools useful for the planning of the research process, some analytical methods to be chosen and used in the exercise of the profession, using the best scientific evidence available in the literature, in the field of Orthoptic Sciences.
- Health Management Sciences aimed at organizing rehabilitation activities through the definition of priorities, appropriateness and the use of available resources, both by delegating to collaborators the activities of competence, and by working in a team, ensuring continuity and quality of care.
- Knowledge of at least one language of the European Union, in addition to Italian, in the specific area of competence and for the exchange of general information and understanding of international scientific literature.
The tools and teaching activities available for the acquisition of knowledge are: classroom activities, laboratory activities, practical lessons in the laboratory and/or in equipped classrooms, internships, reading and interpretation of international literature.
The evaluation of this knowledge and the skills of understanding will be carried out through the following verification methods: oral exams, written exams, practical tests on patients or simulations, discussions of clinical cases, written reports, compilation of orthoptic sheets.

COURSE: [ME011] BIOMEDICAL LABORATORY TECHNIQUES (ENABLING TO THE HEALTH PROFESSION OF BIOMEDICAL LABORATORY TECHNICIAN)
SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unisi.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10366

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 3 years. Degree program: 2011. Status: Open. Credits: 180 CFU. Language: Italian. Type of degree: Bachelor's Degree. Regulatory Decree: D.M. 270/2004. Degree class: L/SNT3 - Class of degrees in technical health professions. Inter-university course: No. Admission: Open access. Location: Siena. Department: Medical Biotechnology. Website: https://laboratorio-biomedico.unisi.it. Informative website: http://unisi-sito04.prod.cineca.it/it/didattica/corsi-di-studio/laurea-delle-professioni-sanitarie/tecniche-di-laboratorio-biomedico. Admission requirements: Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]. Upper secondary school diploma. Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse. Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website "Study plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni. Roles and responsibilities: Student mobility: ALESSANDRA VIVIANI, President/President of the teaching committee. CRISTIANA BELLAN, President. CRISTIANA BELLAN. Program, texts and objectives: Access to further studies: RESEARCH DOCTORATE COURSES (CDR), SPECIALIST DEGREE COURSES/MASTER'S DEGREE COURSES - CLS/CLM, FIRST-LEVEL UNIVERSITY MASTER COURSES. Admission requirements: Students with a high school diploma or equivalent foreign qualification may be admitted. The required knowledge will include: elements of general culture and logical reasoning, mathematics, physics, chemistry, biology. Knowledge of the English language is also required, at least at level A2/2 (CEFR). The number of students admitted to the degree course is determined each year based on regional needs. The entrance exam is common to all degree courses in the health field and is held on the same day in all Italian universities. Professional status conferred by the degree. The main professional opportunities for a biomedical laboratory technician are employment in biomedical and biotechnological analysis laboratories in all public health facilities of the NHS and in private accredited and non-accredited biomedical and biotechnological facilities, both for clinical diagnostics and for research. It is estimated that out of approximately 30,000 employees, there is a need for a turnover of 5-6%. Further professional opportunities include employment in the field of animal health, in zooprophylactic laboratories, as well as in industries for the production of reagents and laboratory equipment, in the pharmaceutical, food and cosmetic industries. Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam is equivalent to a state exam enabling the practice of the profession (Legislative Decree 502/1992, art. 6, paragraph 3) and consists of: a) a practical test during which the student must demonstrate that they have acquired the theoretical and practical knowledge and technical-operational skills specific to the professional profile; b) preparation of a thesis and its presentation. See DM of February 19, 2009, art. 7. 5 CFU will be awarded for the final exam. The final exam cannot be repeated more than once. The criteria for awarding the merit score will include: evaluation of the skills demonstrated during the presentation and discussion of the paper; assessment of the technical skills demonstrated during the practical test. The final score is expressed in hundredths. If the overall score of 110/110 is reached, the president of the final exam commission puts to the vote the awarding of honors, which can only be awarded unanimously by those present. Knowledge required for admission: Students with a high school diploma or equivalent foreign qualification may be admitted. The required knowledge will include: elements of general culture and logical reasoning, mathematics, physics, chemistry, biology. Knowledge of the English language is also required, at least at level A2/2 (CEFR). Specific training objectives: The aim of the course is to provide students with the scientific basis and the theoretical and practical training necessary to be qualified to practice the profession of Biomedical Laboratory Technician and the methodology and culture necessary for lifelong learning, as well as a level of professional, decision-making and operational autonomy, so as to ensure, at the end of the training course, full mastery of all the necessary skills and their immediate applicability in the workplace. The specific training objectives consist in the acquisition of the essential theoretical knowledge derived from the basic sciences, from the perspective of their subsequent professional application and as a prerequisite for understanding the subjects that characterize the profession, the theoretical and practical knowledge of the professional sectors, with particular emphasis on innovative methodologies, and the ability to plan, implement and evaluate the technical-diagnostic activities related to biochemical, molecular biology, hematology and immunohematology, toxicology, pharmacology (including galenic and magistral preparations), radioimmunology, immunological and immunometric, microbiological and virological, genetic, cytological, histological and pathological anatomy (including techniques for diagnostic autopsy); knowledge of the most modern biotechnological methodologies and their applications; knowledge and evaluation of automation, computerization and communication; knowledge of management in the health sector; knowledge of the regulations and laws of the State that govern the technical profession, laboratory activities, medical-surgical devices and public health; the ability to implement and verify Quality Control and Assurance also in order to continuously improve the quality of care; the ability to collaborate with the various professional figures in group health activities; the ability to identify, prevent and address critical events related to the various types of risks associated with activities in the different areas of the laboratory; knowledge and ability to resolve emergency situations in the field of first aid; knowledge of the basics of research methodology and the ability to apply them in order to improve the efficiency and effectiveness of analysis and production processes; knowledge of the rules for the protection of workers' health (in particular, radiation protection); the ability to act in accordance with the disciplinary, ethical and deontological principles of the profession in the technical-diagnostic and production situations foreseen in the training project; the ability to interact and collaborate actively with interprofessional teams in order to plan and manage analysis and production activities, even in decentralized settings; teaching skills aimed at training personnel and tutoring students in internships; the ability to identify environmental risk factors, assess their effects on health and implement protection measures in the workplace; the method for independent study and lifelong learning, also through systematic bibliographic research and critical reading of scientific articles; a good level of knowledge of written and spoken English. Degree awarded: Bachelor's Degree in Biomedical Laboratory Techniques. Teaching delivery method: Conventional. Knowledge and understanding: Area of Basic Sciences: During the first year of the degree course, students acquire knowledge of the basic sciences of the professional disciplines. The subjects learned in this area, as they are preparatory, are essential for the subsequent acquisition of theoretical and practical knowledge in the field of the Biomedical Laboratory, and are also useful in biomedical research laboratories and in the fields of veterinary medicine, pharmacy and biotechnology. The lectures cover basic subjects such as physics and mathematics, chemistry and biochemistry, human anatomy and histology, physiology, and general pathology. Professional area: The educational path naturally continues with the in-depth study of the professional disciplines, which in different areas, but closely interdependent and complementary, lead the student to the completion of their academic path. These courses are aimed at understanding and deepening subjects such as clinical chemistry, microbiology, pathological anatomy and autopsy techniques with direct experience in the autopsy room, immunohematology, medical genetics, pharmacology and toxicology. Particular attention will be paid to the acquisition of specific knowledge and skills in the field of laboratory techniques, both classical and molecular, thanks to courses taught by staff from the specific scientific disciplinary sector of the professional profile. A fundamental part of the course is dedicated to internships and laboratory activities, which involve students in attending the laboratories of both university facilities and the affiliated laboratories of the A.O.U.S. This training allows them, in addition to acquiring work experience and competence, to understand and implement innovative methods that they will encounter during their profession. Knowledge of specific topics in the field of first aid and the ability to implement the basic maneuvers of resuscitation is also required, as is the ability to act in the field of prevention of risks in the places where they operate, implementing all the necessary measures for their own and others' safety, in the field of storage, use and disposal of radioactive isotopes necessary for laboratory diagnostics, in the field of health management to understand the management of health organizations and implement cost analysis with a view to their containment and/or optimization, in the field of information technology and acquiring the necessary skills to conduct research projects in the diagnostic field, adequately documented and supported by the necessary statistical tests.

Professional status conferred by the degree.
Expected employment and professional opportunities for graduates (DI 19 February 2009, Art. 4, paragraph 6).
The graduate in Medical Radiology Techniques for Imaging and Radiotherapy is a health professional who provides his/her services in the departments and services of Diagnostic Imaging, Radiotherapy or Nuclear Medicine of hospital and non-hospital facilities of the NHS and in similar private facilities and in scientific research and care institutes. He/she can also be employed in industries producing, sales agencies operating in the specific sector of diagnostic and therapeutic equipment and supplies, in general. The Technician also finds employment in university and extra-university research centers in the biomedical sector, and can also carry out independent professional activities.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam has the value of a state exam enabling the exercise of the profession (Legislative Decree 502/1992, art. 6, paragraph 3) and consists of:
a) a practical test during which the student must demonstrate that he/she has acquired the theoretical and practical knowledge and skills and the technical-operational skills specific to the professional profile;
b) preparation of a thesis and its discussion. See DM 19 February 2009, art. 7.
5 CFU will be awarded for the final exam.
The final exam cannot be repeated more than once.
The criteria for awarding the merit score will include: evaluation of the skills demonstrated during the presentation and discussion of the paper; assessment of the technical skills demonstrated during the practical test.
The final score is expressed in hundredths.
If the overall score of 110/110 is reached, the president of the final exam commission puts to the vote the awarding of honors, which can only be awarded unanimously by those present.
Knowledge required for access: Students with a Diploma of Upper Secondary School or an equivalent foreign qualification may be admitted. The required knowledge will include: elements of general culture and logical reasoning, mathematics, physics, chemistry, biology. Knowledge of the English language is also required, at least at level A2/2 (CEFR).
Degree awarded: DEGREE IN MEDICAL RADIOLOGY TECHNIQUES FOR IMAGING AND RADIOTHERAPY.
Method of teaching: Conventional.
Knowledge and understanding: Generic Area.
Graduates in Medical Radiology Techniques for Imaging and Radiotherapy will have acquired the scientific basis and theoretical-practical preparation, and therefore the skills necessary to correctly manage and use the various radiological techniques and methods for diagnostics and for radiotherapy treatments according to the standards and guidelines currently adopted by the medical and scientific community.
They will therefore be able to demonstrate a certain professional, decision-making and operational autonomy and guarantee full mastery in the performance of radiological examinations and radiotherapy treatments. They will know and apply rules and behaviors aimed at protecting patients, healthcare personnel and the general population from radiation.
They will have knowledge relating to the management of clinical risk and the methodology for research, but above all the right mindset that encourages continuous training and updating.
Area of Propedeutic, Biomedical and First Aid Sciences: Knowledge of the basic disciplines such as human anatomy, biology, physiology and other biomedical disciplines to understand the basic pathophysiological events that characterize the situations in which adequate care is required, even in emergencies and first aid.
Knowledge of basic computer and statistical concepts in the field of medical statistics, computer science, electrical and electronic measurements used in biomedical and computerized equipment, as well as understanding the use and management of such data and measurements.
Area of Prevention and Health Services Sciences: Know and understand the concept of health and disease. Know the determinants of health and the situations, risk factors, behaviors and activities that can compromise it, as well as the strategies for prevention, collective and individual protection systems, including interventions aimed at protecting the health and safety of workers and users.
Basic knowledge of the main health systems adopted by various countries and the evolution and organization of the Italian health system.
Area of Technical Diagnostic and Therapeutic Sciences: Knowledge of the technologies, techniques and radiological and radiotherapy methods in order to apply them correctly, aimed at the diagnostic question or optimal radiotherapy treatment, applying the necessary measures of radioprotection and safety. Knowledge to manage and improve the technical-diagnostic procedures for acquisition and processing of imaging and radiotherapy treatment according to scientific evidence and diagnostic-therapeutic guidelines. Ability to continuously update in line with technological evolution.
Area of Psychosocial, Ethical, Legal and Health Management Sciences: Know and understand the importance and organizational complexity of a health system, the need and usefulness of acting in accordance with regulations, ethics, with the aim of maintaining or improving the health conditions of the population. Understand the need for optimization and good management of the human and economic resources necessary for this purpose.
Understand the importance of correct working relationships, the interdependence of health activities, teamwork while maintaining but improving one's professional autonomy.
Area of Computer and Linguistic Sciences: Know and understand the English language in order to access international scientific literature, to understand manuals for use and maintenance of equipment and to exchange information with foreign users and colleagues.
Knowledge of basic computer concepts for understanding the processes of management, analysis, processing, computerized archiving of clinical-radiological data and images.

Program, Texts and Objectives: Access to further studies: RESEARCH DOCTORATE COURSES (CDR), SPECIALIST DEGREE COURSES/MASTER'S DEGREE COURSES - CLS/CLM, 1ST LEVEL UNIVERSITY MASTER COURSES.
Admission Requirements: Students with a High School Diploma or equivalent foreign qualification may be admitted.
The required knowledge will include: elements of general culture and logical reasoning, mathematics, physics, chemistry, biology.
Knowledge of the English language is also required, at least at level A2/2 (CEFR).
The number of students admitted to the Degree Course is determined each year based on regional needs.
The entrance exam is common to all Degree Courses in the health field and is held on the same day in all Italian Universities.
Professional Status conferred by the degree.
Expected Employment Opportunities for Graduates: For many years, the number of graduates has been insufficient to meet the demand. The professional opportunities, in relation to the ISTAT Classification, include specialized hospitals and nursing homes, and university institutes, clinics and polyclinics, equipped with centers for cardiac surgery, cardiology or oncology where hyperthermic anti-blast perfusion is performed.
Characteristics of the Final Exam: The final exam has the value of a state exam enabling the exercise of the profession (Legislative Decree 502/1992, art. 6, paragraph 3) and consists of:
a) a practical test during which the student must demonstrate that they have acquired the theoretical and practical knowledge and technical-operational skills specific to the professional profile;
b) preparation of a thesis and its discussion. See DM of February 19, 2009, art. 7.
5 CFU will be awarded for the final exam.
The final exam cannot be repeated more than once.
The criteria for assigning the merit score will include: evaluation of the skills demonstrated during the presentation and discussion of the paper; assessment of the technical skills demonstrated during the practical test.
The final score is expressed in hundredths.
If the overall score of 110/110 is reached, the president of the Final Exam Commission puts to the vote the awarding of honors, which can only be awarded unanimously by those present.
Knowledge required for access: Students with a High School Diploma or equivalent foreign qualification may be admitted. The required knowledge will include: elements of general culture and logical reasoning, mathematics, physics, chemistry, biology. Knowledge of the English language is also required, at least at level A2/2 (CEFR).
Degree awarded: DEGREE IN TECHNIQUES OF CARDIOCIRCULATORY PHYSIOPATHOLOGY AND CARDIOVASCULAR PERFUSION.
Teaching Method: Conventional.
Knowledge and Understanding: Cardiopulmonary Physiopathology: The graduate must have demonstrated a broad spectrum of knowledge and critical understanding of the physiopathological response of adult and pediatric patients with congenital and acquired heart diseases.
Basic Techniques of Extracorporeal Circulation: The graduate must demonstrate extensive knowledge of current clinical technologies, which must include at least:
a. Cardiopulmonary bypass.
b. Pumps, hemofiltration, gas exchange, oxygenators, hemodilution, myocardial protection and preservation, thermal regulation, perfusion circuits, pharmacological interventions, techniques for blood preservation including autotransfusion, platelet gel production, acute normovolemic hemodilution and bloodletting, monitoring, analysis and management of anticoagulation and hemostasis.
Advanced Techniques of Cardiopulmonary Support: The graduate must demonstrate good knowledge of advanced technologies for extracorporeal and intracorporeal life support. The knowledge must relate to the different types of support systems and their relative indications for use.

Location: Siena.
Department: Biomedical Technologies.
Website: https://igiene-dentale.unisi.it
Informative Website: http://unisi-sito04.prod.cineca.it/it/didattica/corsi-di-studio/laurea-delle-professioni-sanitarie/igiene-dentale
Admission Requirements: Mandatory qualifications: [TSS].
Upper Secondary School Diploma.
Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse.
Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni.
Roles and Responsibilities: Student mobility: ALESSANDRA VIVIANI, President/President of the teaching committee.
MARCO FERRARI, President.
MARCO FERRARI.
Program, texts and objectives: Admission requirements: Students in possession of a Diploma of Upper Secondary School or an equivalent foreign qualification may be admitted.
The required knowledge will include: elements of general culture and logical reasoning, mathematics, physics, chemistry, biology.
Knowledge of the English language is also required, at least at level A2/2 (CEFR).
The number of students admitted to the degree course is determined each year based on regional needs.
The entrance exam is common to all degree courses in the health field and is held on the same day in all Italian universities.
Professional status conferred by the degree: DENTAL HYGIENIST. According to the latest official estimates, there are approximately 7,000 dental hygienists working in Italy. In public facilities, the demand for these professionals is still too limited, especially for the care of long-term patients, the elderly and people with limited mobility who need daily care. In the private sector, however, the demand for this professional figure is very high, especially in the north-center and in large cities. No significant changes are expected in the future. The estimate of a possible increase is included in the overall assessment of the national need for graduates in health professions expressed by the Regions.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam, which has the value of a State Exam for the purpose of practicing the profession of Dental Hygienist (Legislative Decree 502/1992, art. 6, paragraph 3), is worth 5 CFU and consists of:
a) Practical exam during which the student must demonstrate that they have acquired the theoretical and practical knowledge and technical-operational skills specific to the professional profile of a Dental Hygienist, the successful completion of which is considered satisfactory and allows access to the thesis discussion.
b) Preparation of a thesis and its presentation, in compliance with D.I. 19 February 2009, art. 7.
The final score is expressed in hundredths.
The merit score is calculated starting from the arithmetic mean of the grades obtained during the training course, expressed as a proportion of 110 (rounded to the nearest whole number), which constitutes the basis of the calculation.
To this value will be added the score relating to the thesis discussion (compulsory experimental), up to a maximum of 10 points, awarded by the Commission which will evaluate:
1) the quality of the presentation and discussion of the thesis in relation to:
- clarity of presentation, essentiality of the presentation;
- richness and updating of scientific information;
- ability to answer questions from members of the Commission in a concise and comprehensive manner;
- completeness of the topic within the allotted time (15 minutes);
- ability to use audiovisual systems appropriately.
2) the quality of the work carried out by the candidate in relation to:
- originality and importance of the research;
- complexity of the experimental work;
- autonomy and actual role played by the candidate.
If the overall score of 110/110 is reached, the president of the final examination commission puts to the vote the awarding of honors, which can only be awarded unanimously by those present.
Knowledge required for access: Students who possess a Diploma of Upper Secondary School or another qualification obtained abroad and recognized as equivalent by the University may be admitted to the Degree Course in Dental Hygiene.
It is necessary to have adequate initial preparation, acquired in previous studies, with regard to mathematics, physics, chemistry and biology.
Access to the course is obtained by passing an entrance test, prepared by the Ministry.
The methods for access to the Degree Course and the content on which the entrance test is based are established by Law 264/1999 "Provisions on access to university courses" (Official Gazette No. 183 of 6-8-1999), as well as by specific ministerial decrees issued annually, concerning both the methods and content of the entrance tests for courses with programmed access at national level, and the definition of the places available for admissions to the Degree Course.
Enrollment in the Degree Course must take place within the deadlines decided annually by the competent structure for teaching.
Students who have been admitted to the course with a score below a predetermined minimum threshold will be assigned additional training obligations, to be completed within the first year of the course.
The adequacy of the student's preparation will be verified according to the methods indicated in the Didactic Regulations of the Degree Course.
Degree awarded: DEGREE COURSE IN DENTAL HYGIENE. Teaching delivery method: Conventional.
Knowledge and understanding: General area: Graduates in Dental Hygiene will have acquired theoretical and practical knowledge in the field of dental hygiene, applicable both in the public and private sectors, relating to assistance and biomedical research aimed at the numerous fields of oral and dental hygiene (prevention, maintenance of good health, therapy in case of dental and/or systemic pathologies and incorrect oral hygiene).
They will possess knowledge in the field of dentistry, genetics, pathological anatomy, pharmacology, microbiology, and food science, which they will acquire through basic knowledge of physics, biochemistry, anatomy and histology, physiology, and general pathology.
In particular, they will have acquired knowledge and skills regarding the technical aspects and practical skills relating to dental hygiene and health education, thanks to the courses taught by staff from the specific scientific-disciplinary sector of the professional profile, the professional activity to be carried out directly in the clinic and/or in the ward, the interpersonal relationship with defined groups of people (school population, patients in protected and care facilities, etc.) under the guidance of qualified and experienced staff, including the tutors of the professional profile, attendance in dental and dental hygiene facilities, laboratories and the carrying out of professional internships. All this will allow the graduate to acquire knowledge that enables them to recognize particular situations and to apply, on a case-by-case basis, what is recognized by the scientific community as "best practice" for that particular problem.
The acquisition of this knowledge and ability to understand will be made possible by the carrying out of the didactic activity as a whole (lectures, seminars, laboratory exercises, internships, preparation of the final exam, etc.) and will be verified by means of exams, the methods of which will depend on the type of didactic activity provided (satisfactory for the internship (1st semester), English language and various types of laboratories, score expressed in thirtieths for the lectures, the self-study activities and the internship of the 2nd semester of the 1st, 2nd and 3rd year, score expressed in hundredths for the final exam).

Program, Texts and Objectives: Admission Requirements: The Master's Degree course in Medicine and Surgery has a limited number of places (Law 2 August 1999 n.264), and a five-year Upper Secondary School Diploma or an equivalent qualification obtained abroad is required for admission. As established annually by ministerial decrees, a basic knowledge of logic and general culture, biology, chemistry, physics and mathematics is also required. The number of students who can access the course, the content and methods of selection are established by the Ministry of University and Research MIUR at a national level.
Professional Status conferred by the degree:
Surgeon. Graduates in Medicine and Surgery carry out their activities as doctors and surgeons in various professional clinical, health and biomedical fields. The Master's Degree in Medicine and Surgery is also a requirement for access to Medical Specialization Schools and the Training Course for General Practitioners.
Characteristics of the final exam: To be admitted to the final degree examination, the student must have attended all courses and passed the relevant exams.
The final degree examination is accessed after completing a practical test for each area of internship at the end of the TPVES (Practical Evaluation Internship for the State Examination), aimed at verifying the level of technical preparation for the authorization to practice the profession. In addition, in order to be admitted to the degree examination, the student must demonstrate the acquisition of specific skills relating to the preparation of a scientific paper in English.
The final degree examination focuses on the discussion of a thesis developed in an original way by the student under the guidance of a supervisor, as provided for in the didactic regulations of the Master's degree course; the figure of a co-supervisor may also be provided.
The degree thesis can also be written and discussed in English.
The final degree mark is influenced by the average of the marks obtained in the curricular exams, the evaluation of the thesis during the discussion, and the evaluation of other activities according to the methods established in the didactic regulations of the Master's degree course.
The final degree mark is expressed in hundredths, with possible honors.
Knowledge required for access: Students in possession of a secondary school diploma or another qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable, are admitted to the single-cycle Master's degree course in Medicine and Surgery.
The study course has national programmed access.
The methods for accessing the Master's Degree course in Medicine and Surgery and the content on which the admission test is based are established by Law 2 August 1999 n. 264 "Provisions on access to university courses" and subsequent amendments and additions, as well as by specific ministerial decrees issued annually concerning both the methods and content of the admission tests for courses with national programmed access and the definition of places available for admission to the Master's Degree course in Medicine and Surgery.
The test focuses on scientific subjects such as biology, chemistry, physics and mathematics, as provided for by the current regulations relating to access to courses with a limited number of places at national level.
Students who pass the admission test must have a level of knowledge of the English language at least equal to A2/2.
Adequate initial preparation in biology, chemistry and physics is also required.
Verification of the possession of these knowledge is mandatory. This will therefore be carried out in the case of admitted students who have obtained a score lower than that fixed annually by the Teaching Committee, with the assignment of specific training obligations (OFA) to be fulfilled in the first year of the course with additional hours of lessons.
Degree awarded:
DEGREE IN MEDICINE AND SURGERY.
Method of teaching: Conventional.
Knowledge and understanding:
General Area: Master's degree graduates must have demonstrated knowledge and understanding skills that enable them to develop and/or apply original ideas within the context of biomedical and translational research. The following learning objectives must be achieved:
Scientific Bases of Medicine
1) Know the structure and normal functionality of the organism as a complex of biological systems in continuous adaptation.
2) Be able to interpret the morphological and functional anomalies of the organism that are found in the various diseases.
3) Be able to identify normal and abnormal human behavior.
4) Know the determinants and main risk factors for health and disease and the interaction between man and his physical and social environment.
5) Know the molecular, cellular, biochemical and physiological mechanisms that maintain the homeostasis of the organism.
6) Know the life cycle of man and the effects of growth, development and aging on the individual, the family and the community.
7) Know the etiology and natural history of acute and chronic diseases.
8) Know the epidemiology, health economics and the basics of health management.
9) Know the principles of drug action and their uses, and the effectiveness of the various pharmacological therapies.
10) Know and be able to implement the main biochemical, pharmacological, surgical, psychological, social and other interventions in acute and chronic diseases, in rehabilitation and in terminal care.
The learning objectives are achieved through didactic tools of the ex-cathedra, seminar, interactive type and through attendance at scientific structures of the Department. The verification of the acquisition of the results is carried out through profit checks (in itinere and final).
INTERDISCIPLINARY APPROACH TO THE PATIENT AND THE HEALTH NEEDS RELATED TO GENDER, AGE AND BELONGING TO DIFFERENT SOCIOCULTURAL CONTEXTS.
Provide the tools to develop a series of skills such as learning through the resolution of clinical problems (PBL), teamwork, deductive reasoning and interpersonal communication.
Encourage cultural exchanges between teachers of different disciplines.
PHYSIOPATHOLOGY, IMMUNOLOGY AND CLINICAL MICROBIOLOGY: Know the tools for understanding the etiopathogenetic and diagnostic mechanisms of diseases.
Know and use the specific language.
FUNDAMENTAL PHYSIOPATHOLOGICAL MECHANISMS OF DIAGNOSTICS AND DRUG ACTION: Know the etiology of diseases and the principles of drug action.
Know and be able to interpret the morphofunctional changes that underlie diseases.
Know the use of technologies and diagnostic tools dedicated to the care of health.
BASIC SEMEIOLOGICAL, CLINICAL AND THERAPEUTIC MEDICINE: Know the semiological tools at the basis of the examination of the physical and mental state of the patient.
Know the methods of collecting the patient's medical history.
Know the specific action of drugs.
BASIC SEMEIOLOGICAL, CLINICAL AND SURGICAL THERAPY: Know the semiotic tools essential for the main surgical pathologies. Understanding of the main pathologies of surgical interest.
MORPHOLOGY AND INTEGRATED BIOLOGICAL FUNCTIONS OF HUMAN ORGANS, SYSTEMS AND APPARATUS: Structural and functional organization of the human body from the microscopic to the macroscopic level: cells, tissues and morpho-functional characteristics of all the apparatus of the human body.

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year Duration: 2 years. Course structure: 2019. Status: Open. Credits: 120 CFU. Language: Italian. Type: Master's Degrees. Regulations: D.M. 270/2004. Degree class: LM/SNT2 - Class of Master's Degrees in Rehabilitation Sciences of Health Professions. Inter-university course: No. Admission: Open access. Location: Siena. Department: Department of Medical, Surgical and Neurological Sciences. Website: https://professioni-sanitarie.unisi.it. Informative website: http://192.167.114.8/index.php. Admission requirements: Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]. Upper secondary school diploma. Optional qualifications (choose from the following): [DU + TSS], University Diploma. [L2 + TSS], First-level degree. [TS + TSS], Foreign qualification. [SANIT + TSS], Professional qualification. [DR], Regional school diploma. [S.F.S.], School for special purposes. Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of tuition fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse. Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni. Roles and responsibilities: Student mobility: ALESSANDRA VIVIANI, President/President of the teaching committee. ANDREA POZZA, Program, texts and objectives. Admission requirements: Those who hold a degree in one of the health professions of the SNT/2 class (Rehabilitation Professions: professional educator, physiotherapist, speech therapist, orthoptist - ophthalmology assistant, podiatrist, therapist of neuro and psychomotor development of the evolutionary age, technician of psychiatric and psychosocial education and rehabilitation, occupational therapist) for whom no educational debts are foreseen; and/or a qualification equivalent to the current regulations or a qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable by the competent bodies with educational debts, which will be indicated by the Didactic Regulations of the CDLM, may be admitted to the Master's Degree Course in "Rehabilitation Sciences of Health Professions". Access to the course requires the theoretical and practical knowledge relating to the educational objectives of the Degree Courses of the health professions of the L/SNT2 class. The course has a limited number of places, defined annually by the Ministry of University and Scientific Research. Knowledge required for admission: theory and practice relating to the exercise of health and rehabilitation professions, general culture and logical reasoning; specific regulation of professional practice and health legislation, culture, science and mathematics, statistics, computer science and English, humanities and social sciences. Professional status conferred by the qualification: Manager/coordinator/responsible for advanced processes in the field of rehabilitation. The Master's Degree graduate in Rehabilitation Sciences of Health Professions finds employment within the National and Regional Health Systems, in private accredited and affiliated structures with the NHS (Clinics, rehabilitation centers, Residential Health Facilities, spas and wellness centers, medical and/or polyclinic clinics) where managerial skills in the area of management, applied research and training are required. Holding a Master's Degree is a legislative and contractual requirement for positions of management and teaching in the NHS (Law 43/2006). Manager - Responsible for the professional activities of the rehabilitation area, both in organizational and teaching fields. The Master's Degree graduate in Rehabilitation Sciences of Health Professions mainly finds employment within the National and Regional Health Systems, in private accredited and affiliated structures with the NHS (Clinics, rehabilitation centers, Residential Health Facilities, spas and wellness centers, medical and/or polyclinic clinics) where managerial skills in the area of management, applied research and training are required. Furthermore, holding a Master's Degree is a legislative and contractual requirement for holding the position of Director of Professional Teaching for the three-year and/or master's degree courses in the specific area. Characteristics of the final exam: To be admitted to the final Master's Degree exam, the student must have passed all the exams, obtained the required qualifications and received a positive evaluation for all internships. The final exam for obtaining the qualification, corresponding to 6 credits, consists of two parts: a) an English language test consisting of reading and translating a scientific article in the rehabilitation area; b) discussion of a written paper (thesis) with an original and/or experimental or theoretical-applied nature, concerning the in-depth study of managerial, research, training and advanced professional methodologies specific to one's professional field. If the overall score of 110/110 is reached, the president of the examination board puts to the vote the awarding of honors, which can only be awarded unanimously by those present. Knowledge required for admission: The Master's Degree course in Rehabilitation Sciences of Health Professions has limited access. To access the selection, it is required to hold a three-year university degree or diploma belonging to the L-SNT/2 class, or another qualification obtained abroad recognized as suitable, or another equivalent qualification. The number of students admitted will be decided each year by Ministerial Decree. The curricular requirements are met by holding a degree in one of the health professions of the L-SNT/2 class (Rehabilitation Professions: professional educator, physiotherapist, speech therapist, orthoptist - ophthalmology assistant, podiatrist, therapist of neuro and psychomotor development of the evolutionary age, technician of psychiatric and psychosocial rehabilitation, occupational therapist) and/or a qualification equivalent to the current regulations or a qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable by the competent bodies, which will be indicated by the Didactic Regulations of the CDLM. Access to the course requires the theoretical and practical knowledge relating to the educational objectives of the Degree Courses of the health professions of the L/SNT2 class. The initial preparation of the student is evaluated through the results obtained in the entrance test for the course, relating to the knowledge required for admission: theory and practice relating to the exercise of health and rehabilitation professions; general culture and logical reasoning; regulation of the specific professional practice and health legislation; scientific-mathematical culture, statistics, computer science and English, humanities and social sciences. The verification of personal preparation is mandatory in any case, and only students who have the curricular requirements can access it; in particular, having these requirements cannot be considered as verification of personal preparation. Qualification awarded: MASTER'S DEGREE IN REHABILITATION SCIENCES. Teaching delivery method: Conventional. Knowledge and understanding: 1) AREA OF ADVANCED REHABILITATION SCIENCES AND RESEARCH METHODOLOGY. Master's degree graduates in Rehabilitation Sciences of Health Professions, who have completed the course of study, must have acquired the following knowledge and understanding skills: a) Knowledge of the guidelines and advanced clinical progress in the disciplines that form the basis of the rehabilitation area; b) Knowledge of advanced clinical progress and the latest research in the rehabilitation field, in neurology, pediatrics, cardio-respiratory, musculoskeletal, otorhinolaryngology, orthoptics, and psychiatry; c) In-depth knowledge of the clinical components that can be rehabilitated, with reference to the profiles included in the class; d) In-depth knowledge of statistical sciences applied to biomedical research and sources of scientific updating, aimed at developing original studies and research projects. 2) AREA OF MANAGEMENT AND MANAGEMENT SCIENCES. Master's degree graduates in Rehabilitation Sciences of Health Professions, who have completed the course of study, must have acquired the following knowledge and understanding skills: a) Knowledge of the principles of public law and administrative law applicable to the relations between administrations and users involved in health services; b) Knowledge of the main theories and techniques of business organization and the processes of optimizing the use of human, computer and technological resources; c) Knowledge of the essential elements of business organization and the management of human resources, with particular reference to issues in the health field; d) Knowledge of the epidemiological methodology relating to the health and rehabilitation field. 3. AREA OF EDUCATION AND TRAINING SCIENCES. Master's degree graduates in Rehabilitation Sciences of Health Professions, who have completed the course of study, must have acquired the following knowledge and understanding skills: a) Knowledge of pedagogical models, theories of learning from experience and tutorial methodologies; b) Knowledge of the "core" principles underlying training, at the basic, post-basic and continuing levels, with specific reference to the health professions sector; c) Knowledge of the principles underlying the historical and educational evolution of health professions; d) Knowledge of the method for independent study and for continuing education; 4. AREA OF PROFESSIONAL COMMUNICATION. Master's degree graduates in Rehabilitation Sciences of Health Professions, who have completed the course of study, must have acquired the following knowledge and understanding skills: a) Knowledge of the theories of communication and relational development at the level of the individual, groups and organizations; b) Knowledge of verbal, non-verbal and written communication techniques for managing interpersonal relationships with individuals, groups, patients and their families; c) Knowledge of the evolution and history of health professions; d) Knowledge of the multicultural and intercultural phenomena of contemporary society.

Upper Secondary School Diploma.
Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse

Roles and Responsibilities: Student Mobility, ALESSANDRA VIVIANI, President/President of the teaching committee, PAOLO VENTURI.
Program, texts and objectives.
Admission requirements: To be admitted to the degree program, applicants must possess a five-year upper secondary school diploma or another equivalent qualification obtained abroad that is recognized as valid, as well as a four-year upper secondary school diploma and the related supplementary year, or, if no longer available, the assigned educational debt.
It is also necessary to have the knowledge required by the didactic regulations of the study program.
Professional status conferred by the degree:
Administrative, communication and organizational staff (administrative secretaries and general affairs technicians), department secretaries, head secretaries of companies, heads of general and secretarial services, communication officers in small and medium-sized companies or public bodies.
Administrative staff at diplomatic and consular representations and at international organizations: Italian diplomatic and consular representations abroad and of foreign states in Italy; intergovernmental, universal and regional organizations (in particular, European). Professional and political activities within local public institutions that require external relations with organizations belonging to international organizations (in particular the EU).
Officials of public and private companies dealing with commercial relations with foreign countries: industrial and tertiary sector companies operating in the public or private sector.
International relations officers in public and private offices and in non-governmental organizations: offices of the state and local administration, universities and research institutions, private companies, non-governmental organizations.
Human resources management staff, official, with medium and senior level, in companies, including multinationals, in personnel selection, labor consultant, industrial relations consultant, employee in temporary work agencies.
Communication staff, publicist and press officer: press officer, communication officer in companies, employee in the institutional relations and public relations offices in associations, parties, companies and institutions.
Staff for the collection, management and transfer of information: data analyst within companies, junior researcher in research institutes, responsible for archiving and analysis in small consulting companies, in law firms and debt collection agencies, data manager and data technician in public offices, university departments.
Cooperation officer: The development worker works for the UN and for other national and international (governmental and non-governmental) organizations involved in cooperation.
Staff for public services for the release of certifications and documentation: Graduates who wish to enter this professional field find employment in public administrations and in organizations that carry out control and verification services for public administrations.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam completes the individual study course and consists of a written report (thesis), prepared individually by the student on a topic that represents an in-depth study of issues related to the disciplines already taught in the study program. It is prepared under the supervision of a teacher of the study program, possibly in collaboration with other teachers or experts. It aims to evaluate the skills and competencies acquired by the student and in particular his ability to apply them in the analysis of a specific topic or problem and in the autonomous development of an illustrative and interpretative reconstruction, accompanied by the expression of his own evaluations and judgments. In the public discussion of the report, the candidate is required to confirm his knowledge of the subject and in particular to demonstrate his autonomous ability to communicate and discuss the analytical and interpretative results achieved.
The final grade, expressed in hundredths, and any honors, is also determined by reference to the weighted average of the grades obtained in the exams taken. The minimum overall grade for passing the final exam is 66/110. The Commission, based on the papers and the outcome of the discussion, may award up to a maximum of 6 points in addition to the average as determined above. 7 CFU are awarded for the final exam.
Knowledge required for admission: To be admitted to the Degree Course, applicants must possess a secondary school diploma or another equivalent qualification obtained abroad that is recognized as valid. The knowledge required for admission to the Degree Course in Political Science is as follows: 1) basic knowledge of contemporary history, including national, European and international experience; 2) knowledge of the evolution of the Italian and European political-institutional system; 3) knowledge of the English language at least at level A2/2; 4) basic knowledge of calculation, computer science and logic. The verification of the knowledge required above takes place according to the criteria described in Framework A3.b and the methods will be better defined in the didactic regulations of the Degree Course. Participation in the entrance test is to be understood as mandatory for all those enrolled in the first year of the L-36, on the dates foreseen each year by the Teaching Committee, and in any case before the start of the second year, failing which the academic career will be suspended until the next test is taken. Students who do not pass the verification will be assigned additional training obligations (OFA) to be completed in the first year of study through the appropriate remedial courses offered by the Department or, for those who have not been able to attend, by obtaining the material of the aforementioned courses for the purpose of an oral verification that will be agreed upon with the teacher in charge of the training/remedial course. The Department has also activated a personalized tutoring service, assigning a certain number of enrolled students to each teacher, and if requested by the students individually, the result of the admission test will be discussed with the tutor, who will provide the appropriate guidance.
Participation in these tests is mandatory, but does not affect access or enrollment in the Degree Course. The methods of carrying out the verification test will be better defined in the didactic regulations of the Degree Course. Students who do not pass the verification will be assigned additional training obligations to be completed in the first year of study through the appropriate remedial courses organized by the Department of Law; at the end of the remedial courses, the acquired preparation will be verified. In case of persistent shortcomings, additional readings will be recommended and the entire training path of the student will be periodically monitored by the tutor teachers.
Degree awarded: Degree in Political Science.
Method of delivery: Conventional.
Knowledge and understanding:
Generic area: Graduates acquire a basic, multidisciplinary training, characterized by the possession of diversified but closely integrated knowledge in the historical-political, economic, legal, political science, sociological and linguistic fields. They are therefore able to understand and interpret the structural arrangements, the dynamics of change and the main phenomena of contemporary society and in particular:
- the social and political-institutional transformations at national, European and international level, framing them in a historical perspective and in the global context;
- the main issues relating to the processes of political and socio-economic development in the various territorial contexts, European and extra-European; the principles, provisions and applications of international law; the dynamics of international relations and foreign policy;
- the legal, organizational and management issues of the systems and operational procedures of public and private administrations;
- the economic and social development processes within the national territory with particular attention to the third sector (cooperation and social utility).
Historical-political area: Comparative study of the political, social and economic history of the contemporary era and the context of the present time, with in-depth studies dedicated to political, economic and social changes, doctrines and political institutions, government and territory and political, diplomatic and legal issues relating to international relations and foreign policy.
Economic area: Study of macro and micro economics, determination of economic equilibrium, issues of economic policy, economic behavior of the State and private operators; functioning of the markets, economy of the third sector and knowledge of territorial and sustainable development policies; geographical analysis of socio-economic phenomena; statistical surveys; demographic studies and demographic trends in developing countries; history of policies and socio-economic transformations.
Legal area: Study of the fundamental principles of the Italian Constitution and the sources of law; comparative methodology in the field of constitutional law; organization and administrative activity; individual and collective labor relations; regulation of the labor market; tax law; fundamental outlines of Italian private law; history of international law and European law; origins, development and content of international law and the international community; overall system of international organization and European Union law.
Political science area: Study of the nature of the State, doctrines and political philosophies; study of political regimes and transition processes, the Italian political system and the system of parties in Italy and Europe; history, dynamics and structures of European institutions; social research and methodology of political science.
Sociological area: Study of classical and contemporary sociological thought and criticism; processes of social transformation and social integration; transformation of societies; development and international cooperation; migration and multiculturalism.
Linguistic area: Knowledge of written and spoken English and a second language of the European Union, chosen between French or Spanish.
