UNIVERSITY: University of Siena
COURSE: [PE003] SOCIAL WORK
SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unisi.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10216

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year.
Duration: 3 years.
Program: 2017.
Status: Open.
Credits: 180 CFU.
Language: Italian.
Type: Degree Courses.
Regulation: D.M. 270/2004.
Degree Class: L-39 - Class of degrees in Social Work.
Inter-university Course: No.
Open Access: Yes.
Location: Siena.
Department: Department of Social, Political and Cognitive Sciences.
Website: https://scienze-servizio-sociale.unisi.it
Informative Website: http://www.dispoc.unisi.it
Admission Requirements:
Mandatory qualifications: [TSS].
Upper Secondary School Diploma.
Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse.
Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni.
Roles and Responsibilities: Student Mobility, ALESSANDRA VIVIANI, President/President of the teaching committee, ANDREA VALZANIA.
Program, texts and objectives: Access to further studies.
Specialist or Master's Degree Courses, First-level University Master's Degrees.
Admission requirements: To be admitted to the degree course, it is necessary to have a five-year upper secondary school diploma or another equivalent qualification obtained abroad that is recognized as suitable, as well as a four-year upper secondary school diploma and the related supplementary year, or, if no longer active, the assigned educational debt.
It is also necessary to have the knowledge required by the didactic regulations of the study course.
Professional status conferred by the degree: Social worker or, after passing the state exam, Social Assistant. Social workers can work in both the public and private sectors, with activities ranging from social worker functions to more organizational and coordination tasks. In particular, it is possible to find employment in Ministries, hospitals, local health authorities, private clinics, family homes, prisons, reception centers, juvenile courts, addiction centers, as well as in research activities (therefore, Universities and research institutions).
Typical areas of work are health and hospital companies, municipalities and their consortia, the external prison execution office and the social service office for minors of the Ministry of Justice, and all the bodies and institutions that make use of the skills of the Social Assistant; furthermore, Social Assistants are increasingly requested by the non-profit sector (for example, in social cooperatives) in the management of health and care residences, day centers, family homes, etc.
Characteristics of the final exam: The degree is obtained after passing a final exam which consists in the preparation of a written report, also in a foreign language, accompanied by the necessary bibliographic references, on topics previously agreed with the teacher responsible for one of the courses present in the candidate's curriculum and subsequently evaluated by a Commission composed according to the current regulations.
Knowledge required for access: Access to the Degree Course is open to all those who have a five-year upper secondary school diploma or another qualification obtained abroad and recognized as suitable under current laws.
The knowledge required for access to the Degree in Social Work Sciences is as follows: 1) basic knowledge of contemporary history not limited to the national and European experience, but also extended to extra-European events; 2) knowledge of the evolution of the Italian and European political-institutional system; 3) knowledge of the English language at least at level A2/2.
Regarding the methods of evaluating the student's initial preparation, the Department of Social, Political and Cognitive Sciences organizes, together with other Italian Universities and with methodologies shared at a national level, an entrance test, the outcome of which does not preclude the possibility of enrolling in one of the study courses offered.
The test is structured in multiple-choice questions divided into four areas of verification:
1. Italian language
2. English language (for those who do not have a certification of at least level B1)
3. Elements of civic education
4. History of the 19th and 20th centuries.
Furthermore, during the test, the IT skills possessed by the students at the ECDL core module level are also assessed. Any additional training obligations will be met through supplementary training activities within the first year of the course.
Since the Department has long activated a personalized tutoring service, assigning a specific number of enrolled students to each teacher, the result of the test will be discussed with the tutor, also in order to provide for an individualized training plan, aimed at filling the gaps that have emerged.
Degree awarded: Degree in Social Work Sciences.
Method of teaching: Conventional.
Knowledge and understanding: General area. The graduate in Social Work Sciences must have basic interdisciplinary knowledge to allow the understanding of the individual in his social action, as well as the knowledge of the institutions (local, national and international) in their skills and functions. To this end, the list of courses in the curriculum is structured following a criterion of gradualness, from broad and general areas (sociology, anthropology, psychology, law, economics) to more specific areas (professional courses that characterize), up to specific disciplinary sectors (such as the sociology of migrations or criminal law).
Sociological and social history knowledge are aimed at the ability to interpret the social structure and macro-phenomena, as well as the events of change that have characterized contemporary society, in the light of the most recent literature, both Italian and international. Knowledge of the constitutional principles and administrative regulations, including European directives, allows the understanding of laws and the organization of collective social life and public and private institutions. Knowledge of private law subjects is essential to act not only for the protection of individual rights but in particular for family law and the protection of minors; equally important is the knowledge of social security law applied to the world of work. Knowledge of general and social psychological notions offers the student the ability to understand individuals and groups in their characteristics and dynamics and therefore represents the basis for the ability to interact not only with the users of the services but with colleagues, superiors, etc. Other essential knowledge, to be deepened, is in particular related to social policies (local, regional, national, EU) and all the professional methodologies and techniques. Finally, the B1 level of knowledge of the English language represents an overall necessary mastery.
Due to the interdisciplinary and professionalizing nature of the Degree Course, all methods and types of activities contribute to the training objectives: the lectures, evenly distributed in two annual semesters and in the three years of the course, with the relative exercises and thematic seminars for in-depth study, together with the individual study of the students on the recommended texts. The verification of the results takes place, depending on the nature and the total number of credits of the individual courses, both with intermediate written tests, generally in the classroom, and especially with final tests in written and oral form.

Program, texts and objectives Admission requirements: A three-year Bachelor's Degree as required by the regulations of the Master's Degree Program.
Professional status conferred by the degree: Specialists in international relations in the public and private sectors. Typical career opportunities for graduates of the program are:
- diplomatic career;
- European institutions;
- international governmental and non-governmental organizations;
- development cooperation agencies;
- companies and other private organizations operating in the international field (multinationals, banks, insurance companies, trade associations and volunteer organizations);
- ministerial offices, embassies and consulates, cultural institutes abroad;
- research and study centers.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam consists of a discussion of a thesis chosen by the student from a course in the individual study plan and on a topic agreed upon with the teacher responsible for that course (thesis supervisor). A co-supervisor is required.
The thesis must be developed in an original way by the student under the guidance of the thesis supervisor and must have the following characteristics: a) be the result of thorough research; b) demonstrate the candidate's ability in the organization and articulation of the work; c) be provided with adequate bibliographic references and be precise in the references and citation of sources; d) verify the issues related to the subject of the study with a scientifically correct method, also showing the candidate's critical skills. At the student's discretion, and subject to the favorable opinion of the thesis supervisor, the student may submit the thesis in a foreign language.
The final grade is expressed in hundredths with possible honors.
Knowledge required for admission: Admission to the Course of Study is subject to the possession of a Bachelor's or three-year university degree, or other qualification obtained abroad and deemed suitable.
In order to be admitted to the course, it is necessary to have adequate personal preparation and certain curricular requirements.
The curricular requirements are met by holding a degree in one of the following classes:
L-11 (foreign languages and cultures)
L-12 (linguistic mediation);
L-14 (legal services);
L-15 (tourism sciences);
L-16 (administrative and organizational sciences);
L-18 (economics and business management);
L-20 (communication sciences);
L-31 (legal sciences)
L-33 (economic sciences);
L-36 (political and international relations);
L-37 (social sciences for cooperation, development and peace);
L-40 (sociology);
L-42 (history);
or the corresponding degree classes referred to in DM 509/1999, provided that they have obtained 40 CFU in specific scientific-disciplinary sectors reported in the following sets:
a. IUS/01 (Private Law); IUS/02 (Comparative Private Law); IUS/04 (Commercial Law); IUS/05 (Law of Economics); IUS/06 (Law of Navigation); IUS/07 (Labor Law); IUS/08 (Constitutional Law); IUS/09 (Institutions of Public Law); IUS/10 (Administrative Law); IUS/11 (Ecclesiastical and Canon Law); IUS/12 (Tax Law); IUS/13 (International Law); IUS/14 (Law of the European Union); IUS/17 (Criminal Law); IUS/18 (Roman Law and the Rights of Antiquity); IUS/19 (History of Medieval and Modern Law); IUS/20 (Philosophy of Law); IUS/21 (Comparative Public Law);
b. c. SECS-P/01 (Political Economy); SECS-P/02 (Economic Policy); SECS-P/03 (Public Finance); SECS-P/04 (History of Economic Thought); SECS-P/05 (Econometrics); SECS-P/06 (Applied Economics); SECS-P/07 (Business Economics); SECS-P/08 (Economics and Management of Enterprises); SECS-P/09 (Corporate Finance); SECS-P/10 (Business Organization); SECS-P/12 (Economic History); SECS-S/01 (Statistics); SECS-S/03 (Economic Statistics); SECS-S/04 (Demography); SECS-S/05 (Social Statistics);
d. SPS/01 (Political Philosophy); SPS/02 (History of Political Thought); SPS/03 (History of Political Institutions); SPS/04 (Political Science); SPS/05 (History and Institutions of the Americas); SPS/06 (History of International Relations); SPS/07 (General Sociology); SPS/08 (Sociology of Cultural and Communication Processes); SPS/09 (Sociology of Economic and Labor Processes); SPS/10 (Sociology of the Environment and Territory); SPS/11 (Sociology of Political Phenomena); SPS/12 (Sociology of Law, Deviance and Social Change); SPS/13 (History and Institutions of Africa); SPS/14 (History and Institutions of Asia);
e. L-LIN/01 (Glottology and Linguistics); L-LIN/03 (French Literature); L-LIN/04 (French Language and Translation); L-LIN/05 (Spanish Literature); L-LIN/06 (Spanish and Latin American Literature); L-LIN/07 (Spanish Language and Translation); L-LIN/08 (Portuguese and Brazilian Literature); L-LIN/09 (Portuguese and Brazilian Language and Translation); L-LIN/10 (English Literature); L-LIN/11 (English and American Literature); L-LIN/12 (English Language and Translation); L-LIN/13 (German Literature); L-LIN/14 (German Language and Translation); L-LIN/15 (Nordic Languages and Literatures); L-LIN/17 (Romanian Language and Literature); L-LIN/18 (Albanian Language and Literature); L-LIN/21 (Slavic Studies); L-OR/08 (Hebrew); L-OR/09 (Languages and Literatures of Africa); L-OR/12 (Arabic Language and Literature); L-OR/15 (Persian Language and Literature); L-OR/19 (Languages and Literatures of the Indian Subcontinent); L-OR/21 (Languages and Literatures of China and Southeast Asia); L-OR/22 (Languages and Literatures of Japan and Korea); L-OR/23 (History of East and Southeast Asia).
For graduates from other degree classes, admission will be subject to having at least 75 CFU obtained in specific scientific-disciplinary sectors reported in the sets listed above.
The Teaching Committee of the Master's Degree Program will evaluate the admission requests of graduates from the pre-DM 509/99 system.
Verification of personal preparation is mandatory and only students who meet the curricular requirements can access it.
The methods of verification are specified in the didactic regulations of the course of study.
Degree awarded: Master's Degree in International Sciences
Method of teaching: Conventional
Knowledge and understanding: General Area: Graduates in International Sciences possess a specialist knowledge of international issues in the legal, economic, historical, political and social fields, supported by adequate language skills in at least two foreign languages. The typical skills of international sciences are administered with different disciplinary dosages and with a different focus, in order to create different paths of knowledge: in particular, the typical processes of formation and transformation of the international community, the areas of action of cooperation with developing areas, the challenges of globalization and the transformation processes that have affected European countries in the institutional, legal, economic, political and social fields over the past half century, and the evolution of the political and administrative organization of the European Union are studied. The understanding of the different disciplines, distributed on a semester basis, is facilitated by the use of appropriate teaching aids. In particular, the courses include active participation in seminars, conferences and meetings with high-profile experts. In consideration of the training objectives, graduates have been offered opportunities to critically discuss the topics covered. The acquisition of knowledge and the ability to understand are assessed during both the final exams and ongoing assessments. The thesis work constitutes a fundamental moment of training, during which future graduates work on original topics directly related to the world of work or susceptible to further scientific investigation.
Historical Area: Specialist knowledge of the processes of development of contemporary history in a comparative key, with attention to the European and extra-European context, the evolution of international relations, the methods of organization and functioning of diplomatic services, the evolution of legal and political systems in the European context.
Economic Area: Specialist knowledge of the issues relating to international economics, monetary economics, environmental economics and development economics.
Legal Area: Specialist knowledge relating to the discipline and functioning of international organizations, the protection of human rights, international economic law and the regulations of the European Union, in its internal and external relevance.
Political Area: Specialist knowledge in the field of comparative analysis of political systems in the European and extra-European context and understanding of the factors that determine the current dynamics of international relations.
Linguistic Area: Knowledge and understanding of two foreign languages.
Acquisition of knowledge of the English language at least at level B2 of the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages of the Council of Europe.

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 3 years. Degree program: 2014. Status: Open. Credits: 180 CFU. Language: Italian. Type of degree: Standard Degree. Regulatory Decree: D.M. 270/2004. Degree class: L-13 - Class of degrees in Biological Sciences. Inter-university course: No. Open access: Yes. Location: Siena. Department: Department of Life Sciences. Website: https://scienze-biologiche.unisi.it. Admission requirements: Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]. Upper secondary school diploma. Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse. Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni. Roles and responsibilities: Student mobility: MARIA FROSINI, President/President of the teaching committee. SANDRA DONNINI. Program, texts and objectives: Admission requirements: Upper secondary school diploma or other qualification obtained abroad recognized as suitable. Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam consists of a discussion of a written paper prepared independently by the student, under the guidance of a lecturer, during the internship/stage period, which can be carried out at the university or an external company. The purpose of the final exam is to ascertain that the graduate has acquired knowledge and understanding skills in the field of biology, that they are able to elaborate them individually and apply this knowledge in a working context.
The final exam consists of the presentation of the paper in a concise form and a discussion of its contents before a Degree Commission.
At the end of the presentation and discussion of the paper, the supervisor proposes an assessment, up to a maximum of four points, in relation to the commitment and preparation demonstrated by the student during the internship/stage, while the Degree Commission proposes an assessment up to a maximum of four points, in relation to the written, oral presentation and discussion skills demonstrated by the student.
The Degree Commission, based on the assessments obtained during the training course and the score of the final exam, expresses the assessment of the candidates in hundredths, with possible honors. The score obtained by each candidate will be made public at the degree session, which will take place two working days after the presentation and discussion of the paper before the Degree Commission.
Upon request, the final exam can also be taken in English.
Knowledge required for admission: The knowledge required for admission and their methods of mandatory verification are defined in the Didactic Regulations of the study course, which also indicate the additional training obligations in the event that the verification of the required knowledge is not positive.
Degree awarded: Degree in Biological Sciences. Teaching delivery method: Conventional. Knowledge and understanding: General area. The graduate will have acquired, through the courses provided for in the course, the ability to understand the biology of microorganisms, animals and plants, and the ability to evaluate the morphological-functional, cellular-molecular and chemical-biochemical aspects of each of these organisms. These skills will include an evolutionary view of the various kingdoms, the general mechanisms of heredity and the mechanisms of reproduction and development of the various organisms. Graduates will also have acquired the elements for the evaluation of the ecological-environmental aspects of ecosystems and the ability to analyze the various areas through elements of mathematics, physics, statistics and computer science. Knowledge and understanding will be assessed through written and/or oral tests for each course.
Molecular-cellular learning area: The molecular-cellular learning area will provide further in-depth knowledge of the molecular, functional and cellular aspects underlying the functioning of living systems, genes, their expression and their alterations. These in-depth studies will also make it possible to acquire knowledge of new technologies useful both for entering the world of work and as a basis for continuing the training path in Master's Degrees in the molecular/cellular field.
Knowledge and understanding will be assessed through written and/or oral tests for each course.
Biological-health learning area: The biological-health learning area will provide further in-depth theoretical and practical knowledge on the aspects relating to human pathophysiology. Knowledge will be acquired relating to anatomy, general etiology, immunology, pharmacology, neuroscience, stem cell biology, as well as knowledge of bioimaging and bioinformatics. This knowledge will enable the graduate to enter the world of work and provide a solid basis for continuing the training path in Master's Degrees in the bio-health field.
Knowledge and understanding will be assessed through written and/or oral tests for each course.
Biodiversity, ecology and evolution learning area: The biodiversity, ecology and evolution learning area will provide further in-depth knowledge of the aspects related to biodiversity, its evolution and its various adaptations. Specific knowledge will be acquired on various ecological and environmental contexts and on the most advanced techniques to analyze them. Topics of environmental botany, animal and plant diversity, biological evolution, management and conservation of wildlife and bioinformatics will be explored in depth. These in-depth studies will enable both entry into the world of work and a solid basis for continuing the training path in Master's Degrees in the field of biodiversity, ecology and evolution.
Knowledge and understanding will be assessed through written and/or oral tests for each teaching unit.

UNIVERSITY: University of Siena
COURSE: [SE002] CHEMICAL SCIENCES
SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unisi.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10225

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 3 years. Admission year: 2021. Status: Open. Credits: 180 CFU. Language: Italian. Type of degree: Bachelor's Degree. Legal basis: D.M. 270/2004. Degree class: L-27 - Class of degrees in Chemical Sciences and Technologies. Inter-university course: No. Open access: Yes. Location: Siena. Department: Biotechnology, Chemistry and Pharmacy. Website: https://scienze-chimiche.unisi.it. Informative website: https://www.unisi.it/didattica/uffici-studenti-e-didattica/sportello-ufficio-studenti-e-didattica-biotecnologie-chimica-e. Admission requirements: Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]. Upper secondary school diploma. Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse. Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni. Roles and responsibilities: Student mobility: ELENA PETRICCI, MATTIA MORI. President/President of the teaching committee: GABRIELLA TAMASI. Program, texts and objectives: Admission requirements: Upper secondary school diploma or other qualification obtained abroad recognized as suitable. Professional status conferred by the degree: Junior Chemist. In addition to being prepared for further studies, graduates in Chemical Sciences will be able to access professional and technical activities in research institutions, industrial laboratories, and all chemical, physical, environmental, and biological fields where the presence of a chemist is required. In particular, the three-year graduate in Chemical Sciences will be able to find employment in: Private companies: industries in different industrial sectors. Private analysis laboratories: structures specializing in environmental, food, quality control analyses, etc., where chemical analyses are carried out. Laboratories, research centers, public and private bodies that carry out, promote, and disseminate research activities in the chemical field, technology transfer activities to companies, territorial planning, issuing production authorizations, etc. The three-year degree in Chemical Sciences also allows admission to the practice of the Junior Chemist profession, after passing the state exam and registration with the Order of Chemists (Section A). Three-year graduates in Chemical Sciences can also continue their studies in both first-level postgraduate courses and master's degree courses in chemistry, but also in other related disciplines, thanks to the solid preparation achieved in basic subjects. Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam consists of preparing and discussing a written paper (experimental thesis, with the aim of integrating the different disciplinary knowledge acquired throughout the course), prepared independently by the student, under the guidance of a tutor teacher, during the internship period which can take place at the University or at an external institution/company (in Italy or abroad). The final exam will also be evaluated based on the verification of the knowledge and skills acquired, the ability to apply them in a work context, and the ability to individual elaboration. At the request of the graduate, the exam can be held in English. The grade of the final exam will be expressed in hundredths with possible honors. Knowledge required for access: To access the degree course in Chemical Sciences (L-27), it is necessary to have an upper secondary school diploma or another qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable. Students who enroll in the degree course are required to have adequate basic preparation consisting of a good level of general and scientific culture, as well as basic knowledge of mathematics and physics acquired in upper secondary school. The verification of the required knowledge takes place through an entrance assessment test, followed by guidance. Failure to pass the test does not preclude enrollment. A recovery and integration process will follow for any additional training obligations to be met within the first year of the course, also through a personalized tutoring path for each student. Knowledge of the English language is required at a level of competence of at least B1. For students who do not have a certificate, the University Language Center will verify the knowledge of the required level of English (placement test). The acquisition of the B1 level must take place within the first year of the course. Degree awarded: Bachelor's Degree in Chemical Sciences. Teaching method: Conventional. Knowledge and understanding: GENERAL AREA Graduates in Chemical Sciences will have acquired the necessary principles for understanding basic chemistry. In particular, the basics of inorganic chemistry, organic chemistry, physical chemistry, analytical chemistry, and elements of biological chemistry. These concepts will be adequate for understanding and framing a chemical problem. In addition, students must be able to read texts and articles in the field, even in English. The certification of the English language will be handled by the University Language Center (CLA) and will reach the B2 level at the end of the course. Finally, students must be able to design and carry out an experiment in the chemical field, taking into account the current regulations on "Health and safety in the workplace". These knowledge and skills will be acquired through participation in lectures, guided personal study, and independent study, as provided for in the training activities. The verification of the achievement of the results of knowledge and understanding will take place at the end of the relevant training activities, through written and/or oral exams. BASIC PHYSICAL-MATHEMATICAL AND COMPUTER AREA Graduates in Chemical Sciences will have acquired: - solid theoretical knowledge of the basic concepts of exact sciences, mathematics, and physics, which are preparatory for teaching chemistry. In particular, for mathematics, graduates must know the tools of mathematical analysis and the basics of numerical calculation, and for physics, the knowledge of basic classical mechanics and elements of electromagnetism. - knowledge of theoretical and applied computer science; knowledge of the logical bases of programming, knowledge of a programming language, knowledge of operating systems and the most widely used applications. BASIC CHEMISTRY AREA Graduates in Chemical Sciences will have acquired: - in-depth knowledge of the theoretical foundations of the main disciplinary sectors of Chemistry: Analytical Chemistry, Physical Chemistry, General and Inorganic Chemistry, Organic Chemistry. - theoretical and practical skills in carrying out numerical exercises and laboratory activities in the various scientific disciplinary sectors. - knowledge and skills in the use of methods for the preparation and characterization of chemical systems and for the study of the relationships between structure and properties. - knowledge of scientific instrumentation for analytical and structural investigations of matter. With regard to the last three points, in this degree course, practical activities (laboratory) are considered of considerable importance, as the ability to work in the laboratory, to be able to design an experiment, and to have the ability to coordinate an experiment together with other operators is one of the fundamental characteristics of the graduate in Chemical Sciences. BASIC BIOLOGICAL AREA Graduates in Chemical Sciences will acquire basic knowledge of cellular and molecular biology, with particular regard to the structure of bio-macromolecules: nucleic acids, proteins, polysaccharides.

SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year.
Duration: 3 years.
Program: 2015.
Status: Open.
Credits: 180 CFU.
Language: Italian.
Type: Degree Programs.
Regulation: D.M. 270/2004.
Degree Class: L-30 - Class of degrees in Physics and Technology Sciences.
Inter-university Course: No.
Open Access: Yes.
Location: Siena.
Department: Department of Physics, Earth and Environment.
Website: https://fisica-tecnologie-avanzate.unisi.it
Informative Website: https://www.unisi.it/didattica/uffici-studenti-e-didattica/sportello-ufficio-servizi-agli-studenti-mattioli
Admission Requirements:
Mandatory qualifications: [TSS]
Upper Secondary School Diploma.
Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse
Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan" www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni
Roles and Responsibilities:
Student Mobility: LEONARDO DISPERATI, President/President of the teaching committee.
CARMELA MARINELLI.
Program, texts and objectives:
Admission requirements: Upper secondary school diploma or other qualification obtained abroad recognized as suitable.
Professional status conferred by the degree:
Technical professions in physics, natural sciences, engineering and related fields.
The graduate in Physics and Advanced Technologies will have a methodological training, a range of knowledge and operational flexibility that will allow them to find professional opportunities related to ISTAT activities (ref: Classification of economic activities Ateco 2007):
72 (INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY AND RELATED ACTIVITIES)
73 (RESEARCH AND DEVELOPMENT OF (MANUFACTURE OF ELECTRICAL MACHINERY AND EQUIPMENT, ELECTRICAL, ELECTRONIC AND OPTICAL EQUIPMENT)
85.1 (HEALTH SERVICES ACTIVITIES)
thanks to the ability to use software techniques, in the modeling and analysis of data assisted by computer, to the specific training in the development and use of advanced instrumentation in the fields of electronics, optics, laser techniques, medical imaging.
Characteristics of the final exam:
The candidate must demonstrate that they have achieved sufficient scientific maturity to undertake and complete a short thesis work. This activity is normally facilitated by the experience acquired during the internship, during which the candidate's activity can already be directed towards the thesis work.
The final exam consists of a written dissertation which is discussed publicly by the candidate in front of the commission on the day of graduation with a presentation lasting approximately 30 minutes in total. This dissertation concerns a scientific topic that allows to verify that the general training objectives of the study course have been achieved; in particular, the contents may concern a topic related to one of the research activities present in the department.
The thesis can be written and/or presented, at the candidate's discretion, in a foreign language; credits are assigned to this work and a merit score is calculated based on the criteria indicated in the Course Regulations.
Knowledge required for access:
Basic scientific knowledge acquired in secondary school is required, certified by the possession of a secondary school diploma or other qualification obtained abroad recognized as equivalent, and basic knowledge of the English language (at least at level A2/2).
In compliance with Article 6, paragraph 1 of D.M. 270/04, students must have adequate initial preparation. To this end, students will have to take a test to assess their knowledge upon entry, which will allow them to identify the level of preparation achieved with respect to the chosen degree course and which will encourage them to deepen their studies, so as to have a performance in line with a university curriculum. The results of the test are used to establish the necessary corrections and any integrations to be carried out during the 1st year of study. This test is normally held in October, before the start of classes. The methods of verification and integration as described above will take place under the control of the Teaching Committee of the Degree Course in Physics and Advanced Technologies, as well as the tutor teachers.
Degree awarded:
Degree in Physics and Advanced Technologies.
Teaching delivery method: Conventional.
Knowledge and understanding:
Generic Area: Graduates in Physics and Advanced Technologies have acquired knowledge and understanding skills relating to:
- the appropriate mathematical tools for a "quantitative" analysis of physical systems and for the study of theoretical models for the description of physical phenomena and as a guide to problem solving.
- the physical principles with a thorough knowledge and understanding of Classical Physics and the solid introductory foundations of the concepts of Modern Physics, and of non-relativistic Quantum Mechanics.
- physical phenomena from an experimental point of view: carrying out direct experiments with physical phenomena, designing experiments and developing them in every phase, critical analysis of the data collected.
- applications of the physical sciences both in the field of research and in the technological field, physical mechanisms underlying the operation of specific applications of current interest.
Scientific and methodological foundations: At the end of the studies, the graduate has acquired knowledge and understanding skills:
fundamental scientific and methodological aspects of the basic disciplines (mathematics, chemistry, computer science): differential and integral calculus for functions in one or more real and complex variables, linear algebra and analytical geometry, linear systems and differential equations, architecture of a processing system and related languages, elements of inorganic and organic chemistry.
Basic Physical Sciences: At the end of the studies, the graduate has acquired knowledge and understanding skills:
in-depth basic physics, theoretical and formal aspects relating to the dynamics of the point material and systems, conservation laws of momentum, energy and angular momentum, fluid dynamics, thermodynamics, electromagnetism, generation and propagation of electromagnetic waves, physical and geometric optics.
Mathematical Physics and Theoretical Physics: At the end of the studies, the graduate has acquired knowledge and understanding skills:
of the models and methods for analyzing the mechanical behavior of systems, with a finite number of degrees of freedom, free and constrained, on the Lagrangian and Hamiltonian formulation of mechanical systems, on Laplace and Fourier transforms; advanced mathematical knowledge relating to the algebras of observables, Hilbert spaces and linear operators in Hilbert spaces, foundations of quantum mechanics and first elements of physics of matter.
Physics Laboratory and Experimental Physics Activities: At the end of the studies, the graduate has acquired knowledge and understanding skills:
on the general methodologies for the design and execution of the measurement of a physical quantity in mechanical, thermodynamic, electrical, electronic, optical fields; on the technology of semiconductors, transistors, amplifiers and related circuit models in optical devices, microprocessors and microcontrollers; on data acquisition and analysis systems, their statistical processing, on analog and digital signals; on the operation of various types of detectors, on electronic instrumentation and on the modern experimental techniques used in Nuclear and Subnuclear Physics experiments, Atomic Spectroscopy, Laser Physics.
Modern Physics: At the end of the studies, the graduate has acquired knowledge and understanding skills:
on relevant aspects for specific training in the field of modern research, advanced technology and on the most advanced theories of this area as elements of special relativity, astrophysics, experiments that led to the crisis of Classical Mechanics, elements of Atomic and Molecular Physics, introduction to Solid State Physics, Elements of Nuclear and Particle Physics, radioactive phenomena, Medical Physics.
Applications in related fields: At the end of the studies, the graduate has acquired knowledge and understanding skills in complementary scientific fields, aimed at forming a general cultural heritage at university level in sectors not strictly related to the specific learning project.
He has acquired the knowledge and skills that allow him to enter the regional list of qualified technicians in Acoustics, registration required by law for three-year L-30 graduates who have acquired the credits provided for in the Acoustics teaching (offered as an option in the free credits).
He has acquired knowledge in the field of Physics teaching useful for subsequent training courses to enable teaching.

Upper secondary school diploma. Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse. Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study plan": www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni. Roles and responsibilities: Student mobility, LEONARDO DISPERATI, President/President of the teaching committee, FEDERICO MARIA PULSELLI. Program, texts and objectives: Admission requirements: Upper secondary school diploma or other qualification obtained abroad recognized as suitable. Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam consists of the discussion of a paper relating to a project carried out individually. The discussion will take place in a public session before a commission of at least 5 teachers, who will express the overall evaluation in hundredths, with possible honors. The conversion of the grades obtained in the various teaching activities, which give rise to a grade in thirtieths, into hundredths will involve a weighted average with respect to the relevant credits obtained. The final assessment will aim to assess whether the graduate student has achieved the learning objectives and whether the exam itself is consistent with the specific objectives of the degree program. The criteria for assigning the merit score consist of evaluating the student's previous academic record, the mastery shown in discussing the topic of the paper, and the time taken to graduate. For the procedures relating to admission to the final exam and the preparation of the paper, please refer to the Didactic Regulations of the Degree Course. Required knowledge for access: Basic scientific knowledge acquired in secondary school is required, certified by the possession of an upper secondary school diploma or other equivalent qualification, also obtained abroad. Knowledge of the English language at a level of competence of at least A2/2 is also required, as defined by the Common European Framework of Reference for Languages. For students who do not have the English language skills mentioned above, the University Language Center organizes remedial courses to be held preferably before the start of the official courses. In compliance with Article 6, paragraphs 1 and 2 of D.M. 270/04, students must have adequate initial preparation. To this end, students will have to take a test to assess their knowledge upon entry, which will allow them to identify the level of preparation achieved with respect to the chosen degree course and which will encourage them to deepen their studies, so as to have a performance in line with a university curriculum. The results of the test are used to establish the necessary corrections and any integrations to be carried out during the 1st year of study. This test is normally held in October, before the start of classes, and will be repeated in December/January. The verification and integration procedures described above will take place under the supervision of the Single Committee for the Teaching of Degree Courses in Environmental and Natural Sciences, as well as the tutor teachers. The knowledge required for access to the Degree Course in Environmental and Natural Sciences will be specified in the relevant Didactic Regulations, where the additional training obligations will also be indicated in the event that the assessment is not positive. Degree awarded: DEGREE COURSE IN ENVIRONMENTAL AND NATURAL SCIENCES. Teaching delivery method: Conventional. Knowledge and understanding. Generic area: Knowledge of the fundamentals of Mathematics, Physics, Chemistry, Computer Science and Statistics, to acquire the basic languages of the individual disciplines and of the scientific method. Knowledge and understanding of the processes and mechanisms by which organisms and the physical landscape function, have formed and interact. Understanding of the interdisciplinary aspects of studies on the environment and nature. Knowledge and ability to recognize and classify the biotic, animal and plant, and abiotic, mineral and rock, components of the landscape and ability to analyze the ecosystem as a whole. This knowledge is acquired through attendance at lectures, the use of advanced, audiovisual and hypertextual texts. Individual study may also be supported by tutors. The quality and level of knowledge achieved will be verified by means of oral and/or written tests during the course and by the final exam for each subject. Scientific and methodological foundations: This area of learning, mainly in the first year, includes the basic knowledge in the fields of mathematics, statistics for the environment, chemistry, physics and computer science to the extent that these disciplines are essential for naturalistic and environmental studies. The relevant courses are placed in the early years as they are considered preparatory for an optimal course of study. Naturalistic and environmental foundations: This area of learning, located between the first and second year, includes the basic knowledge common to naturalists and environmentalists, in particular biology, general zoology, general botany, physiology, ecology, geology. They provide a series of general notions that are functional for an adequate understanding of the contents of the naturalistic and ecological-environmental subjects. Naturalistic deepening: This area of learning includes both compulsory and optional courses aimed at acquiring the knowledge for the recognition and classification of biotic components (systematic zoology of vertebrates and invertebrates, systematic botany, mycology, biocenosis of the soil, urban biodiversity, comparative anatomy, paleontology, biogeography), the interpretation of the abiotic landscape (mineralogy, physical geography, identification, ecology and management of habitats of community interest, etc.) and of the prehistoric environment (in-depth studies of prehistoric ecology and anthropology). Ecological and environmental deepening: This area of learning includes both compulsory and optional courses aimed at acquiring the knowledge for the analysis, monitoring and sustainable management of the environment through the acquisition of chemical-analytical, chemical-physical, toxicological data (environmental chemistry, applied ecology, biological effects of pollutants, pharmaceutical botany) and ecological (conservation of nature, ecodynamics, identification and management of habitats of community interest, ecology of communities, agroecology and marine ecology) and also through the study of the possible use of production waste as a secondary raw material to be used in the medical, pharmaceutical, cosmetic and food supplement fields.

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year.

Location: Siena.
Department: Department of Information Engineering and Mathematics.

Informative Website: https://www.diism.unisi.it/it/didattica
Admission Requirements: Mandatory qualifications: [TSS].
Upper Secondary School Diploma.
Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse.
Dates and deadlines: For information on dates and deadlines, please visit the following page on the University website: "Study Plan" www.unisi.it/didattica/immatricolazioni-e-iscrizioni/immatricolazioni.
Roles and Responsibilities: Student Mobility, GIOVANNI GIAMBENE, President/Chairman of the teaching committee, CRISTIANO BOCCI.
Program, texts and objectives: Access to further studies.
Bachelor's Degree Programs.
University Master's Degrees (1st level).
Admission requirements: Upper secondary school diploma or other qualification obtained abroad recognized as suitable.
Professional status conferred by the degree.
In general, graduates in Mathematics will be able to carry out professional activities:
(a) in companies and industry;
(b) in laboratories and research centers;
(c) in the field of scientific culture dissemination;
(d) in the service sector;
(e) in the public administration.
In particular, the Mathematics degree from the University of Siena provides skills (to be completed with studies in a Master's Degree) for a career perspective both in teaching mathematics and science and in scientific research in the same fields (in particular, the presence of the Doctoral Program in Mathematical Logic, Computer Science and Bioinformatics should be considered). These prospects are "definitely interesting" for - respectively - over 30% and over 50% of graduates in the class (Source: Alma Laurea, Graduate Profile 2006 and 2007). Furthermore, the Mathematics degree provides more immediate professional skills in the field of modeling phenomena in the natural, biological, health, engineering, social and human sciences through the use of the computer and computational tools acquired.
It should also be noted that an appropriate choice of the course, which involves the acquisition of further statistical and computer skills, provides the graduate, in addition to those already illustrated, with specific career prospects:
• in the economic and financial world,
• in the field of management and interpretation of data relating to both human activities (financial, banking, insurance and in general economic on the one hand, socio-health and socio-political on the other) and from the natural and biological sciences.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam consists of the presentation, with discussion before a Degree Commission in public session, of an individual written dissertation on topics of mathematical interest. The dissertation and the presentation are in Italian, unless otherwise requested by the student to the Teaching Committee.
The dissertation must be prepared under the supervision of a supervisor, chosen from among the University's lecturers. The choice of supervisor is made by the candidate and is subject to the approval of the Teaching Committee, after consulting the chosen lecturer. For the preparation of the dissertation, the student may - with the approval of the Teaching Committee - make use of internships at a company or external body, or periods of study in structures other than the University of Siena.
With the dissertation and its public discussion, the student must demonstrate - through the illustration of the specific topic discussed - that he/she has achieved the specific training objectives of the degree course.
The grade for the final exam is expressed in hundredths, with possible honors, and 6 CFU are assigned to it.
The merit score is mainly assigned based on the quality of the entire study path carried out by the student, and also based on the demonstration of the maturity and mastery achieved in the acquisition of the learning objectives indicated through the illustration of the specific topic discussed in the dissertation.
Knowledge required for admission: To be admitted to the Mathematics degree course, you must have the following basic mathematical knowledge:
Operations and inequalities between fractions; operations and inequalities between real numbers; familiarity with the manipulation of simple algebraic expressions and with the solution of algebraic equations and inequalities of the first and second degree; elements of Euclidean geometry and analytical geometry; familiarity with the definitions and first properties of elementary functions (polynomials, exponentials, logarithms and trigonometric functions).
This knowledge is usually acquired during the upper secondary school studies (or equivalent for foreign students); in any case, the verification of the possession of this knowledge is a prerequisite for admission.
Knowledge of the English language at least at level A2/2 is also required.
The following requirements are also considered important:
• Adequate motivation and good ability to adopt a correct methodological approach to study.
• Good logical reasoning and symbolic manipulation skills.
• Aptitude for the scientific method.
Methods for evaluating the student's initial preparation: entrance/orientation test of the Faculty of Mathematical, Physical and Natural Sciences.
In accordance with the initiatives of the Faculty, remedial courses are planned for any learning deficiencies identified following the evaluation of the entrance test.
Degree awarded: Degree in MATHEMATICS.
Method of teaching: Conventional.
Knowledge and understanding.
Area of basic training: Graduates in Mathematics:
Know and know how to use calculus in one and more variables, linear algebra and the basics of algebra and geometry.
They also possess the following knowledge and skills:
- Basic knowledge of physics and computer science.
- Knowledge and understanding of the basic applications of mathematics to physics.
- Computational and computer skills including knowledge of programming languages and specific software.
These objectives are achieved through the activities related to some fundamental subjects compulsory for all students, concentrated in the first year of the Study Course, and verified through the related intermediate tests (where provided for by the Teaching Regulations) and the related final exams (in any case compulsory). These fundamental subjects are structured with lessons and exercises. Furthermore, the Computer Science and Physics subjects provide for adequate laboratory activities.
Area of theoretical training: Graduates in mathematics also possess the following knowledge:
• Knowledge of mathematical logic
• Knowledge of analytical and differential geometry
• Knowledge of algebraic structures
• Knowledge of metric spaces, sequences and series of functions and differential equations.
These objectives are achieved through the activities related to some subjects - mostly compulsory for all students - and verified through the related intermediate tests (where provided for by the Teaching Regulations) and the related final exams (in any case compulsory).
Furthermore, graduates in mathematics are able to:
• read and understand texts of Mathematics and consult research articles in Mathematics.
This objective is achieved partly through the systematic use of reference texts to be adopted compulsorily for all subjects, and partly - as regards more advanced texts and research articles in mathematics - through the preparation of the dissertation for the final exam, which will constitute the main verification of the achievement of the result.
Area of modeling and application training: Graduates in mathematics also possess the following knowledge:
• Knowledge of probability and statistics
• Knowledge of some numerical methods
• Knowledge of mathematical physics
• Knowledge of operations research.
This objective is achieved through a group of fundamental subjects compulsory for all students and the related training activities (lessons, exercises, attendance at numerical analysis laboratories, individual study, intermediate tests), and verified through the written, practical and/or oral tests provided for the related exams.
Area of transversal training: Depending on the chosen path, graduates in mathematics acquire some of the following knowledge:
• Further and more advanced knowledge in physics, both from a theoretical and experimental point of view;
• Further and more advanced knowledge in computer science, both from a logical-theoretical and computational point of view;
• Historical-critical knowledge on the development of mathematics;
• Knowledge of the modeling of dynamic systems;
• Knowledge of Artificial Intelligence;
• Adequate statistical skills, both from the point of view of methods and applications to economics, life sciences and social sciences.
This objective is achieved through a significant number of subjects, organized in "packages" consistent with the desired training objective, which the student will follow once he has chosen the path. The verification of the achievement of the objective takes place through the related intermediate tests (where provided for by the Teaching Regulations) and the related final exams (in any case compulsory).

Admission Requirements:
Mandatory qualifications: [TSS].
Upper Secondary School Diploma.

First-Level Degree: [L1 cons+TSS].
Fees and contributions: For information on the amounts of university fees and contributions, please visit the following page on the University website: www.unisi.it/tasse.
Roles and Responsibilities: Student Mobility, MARINA ZICHE, President/Chair of the teaching committee, ELEONORA CAVARRA.
Program, texts and objectives: Admission requirements are indicated in the Didactic Regulations of the Master's Degree Course, which can be consulted at: http://www.unisi.it.
Professional status conferred by the degree: Senior Biologist, Researcher. The Master's Degree in Health Biology allows graduates to work in public or private institutions, either as employees or as independent professionals, such as:
- universities and other public and private biomedical research institutions (IRCCS);
- pharmaceutical and diagnostic product industries;
- applied biotechnology laboratories;
- food and diet industries;
- structures of the National Health System;
- diagnostic laboratories affiliated with the NHS;
- public and private institutions dealing with assisted medical procreation;
- study and monitoring centers for toxicology and environmental issues;
- bodies responsible for the development of technical regulations or quality certification;
- industries interested in the development of biological and biochemical applications in the health field.
Master's degree graduates who meet the requirements set out in current regulations may participate in the entrance tests for teacher training courses for secondary schools.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam must verify that the Master's degree graduate has acquired the ability to apply their knowledge, autonomy of judgment, and communication skills.
The final exam consists of writing and presenting a thesis developed originally by the student under the guidance of a supervisor. The thesis must be organized according to the standards accepted by the international scientific community, i.e., with a detailed description and in accordance with the scientific standard of the state of knowledge on the subject, the description of the scientific problem addressed, the experimental approach used, the methodology and materials used, the results obtained, completed with a discussion of the results and the cited bibliography. The topic of the paper may relate to the experimental activity carried out by the student during the internship and is related to the various aspects of Health Biology.
Passing the final exam leads to the acquisition of the required credits.
Knowledge required for admission: To access the Master's Degree Course in Health Biology, it is necessary to have a degree or a three-year university diploma or another equivalent qualification obtained abroad.
Students who wish to enroll in the Master's Degree Course in Health Biology must have both specific curricular requirements and adequate preparation in the fundamental subjects for the training of a Biologist, such as Mathematics, Physics, Chemistry, and Computer Science, as well as in the basic biological disciplines that provide knowledge of the organization of living beings at the morphological, functional, and structural levels.
They must also have knowledge of the functional, cellular, and molecular aspects underlying the organization of living organisms and the molecular mechanisms that regulate heredity, reproduction, and development. In particular, they must demonstrate previous acquisition of sufficient knowledge in the following disciplines that characterize the Master's Degree Course: Human Anatomy, Biochemistry, Physiology, Immunology, Microbiology, Cellular Pathology, Pharmacology, and Hygiene.
Specifically, the curricular requirements are those listed below in points "a" or "b":
a- have a degree in one of the following classes according to D.M. 270/04: L-13 Biological Sciences, L-2 Biotechnology, and the following classes according to D.M. 509/99: 12 Biological Sciences, 1 Biotechnology; alternatively, have obtained a degree in the L-SNT/3 class; or have obtained a degree in Biological Sciences under the previous regulations to D.M. 509/99 (five-year degrees).
In addition, it is necessary to have obtained at least 30 CFU among the following scientific-disciplinary sectors (SSD): SSD MED/03, MED/04, MED/05, MED/07, MED/42, MED/46, BIO/09, BIO/12, BIO/14, BIO/16, BIO/17, VET/02, VET/03, VET/06, VET/07.
b- have a three-year university degree or diploma, other than those mentioned in the previous point, provided that at least 75 CFU have been obtained among the following scientific-disciplinary sectors (SSD):
SSD MED/03, MED/04, MED/05, MED/07, MED/42, MED/46, BIO/09, BIO/10, BIO/11, BIO/12, BIO/13, BIO/14, BIO/15, BIO/16, BIO/17, BIO/18, BIO/19.
Knowledge of the English language at least at level B1 is also required.
For graduates from foreign universities, the adequacy of the curricular requirements will be evaluated on a case-by-case basis based on the consistency between the programs carried out in the various disciplinary areas and the basic training deemed necessary for the advanced training offered by the course of study.
The verification of personal preparation takes place according to the methods provided for in the didactic regulations of the course of study. This verification is mandatory in any case and only students who have the curricular requirements can access it; having these requirements cannot be considered as verification of personal preparation.
Degree awarded: Master's Degree in Health Biology.
Method of teaching: Conventional.
Knowledge and understanding:
General Area: With reference to the system of descriptors of study titles adopted at European level (Dublin descriptors), graduates of the Master's Degree Course in Health Biology belonging to the LM 6 class:
acquire knowledge and understanding of the biological processes underlying the pathology of organs and systems, their pharmacological modulation, oncology, and infectious and parasitic diseases, and in the field of epidemiology and food hygiene, and general and forensic toxicology. Knowledge of some cutting-edge themes in the field of biomedical research will also be acquired.
This knowledge is acquired through lectures, exercises, and related independent study activities. The lectures will be supplemented with the analysis of scientific publications relevant to each subject, which will be discussed collegially and critically with the assistance of the teacher.
The achievement of the specific training objectives of each teaching activity will be verified through tests, written or oral, for each subject, which contain questions aimed at stimulating the critical re-elaboration of knowledge.
AREA BIODIVERSITY AND ENVIRONMENT: Approaches for studying the environmental fate of contaminants.
Classical Toxicology and Environmental Toxicology.
Hazard assessments and risk analysis.
Acquisition of knowledge and understanding of the biological processes underlying parasitic diseases.
Acquisition of knowledge and ability to recognize arthropods that are vectors of diseases for humans and animals.
Learning diagnostic methods in human parasitology and the principles of epidemiological parasitology.
BIOMEDICAL AREA: Acquisition of knowledge and understanding of the biological processes underlying the pathology of organs and systems, their pharmacological modulation, oncology, and infectious and parasitic diseases, and in the field of epidemiology and food hygiene, and general and forensic toxicology.
Acquisition of knowledge on some cutting-edge themes in the field of biomedical research.
CONTEXTUAL KNOWLEDGE AND FINAL EXAM: Knowledge and understanding of scientific instrumentation. Organization and management of biomedical laboratories.
Knowledge and understanding of practical tools for acquiring, processing, and transmitting data and information, including electronic tools.
Knowledge and understanding of the English language at level B2.

INFO ON STUDY PLANS General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 2 years. Program: 2014. Status: Open. Credits: 120 CFU. Language: Italian. Type: Master's Degrees. Regulations: D.M. 270/2004. Degree Class: LM-75 - Class of Master's Degrees in Environmental and Territorial Sciences and Technologies. Inter-university Course: No. Open Access: Yes. Location: Siena. Department: Department of Physical Sciences, Earth and Environment. Website: https://ecotossicologia-ambiente.unisi.it. Roles and Responsibilities: Student Mobility, LEONARDO DISPERATI, President/Chairman of the teaching committee, FEDERICO MARIA PULSELLI. Program, texts and objectives. Admission requirements. The requirements for admission are indicated in the Didactic Regulations of the Master's Degree Course, which can be consulted at: http://www.unisi.it. Characteristics of the final exam. In the proposed Master's Degree, great importance is given to the final exam and its preparation. The final exam will consist of a discussion before a degree commission of an experimental thesis that reports the results of original research on topics consistent with the educational objectives of the proposed Master's Degree, assigned by the CD and under the supervision of one or more tutors (a supervisor and possibly one or more co-supervisors), carried out at a scientific structure of the University of Siena or another affiliated structure (public or private organization) (internship). The regulations for internships refer to the relevant University Regulations. The thesis work should be based on the application of the knowledge and methodologies acquired to ecotoxicological or environmental sustainability issues. The Degree Thesis must have the form and structure of a real scientific work, with a clear indication of the objectives to be pursued, the methodology applied, the results obtained, and their relevance in the context of current scientific knowledge. The student must demonstrate that they have developed a thorough knowledge of modern scientific approach, a good ability to process and synthesize the data acquired, and an adequate ability to present it in writing and orally.
Knowledge required for admission. Basic training in mathematical, physical and chemical disciplines and adequate knowledge in biological and ecological disciplines is required. The curricular requirements necessary for admission to the Master's Degree Course in Ecotoxicology and Environmental Sustainability, the quantification of the relevant minimum CFUs, and the methods for verifying the adequacy of personal preparation will be specified in the Didactic Regulations of the Course.
Degree awarded: Master's Degree in ECOTOXICOLOGY AND ENVIRONMENTAL SUSTAINABILITY. Teaching delivery method: Conventional. Knowledge and understanding.
Generic Area. The Master's Degree Course is characterized by the presence of two areas of knowledge: that of Ecotoxicology and that of Environmental Sustainability. The two areas prove to be perfectly complementary since they address the problems related to the influence of human activity on ecosystems from two different points of view: the more analytical one of Ecotoxicology and the more systemic one of Environmental Sustainability.
In the Master's degree program, the student must be able to manage the information and knowledge offered by both scientific proposals in order to have, once graduated, a complete picture of the complexity of human-nature relationships and the skills to professionally manage the problems that these relationships progressively generate.
In order to satisfy the specific requirements of each descriptor, the verification of knowledge and understanding skills will be carried out at the end of each training activity by means of teaching tools consistent with the theoretical and experimental issues addressed in the teaching courses, including: critical discussion of data collected during the course, in advanced texts or reported in the most recent international scientific literature; theoretical-practical individual or group activities, the results of which will be presented independently both during the training activity and in a single final verification.
Ecotoxicological Foundations. The specialized knowledge and understanding skills of the various sectors of Ecotoxicology will be aimed at describing, explaining and discussing the theoretical and practical foundations of the methods for acquiring, analyzing and interpreting ecotoxicological data; starting from the ability to read and understand specialist texts and scientific articles on related topics and problems, this knowledge and skills will also be useful for acquiring autonomy and originality in formulating and/or applying ideas and solutions in the field of ecotoxicology and for communicating the results, often in a context of rapid evolution and development in line with the emergence of ever new problems related to the interaction between human activity and the natural environment.
Fundamentals of Environmental Sustainability. The specialized knowledge and understanding skills of the various sectors of Environmental Sustainability will make it possible to identify and interpret the epistemological bases, the biophysical foundations and the methods of analysis and interpretation in the field of investigations on the environmental sustainability of different systems. This knowledge ranges from methods of environmental accounting on a physical basis to the use of specialized instruments and software and will make it possible to act autonomously in formulating and/or applying ideas and solutions in the field of environmental sustainability, often in a research context. This cultural development, fueled globally by the United Nations' 2030 Agenda and the Sustainable Development Goals (SDG), will be supported by the ability to update through international scientific literature, which will enable students to contribute to the progressive dissemination of the concept of sustainability in all areas of life.
